We now study limiting activities. Let us define, as in (
2.42
), the candidate for the limit of
\mathsf{M}_{\beta,\lambda}(S)
for
|S|\leq p^{*}(\beta)
. Recall from (
2.38
) that

\mathsf{M}_{\beta,\lambda}(S)=\mathbb{E}_{\mu_{\beta,\lambda}^{\otimes|S|}}\left[\mathbf{1}_{\mathcal{B}_{S}}\prod_{i,j\in S:i<j}e^{-\beta v_{ij}}\mathbf{1}_{\mathcal{A}_{ij}}\right].

There exists a function
G_{S}:(\mathbb{R}^{2})^{2|S|-1}\to\mathbb{R}
such that

(4.45)

\mathbf{1}_{\mathcal{B}_{S}}\prod_{i,j\in S:i<j}e^{-\beta v_{ij}}\mathbf{1}_{\mathcal{A}_{ij}}\prod_{i\in S}e^{\beta\mathsf{g}_{\lambda}(x_{i}-y_{i})}\mathbf{1}_{x_{i}\in\Lambda}\mathbf{1}_{y_{i}\in\Lambda}=G_{S}(x_{2}-x_{1},\ldots,x_{|S|}-x_{1},y_{1}-x_{1},\ldots,y_{|S|}-x_{1}).

We thus set

(4.46)

\mathsf{m}_{\beta,\lambda}(|S|)\coloneqq\frac{\displaystyle{\int_{(\mathbb{R}^{2})^{(2|S|-1)}}G_{S}}}{\displaystyle{\left(\int_{\mathbb{R}^{2}}e^{\beta\mathsf{g}_{\lambda}(x)}\mathrm{d}x\right)^{|S|}}}.

Let
E\in\mathcal{G}_{c}(S)
. Observe that there exists a map
G^{\prime}_{S,E}:(\mathbb{R}^{2})^{2|S|-1}\to\mathbb{R}
such that

(4.47)

\prod_{ij\in E}f_{ij}^{v}\prod_{i\in S}e^{\beta\mathsf{g}_{\lambda}(x_{i}-y_{i})}\mathbf{1}_{x_{i}\in\Lambda}\mathbf{1}_{y_{i}\in\Lambda}=G^{\prime}_{S,E}(x_{2}-x_{1},\ldots,x_{|S|}-x_{1},y_{1}-x_{1},\ldots,y_{|S|}-x_{1}).

Therefore, we set

(4.48)

\mathsf{k}_{\beta,\lambda}^{\mathrm{dip}}(|S|)\coloneqq\frac{\displaystyle{\sum_{E\in\mathcal{G}_{c}(S)}\int_{(\mathbb{R}^{2})^{(2|S|-1)}}G^{\prime}_{S,E}}}{\displaystyle{\left(\int_{\mathbb{R}^{2}}e^{\beta\mathsf{g}_{\lambda}(x)}\mathrm{d}x\right)^{|S|}}}.

Let
X
be a subpartition of
[N]
with
n_{i}
parts of cardinality
i
for every
i\in\{1,\ldots,p(\beta)\}
. Let
E\in\mathsf{E}^{X}
. Again, there exists a map
G^{\prime\prime}_{X,E}:(\mathbb{R}^{2})^{2|V_{X}|-1}\to\mathbb{R}
such that

(4.49)

\prod_{ij\in E}f_{ij}^{v}\prod_{S\in X}\mathbf{1}_{\mathcal{B}_{S}}\prod_{S\in X}\prod_{i,j\in S:i<j}e^{-\beta v_{ij}}\mathbf{1}_{\mathcal{A}_{ij}}\prod_{i\in V_{X}}e^{\beta\mathsf{g}_{\lambda}(x_{i}-y_{i})}\mathbf{1}_{x_{i}\in\Lambda}\mathbf{1}_{y_{i}\in\Lambda}\\
=G^{\prime\prime}_{X,E}(x_{2}-x_{1},\ldots,x_{|V_{X}|}-x_{1},y_{1}-x_{1},\ldots,y_{|V_{X}|}-x_{1}).

Hence, whenever
|V_{X}|\leq p^{*}(\beta)
, we set

(4.50)

\mathsf{k}_{\beta,\lambda}^{\mathrm{mult}}(n_{1},\ldots,n_{p(\beta)})=\frac{\displaystyle{\sum_{E\in\mathsf{E}^{X}}\int_{(\mathbb{R}^{2})^{(2|V|-1)}}G^{\prime\prime}_{X,E}}}{\displaystyle{\prod_{S\in X}\int_{(\mathbb{R}^{2})^{(2|S|-1)}}G_{S}}}.

In the following lemma, we assert that the quantities
\mathsf{k}_{\beta,\lambda}^{\mathrm{dip}}
,
\mathsf{m}_{\beta,\lambda}
and
\mathsf{k}_{\beta,\lambda}^{\mathrm{mult}}
are well defined and are indeed the limits of the normalized activities.

Lemma 4.22

(Limiting activities)
.

Let
\beta\in(2,+\infty)
and let
p^{*}(\beta)
be as in (
2.30
).

Let
S\subset[N]
with
|S|\leq p^{*}(\beta)
. The quantity (
4.46
) is well defined. Moreover,

(4.51)

\lim_{N\to\infty}N^{|S|-1}\mathsf{M}_{\beta,\lambda}(S)=\mathsf{m}_{\beta,\lambda}(|S|).

The quantity (
4.48
) is well defined. Moreover,

(4.52)

\lim_{N\to\infty}N^{|S|-1}\mathsf{K}^{\mathrm{dip}}_{\beta,\lambda}(S)=\mathsf{k}^{\mathrm{dip}}_{\beta,\lambda}(|S|).

Let
X
be a subpartition of
[N]
such that
|V_{X}|\leq p^{*}(\beta)
. Suppose that
X
has
n_{i}
elements of cardinality
i
for every
i=1,\ldots,p(\beta)
and no element of cardinality strictly larger than
p(\beta)
. Then, the quantity
\mathsf{k}_{\beta,\lambda}^{\mathrm{mult}}(n_{1},\ldots,n_{p(\beta)})
(
4.50
) is well defined. Moreover,

(4.53)

\lim_{N\to\infty}N^{|X|-1}\mathsf{K}_{\beta,\lambda}^{\mathrm{mult}}(X)=\mathsf{k}_{\beta,\lambda}^{\mathrm{mult}}(n_{1},\dots,n_{p(\beta)}).

The proof of Lemma
4.22
is postponed to Section
4.14
.

4.8.
Control of activities for unbounded clusters, statement

We next state the main result of this section, which is the control of activities for possibly unbounded clusters.

Proposition 4.23

(Absolute convergence of the cluster expansion series)
.

Let
\beta\in(2,\infty)
, and let
p(\beta)
be as in Definition
2.7
. Let
\varepsilon_{0}\in(0,1)
.
Let
\pi
be a partition of
[N]
such that for every
S\in\pi
, one has
|S|\leq p(\beta)
. Assume that for every
k\in\{2,\ldots,p(\beta)\}
,

(4.54)

n_{k}\coloneqq\#_{k}(\pi)\leq\varepsilon_{0}^{-\alpha(\beta)}N\lambda^{2(k-1)},

where
\alpha(\beta)
is as in (
4.21
).

For
M
large enough with respect to
\beta
and
p(\beta)
,
\varepsilon_{0}
small enough with respect to
\beta
,
p(\beta)
and
M
, and
\lambda
small enough, there exists
C>0
depending only on
\beta,M,p(\beta)
and
\varepsilon_{0}
such that

(4.55)

\sum_{X\in\mathcal{P}(\pi)}|\mathsf{K}_{\varepsilon_{0}}^{-}(X)|\mathbf{1}_{|V_{X}|>p(\beta)}\leq CN\delta_{\beta,\lambda},

with
\delta_{\beta,\lambda}
as in (
2.34
), in particular the series
\sum_{X\in\mathcal{P}(\pi)}\mathsf{K}_{\varepsilon_{0}}^{-}(X)
is absolutely convergent.

Moreover, there exists
C>0
depending only on
\beta,M,p(\beta)
and
\varepsilon_{0}
such that

(4.56)

\sum_{n=1}^{\infty}\frac{1}{n!}\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}X_{1},\ldots,X_{n}\in\mathcal{P}(\pi)\\
\mathrm{connected},\,\exists i,|V_{X_{i}}|>p(\beta)\end{subarray}}|\mathsf{K}_{\varepsilon_{0}}^{-}(X_{1})\ldots\mathsf{K}_{\varepsilon_{0}}^{-}(X_{n})\mathrm{I}(G(X_{1},\ldots,X_{n}))|\\
\leq CN\delta_{\beta,\lambda}.

The first step consists in splitting the Mayer bond
f_{ij}^{\tilde{v}}
into its odd and even parts, and using cancellations of the odd parts to reduce to Eulerian graphs, and then using the Penrose resummation argument.

4.9.
Parity and cancellations

This section contains the cancellation arguments that form the starting point of the proof of the absolute convergence of the cluster expansion series appearing in Lemma
4.16
. Recall that by Lemma
3.10
, the weight
\tilde{v}_{ij}
typically decays as the inverse of the square distance if the distance between dipoles
i
and
j
. The key issue is that
\mathrm{dist}^{-2}
is not integrable at infinity in dimension
2
. Therefore, the absolute convergence of the cluster expansion series, even with truncated weights at distance
\varepsilon_{0}\bar{R}_{\beta,\lambda}
, is nontrivial.

A crucial ingredient in the argument is the observation that if one of the dipoles is flipped, then the interaction is changed into its opposite. In other words, the dipole-dipole interaction is an odd function of both dipole vectors.

Recalling from Definition
4.13
, the Mayer bond
f_{ij}^{\tilde{v}}
is given by

f_{ij}^{\tilde{v}}=e^{-\beta\tilde{v}_{ij}}\mathbf{1}_{\mathcal{A}_{ij}}-1.

One can decompose
f_{ij}^{\tilde{v}}
as
a_{ij}^{\tilde{v}}+b_{ij}^{\tilde{v}}
, where
a_{ij}^{\tilde{v}}
and
b_{ij}^{\tilde{v}}
are given as follows:

Definition 4.24

(Odd and even parts of the Mayer bond)
.

Let
w=(w_{ij})_{i<j}
be a collection of weights. We set

a^{w}_{ij}\coloneqq-\sum_{k\,\mathrm{odd}}\frac{\beta^{k}}{k!}w_{ij}^{k}\mathbf{1}_{\mathcal{A}_{ij}}\quad\mathrm{and}\,\quad b_{ij}^{w}\coloneqq\left(\sum_{k\,\mathrm{even},\,k\neq 0}\frac{\beta^{k}}{k!}w_{ij}^{k}\mathbf{1}_{\mathcal{A}_{ij}}\right)-\mathbf{1}_{\mathcal{A}_{ij}^{c}}.

For later use, we introduce the following weights:

Definition 4.25
.

We let

(4.57)

a^{\mathrm{abs}}_{ij}\coloneqq\frac{r_{i}r_{j}}{d_{ij}\max(r_{i},r_{j},d_{ij})}\mathbf{1}_{\mathcal{B}_{ij}^{c}}\mathbf{1}_{d_{ij}\leq 16\varepsilon_{0}\bar{R}_{\beta,\lambda}}

and

(4.58)

b^{\mathrm{abs}}_{ij}\coloneqq(a_{ij}^{\mathrm{abs}})^{2}+\mathbf{1}_{\mathcal{B}_{ij}}.

Remark 4.26
.

From Lemma
3.10
applied with
\tau=8\varepsilon_{0}\bar{R}_{\beta,\lambda}
, if
\max(r_{i},r_{j})\leq\varepsilon_{0}\bar{R}_{\beta,\lambda}
, we have

(4.59)

|a_{ij}^{\tilde{v}}\mathbf{1}_{\mathcal{B}_{ij}^{c}}|\leq Ca_{ij}^{\mathrm{abs}},

with
C
depending on
\beta
.
Indeed, on
\mathcal{B}_{ij}^{c}
we have
d_{ij}\geq M\min(r_{i},r_{j})
with
M>1
, hence the assumptions of the lemma are verified.

Since
\mathcal{B}_{ij}^{c}\subset\mathcal{A}_{ij}
we have
\mathbf{1}_{\mathcal{A}_{ij}^{c}}\leq\mathbf{1}_{\mathcal{B}_{ij}}
hence, in view of of Lemma
3.10
again, if
\max(r_{i},r_{j})\leq\varepsilon_{0}\bar{R}_{\beta,\lambda}
, we have

(4.60)

|b_{ij}^{\tilde{v}}\mathbf{1}_{\mathcal{B}_{ij}^{c}}|\leq Cb_{ij}^{\mathrm{abs}},

with
C
depending on
\beta
.

Remark 4.27

(Parity)
.

One can observe that for the weight
w=\tilde{v}
we are interested in,
a_{ij}^{w}
is odd in
\vec{r}_{i}
and
\vec{r}_{j}
while
b_{ij}^{w}
is even in
\vec{r}_{i}
and
\vec{r}_{j}
.
Indeed, changing
\vec{r}_{i}
into
-\vec{r}_{i}
keeping the midpoint
z_{i}
fixed (see Lemma
3.4
) corresponds to swapping the positions of
x_{i}
and
y_{i}
. Thus, the quantity
u_{i}
defined in (
3.16
) is changed into
-u_{i}
and by definition (
3.18
),
\tilde{v}_{ij}
is changed into its opposite. Thus
\tilde{v}_{ij}
is odd in
\vec{r}_{i}
and in
\vec{r}_{j}
, keeping
z_{i}
and
z_{j}
fixed.

Lemma 4.28
.

Let
X
be a subpartition of
[N]
. Let
\mathbb{S}^{1}
be the circle in
\mathbb{R}^{2}
.

Let
E_{1},E_{2}
be disjoint sets of edges included in
\mathcal{E}^{\mathrm{inter}}(X)
. For each
ij\in E_{1}\cup E_{2}
, let
a_{ij}:(\mathbb{S}^{1})^{2}\to\mathbb{R}
be a smooth function with the property that
a_{ij}
is an odd function in both variables, i.e.

a_{ij}(-x,y)=-a_{ij}(x,y)=a_{ij}(x,-y)\quad\text{for every $x,y\in\mathbb{S}^{1}$}.

For each
ij\in E_{1}\cup E_{2}
, let
b_{ij}:(\mathbb{S}^{1})^{2}\to\mathbb{R}
be a smooth function with the property that
b_{ij}
is an even function in both variables, i.e.

b_{ij}(-x,y)=b_{ij}(x,y)=b_{ij}(x,-y)\quad\text{for every $x,y\in\mathbb{S}^{1}$}.

For each
S\in X
, let
F_{S}:(\mathbb{S}^{1})^{|S|}\to\mathbb{R}
be a smooth function with the property that

F_{S}(-x_{1},\ldots,-x_{|S|})=F_{S}(x_{1},\ldots,x_{|S|})\quad\text{for every $x_{1},\ldots,x_{|S|}\in\mathbb{S}^{1}$}.

Suppose that there exists
S_{0}\in X
such that
\deg_{E_{1}}(S_{0})
is odd. Then

\int_{(\mathbb{S}^{1})^{|V_{X}|}}\prod_{ij\in E_{1}}a_{ij}(\vec{r}_{i},\vec{r}_{j})\prod_{ij\in E_{2}}b_{ij}(\vec{r}_{i},\vec{r}_{j})\prod_{S\in X}F_{S}((\vec{r}_{i})_{i\in S})\prod_{i\in V_{X}}\mathrm{d}\vec{r}_{i}=0.

Proof.

Performing the change of variables
\vec{r}_{i}\mapsto\vec{r}_{i}
for every
i\in\cup_{S\in X,S\neq S_{0}}S
and
\vec{r}_{i}\mapsto-\vec{r}_{i}
for every
i\in S_{0}
,
we find

\int_{(\mathbb{S}^{1})^{|V_{X}|}}\prod_{ij\in E_{1}}a_{ij}(\vec{r}_{i},\vec{r}_{j})\prod_{ij\in E_{2}}b_{ij}(\vec{r}_{i},\vec{r}_{j})\prod_{S\in X}F_{S}((\vec{r}_{i})_{i\in S})\prod_{i\in V_{X}}\mathrm{d}\vec{r}_{i}=0.

∎

Definition 4.29

(Eulerian graphs relative to
X
)
.

Let
X
be a subpartition of
[N]
and
E\subset\mathcal{E}^{\mathrm{inter}}(X)
. We say that
E\in\mathrm{Eul}^{X}
if

\sum_{i\in S}\deg_{E}(i)\equiv 0\pmod{2}\quad\text{for every }S\in X,

where for every
i\in V_{X}
,
\deg_{E}(i)
denotes the degree of
i
in the graph
(V_{X},E)
.

Moreover, we say that
E\in\mathrm{Eul}_{\mathrm{c}}^{X}
if in addition
E
is connected relative to
X
, i.e. we set

\mathrm{Eul}_{\mathrm{c}}^{X}=\mathsf{E}^{X}\cap\mathrm{Eul}^{X}.

(a)

(b)

Figure 7
.
Unlike the graph in Figure B, the graph in Figure A is not Eulerian relative to the partition
X\coloneqq\{S_{1},S_{2},S_{3}\}
, although its quotient graph is Eulerian.

In other words,
E
is Eulerian relative to
X
if for every
S\in X
,
S
is adjacent to an even number of edges in
E
.

Corollary 4.30
.

Let
X
be a subpartition of
[N]
. We have

(4.61)

\mathsf{K}_{\varepsilon_{0}}^{-}(X)=\sum_{n=0}^{\infty}\frac{1}{n!}\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}X_{1},\ldots,X_{n}\subset X\\
\mathrm{disjoint}\end{subarray}}\prod_{l=1}^{n}\Biggr(\sum_{E_{l,1}\in\mathrm{Eul}^{X_{l}}}\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}E_{l,2}:E_{l,1}\cup E_{l,2}\in\mathsf{E}^{X_{l}}\\
E_{l,1}\cap E_{l,2}=\emptyset\end{subarray}}\Biggr)\sum_{F\in\mathsf{E}^{\mathrm{Coa}_{X}(X_{1},\ldots,X_{n})}}\\
\mathbb{E}_{\mathsf{P}_{X}^{-,\varepsilon_{0}}}\left[\prod_{l=1}^{n}\left(\prod_{ij\in E_{l,1}}a^{\tilde{v}}_{ij}\prod_{ij\in E_{l,2}}b^{\tilde{v}}_{ij}\right)\prod_{ij\in\cup_{l}\mathcal{E}^{\mathrm{inter}}(X_{l})}\mathbf{1}_{\mathcal{B}_{ij}^{c}}\prod_{ij\in F}(-\mathbf{1}_{\mathcal{B}_{ij}})\right].

Proof.

Starting back from (
4.19
), the proof follows from Lemma
4.28
and the fact that both
\mathcal{B}_{ij}
and
\mathcal{B}_{ij}^{c}
are even in
\vec{r}_{i}
and
\vec{r}_{j}
keeping the midpoints
z_{i}
and
z_{j}
fixed, and the fact that
\mathbf{1}_{z_{i}+\vec{r}_{i}\in\Lambda}\mathbf{1}_{z_{i}-\vec{r}_{i}\in\Lambda}
is even.
∎

4.10.
Reduction to 2-edge-connected graphs

We recall here some standard graph notions that we next adapt to our partition setting.

Definition 4.31

(2-edge-connected graphs)
.

Let
G=(V,E)
be a finite graph.

(1)

One says that
G
is 2-edge-connected if for every
e\in E
, the graph
(V,E\setminus\{e\})
is connected.

(2)

One says that
G
is minimally 2-edge-connected if for every
e\in E
, the graph
(V,E\setminus\{e\})
is not 2-edge-connected.

(3)

One says that an edge
e
is a bridge in
G
if
(V,E\setminus\{e\})
is disconnected.

Definition 4.32

(2-edge-connected graph relative to a partition)
.

Let
X
be a subpartition of
[N]
, let
E\in\mathsf{E}^{X}
and set
G\coloneqq(V_{X},E)
.

(1)

One says that
G
is 2-edge-connected relative to
X
if for every
e\in E
, the graph
(V_{X},E\setminus\{e\})
is connected relative to
X
. This means that
(V_{X},E\cup\mathcal{E}^{\mathrm{intra}}(X))
has no bridge in
E
.

(2)

One says that
G
is minimally 2-edge-connected relative to
X
if for every
e\in E
, the graph
(V_{X},E\setminus\{e\})
is not 2-edge-connected relative to
X
.

Lemma 4.33
.

Let
X
be a subpartition of
[N]
and
E\in\mathrm{Eul}_{\mathrm{c}}^{X}
. Then,
(V_{X},E)
is 2-edge-connected relative to
X
.

Proof.

Suppose by contradiction that
(V_{X},E\cup\mathcal{E}^{\mathrm{intra}}(X))
admits a bridge
ab\in E
. Let
X^{\prime}
be the connected component of
[a]^{X}
in
(V_{X},E\setminus\{ab\})/X
. Let
E^{\prime}
be the set of edges in
E\setminus\{ab\}
adjacent to some vertex in
X^{\prime}
.

Then, since
E\in\mathrm{Eul}_{\mathrm{c}}^{X}
, the degree of
[a]^{X}
in
E
is even. Hence, the degree of
[a]^{X}
in
E^{\prime}
is odd. By the handshaking lemma,

\sum_{S\in X^{\prime}}\deg_{E^{\prime}}(S)=2(|E^{\prime}|-1).

Therefore, there is an even number of
S\in X^{\prime}
such that
\deg_{E^{\prime}}(S)
is odd. Hence, there exists
S\in X^{\prime}
with
S\neq[a]^{X}
such that
\deg_{E^{\prime}}(S)
is odd. Since
ab
is a bridge,
b
is not in
X^{\prime}
, hence
\deg_{E^{\prime}}(S)=\deg_{E}(S)
, which is odd, a contradiction with the fact that
E\in\mathrm{Eul}_{\mathrm{c}}^{X}
.∎

Definition 4.34

(Peeling into a minimal 2-edge-connected graph)
.

Let
X
be a subpartition of
[N]
and
E\in\mathsf{E}^{X}
be such that
(V_{X},E)
is 2-edge-connected relative to
X
. We select a minimal subset of edges
E^{\prime}\subset E
such that the graph
(V_{X},E^{\prime}\cup\mathcal{E}^{\mathrm{intra}})
is 2-edge-connected.
(If several exist, select one according to the lexicographical order.)

Define
\mathrm{Peeled}_{X}(E)\coloneqq E^{\prime}
.

Definition 4.35

(Strict ear decomposition)
.

Let
X
be a subpartition of
[N]
and let
E\in\mathsf{E}^{X}
. A sequence of graphs
(P_{1},\ldots,P_{K})
is called a strict ear decomposition of
(V_{X},E)
if
P_{1}\cup\cdots\cup P_{K}=(V_{X},E)
and;

(1)

The multigraph quotient (see Definition
4.9
) of
P_{1}
by
X
is a simple cycle.

(2)

For every
i=2,\ldots,K
, the multigraph quotient of
P_{i}
by
X
is a simple path with at least two vertices, at least one vertex which is not an endpoint, endpoints in
(P_{1}\cup\cdots\cup P_{i-1})/X
, and vertices that are not endpoints disjoint from
(P_{1}\cup\cdots\cup P_{i-1})/X
.

See Figure
8
for an example of a strict ear decomposition. A classical theorem
[
Die12
, Prop 3.1.1]
asserts that a graph admits an ear decomposition if and only if it is
2
-edge-connected. One can show that a minimally
2
-edge-connected graph admits a strict ear decomposition.

(a)

(b)

Figure 8
.
In Figure A, a strict ear decomposition of a minimal
2
-edge-connected graph. In Figure B, a spanning tree obtained by opening each ear and the base cycle.

Lemma 4.36
.

Let
X
be a subpartition of
[N]
,
E\in\mathsf{E}^{X}
, and
(V_{X},E)
be a minimally 2-edge-connected graph relative to
X
. Then
(V_{X},E)
admits a strict ear decomposition relative to
X
.

The proof is omitted.

4.11.
Peeling lemma

Our next goal is to handle the contribution of the
a_{ij}^{\tilde{v}}
terms appearing in (
4.61
). The cancellation in the interaction has already allowed us to reduce to Eulerian graphs.

Remark 4.37

(Why a naive strategy fails)
.

We claim that a crude peeling procedure fails as soon as
\beta\geq 3
. Suppose to simplify that
\beta\in(3,4)
and let
p\geq 1
be such that
\beta\in(\beta_{p},\beta_{p+1}]
. Suppose also that
X
is made only of pure dipoles, and that
(V_{X},E_{1})
is connected. Denote
k\coloneqq|V_{X}|
. Recall
d_{ij}=\mathrm{dist}(\{x_{i},y_{i}\},\{x_{j},y_{j}\})
.

We work on the event where the edge in
E_{1}
such that
d_{e}
is maximal is fixed and equal to
e_{0}
. Let
T
be a spanning tree of
(V_{X},E_{1})
, which does not contain
e_{0}
(which is possible since
E_{1}
is Eulerian, hence not a tree). We have

(4.62)

\prod_{ij\in E_{1}}\frac{r_{i}r_{j}}{d_{ij}\max(d_{ij},r_{i},r_{j})}\leq\prod_{ij\in T}\frac{r_{i}r_{j}}{d_{ij}\max(d_{ij},r_{i},r_{j})}\min\Bigr(\frac{\max_{i\in V_{X}}r_{i}}{\max_{i\in T}d_{e}},1\Bigr)^{2}.

Integrating the above under the non-normalized dipole measure
(C_{\beta,\lambda,\varepsilon_{0}}\mathrm{d}\mu_{\beta,\lambda,\varepsilon_{0}})^{\otimes k}
of Definition
4.10
, and integrating out the
d_{ij}
, we reduce to controlling

N\int\prod_{ij\in T}(r_{i}r_{j})\prod_{i\in V_{X}}e^{\beta\mathsf{g}_{\lambda}(r_{i})}r_{i}\mathrm{d}r_{i}=N\int\prod_{i\in V_{X}}r_{i}^{\deg_{T}(i)}e^{\beta\mathsf{g}_{\lambda}(r_{i})}r_{i}\mathrm{d}r_{i}\leq CN\int\prod_{i\in V_{X}}\max(r_{i},\lambda)^{\deg_{T}(i)+1-\beta}.

Because
\beta>3
, for every leaf
i
of
T
the exponent
\deg_{T}(i)+1-\beta=-1-(\beta-3)
is strictly less than
-1
; thus the corresponding
r_{i}
are concentrated around
\lambda
, which spoils the estimate as we shall see below. Integrating over all leaves, as well as all but the largest
r_{i}
at vertices of degree
{}\geq 2
, gives

CN(\lambda^{3-\beta})^{|\mathrm{Leaves}(T)|}\int_{0}^{\varepsilon_{0}R_{\beta,\lambda}}\max(r,\lambda)^{\alpha}\mathrm{d}r,

where, thanks to the handshaking lemma,

\alpha=-1+\sum_{i:\deg_{T}(i)>1}(\deg_{T}(i)+2-\beta)=(4-\beta)k-3+|\mathrm{Leaves}(T)|(\beta-3).

Since
\beta\in(3,4)
, fixing
|\mathrm{Leaves}(T)|
and letting
k
grow forces
\alpha>-1
and hence

\int\prod_{i\in V_{X}}r_{i}^{\deg_{T}(i)}e^{\beta\mathsf{g}_{\lambda}(r_{i})}r_{i}\mathrm{d}r_{i}\leq C(\varepsilon_{0}R_{\beta,\lambda})^{(4-\beta)k-2}\Bigr(\frac{\varepsilon_{0}R_{\beta,\lambda}}{\lambda}\Bigr)^{(\beta-3)|\mathrm{Leaves}(T)|}.

Together with (
4.10
) this yields

\frac{1}{(NC_{\beta,\lambda,\varepsilon_{0}})^{k}}\int_{(\Lambda)^{2k}}\prod_{ij\in E_{1}}|a_{ij}^{\tilde{v}}|\prod_{i\in V_{X}}e^{\beta\mathsf{g}_{\lambda}(x_{i}-y_{i})}\mathbf{1}_{|x_{i}-y_{i}|\leq\varepsilon_{0}R_{\beta,\lambda}}\mathrm{d}x_{i}\mathrm{d}y_{i}\\
\leq CN^{1-k}\lambda^{(2-\beta)k}R_{\beta,\lambda}^{(4-\beta)k-2}\Bigr(\frac{R_{\beta,\lambda}}{\lambda}\Bigr)^{(\beta-3)l_{0}},

for every
l_{0}\geq 1
, once
k\gg_{p,l_{0}}1
. Using
R_{\beta,\lambda}^{4-\beta}=\lambda^{2-\beta}
this simplifies to

|\mathsf{K}_{\varepsilon_{0}}^{-}(X)|\leq CN^{1-k}R_{\beta,\lambda}^{-2}\Bigr(\frac{R_{\beta,\lambda}}{\lambda}\Bigr)^{(\beta-3)l_{0}}.

In contrast, Proposition
5.17
asserts that, whenever
k>p
and
\beta\neq\beta_{p+1}
,

|\mathsf{K}_{\varepsilon_{0}}^{-}(X)|\leq CN^{1-k}R_{\beta,\lambda}^{-2}.

Hence the naive approach, based on the crude estimate (
4.62
), is insufficiently sharp when
\beta\in(3,4)
(and for
\beta\geq 4
as well).

In fact, we need to incorporate some geometric constraints on the loops and some properties of Eulerian graphs in order to replace the crude peeling procedure and the control (
4.62
). The simplest nontrivial case of an Eulerian graph is that of a triangle with edges
12,23,13
, between multipoles reduced to singletons (i.e. pure dipoles).
Let us describe our procedure in that case to give an idea (as already done in the introduction in Section
2.5
).

For the sake of exposition, we assume that
d_{ij}\geq\max(r_{i},r_{j})
for every edge. By Lemma
3.10
,

(4.63)

|a_{12}^{\tilde{v}}||a_{23}^{\tilde{v}}||a_{31}^{\tilde{v}}|\leq C\frac{r_{1}r_{2}}{d_{12}^{2}}\frac{r_{2}r_{3}}{d_{23}^{2}}\frac{r_{3}r_{1}}{d_{13}^{2}}.

We assume that
r_{1}
is the largest of
r_{1},r_{2},r_{3}
, and up to changing the labeling, that
d_{12}\geq d_{31}
.
We may then define

g_{12}\coloneqq\frac{r_{1}^{2}}{d_{12}^{2}}\leq 1.

Inserting this to (
4.63
) gives

\frac{r_{1}r_{2}}{d_{12}^{2}}\frac{r_{2}r_{3}}{d_{23}^{2}}\frac{r_{3}r_{1}}{d_{13}^{2}}=\frac{r_{1}^{2}r_{2}^{2}r_{3}^{2}}{d_{12}^{2}d_{23}^{2}d_{13}^{2}}\leq g_{12}\frac{r_{2}^{2}r_{3}^{2}}{d_{13}^{2}d_{23}^{2}}.

Thus, instead of (
4.63
), we have bounded the product of the
|a_{ij}^{\tilde{v}}|
by a product where the largest
r_{i}^{2}
has been removed, the edge
12
has been removed from the product in the denominator, and replaced by a multiplicative “error”
g_{12}
, which is smaller than
1
. This means that in effect, we have “opened” the triangle and replaced it by the tree
13,23
, while removing from (
4.63
) the largest
r_{i}^{2}
.

We next explain how to generalize this to arbitrary Eulerian graphs. The key point is that if a graph
(V_{X},E)
is Eulerian relative to
X
, then the augmented graph
(V_{X},E\cup\mathcal{E}^{\mathrm{intra}})
is 2-edge-connected, i.e.
(V_{X},E)
is 2-edge-connected relative to
X
. We will consider a minimally 2-edge-connected subgraph of
(V_{X},E)
relative to
X
, which as such, admits a strict ear decomposition (see Definition
4.35
and Lemma
4.36
). To construct the desired spanning tree of
(V_{X},E)
, we will then open each ear by removing a carefully chosen edge similarly
to what we did for the triangle case.

We first isolate the largest dipole size in each multipole.

Definition 4.38
.

For every
S\subset[N]
, we let
r_{S}\coloneqq\max_{i\in S}r_{i}
.

Definition 4.39

(Peeling of a minimally
2
-edge-connected graph)
.

Let
X
be a subpartition of
[N]
and let

(x_{i},y_{i})_{i\in V_{X}}\in(\Lambda^{2})^{|V_{X}|}
.
For an edge
ij
, recall

d_{ij}\coloneqq\mathrm{dist}\bigl(\{x_{i},y_{i}\},\{x_{j},y_{j}\}\bigr).

Let
E\in\mathsf{E}^{X}
be a minimally 2-edge-connected graph relative to
X
.

Let
v_{1}\in V_{X}
be such that
r_{[v_{1}]^{X}}
is maximal within
S\in X
. Let
(P_{1},\ldots,P_{K})
be a strict ear decomposition of
E
relative to
X
such that
[v_{1}]^{X}
is adjacent to some edge in
P_{1}
.
Let

e_{0}\in E\quad\text{with}\quad d_{e_{0}}\coloneqq\max_{ij\in E}d_{ij},

and let
P_{\ell_{0}}
be the (unique) ear containing
e_{0}
.

We now define a deletion rule for each ear
P_{\ell}
together with an orientation on the deleted edge.

(1)

If
\ell=\ell_{0}
and
d_{e_{0}}\geq\max_{i\in V_{X}}r_{i}
,
orient
e_{0}
from the endpoint of smaller radius to the larger, and
set
\vec{\mathcal{S}}(P_{\ell})\coloneqq\{\vec{e}_{0}\}
.

(2)

Suppose either

\ell\neq\ell_{0}
or
\bigl(\ell=\ell_{0}\text{ and }d_{e_{0}}<\max r_{i}\bigr)
.
Write
P_{\ell}
as the ordered simple path

v_{1}v_{2}^{\prime},\,v_{2}v_{3}^{\prime},\dots,\,v_{n-1}v_{n}^{\prime}\quad\text{with}\ [v_{i}^{\prime}]^{X}=[v_{i}]^{X}\qquad(n\geq 3).

•

If

d_{v_{1}v_{2}^{\prime}}\geq\max(r_{v_{1}},r_{v_{2}^{\prime}})

and

d_{v_{n-1}v_{n}^{\prime}}\geq\max(r_{v_{n-1}},r_{v_{n}^{\prime}})
, define

(4.64)

\vec{\mathcal{S}}(P_{\ell})\coloneqq\begin{cases}v_{2}^{\prime}\!\to\!v_{1}&\text{if }r_{v_{1}}\geq r_{v_{n}^{\prime}},\\[2.0pt]
v_{n-1}\!\to\!v_{n}^{\prime}&\text{otherwise}.\end{cases}

•

Otherwise, let
e_{\ell}
be the
lexicographically smallest edge in

\{\,v_{1}v_{2}^{\prime},\,v_{n-1}v_{n}^{\prime}\,\}

that satisfies

d_{e_{\ell}}<\max(r_{u},r_{v})
for its endpoints
u,v
.
Set

\vec{\mathcal{S}}(P_{\ell})\coloneqq\{\vec{e}_{\ell}\}.

(3)

For the base cycle
P_{1}
(if either
\ell_{0}\neq 1
or

d_{e_{0}}<\max r_{i}
):
write it as

(v_{1}v_{2}^{\prime},\,v_{2}v_{3}^{\prime},\dots,\,v_{n}v_{1}^{\prime})

with
[v_{1}]=[v_{1}^{\prime}]
the part of maximal radius in the sense of Definition
4.38
.
Use exactly the same deletion and orientation rule as in (2),
replacing
v_{n-1}v_{n}^{\prime}
by
v_{n}v_{1}^{\prime}
.

Define the oriented discarded-edge set

\vec{\mathcal{F}}^{X}\bigl((x_{i},y_{i})_{i\in V_{X}},E\bigr)\coloneqq\bigcup_{\ell=1}^{K}\vec{\mathcal{S}}(P_{\ell}),

let

\mathcal{F}^{X}((x_{i},y_{i}),E)
be its undirected version, and put

\mathcal{T}^{X}((x_{i},y_{i})_{i\in V_{X}},E)\coloneqq E\setminus\mathcal{F}^{X}((x_{i},y_{i})_{i\in V_{X}},E).

The edges in
\mathcal{F}^{X}
are “peeled off”, while the set

\mathcal{T}^{X}\in\mathsf{T}^{X}
.

Definition 4.40

(Peeling of a Eulerian graph)
.

Let
X
be a subpartition of
[N]
and let
E\in\mathrm{Eul}_{\mathrm{c}}^{X}
. Recall
\mathrm{Peeled}_{X}(E)
from Definition
4.34
. We extend the map
\mathcal{T}^{X}
,
\vec{F}^{X}
and
\mathcal{F}^{X}
from Definition
4.39
by setting

\displaystyle\mathcal{T}^{X}(\cdot,E)

\displaystyle\coloneqq\mathcal{T}^{X}(\cdot,\mathrm{Peeled}_{X}(E)),

\displaystyle\vec{\mathcal{F}}^{X}(\cdot,E)

\displaystyle\coloneqq\vec{\mathcal{F}}^{X}(\cdot,\mathrm{Peeled}_{X}(E)),

\displaystyle\mathcal{F}^{X}(\cdot,E)

\displaystyle\coloneqq\mathcal{F}^{X}(\cdot,\mathrm{Peeled}_{X}(E)).

The next lemma allows us to control the product of the weights
|a_{ij}^{\tilde{v}}|
on the edges of each ear (of the strict ear decomposition) in terms of the product of the
r_{i}^{2}
of the internal vertices of that ear.

Lemma 4.41

(Opening of the paths and cycles)
.

Let
X
be a subpartition of
[N]
and let
E^{\prime}\in\mathrm{Eul}_{\mathrm{c}}^{X}
. Let
a_{ij}^{\mathrm{abs}}
be as in Definition
4.25
. Let
e_{0}
be the edge
e\in E^{\prime}
such that
d_{e}
is maximal.

Let
P=(v_{1}v_{2}^{\prime},v_{2}v_{3}^{\prime},\ldots,v_{n-1}v_{n}^{\prime})
be
such that for every
i=2,\ldots,n-1
,
[v_{i}^{\prime}]^{X}=[v_{i}]^{X}
. With the same notation as in the previous definition, suppose up to reversing the orientation along the path, that
\vec{\mathcal{S}}(P)=v_{1}\to v_{2}^{\prime}
. Then, there exists a constant
C>0
depending on
\beta
such that

(4.65)

\displaystyle\prod_{ij\in P}a_{ij}^{\mathrm{abs}}

\displaystyle\leq C^{|P|}\prod_{i=2}^{n-1}(r_{v_{i}}r_{v_{i}^{\prime}})\Biggr(\prod_{ij\in P\setminus\{v_{1}v_{2}^{\prime}\}\cup\{v_{n-1}v_{n}^{\prime}\}}\frac{1}{d_{ij}^{2}}\mathbf{1}_{d_{ij}\leq 16\varepsilon_{0}\bar{R}_{\beta,\lambda}}\mathbf{1}_{\mathcal{B}_{ij}^{c}}\Biggr)

\displaystyle\times\left(\max\left(\frac{1}{d_{v_{1}v_{2}^{\prime}}^{2}},\frac{1}{d_{v_{n-1}v_{n}^{\prime}}^{2}}\right)\mathbf{1}_{d_{v_{1}v_{2}^{\prime}}<\max(r_{v_{1}},r_{v_{2}^{\prime}})}+\frac{1}{d_{v_{n-1v_{n}^{\prime}}}^{2}}\mathbf{1}_{d_{v_{1}v_{2}^{\prime}\geq\max(r_{v_{1}},r_{v_{2}^{\prime}})}}\right)g_{v_{1}v_{2}^{\prime}},

where for every
i,j\in[N],i\neq j
,

(4.66)

g_{ij}\coloneqq\mathbf{1}_{\mathcal{B}_{ij}^{c}}\begin{cases}\mathbf{1}_{d_{ij}\leq 16\varepsilon_{0}\bar{R}_{\beta,\lambda}}&\text{if $d_{ij}<\max(r_{i},r_{j})$}\\
\frac{\max_{i\in V_{X}}r_{i}^{2}}{d_{e_{0}}^{2}}\mathbf{1}_{d_{e_{0}}\leq 16\varepsilon_{0}\bar{R}_{\beta,\lambda}}&\text{if $d_{ij}\geq\max_{i\in V_{X}}r_{i}$ \text{and $ij=e_{0}$}}\\
\frac{\max(r_{i},r_{j})^{2}}{d_{ij}^{2}}\mathbf{1}_{d_{ij}\leq 16\varepsilon_{0}\bar{R}_{\beta,\lambda}}&\text{otherwise}.\end{cases}

Figure 9
.
Opening of the path
(v_{1}v_{2},v_{2}v_{3},\ldots,v_{n-1}v_{n})
in the pure dipole case.

Proof of Lemma
4.41
.

Suppose that
e^{\prime}\notin P
, or that
e^{\prime}\in P
and
d_{e^{\prime}}<\max_{i\in V_{X}}r_{i}
. By the definition of
a_{ij}^{\mathrm{abs}}
,

(4.67)

\begin{split}\prod_{ij\in P}a_{ij}^{\mathrm{abs}}&=r_{v_{1}}r_{v_{n}^{\prime}}\prod_{i=2}^{n-1}(r_{v_{i}}r_{v_{i}^{\prime}})\prod_{ij\in P}\frac{1}{d_{ij}\max(d_{ij},r_{i},r_{j})}\mathbf{1}_{d_{ij}\leq 16\varepsilon_{0}\bar{R}_{\beta,\lambda}}\mathbf{1}_{\mathcal{B}_{ij}^{c}}\\
&=C\left(r_{v_{1}}r_{v_{n}^{\prime}}\prod_{ij\in\{v_{1}v_{2}^{\prime}\},\{v_{n-1}v_{n}^{\prime}\}}\frac{1}{d_{ij}\max(d_{ij},r_{i},r_{j})}\mathbf{1}_{d_{ij}\leq 16\varepsilon_{0}\bar{R}_{\beta,\lambda}}\right)\\
&\times\prod_{i=2}^{n-1}(r_{v_{i}}r_{v_{i}^{\prime}})\left(\prod_{ij\in P\setminus\{v_{1}v_{2}^{\prime},v_{n-1}v_{n}^{\prime}\}}\frac{1}{d_{ij}\max(d_{ij},r_{i},r_{j})}\mathbf{1}_{d_{ij}\leq 16\varepsilon_{0}\bar{R}_{\beta,\lambda}}\right)\prod_{ij\in P}\mathbf{1}_{\mathcal{B}_{ij}^{c}}.\end{split}

Suppose that
d_{v_{1}v_{2}^{\prime}}\geq\max(r_{v_{1}},r_{v_{2}^{\prime}})
and
d_{v_{n-1}v_{n}^{\prime}}\geq\max(r_{v_{n-1}},r_{v_{n}^{\prime}})
. Then,

r_{v_{1}}r_{v_{n}^{\prime}}\prod_{ij\in\{v_{1}v_{2}^{\prime}\},\{v_{n-1}v_{n}^{\prime}\}}\frac{1}{d_{ij}\max(d_{ij},r_{i},r_{j})}\mathbf{1}_{d_{ij}\leq 16\varepsilon_{0}\bar{R}_{\beta,\lambda}}\\
\leq C\frac{1}{d_{v_{n-1}v_{n}^{\prime}}^{2}}\frac{r_{v_{1}}r_{v_{n}^{\prime}}}{d_{v_{1}v_{2}^{\prime}}^{2}}\mathbf{1}_{d_{v_{n-1}v_{n}^{\prime}}\leq 16\varepsilon_{0}\bar{R}_{\beta,\lambda}}\mathbf{1}_{d_{v_{1}v_{2}^{\prime}}\leq 16\varepsilon_{0}\bar{R}_{\beta,\lambda}}.

By Definition
4.40
and our assumption on
\vec{\mathcal{S}}(P)
, in this case we have
r_{v_{1}}\geq r_{v_{n}^{\prime}}
. Thus,

r_{v_{1}}r_{v_{n}^{\prime}}\prod_{ij\in\{v_{1}v_{2}^{\prime}\},\{v_{n-1}v_{n}^{\prime}\}}\frac{1}{d_{ij}\max(d_{ij},r_{i},r_{j})}\mathbf{1}_{d_{ij}\leq 16\varepsilon_{0}\bar{R}_{\beta,\lambda}}\leq C\Bigr(\frac{1}{d_{v_{n-1}v_{n}^{\prime}}^{2}}\mathbf{1}_{d_{v_{n-1}v_{n}^{\prime}}\leq 16\varepsilon_{0}\bar{R}_{\beta,\lambda}}\Bigr)g_{v_{1}v_{2}^{\prime}}.

Suppose that
d_{v_{1}v_{2}^{\prime}}<\max(r_{v_{1}},r_{v_{2}^{\prime}})
. Then, recalling that we assumed
\vec{\mathcal{S}}(P)=v_{1}\to v_{2}^{\prime}
, we have

r_{v_{1}}r_{v_{n}^{\prime}}\prod_{ij\in\{v_{1}v_{2}^{\prime}\},\{v_{n-1}v_{n}^{\prime}\}}\frac{1}{d_{ij}\max(d_{ij},r_{i},r_{j})}\mathbf{1}_{d_{ij}\leq 16\varepsilon_{0}\bar{R}_{\beta,\lambda}}\leq\frac{C}{d_{v_{1}v_{2}^{\prime}}d_{v_{n-1}v_{n}^{\prime}}}\mathbf{1}_{d_{v_{1}v_{2}^{\prime}}\leq 16\varepsilon_{0}\bar{R}_{\beta,\lambda}}\\
\leq C\left(\frac{1}{d_{v_{1}v_{2}^{\prime}}^{2}}+\frac{1}{d_{v_{n}v_{n-1}^{\prime}}^{2}}\right)\mathbf{1}_{d_{v_{1}v_{2}^{\prime}}\leq 16\varepsilon_{0}\bar{R}_{\beta,\lambda}}.

Inserting these into (
4.67
), we obtain the result.

The case where
e_{0}\in P
and
d_{e_{0}}\geq\max_{i\in V_{X}}r_{i}
is straightforward.
∎

Consider a minimally
2
-edge-connected graph, which by Lemma
4.36
admits a strict ear decomposition. We are going to apply Lemma
4.41
successively to each ear, while replacing each
r_{i}
by the maximal one over its multipole.
This allows us to bound the product of the

a_{ij}^{\mathrm{abs}}
over the graph by the product of the
r_{S}^{2}
,
after removing the contribution of the largest one
, keeping the product of the
d_{ij}^{-2}
only on the tree left after peeling, with the multiplicative terms
g_{ij}
appearing for the peeled-out edges. Notice that the terms
g_{ij}
make the cluster expansion series summable (and the integral convergent).

Definition 4.42
.

Let
X
be a subpartition of
[N]
and
T\in\mathsf{T}^{X}
. Let
(x_{i},y_{i})_{i\in V_{X}}\in(\Lambda^{2})^{|V_{X}|}
. We let
B((x_{i},y_{i})_{i\in V_{X}},T)
be the set of
T^{\prime}\in\mathsf{T}^{X}
such that

T^{\prime}\cap\{ij:d_{ij}\geq\max(r_{i},r_{j})\}=T\cap\{ij:d_{ij}\geq\max(r_{i},r_{j})\}.

Corollary 4.43

(Quadratic estimate for minimal 2-edge-connected graphs)
.

Let
X
be a subpartition of
[N]
. Let
a_{ij}^{\mathrm{abs}}
be as in Definition
4.25
. Let
E\in\mathsf{E}^{X}
be such that
(V_{X},E)
is minimally 2-edge-connected relative to
X
.

Let
(x_{i},y_{i})_{i\in V_{X}}\in(\Lambda^{2})^{|V_{X}|}
. Let
T=\mathcal{T}^{X}(\cdot,E)
and
F=\mathcal{F}^{X}(\cdot,E)
be as in Definition
4.40
. Let
B((x_{i},y_{i})_{i\in V_{X}},T)
be as in Definition
4.42
. Let
v_{1}
be the index
i\in V_{X}
such that
r_{i}
is maximal.

Then, there exists a constant
C>0
depending on
\beta
such that

(4.68)

\prod_{ij\in E}a_{ij}^{\mathrm{abs}}\leq C^{|V_{X}|}\prod_{S\in X:S\neq[v_{1}]^{X}}r_{S}^{2}\sum_{T^{\prime}\in B((x_{i},y_{i})_{i\in V_{X}},T)}\prod_{ij\in T^{\prime}}\Bigr(\frac{1}{d_{ij}^{2}}\mathbf{1}_{d_{ij}\leq 16\varepsilon_{0}\bar{R}_{\beta,\lambda}}\mathbf{1}_{\mathcal{B}_{ij}^{c}}\Bigr)\prod_{ij\in F}g_{ij},

where
g_{ij}
is as in (
4.66
).

Proof.

Applying iteratively Lemma
4.41
to every ear of the strict ear decomposition and to the base cycle, we get

(4.69)

\prod_{ij\in E}a_{ij}^{\mathrm{abs}}\leq C^{|E|}\prod_{S\in X:S\neq[v_{1}]^{X}}r_{S}^{2}\sum_{T^{\prime}\in B((x_{i},y_{i})_{i\in V_{X}},T)}\prod_{ij\in T^{\prime}}\Bigr(\frac{1}{d_{ij}^{2}}\mathbf{1}_{d_{ij}\leq 16\varepsilon_{0}\bar{R}_{\beta,\lambda}}\mathbf{1}_{\mathcal{B}_{ij}^{c}}\Bigr)\prod_{ij\in F}g_{ij}.

Since
E
is minimally 2-edge-connected relative to
X
, for every
T\in\mathsf{T}^{X}
,
E\setminus T
is a forest relative to
X
. In particular,
|E\setminus T|\leq|V_{X}|-1
, which yields

|E|\leq 2(|V_{X}|-1).

Inserting this into (
4.69
) concludes the proof.
∎

We now state a technical lemma which will be used later in the activity controls. We begin with a useful definition
[
GW92
]
.

Definition 4.44

(Pseudoforest)
.

Let
V\subset[N]
.
We denote by
\mathrm{PF}(V)
the set of edges
F
on
V
such that
(V,F)
is a pseudoforest, i.e., each connected component of
(V,F)
has at most one cycle. Equivalently
F\in\mathrm{PF}(V)
if and only if
(V,F)
admits an orientation such that the out-degree of every vertex is less than
1
.

Lemma 4.45

(Properties of the peeling)
.

Let
X
be a subpartition of
[N]
such that for each
S\in X
,
|S|\leq p(\beta)
. Let
(x_{i},y_{i})_{i\in V_{X}}\in(\Lambda^{2})^{|V_{X}|}
. Let
E\in\mathrm{Eul}_{\mathrm{c}}^{X}
. Set

T\coloneqq\mathcal{T}^{X}((x_{i},y_{i})_{i\in V_{X}},E)\quad\text{and}\quad F\coloneqq\mathcal{F}^{X}((x_{i},y_{i})_{i\in V_{X}},E).

(1)

Then,
F\in\mathrm{PF}(V_{X})
. Moreover,

(4.70)

\sum_{F\in\mathrm{PF}(V_{X})}\prod_{ij\in F}g_{ij}\leq\sum_{i\in V_{X}}\left(1+\sum_{j\in V_{X}:j\neq i}g_{ij}\right).

(2)

We have

(4.71)

\prod_{ij\in F}g_{ij}\leq\min\Bigr(\frac{\max_{i\in V_{X}}r_{i}}{\max_{e\in T}d_{e}},1\Bigr)^{2}.

(3)

Let
T_{0}^{\prime}\in\mathsf{T}^{X}
. Then, on
\cap_{i,j\in V_{X},i\neq j}\mathcal{B}_{ij}^{c}
, there exists a constant
C
depending on
p(\beta)
such that

(4.72)

|\{T_{0}\in\mathsf{T}^{X}:T_{0}^{\prime}\in B((x_{i},y_{i})_{i\in V_{X}},T_{0})\}|\leq C^{|V_{X}|}\prod_{i\in V_{X}}\log_{M}\Bigr(\frac{16\varepsilon_{0}\bar{R}_{\beta,\lambda}}{r_{i}}\Bigr).

Proof.

Let
\vec{F}\coloneqq\vec{\mathcal{F}}^{X}(\cdot,E)
. We claim that every
i\in V_{X}
has out degree
\leq 1
in
\vec{F}
.

Indeed, by construction, when
i\to j
is added to
\vec{F}
, then
i
is an internal vertex of an ear, glued to the anterior ear at the vertex
j
. Therefore, by definition of a strict ear decomposition, an edge which is outgoing from
i
can only be discarded once. Hence,
F\in\mathrm{PF}(V_{X})
.

For every
F\in\mathrm{PF}(V_{X})
, let
O(F)
be an orientation of
F
such that the out degree of every vertex is less than
1
. Then,

\sum_{F\in\mathrm{PF}(V_{X})}\prod_{ij\in F}g_{ij}=\sum_{F\in\mathrm{PF}(V_{X})}\prod_{i\to j\in O(F)}g_{ij}\leq\sum_{i\in V_{X}}\left(1+\sum_{j\in V_{X}:j\neq i}g_{ij}\right).

where the
1
serves to include the possibility of an empty product.

We turn to the proof of (
4.71
). Suppose that
\max_{e\in T}d_{e}\geq\max_{i\in V_{X}}r_{i}
, otherwise the result is clear. Let
e_{0}
be the index of the largest
d_{e}
for
e\in T\cup F
. Then,
d_{e_{0}}\geq\max_{i\in V_{X}}r_{i}
. Therefore, by definition of the peeling algorithm (see Definition
4.39
, case
\ell=\ell_{0}
), we have that
e_{0}\in F
. Therefore, by (
4.66
),

g_{e_{0}}\leq\Bigr(\frac{\max_{i\in V_{X}}r_{i}}{d_{e_{0}}}\Bigr)^{2}\leq\Bigr(\frac{\max_{i\in V_{X}}r_{i}}{\max_{e\in T}d_{e}}\Bigr)^{2}=\min\Bigr(\frac{\max_{i\in V_{X}}r_{i}}{\max_{e\in T}d_{e}},1\Bigr)^{2}.

Using that
g_{ij}\leq 1
for every
ij\in F\setminus\{e_{0}\}
, this proves (
4.71
).

Finally, we establish (
4.72
). Notice that
T_{0}^{\prime}\in B((x_{i},y_{i})_{i\in V_{X}},T_{0})
is equivalent to
T_{0}\in B((x_{i},y_{i})_{i\in V_{X}},T_{0}^{\prime})
. Hence,

|\{T_{0}\in\mathsf{T}^{X}:T_{0}^{\prime}\in B((x_{i},y_{i})_{i\in V_{X}},T_{0})\}|=|B((x_{i},y_{i})_{i\in V_{X}},T_{0}^{\prime})|.

Let
i\in V_{X}
. For every
t\in(\lambda,16\varepsilon_{0}\bar{R}_{\beta,\lambda})
, let

\mathcal{N}_{i}(t)=\{j\in V_{X}:j\neq i,r_{j}\geq r_{i},Mr_{i}\leq d_{ij}\leq r_{j},d_{ij}\in[t,\tfrac{M}{2}t)\}.

Since we work in
\cap_{i\neq j}\mathbf{1}_{\mathcal{B}_{ij}^{c}}
, we may restrict to the situation where
r_{j}\geq r_{i}
and
d_{ij}\geq Mr_{i}
.
Notice that if
j_{1}
,
j_{2}\in\mathcal{N}_{i}(t)
, then by the triangle inequality,
d_{j_{1}j_{2}}\leq Mt\leq M\min(r_{j_{1}},r_{j_{2}})
. Therefore, recalling Definition
2.2
, we see that
j_{1}
and
j_{2}
are in the same multipole. It follows that
|\mathcal{N}_{i}(t)|\leq p(\beta)
. Therefore, summing over
\tfrac{M}{2}
-adic scales, we get that

|\{j\in V_{X}:j\neq i,r_{j}\geq r_{i},Mr_{i}\leq d_{ij}\leq r_{j}\}|\leq C\log_{M}\Bigr(\frac{16\varepsilon_{0}\bar{R}_{\beta,\lambda}}{r_{i}}\Bigr).

Taking the product of this over
i\in V_{X}
, this concludes the proof of (
4.72
).
∎

4.12.
Control of bounded clusters

We now proceed to the proof of Proposition
4.19
.

Proof of Proposition
4.19
.

Let us define

(4.73)

I_{1}(S)=\int_{(\Lambda^{2})^{|S|}}\mathbf{1}_{\mathcal{B}_{S}}\prod_{i,j\in S:i<j}e^{-\beta\tilde{v}_{ij}}\mathbf{1}_{\mathcal{A}_{ij}}\prod_{i\in S}e^{-L\frac{|x_{i}-y_{i}|^{2}}{(\varepsilon_{0}\bar{R}_{\beta,\lambda})^{2}}}\prod_{i\in S}e^{\beta\mathsf{g}_{\lambda}(x_{i}-y_{i})}\mathbf{1}_{|x_{i}-y_{i}|\leq\varepsilon_{0}\bar{R}_{\beta,\lambda}}\mathrm{d}x_{i}\mathrm{d}y_{i}.

Observe that

\mathsf{M}_{\varepsilon_{0}}^{-}(S)=\frac{1}{(NC_{\beta,\lambda,\varepsilon_{0}})^{|S|}}I_{1}(S).

Taking
Z=\emptyset
,
\tau=8\varepsilon_{0}\bar{R}_{\beta,\lambda}
and
w=\tilde{v}
in Lemma
A.3
, we get that there exists
C>0
depending only on
\beta
,
M
and
|S|
such that

\frac{1}{C}N\lambda^{(2-\beta)|S|+2(|S|-1)}\leq I_{1}(S)\leq CN\lambda^{(2-\beta)|S|+2(|S|-1)}.

Moreover, by (
4.10
), there exists
C>0
depending on
\beta
such that
C_{\beta,\lambda,\varepsilon_{0}}\geq\frac{1}{C}\lambda^{2-\beta}
. This proves (
4.37
) and (
4.38
).

Let us now prove (
4.40
). Set

I^{\mathrm{dip}}_{k}(E)=\int_{(\Lambda^{2})^{|V|}}\prod_{ij\in E}f_{ij}^{v}\prod_{i\in V_{X}}e^{\beta\mathsf{g}_{\lambda}(x_{i}-y_{i})}\mathrm{d}x_{i}\mathrm{d}y_{i}.

In view of Definition
2.4
, the definition of
f_{ij}
and (
4.8
), we have that

\mathsf{K}_{\beta,\lambda}^{\mathrm{dip}}(V)=\sum_{E\in\mathcal{G}_{c}(V)}\frac{I^{\mathrm{dip}}_{|V|}(E)}{(NC_{\beta,\lambda})^{|V|}},

where
\mathcal{G}_{c}(V)
stands for the set of collections of edges
E
on
V
such that
(V,E)
is connected.

In Lemma
A.5
, we show that there exists
C>0
depending on
\beta
and
|V|
such that for every
E\in\mathcal{G}_{c}(V)
,

|I^{\mathrm{dip}}_{k}(E)|\leq CN\lambda^{k(2-\beta)+2(k-1)}.

Besides, recall from Lemma
4.11
that there exists
C>0
depending on
\beta
such that
C_{\beta,\lambda}\geq\frac{1}{C}\lambda^{2-\beta}
. Hence, combining the above relations, and using that the number of graphs is bounded by a constant depending only on
|V|
establishes (
4.40
).

It therefore remains to prove (
4.39
). Let
k=|V_{X}|
. Recall (
4.19
).
We use that

(4.74)

\begin{split}|\mathsf{K}_{\varepsilon_{0}}^{-}(X)|&\leq|\mathsf{E}^{X}|^{2}\max_{\begin{subarray}{c}n\geq 1,X_{1},\ldots,X_{n}\mathrm{disjoint}\\
E_{1}\in\mathsf{E}^{X_{1}},\ldots,E_{n}\in\mathsf{E}^{X_{n}}\\
F\in\mathrm{Coa}_{X}(X_{1},\ldots,X_{n})\end{subarray}}\left|\mathbb{E}_{\mathsf{P}_{X}^{-,\varepsilon_{0}}}\left[\prod_{ij\in E_{1}\cup\cdots\cup E_{n}}f_{ij}^{\tilde{v}}\prod_{ij\in\cup_{l}\mathcal{E}^{\mathrm{inter}}(X_{l})}\mathbf{1}_{\mathcal{B}_{ij}^{c}}\prod_{ij\in F}(-\mathbf{1}_{\mathcal{B}_{ij}})\right]\right|\\
&\leq 2^{2\binom{k}{2}}\max_{\begin{subarray}{c}n\geq 1,X_{1},\ldots,X_{n}\mathrm{disjoint}\\
E_{1}\in\mathsf{E}^{X_{1}},\ldots,E_{n}\in\mathsf{E}^{X_{n}}\\
F\in\mathrm{Coa}_{X}(X_{1},\ldots,X_{n})\end{subarray}}\left|\mathbb{E}_{\mathsf{P}_{X}^{-,\varepsilon_{0}}}\left[\prod_{ij\in E_{1}\cup\cdots\cup E_{n}}f_{ij}^{\tilde{v}}\prod_{ij\in\cup_{l}\mathcal{E}^{\mathrm{inter}}(X_{l})}\mathbf{1}_{\mathcal{B}_{ij}^{c}}\prod_{ij\in F}(-\mathbf{1}_{\mathcal{B}_{ij}})\right]\right|.\end{split}

Moreover, by the parity argument of Lemma
4.28
, there exists
C>0
depending on
k
such that

|\mathsf{K}_{\varepsilon_{0}}^{-}(X)|\leq C\max_{\begin{subarray}{c}n\geq 1,X_{1},\ldots,X_{n}\mathrm{disjoint}\end{subarray}}\Bigr(\prod_{l=1}^{n}\max_{\begin{subarray}{c}E_{l,1}\in\mathrm{Eul}^{X_{l}}\\
E_{l,1}\subset\mathcal{E}^{\mathrm{inter}}(X_{l}):E_{l,1}\cap E_{l,2}=\emptyset\\
E_{l,1}\cup E_{l,2}\in\mathsf{E}^{X_{l}}\end{subarray}}\Bigr)\max_{F\in\mathsf{E}^{\mathrm{Coa}_{X}(X_{1},\ldots,X_{n})}}\\
\left|\mathbb{E}_{\mathsf{P}_{X}^{-,\varepsilon_{0}}}\left[\prod_{ij\in\cup_{l}E_{l,1}}a_{ij}^{\tilde{v}}\prod_{ij\in\cup_{l}E_{l,2}}b_{ij}^{\tilde{v}}\prod_{ij\in\cup_{l}\mathcal{E}^{\mathrm{inter}}(X_{l})}\mathbf{1}_{\mathcal{B}_{ij}^{c}}\prod_{ij\in F}(-\mathbf{1}_{\mathcal{B}_{ij}})\right]\right|.

Recall from Definition
4.25
that there exists
C>0
depending on
\beta
such that
|a_{ij}^{\tilde{v}}|\mathbf{1}_{\mathcal{B}_{ij}^{c}}\leq Ca_{ij}^{\mathrm{abs}}
and
|b_{ij}^{\tilde{v}}|\mathbf{1}_{\mathcal{B}_{ij}^{c}}+\mathbf{1}_{\mathcal{B}_{ij}}\leq Cb_{ij}^{\mathrm{abs}}
. Thus, taking the maximum according to the connected components of the graph
(V_{X},\cup_{l}E_{1,l}\cup\mathcal{E}^{\mathrm{intra}}(X))
first, we get that there exists
C>0
depending on
\beta
and
k
such that

|\mathsf{K}_{\varepsilon_{0}}^{-}(X)|\leq C\max_{\begin{subarray}{c}n\geq 1,X_{1},\ldots,X_{n}\mathrm{disjoint}\end{subarray}}\Bigr(\prod_{l=1}^{n}\max_{E_{l}\in\mathrm{Eul}_{\mathrm{c}}^{X_{l}}}\Bigr)\max_{T^{b}\in\mathsf{T}^{\mathrm{Coa}_{X}(X_{1},\ldots,X_{n})}}\mathbb{E}_{\mathsf{P}_{X}^{-,\varepsilon_{0}}}\left[\prod_{ij\in\cup_{l}E_{l}}a_{ij}^{\mathrm{abs}}\prod_{ij\in T^{b}}b_{ij}^{\mathrm{abs}}\right].

For every
l\in[n]
, let
\hat{S}_{l}(X_{1},\ldots,X_{n})
be the
S\in X_{l}
such that
r_{S}
is maximal. Applying Corollary
4.43
and the estimate (
4.71
) of Lemma
4.45
, we get that there exists
C>0
depending on
\beta,M
and
k
such that

(4.75)

|\mathsf{K}_{\varepsilon_{0}}^{-}(X)|\leq C\max_{\begin{subarray}{c}n\geq 1,X_{1},\ldots,X_{n}\mathrm{disjoint}\end{subarray}}\Bigr(\prod_{l=1}^{n}\max_{E_{l}\in\mathrm{Eul}_{\mathrm{c}}^{X_{l}}}\max_{T_{l}^{a}\in\mathsf{T}^{X_{l}}}\Bigr)\max_{T^{b}\in\mathsf{T}^{\mathrm{Coa}_{X}(X_{1},\ldots,X_{n})}}\\
\frac{C^{k}}{(NC_{\beta,\lambda,\varepsilon_{0}})^{k}\mathsf{M}_{\varepsilon_{0}}^{-}(X)}\int_{(\Lambda^{2})^{k}}\prod_{l=1}^{n}\prod_{\begin{subarray}{c}S\in X_{l}:\\
S\neq\hat{S}_{l}(X_{1},\ldots,X_{n})\end{subarray}}r_{S}^{2}\prod_{l=1}^{n}\frac{1}{d_{ij}^{2}}\mathbf{1}_{d_{ij}\leq 16\varepsilon_{0}\bar{R}_{\beta,\lambda}}\mathbf{1}_{\mathcal{B}_{ij}^{c}}\prod_{ij\in T^{b}}b_{ij}^{\mathrm{abs}}\min\Bigr(\frac{\max_{i\in V_{X}}r_{i}}{\max_{ij\in\cup_{l}T_{l}^{a}}d_{ij}},1\Bigr)^{2}\\
\times\prod_{S\in X}\mathbf{1}_{\mathcal{B}_{S}}\prod_{i\in V_{X}}e^{\beta\mathsf{g}_{\lambda}(x_{i}-y_{i})}\mathbf{1}_{|x_{i}-y_{i}|\leq\varepsilon_{0}\bar{R}_{\beta,\lambda}}\mathrm{d}x_{i}\mathrm{d}y_{i}.

In Lemma
A.4
, we control the integral in the right-hand side of (
4.75
). Inserting (
A.20
) into (
4.75
), there exists
C>0
depending on
\beta,M
and
k
such that

|\mathsf{K}_{\varepsilon_{0}}^{-}(X)|\leq C\frac{N\lambda^{(2-\beta)k}}{(NC_{\beta,\lambda,\varepsilon_{0}})^{k}\mathsf{M}_{\varepsilon_{0}}^{-}(X)}\gamma_{\beta,\lambda,k},

where
\gamma_{\beta,\lambda,k}
is as in Definition
4.18
. Recall that
C_{\beta,\lambda,\varepsilon_{0}}\geq\frac{1}{C}\lambda^{2-\beta}
. Moreover by (
4.37
) and (
4.41
), there exists
C>0
depending on
\beta
,
M
and
k
such that
\mathsf{M}_{\varepsilon_{0}}^{-}(X)\geq\frac{1}{C}\mathsf{M}^{0}_{\varepsilon_{0}}(X)
. Assembling the above concludes the proof of (
4.39
).
∎

4.13.
Control of expansion errors

We can now complete the proof of Proposition
4.20
.

Proof of Proposition
4.20
.

We first prove (
4.41
). Let
I_{1}(S)
be as in (
4.73
) and

I_{1}^{\prime}(S)\coloneqq\int_{(\Lambda^{2})^{|S|}}\prod_{i\in S}\mathbf{1}_{\mathcal{B}_{S}}\prod_{i,j\in S:i<j}e^{-\beta v_{ij}}\mathbf{1}_{\mathcal{A}_{ij}}\prod_{i\in S}e^{\beta\mathsf{g}_{\lambda}(x_{i}-y_{i})}\mathrm{d}x_{i}\mathrm{d}y_{i}.

By Definition
4.12
, notice that

(4.76)

\mathsf{M}_{\varepsilon_{0}}^{-}(S)-\mathsf{M}^{0}_{\infty}(S)=\frac{1}{(NC_{\beta,\lambda,\varepsilon_{0}})^{|S|}}(I_{1}(S)-I_{1}^{\prime}(S))+I_{1}^{\prime}(S)\left(\frac{1}{(NC_{\beta,\lambda,\varepsilon_{0}})^{|S|}}-\frac{1}{(NC_{\beta,\lambda})^{|S|}}\right).

Taking
Z=\emptyset
and
\tau=8\varepsilon_{0}\bar{R}_{\beta,\lambda}
in Lemma
A.6
, we obtain that there exists
C>0
depending on
\beta,M,|S|
and
\varepsilon_{0}
such that

|I_{1}(S)-I_{1}^{\prime}(S)|\leq CN\bar{R}_{\beta,\lambda}^{-2}\lambda^{(2-\beta)|S|}.

Therefore, by (
4.10
), there exists
C>0
depending on
\beta,M,|S|
and
\varepsilon_{0}
such that

(4.77)

\frac{1}{(NC_{\beta,\lambda,\varepsilon_{0}})^{|S|}}|I_{1}(S)-I_{1}^{\prime}(S)|\leq CN^{1-|S|}\bar{R}_{\beta,\lambda}^{-2}.

On the other hand, by (
4.11
), there exists
C>0
depending on
\beta
,
|S|
, and
\varepsilon_{0}
such that

\left|\frac{1}{C_{\beta,\lambda,\varepsilon_{0}}^{|S|}}-\frac{1}{C_{\beta,\lambda}^{|S|}}\right|\leq C\frac{\bar{R}_{\beta,\lambda}^{-2}}{\lambda^{(2-\beta)|S|}}.

In Lemma
A.3
in the Appendix, see (
A.6
), we show that there exists
C>0
depending on
\beta
,
M
, and
|S|
such that

I_{1}^{\prime}(S)\leq CN\lambda^{(2-\beta)|S|+2(|S|-1)}.

Hence, there exists
C>0
depending on
\beta,M,|S|
and
\varepsilon_{0}
such that

(4.78)

I_{1}^{\prime}(S)\left|\frac{1}{(NC_{\beta,\lambda,\varepsilon_{0}})^{|S|}}-\frac{1}{(NC_{\beta,\lambda})^{|S|}}\right|\leq CN^{1-|S|}\lambda^{2(|S|-1)}\bar{R}_{\beta,\lambda}^{-2}\leq CN^{1-|S|}\bar{R}_{\beta,\lambda}^{-2}.

Combining (
4.77
) and (
4.78
) proves (
4.41
).

Let us now prove (
4.43
). Let
X
be a subpartition of
[N]
and set
k\coloneqq|V_{X}|
. Recall
\mathsf{K}_{\varepsilon_{0}}^{-}(X)
and
\mathsf{K}_{\infty}^{0}
are defined in Definition
4.14
. Denote

I_{2}(E_{1},\ldots,E_{n},F)=\int_{(\Lambda^{2})^{k}}\prod_{ij\in\cup_{l}E_{l}}f_{ij}^{\tilde{v}}\prod_{ij\in F}(-\mathbf{1}_{\mathcal{B}_{ij}})\prod_{ij\in\cup_{l=1}^{n}\mathcal{E}^{\mathrm{inter}}(X_{l})}\mathbf{1}_{\mathcal{B}_{ij}^{c}}\left(\prod_{S\in X}\mathbf{1}_{\mathcal{B}_{S}}\prod_{ij\in S:i<j}e^{-\beta\tilde{v}_{ij}}\right)\\
\times\prod_{i\in V_{X}}e^{-L\frac{|x_{i}-y_{i}|^{2}}{\tilde{\tau_{i}}^{2}}}\prod_{i\in V_{X}}e^{\beta\mathsf{g}_{\lambda}(x_{i}-y_{i})}\mathbf{1}_{|x_{i}-y_{i}|\leq\varepsilon_{0}\bar{R}_{\beta,\lambda}}\mathrm{d}x_{i}\mathrm{d}y_{i}

and

I_{2}^{\prime}(E_{1},\ldots,E_{n},F)=\int_{(\Lambda^{2})^{k}}\prod_{ij\in\cup_{l}E_{l}}f_{ij}^{v}\prod_{ij\in F}(-\mathbf{1}_{\mathcal{B}_{ij}})\prod_{ij\in\cup_{l=1}^{n}\mathcal{E}^{\mathrm{inter}}(X_{l})}\mathbf{1}_{\mathcal{B}_{ij}^{c}}\left(\prod_{S\in X}\mathbf{1}_{\mathcal{B}_{S}}\prod_{ij\in S:i<j}e^{-\beta v_{ij}}\right)\\
\times\prod_{i\in V_{X}}e^{\beta\mathsf{g}_{\lambda}(x_{i}-y_{i})}\mathrm{d}x_{i}\mathrm{d}y_{i}

Fix
X_{1},\ldots,X_{n}\subset X
disjoint,
E_{1}\in\mathsf{E}^{X_{1}},\ldots,E_{n}\in\mathsf{E}^{X_{n}}
and
F\in\mathsf{E}^{\mathrm{Coa}_{X}(X_{1},\ldots,X_{n})}
. We may write

\mathbb{E}_{\mathsf{P}_{X}^{-,\varepsilon_{0}}}\left[\prod_{ij\in E_{1}\cup\cdots\cup E_{n}}f^{\tilde{v}}_{ij}\prod_{ij\in\mathcal{E}^{\mathrm{inter}}(X_{1})\cup\cdots\cup\mathcal{E}^{\mathrm{inter}}(X_{n})}\mathbf{1}_{\mathcal{B}_{ij}^{c}}\prod_{ij\in F}(-\mathbf{1}_{\mathcal{B}_{ij}})\right]=\frac{I_{2}(E_{1},\ldots,E_{n},F)}{\mathsf{M}_{\varepsilon_{0}}^{-}(X)(NC_{\beta,\lambda,\varepsilon_{0}})^{k}}

and

\mathbb{E}_{\mathsf{P}_{X}^{-,\infty}}\left[\prod_{ij\in E_{1}\cup\cdots\cup E_{n}}f^{v}_{ij}\prod_{ij\in\mathcal{E}^{\mathrm{inter}}(X_{1})\cup\cdots\cup\mathcal{E}^{\mathrm{inter}}(X_{n})}\mathbf{1}_{\mathcal{B}_{ij}^{c}}\prod_{ij\in F}(-\mathbf{1}_{\mathcal{B}_{ij}})\right]=\frac{I_{2}^{\prime}(E_{1},\ldots,E_{n},F)}{\mathsf{M}^{0}_{\infty}(X)(NC_{\beta,\lambda})^{k}}.

Next, we use

(4.79)

\displaystyle\left|\mathbb{E}_{\mathsf{P}_{X}^{-,\varepsilon_{0}}}\left[\prod_{ij\in E_{1}\cup\cdots\cup E_{n}}f^{\tilde{v}}_{ij}\prod_{ij\in\mathcal{E}^{\mathrm{inter}}(X_{1})\cup\cdots\cup\mathcal{E}^{\mathrm{inter}}(X_{n})}\mathbf{1}_{\mathcal{B}_{ij}^{c}}\prod_{ij\in F}(-\mathbf{1}_{\mathcal{B}_{ij}})\right]\right.

\displaystyle\qquad-\left.\mathbb{E}_{\mathsf{P}_{X}^{-,\infty}}\left[\prod_{ij\in E_{1}\cup\cdots\cup E_{n}}f^{v}_{ij}\prod_{ij\in\mathcal{E}^{\mathrm{inter}}(X_{1})\cup\cdots\cup\mathcal{E}^{\mathrm{inter}}(X_{n})}\mathbf{1}_{\mathcal{B}_{ij}^{c}}\prod_{ij\in F}(-\mathbf{1}_{\mathcal{B}_{ij}})\right]\right|

\displaystyle\qquad\leq\frac{1}{\mathsf{M}_{\varepsilon_{0}}^{-}(X)(NC_{\beta,\lambda,\varepsilon_{0}})^{k}}\Bigr|I_{2}(E_{1},\ldots,E_{n},F)-I_{2}^{\prime}(E_{1},\ldots,E_{n},F)\Bigr|

\displaystyle\qquad+|I_{2}^{\prime}(E_{1},\ldots,E_{n},F)|\times\left|\frac{1}{(NC_{\beta,\lambda,\varepsilon_{0}})^{k}\mathsf{M}_{\varepsilon_{0}}^{-}(X)}-\frac{1}{(NC_{\beta,\lambda})^{k}\mathsf{M}^{0}_{\infty}(X)}\right|.

In Lemma
A.7
, we prove that there exists
C>0
depending on
\beta,M,k
and
\varepsilon_{0}
such that

\Bigr|I_{2}(E_{1},\ldots,E_{n},F)-I_{2}^{\prime}(E_{1},\ldots,E_{n},F)\Bigr|\leq CN\bar{R}_{\beta,\lambda}^{-2}\lambda^{(2-\beta)k}.

Therefore, by Proposition
4.19
and (
4.10
), there exists
C>0
depending on
\beta,M,k
and
\varepsilon_{0}
such that

(4.80)

\frac{1}{\mathsf{M}_{\varepsilon_{0}}^{-}(X)(NC_{\beta,\lambda,\varepsilon_{0}})^{k}}\Bigr|I_{2}(E_{1},\ldots,E_{n},F)-I_{2}^{\prime}(E_{1},\ldots,E_{n},F)\Bigr|\leq\frac{CN^{1-k}}{\mathsf{M}^{0}_{\varepsilon_{0}}(X)}\bar{R}_{\beta,\lambda}^{-2}.

Notice that

\left|\frac{1}{(NC_{\beta,\lambda,\varepsilon_{0}})^{k}\mathsf{M}_{\varepsilon_{0}}^{-}(X)}-\frac{1}{(NC_{\beta,\lambda})^{k}\mathsf{M}^{0}_{\infty}(X)}\right|=\left|\frac{1}{\prod_{S\in X}I_{1}(S)}-\frac{1}{\prod_{S\in X}I_{1}^{\prime}(S)}\right|.

Combining Lemma
A.3
and Lemma
A.6
, we get that there exists
C>0
depending on
\beta,M,k
and
\varepsilon_{0}
such that

\frac{\left|\prod_{S\in X}I_{1}(S)-\prod_{S\in X}I_{1}^{\prime}(S)\right|}{\prod_{S\in X}I_{1}(S)}\leq C\bar{R}_{\beta,\lambda}^{-2}\lambda^{-2(\max_{S\in X}|S|-1)}.

Hence, by (
A.6
), there exists
C>0
depending on
\beta,M,k
and
\varepsilon_{0}
such that

\left|\frac{1}{\prod_{S\in X}I_{1}(S)}-\frac{1}{\prod_{S\in X}I_{1}^{\prime}(S)}\right|\leq C\frac{\bar{R}_{\beta,\lambda}^{-2}\lambda^{-2(\max_{S\in X}|S|-1)}}{N^{|X|}\lambda^{(2-\beta)k}\prod_{S\in X}\lambda^{2(|S|-1)}}.

Moreover, one can check that there exists
C>0
depending on
\beta,M,k
and
\varepsilon_{0}
such that

(4.81)

|I_{2}^{\prime}(E_{1},\ldots,E_{n},F)|\leq CN\lambda^{(2-\beta)k+2(1-k)}.

Assembling the two above displays yields the existence of
C>0
depending on
\beta,M,k
and
\varepsilon_{0}
such that

|I_{2}^{\prime}(E_{1},\ldots,E_{n},F)|\times\left|\frac{1}{\prod_{S\in X}I_{1}(S)}-\frac{1}{\prod_{S\in X}I_{1}^{\prime}(S)}\right|\\
\leq CN^{1-|X|}\bar{R}_{\beta,\lambda}^{-2}\frac{1}{\prod_{S\in X}\lambda^{2(|S|-1)}}\lambda^{2(k-1)}\lambda^{-2(\max_{S\in X}|S|-1)}\leq CN^{1-|X|}\bar{R}_{\beta,\lambda}^{-2}\frac{1}{\prod_{S\in X}\lambda^{2(|S|-1)}}.

Therefore, by (
4.37
), there exists
C>0
depending on
\beta,M,k
and
\varepsilon_{0}
such that

(4.82)

|I_{2}^{\prime}(E_{1},\ldots,E_{n},F)|\times\left|\frac{1}{\prod_{S\in X}I_{1}(S)}-\frac{1}{\prod_{S\in X}I_{1}^{\prime}(S)}\right|\leq\frac{CN^{1-k}}{\mathsf{M}^{0}_{\infty}(X)}\bar{R}_{\beta,\lambda}^{-2}.

Inserting (
4.80
) and (
4.82
) into (
4.79
) gives

\left|\mathbb{E}_{\mathsf{P}_{X}^{-,\varepsilon_{0}}}\left[\prod_{ij\in E_{1}\cup\cdots\cup E_{n}}f^{\tilde{v}}_{ij}\prod_{ij\in\mathcal{E}^{\mathrm{inter}}(X_{1})\cup\cdots\cup\mathcal{E}^{\mathrm{inter}}(X_{n})}\mathbf{1}_{\mathcal{B}_{ij}^{c}}\prod_{ij\in F}(-\mathbf{1}_{\mathcal{B}_{ij}})\right]\right.\\
-\left.\mathbb{E}_{\mathsf{P}_{X}^{-,\infty}}\left[\prod_{ij\in E_{1}\cup\cdots\cup E_{n}}f^{v}_{ij}\prod_{ij\in\mathcal{E}^{\mathrm{inter}}(X_{1})\cup\cdots\cup\mathcal{E}^{\mathrm{inter}}(X_{n})}\mathbf{1}_{\mathcal{B}_{ij}^{c}}\prod_{ij\in F}(-\mathbf{1}_{\mathcal{B}_{ij}})\right]\right|\leq\frac{CN^{1-k}}{\mathsf{M}^{0}_{\infty}(X)}\bar{R}_{\beta,\lambda}^{-2},

for some constant
C>0
depending on
\beta,M,k
and
\varepsilon_{0}
. This concludes the proof of (
4.43
). The estimate (
4.44
) is proved similarly using the estimate (
A.54
) in Lemma
A.7
.

The proof of (
4.42
) follows by combining (
4.11
) and the integral estimate of Lemma
A.8
.
∎

4.14.
Study of limiting activities

Proof of Lemma
4.22
.

Let
G:(\mathbb{R}^{2})^{n-1}\to\mathbb{R}
be integrable. Define

I_{N}\coloneqq\int_{\Lambda^{n}}G(z_{2}-z_{1},z_{3}-z_{1},\ldots,z_{n}-z_{1})\mathrm{d}z_{1}\ldots\mathrm{d}z_{n},

where we recall that
\Lambda=\Lambda(N)=[0,\sqrt{N}]^{2}
. Then, we claim that

(4.83)

\lim_{N\to\infty}\frac{I_{N}}{N}=\int_{(\mathbb{R}^{2})^{n-1}}G(y_{1},\ldots,y_{n-1})\mathrm{d}y_{1}\ldots\mathrm{d}y_{n-1}.

Indeed,

\frac{I_{N}}{N}=\int_{(\mathbb{R}^{2})^{n-1}}G(y_{1},\ldots,y_{n-1})V_{N}(y_{1},\ldots,y_{n-1})\mathrm{d}y_{1}\ldots y_{n-1},

where

V_{N}(y_{1},\ldots,y_{n-1})=\frac{1}{N}|\{x\in\Lambda:x+y_{1}\in\Lambda,\ldots,x+y_{n-1}\in\Lambda\}|,

which is the normalized measure of the intersection
\Lambda\cap(\Lambda-y_{1})\cap\cdots\cap(\Lambda-y_{n-1})
. We can notice that
0\leq V_{N}\leq 1
. Moreover,
V_{N}
converges pointwise to
1
. Therefore, by dominated convergence, we obtain (
4.83
).

Let
S\subset[N]
be such that
|S|\leq p^{*}(\beta)
. Recall that

\mathsf{M}^{0}_{\infty}(S)=\frac{1}{\displaystyle{\left(\int_{\Lambda^{2}}e^{\beta\mathsf{g}_{\lambda}(x-y)}\mathrm{d}x\mathrm{d}y\right)^{|S|}}}\int_{(\Lambda^{2})^{|S|}}\mathbf{1}_{\mathcal{B}_{S}}\prod_{i,j\in S:i<j}e^{-\beta v_{ij}}\mathbf{1}_{\mathcal{A}_{ij}}\prod_{i\in S}e^{\beta\mathsf{g}_{\lambda}(x_{i}-y_{i})}\mathrm{d}x_{i}\mathrm{d}y_{i}.

Since
\beta>2
, the map
e^{\beta\mathsf{g}_{\lambda}}:\mathbb{R}^{2}\to\mathbb{R}
is integrable. Therefore, by (
4.83
),

(4.84)

\int_{\Lambda^{2}}e^{\beta\mathsf{g}_{\lambda}(x-y)}\mathrm{d}x\mathrm{d}y=N\int_{\mathbb{R}^{2}}e^{\beta\mathsf{g}_{\lambda}(y)}\mathrm{d}y+o(N).

Recall from (
4.45
) that there exists a map
G_{S}:(\mathbb{R}^{2})^{2|S|-1}\to\mathbb{R}
such that

\mathbf{1}_{\mathcal{B}_{S}}\prod_{i,j\in S:i<j}e^{-\beta v_{ij}}\mathbf{1}_{\mathcal{A}_{ij}}\prod_{i\in S}e^{\beta\mathsf{g}_{\lambda}(x_{i}-y_{i})}\mathbf{1}_{x_{i}\in\Lambda}\mathbf{1}_{y_{i}\in\Lambda}=G_{S}(x_{2}-x_{1},\ldots,x_{|S|}-x_{1},y_{1}-x_{1},\ldots,y_{|S|}-x_{1}).

Proceeding as in the proof of (
A.6
), one can see that
G_{S}
is integrable (which uses crucially the fact that
|S|\leq p^{*}(\beta)
). Thus, by (
4.83
)

(4.85)

\int_{(\Lambda^{2})^{|S|}}\mathbf{1}_{\mathcal{B}_{S}}\prod_{i,j\in S:i<j}e^{-\beta v_{ij}}\mathbf{1}_{\mathcal{A}_{ij}}\prod_{i\in S}e^{\beta\mathsf{g}_{\lambda}(x_{i}-y_{i})}\mathrm{d}x_{i}\mathrm{d}y_{i}\\
=N\int_{(\mathbb{R}^{2})^{2|S|-1}}G_{S}(y_{1},\ldots,y_{2|S|-1})\mathrm{d}y_{1}\ldots\mathrm{d}y_{2|S|-1}+o(N).

Combining (
4.84
) and (
4.85
) shows that

\lim_{N\to\infty}N^{|S|-1}\mathsf{M}_{\infty}^{0}(S)=\frac{\int_{(\mathbb{R}^{2})^{2|S|-1}}G_{S}(y_{1},\ldots,y_{2|S|-1})\mathrm{d}y_{1}\ldots\mathrm{d}y_{2|S|-1}}{\Bigr(\int_{\mathbb{R}^{2}}e^{\beta\mathsf{g}_{\lambda}(y)}\mathrm{d}y\Bigr)^{|S|}}=\mathsf{m}_{\beta,\lambda}^{\mathrm{dip}}(|S|).

The proof of (
4.51
) and (
4.53
) proceeds along the same lines, resting critically on the bounds
|S|\leq p^{*}(\beta)
and
|V_{X}|\leq p^{*}(\beta)
. Notice that for
E\in\mathcal{G}_{c}(V)
and
F\in\mathsf{E}^{X}
the maps
G_{S,E}^{\prime}
and
G_{X,F}^{\prime\prime}
from (
4.47
) and (
4.49
) can both be written as a sum of integrable functions and a sum of functions of integral
0
(the integrable parts correspond to restricting the subgraphs with odd bond to Eulerian graphs).
∎

4.15.
Kruskal’s algorithm and Penrose resummation

We have already established the bounded-cluster control of Proposition
4.19
, and it remains only to prove the absolute convergence asserted in Proposition
4.23
which concerns large clusters. In (
4.74
), we appealed to the crude bound

|\mathsf{E}^{X}|\leq 2^{\binom{|V_{X}|}{2}},

which grows like
\exp(O(|V_{X}|^{2}))
. Since clusters can now be arbitrarily large, this estimate is too coarse to close the argument. We must therefore control the sum over graphs more delicately.

We recall the well-known Kruskal’s algorithm used to produce a spanning tree on a general connected graph.

Definition 4.46

(Kruskal’s algorithm)
.

Let
X
be a subpartition of
[N]
. Consider the lexicographic order on the edges in
\mathcal{E}^{\mathrm{inter}}(X)
. For every
E\subset\mathcal{E}^{\mathrm{inter}}
, we say that
(V_{X},E)
has a cycle relative to
X
if the augmented graph
(V_{X},E\cup\mathcal{E}^{\mathrm{intra}}(X))
has a cycle that is not included in a single component of
X
.

(1)

Let
E\in\mathsf{E}^{X}
. We construct a spanning tree
T\in\mathsf{T}^{X}
with
T\subset E
by selecting the edges in
E
in increasing order, discarding the edges that form a cycle relative to
X
among the prior edges. This defines a map

(4.86)

\mathcal{T}^{\mathrm{KA}}_{X}:\mathsf{E}^{X}\to\mathsf{T}^{X}.

(2)

Let
T\in\mathsf{T}^{X}
. There exists a maximal
\mathcal{E}^{\mathrm{KA}}_{\max}(X,T)\in\mathsf{E}^{X}
such that
\mathcal{T}^{\mathrm{KA}}_{X}(\mathcal{E}_{\max}^{\mathrm{KA}}(X,T))=T
. The set of edges
\mathcal{E}^{\mathrm{KA}}_{\max}(X,T)
can be constructed as follows: select all edges in
T
. Then select, in the increasing order the edges in
\mathcal{E}^{\mathrm{inter}}(X)\setminus T
by discarding the edges which do not form a cycle in
X
among prior edges.

Below is the well known Penrose lemma, see for instance
[
Bau16
, Section 4]
. It allows to factor out the contribution of trees from a cluster expansion series.

Lemma 4.47

(Penrose resummation)
.

Let
X
be a subpartition of
[N]
. For every
ij\in\mathcal{E}^{\mathrm{inter}}(X)
, let
c_{ij}\in\mathbb{R}
. We have

(4.87)

\sum_{H\in\mathsf{E}^{X}}\prod_{ij\in H}c_{ij}=\sum_{T\in\mathsf{T}^{X}}\prod_{ij\in T}c_{ij}\prod_{ij\in\mathcal{E}_{\max}^{\mathrm{KA}}(X,T)\setminus T}(1+c_{ij}).

Proof.

One has

\begin{split}\sum_{H\in\mathsf{E}^{X}}\prod_{ij\in H}c_{ij}&=\sum_{T\in\mathsf{T}^{X}}\sum_{H:\mathcal{T}^{\mathrm{KA}}_{X}(H)=T}\prod_{ij\in H}c_{ij}\\
&=\sum_{T\in\mathsf{T}^{X}}\prod_{ij\in T}c_{ij}\sum_{H:\mathcal{T}^{\mathrm{KA}}_{X}(H)=T}\prod_{ij\in H\setminus T}c_{ij}.\end{split}

Note that
\mathcal{T}^{\mathrm{KA}}_{X}(H)=T
is equivalent to
T\subset H
and
H\setminus T\subset\mathcal{E}_{\max}^{\mathrm{KA}}(X,T)\setminus T
. Hence

\begin{split}\sum_{H\in\mathsf{E}^{X}}\prod_{ij\in H}c_{ij}&=\sum_{T\in\mathsf{T}^{X}}\prod_{ij\in T}c_{ij}\sum_{H^{\prime}\subset\mathcal{E}_{\max}^{\mathrm{KA}}(X,T)\setminus T}\prod_{ij\in H^{\prime}}c_{ij}\\
&=\sum_{T\in\mathsf{T}^{X}}\prod_{ij\in T}c_{ij}\prod_{ij\in\mathcal{E}^{\mathrm{KA}}_{\max}(X,T)\setminus T}(1+c_{ij}).\end{split}

∎

A consequence of the Penrose resummation lemma is the following bound on the Ursell function recalled in Definition
2.26
. This bound is known as Rota’s theorem and will be used in the proof of Proposition
4.23
in Section
4.18
.

Remark 4.48

(Rota’s theorem)
.

Recall that for any connected graph
G
, the Ursell function
\mathrm{I}(G)
is defined by

\mathrm{I}(G)=\sum_{H\subset G}(-1)^{|E(H)|},

where the sum runs over all spanning graphs
H
of
G
(i.e.
H
connected and with edges included in those of
G
). Recalling the notation from Definition
4.46
, one can rewrite this as

\mathrm{I}(G)=\sum_{T\subset G}\sum_{H:H\subset\mathcal{E}^{\mathrm{KA}}_{\max}(X,T)\setminus T}(-1)^{|E(T)|+|E(H)|}=\sum_{T\subset G}(-1)^{|E(T)|}\prod_{ij\in\mathcal{E}^{\mathrm{KA}}_{\max}(X,T)\setminus T}(1-1),

where the sum runs over spanning trees
T
of
G
. The above product is either
0
if nonempty or
1
if empty. Therefore

(4.88)

|\mathrm{I}(G)|\leq\sum_{T\subset G}1.

We next apply the Penrose resummation lemma to replace the sum over graphs with integrable weights
-\mathbf{1}_{\mathcal{B}_{ij}}
and
b_{ij}^{\tilde{v}}
by a sum over trees with weight
b_{ij}^{\mathrm{abs}}
.

Lemma 4.49

(Penrose resummation for integrable weights)
.

Let
X
be a subpartition of
[N]
. Let
a_{ij}^{\mathrm{abs}}
and
b_{ij}^{\mathrm{abs}}
be as in Definition
4.25
.
Then, there exists
C_{0}>0
depending on
\beta
,
p(\beta)
and
M
such that

(4.89)

|\mathsf{K}_{\varepsilon_{0}}^{-}(X)|\leq C_{0}^{|V_{X}|}\sum_{n=1}^{\infty}\frac{1}{n!}\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}X_{1},\ldots,X_{n}\subset X\\
\mathrm{disjoint}\end{subarray}}\sum_{T^{b}\in\mathsf{T}^{\mathrm{Coa}_{X}(X_{1},\ldots,X_{n})}}\mathbb{E}_{\mathsf{P}_{X}^{-,\varepsilon_{0}}}\left[\prod_{l=1}^{n}\mathcal{L}_{C_{0}}(X_{1})\cdots\mathcal{L}_{C_{0}}(X_{n})\prod_{ij\in T^{b}}b_{ij}^{\mathrm{abs}}\right],

where for every subpartition
X^{\prime}
of
[N]
,

(4.90)

\mathcal{L}_{C}(X^{\prime})\coloneqq\sum_{k=1}^{\infty}\frac{1}{k!}\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}X_{1}^{\prime},\ldots,X_{k}^{\prime}\subset X^{\prime}\\
\mathrm{disjoint}\end{subarray}}\sum_{E_{1}\in\mathrm{Eul}_{\mathrm{c}}^{X_{1}^{\prime}}}\ldots\sum_{E_{k}\in\mathrm{Eul}_{\mathrm{c}}^{X_{k}^{\prime}}}\sum_{\tilde{T}^{b}\in\mathsf{T}^{\mathrm{Coa}_{X^{\prime}}(X_{1}^{\prime},\ldots,X_{k}^{\prime})}}\\
\prod_{ij\in E_{1}\cup\cdots\cup E_{k}}(Ca_{ij}^{\mathrm{abs}})\prod_{ij\in\tilde{T}^{b}}b_{ij}^{\mathrm{abs}}\prod_{ij\in\mathcal{E}^{\mathrm{inter}}(X^{\prime})}e^{Ca_{ij}^{\mathrm{abs}}}\prod_{ij\in\mathcal{E}^{\mathrm{inter}}(X^{\prime})}\mathbf{1}_{\mathcal{B}_{ij}^{c}}.

Proof.

Recall from Corollary
4.30
the formula (
4.61
).
Fix
n\geq 1
,
X_{1},\ldots,X_{n}\subset X
disjoint. Note that even though the
X_{i}
’s are connected, the
X_{i}\cap E_{1}
may not be.

Step 1: summing over graphs for a given component

For every
X^{\prime}\subset X
, set

\mathcal{U}(X^{\prime})\coloneqq\sum_{E_{1}\in\mathrm{Eul}^{X^{\prime}}}\left(\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}E_{2}:E_{1}\cup E_{2}\in\mathsf{E}^{X^{\prime}}\\
E_{1}\cap E_{2}=\emptyset\end{subarray}}\prod_{ij\in E_{1}}a^{\tilde{v}}_{ij}\prod_{ij\in E_{2}}b^{\tilde{v}}_{ij}\right)\prod_{ij\in\mathcal{E}^{\mathrm{inter}}(X^{\prime})}\mathbf{1}_{\mathcal{B}_{ij}^{c}}.

We will later take
X^{\prime}\in\{X_{1},\ldots,X_{n}\}
.

We first resum this according to the connected components
X_{1}^{\prime},\ldots,X_{k}^{\prime}
relative to
X
that contain at least two multipoles of the graph
(V_{X^{\prime}},E_{1})
. This yields

(4.91)

\mathcal{U}(X^{\prime})=\left(\sum_{k=1}^{\infty}\frac{1}{k!}\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}X_{1}^{\prime},\ldots,X_{k}^{\prime}\\
\mathrm{disjoint}\end{subarray}}\sum_{E_{1}\in\mathrm{Eul}_{\mathrm{c}}^{X_{1}^{\prime}},\ldots,E_{k}\in\mathrm{Eul}_{\mathrm{c}}^{X_{k}^{\prime}}}\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}E^{\prime}\subset\mathcal{E}^{\mathrm{inter}}(X^{\prime})\\
(E_{1}\cup\cdots\cup E_{k})\cap E^{\prime}=\emptyset\\
E_{1}\cup\cdots\cup E_{k}\cup E^{\prime}\in\mathsf{E}^{X^{\prime}}\end{subarray}}\prod_{ij\in E_{1}\cup\cdots\cup E_{k}}a^{\tilde{v}}_{ij}\prod_{ij\in E^{\prime}}b^{\tilde{v}}_{ij}\right)\prod_{ij\in\mathcal{E}^{\mathrm{inter}}(X^{\prime})}\mathbf{1}_{\mathcal{B}_{ij}^{c}}.

Fix
k\geq 1
,
X^{\prime}_{1},\ldots,X^{\prime}_{k}\subset X^{\prime}
disjoint and denote for shorthand
Y_{0}\coloneqq\mathrm{Coa}_{X^{\prime}}(X_{1}^{\prime},\ldots,X_{k}^{\prime})
. Let
E_{1}\in\mathsf{E}^{X^{\prime}_{1}},\ldots,E_{n}\in\mathsf{E}^{X^{\prime}_{k}}
. We use an argument similar to Lemma
4.47
. First, as in Step 7 of the proof of Lemma
4.16
, one can observe the following: if
E^{\prime}\subset\mathcal{E}^{\mathrm{inter}}(X)
, then

(4.92)

E_{1}\cup\cdots\cup E_{k}\cup E^{\prime}\in\mathsf{E}^{X^{\prime}}\\
\Longleftrightarrow\text{there exists $T^{b}\in\mathsf{T}^{Y_{0}}$ such that $T^{b}\subset E^{\prime}$}.

We now let
\mathcal{T}_{Y_{0}}^{\mathrm{KA}}
be the peeling map given by Kruskal’s algorithm, see Definition
4.46
. Let us also recall from Definition
4.46
that for every
T\in\mathsf{T}^{Y_{0}}
,
\mathcal{E}^{\mathrm{KA}}_{\max}(Y_{0},T)
stands for the maximal set of edges in
\mathcal{E}^{\mathrm{inter}}(Y_{0})
whose peeling by Kruskal’s algorithm equals
T
. By (
4.92
), we have

\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}E^{\prime}\subset\mathcal{E}^{\mathrm{inter}}(X^{\prime}):\\
(E_{1}\cup\cdots\cup E_{k})\cap E^{\prime}=\emptyset\\
E_{1}\cup\cdots\cup E_{k}\cup E^{\prime}\in\mathsf{E}^{X^{\prime}}\end{subarray}}\prod_{ij\in E^{\prime}}b_{ij}^{\tilde{v}}=\sum_{T^{b}\in\mathsf{T}^{Y_{0}}}\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}E^{\prime}\subset\mathcal{E}^{\mathrm{inter}}(X^{\prime}):\\
(E_{1}\cup\cdots\cup E_{k})\cap E^{\prime}=\emptyset\\
\mathcal{T}_{Y_{0}}^{\mathrm{KA}}(E_{1}\cup\cdots\cup E_{k}\cup E^{\prime})=T^{b}\end{subarray}}\prod_{ij\in E^{\prime}}b_{ij}^{\tilde{v}}.

Fix
T^{b}\in\mathsf{T}^{Y_{0}}
. Then, if
E^{\prime}\subset\mathcal{E}^{\mathrm{inter}}(X^{\prime})
, we have

(E_{1}\cup\cdots\cup E_{k})\cap E^{\prime}=\emptyset\quad\text{and}\quad\mathcal{T}_{Y_{0}}^{\mathrm{KA}}(E_{1}\cup\cdots\cup E_{k}\cup E^{\prime})=T^{b}\\
\Longleftrightarrow E^{\prime}\subset\mathcal{E}^{\mathrm{KA}}_{\max}(Y_{0},T^{b})\cup\bigcup_{l=1}^{k}(\mathcal{E}^{\mathrm{inter}}(X_{l}^{\prime})\setminus E_{l})\quad\text{and}\quad T^{b}\subset E^{\prime}.

Thus, and this is our Penrose resummation,

\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}E^{\prime}\subset\mathcal{E}^{\mathrm{inter}}(X^{\prime}):\\
(E_{1}\cup\cdots\cup E_{k})\cap E^{\prime}=\emptyset\\
\mathcal{T}_{Y_{0}}^{\mathrm{KA}}(E_{1}\cup\cdots\cup E_{k}\cup E^{\prime})=T^{b}\end{subarray}}\prod_{ij\in E^{\prime}}b_{ij}^{\tilde{v}}=\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}E^{\prime}\subset\mathcal{E}^{\mathrm{KA}}_{\max}(Y_{0},T^{b})\\
\cup\cup_{l=1}^{n}(\mathcal{E}^{\mathrm{inter}}(X_{l}^{\prime})\setminus E_{l}),\\
T^{b}\subset E^{\prime}\end{subarray}}\prod_{ij\in E^{\prime}}b_{ij}^{\tilde{v}}\\
=\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}E^{\prime\prime}:E^{\prime\prime}\subset\mathcal{E}^{\mathrm{KA}}_{\max}(Y_{0},T^{b})\setminus T^{b}\\
\cup\cup_{l=1}^{k}(\mathcal{E}^{\mathrm{inter}}(X_{l}^{\prime})\setminus E_{l})\end{subarray}}\prod_{ij\in T^{b}\cup E^{\prime\prime}}b_{ij}^{\tilde{v}}=\prod_{ij\in T^{b}}b_{ij}^{\tilde{v}}\prod_{\begin{subarray}{c}ij\in\mathcal{E}^{\mathrm{KA}}_{\max}(Y_{0},T^{b})\setminus T^{b}\\
\cup\cup_{l=1}^{k}(\mathcal{E}^{\mathrm{inter}}(X_{l}^{\prime})\setminus E_{l})\end{subarray}}(1+b_{ij}^{\tilde{v}})\leq\prod_{ij\in T^{b}}\prod_{ij\in\mathcal{E}^{\mathrm{inter}}(X^{\prime})}(1+b_{ij}^{\tilde{v}}).

Inserting this into (
4.91
) gives

\mathcal{U}(X^{\prime})=\sum_{k=1}^{\infty}\frac{1}{k!}\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}X_{1}^{\prime},\ldots,X_{k}^{\prime}\subset X^{\prime}\\
\mathrm{disjoint}\end{subarray}}\sum_{E_{1}\in\mathrm{Eul}_{\mathrm{c}}^{X_{1}^{\prime}}}\ldots\sum_{E_{k}\in\mathrm{Eul}_{\mathrm{c}}^{X_{k}^{\prime}}}\sum_{T^{b}\in\mathsf{T}^{Y_{0}}}\\
\prod_{ij\in E_{1}\cup\cdots\cup E_{k}}a_{ij}^{\tilde{v}}\prod_{ij\in T^{b}}b_{ij}^{\tilde{v}}\prod_{ij\in\mathcal{E}^{\mathrm{inter}}(X^{\prime})}(1+b_{ij}^{\tilde{v}})\prod_{ij\in\mathcal{E}^{\mathrm{inter}}(X^{\prime})}\mathbf{1}_{\mathcal{B}_{ij}^{c}}.

By Lemma
3.10
, there exists
C>0
depending only on
\beta
such that
|a_{ij}^{\tilde{v}}|\leq Ca_{ij}^{\mathrm{abs}}
and
|b_{ij}^{\tilde{v}}|\leq Cb_{ij}^{\mathrm{abs}}
. Inserting this into the last display, we deduce that there exists
C>0
depending on
\beta
such that, given
T^{b}\in\mathsf{T}^{Y_{0}}
,

|\mathcal{U}(X^{\prime})|\leq C^{|V_{X^{\prime}}|}\sum_{k=1}^{\infty}\frac{1}{k!}\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}X_{1}^{\prime},\ldots,X_{k}^{\prime}\subset X^{\prime}\\
\mathrm{disjoint}\end{subarray}}\sum_{E_{1}\in\mathrm{Eul}_{\mathrm{c}}^{X_{1}^{\prime}}}\ldots\sum_{E_{k}\in\mathrm{Eul}_{\mathrm{c}}^{X_{k}^{\prime}}}\sum_{T^{b}\in\mathsf{T}^{\mathrm{Coa}_{X^{\prime}}(X_{1}^{\prime},\ldots,X_{k}^{\prime})}}\\
\prod_{ij\in E_{1}\cup\cdots\cup E_{k}}(Ca_{ij}^{\mathrm{abs}})\prod_{ij\in T^{b}}b_{ij}^{\mathrm{abs}}\prod_{ij\in\mathcal{E}^{\mathrm{inter}}(X^{\prime})}|1+b_{ij}^{\tilde{v}}|.

Using
|1+b_{ij}^{\tilde{v}}|\leq e^{b_{ij}^{\tilde{v}}}\leq e^{Cb_{ij}^{\mathrm{abs}}}\leq e^{Ca_{ij}^{\mathrm{abs}}}
, there exists
C>0
depending only on
\beta
such that

(4.93)

|F(X^{\prime})|\leq C^{|V_{X^{\prime}}|}\mathcal{L}_{C}(X^{\prime}),

where
\mathcal{L}_{C}
is as in (
4.90
).

Step 2: summing over the edges in
F

We now rewrite the term

\sum_{F\in\mathsf{E}^{\mathrm{Coa}_{X}(X_{1},\ldots,X_{n})}}\prod_{ij\in F}(-\mathbf{1}_{\mathcal{B}_{ij}})

from (
4.61
).
Denote for shorthand
Y_{1}\coloneqq\mathrm{Coa}_{X}(X_{1},\ldots,X_{n})
.
Arguing by resummation as in Step 1, we can write

\sum_{F\in\mathsf{E}^{Y_{1}}}\prod_{ij\in F}(-\mathbf{1}_{\mathcal{B}_{ij}})=\sum_{\tilde{T}^{b}\in\mathsf{T}^{Y_{1}}}\sum_{E^{\prime}\subset\mathcal{E}^{\mathrm{KA}}_{\max}(Y_{1},\tilde{T}^{b})\setminus\tilde{T}^{b}}\prod_{ij\in\tilde{T}^{b}\cup E^{\prime}}(-\mathbf{1}_{\mathcal{B}_{ij}}).

Fix
\tilde{T}^{b}\in\mathsf{T}^{Y_{1}}
. We have

\sum_{E^{\prime}\subset\mathcal{E}_{\max}^{\mathrm{KA}}(Y_{1},\tilde{T}^{b})\setminus\tilde{T}^{b}}\prod_{ij\in E^{\prime}}(-\mathbf{1}_{\mathcal{B}_{ij}})=\prod_{ij\in\mathcal{E}_{\max}^{\mathrm{KA}}(Y_{1},\tilde{T}^{b})\setminus\tilde{T}^{b}}(1-\mathbf{1}_{\mathcal{B}_{ij}})=\prod_{ij\in\mathcal{E}_{\max}^{\mathrm{KA}}(Y_{1},\tilde{T}^{b})\setminus\tilde{T}^{b}}\mathbf{1}_{\mathcal{B}_{ij}^{c}}.

Summing over
\tilde{T}^{b}
and using the above displays, we find

(4.94)

\left|\sum_{F\in\mathsf{E}^{\mathrm{Coa}_{X}(X_{1},\ldots,X_{n})}}\prod_{ij\in F}(-\mathbf{1}_{\mathcal{B}_{ij}})\right|\leq\sum_{\tilde{T}^{b}\in\mathsf{T}^{Y_{1}}}\prod_{ij\in\tilde{T}^{b}}\mathbf{1}_{\mathcal{B}_{ij}}.

By Definition
4.25
,
\mathbf{1}_{\mathcal{B}_{ij}}\leq b_{ij}^{\mathrm{abs}}
. Hence,

(4.95)

\left|\sum_{F\in\mathsf{E}^{\mathrm{Coa}_{X}(X_{1},\ldots,X_{n})}}\prod_{ij\in F}(-\mathbf{1}_{\mathcal{B}_{ij}})\right|\leq\sum_{\tilde{T}^{b}\in\mathsf{T}^{Y_{1}}}\prod_{ij\in\tilde{T}^{b}}b_{ij}^{\mathrm{abs}}.

Step 3: conclusion

With the notation (
4.91
), we have

\mathsf{K}_{\varepsilon_{0}}^{-}(X)=\sum_{n=1}^{\infty}\frac{1}{n!}\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}X_{1},\ldots,X_{n}\subset X\\
\mathrm{disjoint}\end{subarray}}\sum_{F\in\mathsf{E}^{\mathrm{Coa}_{X}(X_{1},\ldots,X_{n})}}\mathbb{E}_{\mathsf{P}_{X}^{-,\varepsilon_{0}}}\left[\mathcal{U}(X_{1})\cdots\mathcal{U}(X_{n})\prod_{ij\in F}(-\mathbf{1}_{\mathcal{B}_{ij}})\right].

Therefore, inserting the bounds (
4.93
) and (
4.95
), this proves the result.
∎

4.16.
Rewriting the sum over Eulerian graphs as a sum over trees

Our aim is now to bound the term
\mathcal{L}_{C_{0}}(X)
defined in (
4.90
). We rewrite the sum over Eulerian graphs by incorporating the result of the peeling procedure of Definition
4.40
. Using the key inequality (
4.68
) of Corollary
4.43
, we bound the product of the weights by a quantity involving the product over the set of edges
F
of the
g_{ij}
’s defined in (
4.66
), and a product of squares of dipole lengths.

Lemma 4.50

(Summing over Eulerian graphs)
.

Let
X
be a subpartition of
[N]
such that for every
S\in X
, one has
|S|\leq p(\beta)
. Let
X_{1},\ldots,X_{n}\subset X
be disjoint. Let
\mathcal{L}_{C_{0}}(X)
be as in (
4.90
). For every
l\in[n]
, denote

(4.96)

\hat{S_{l}}(X_{1},\ldots,X_{n})\coloneqq\mathrm{argmax}\{r_{S}:S\in X_{l}\},

where
r_{S}
is as in Definition
4.38
.

Then, there exists a constant
C>0
depending on
\beta
,
p(\beta)
and
M
such that

(4.97)

\mathcal{L}_{C_{0}}(X)\leq e^{C|V_{X}|}\sum_{n=1}^{\infty}\frac{1}{n!}\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}X_{1},\ldots,X_{n}\subset X\\
\mathrm{disjoint}\end{subarray}}H_{X}(X_{1},\ldots,X_{n}),

where

(4.98)

H_{X}(X_{1},\ldots,X_{n})\coloneqq\prod_{l=1}^{n}\left(\prod_{\begin{subarray}{c}S\in X_{l}:\\
S\neq\hat{S}_{l}(X_{1},\ldots,X_{n})\end{subarray}}r_{S}^{2}\sum_{T_{l}^{a}\in\mathsf{T}^{X_{l}}}\prod_{ij\in T_{l}^{a}}\frac{1}{d_{ij}^{2}}\mathbf{1}_{d_{ij}\leq 16\varepsilon_{0}\bar{R}_{\beta,\lambda}}\mathbf{1}_{\mathcal{B}_{ij}^{c}}\right)\left(\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}T^{b}\in\mathsf{T}^{\mathrm{Coa}_{X}(X_{1},\ldots,X_{n})}\end{subarray}}\prod_{ij\in T^{b}}b_{ij}^{\mathrm{abs}}\right)\\
\times\left(\prod_{i\in V_{X}}\log_{M}\Bigr(\frac{\varepsilon_{0}\bar{R}_{\beta,\lambda}}{r_{i}}\Bigr)\right)\exp\left(C_{0}\sum_{ij\in\mathcal{E}^{\mathrm{inter}}(X)}a_{ij}^{\mathrm{abs}}\right)\left(\prod_{i\in V_{X}}\Big(1+\sum_{j\in V_{X}:j\neq i}g_{ij}\Big)\right).

Proof.

Fix
l\in[n]
. Recall
\mathcal{T}^{X_{l}}
and
\mathcal{F}^{X_{l}}
from Definition
4.40
. By Lemma
4.45
, item (1),

(4.99)

\sum_{E\in\mathrm{Eul}_{\mathrm{c}}^{X_{l}}}\prod_{ij\in E}(C_{0}a_{ij}^{\mathrm{abs}})=\sum_{T_{l}^{a}\in\mathsf{T}^{X_{l}}}\sum_{F_{l}\in\mathrm{PF}(V_{X_{l}})}\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}E\in\mathrm{Eul}_{\mathrm{c}}^{X_{l}}:\\
\mathcal{T}^{X_{l}}(\cdot,E)=T_{l}^{a}\\
\mathcal{F}^{X_{l}}(\cdot,E)={F}_{l}\end{subarray}}\prod_{ij\in E}(C_{0}a_{ij}^{\mathrm{abs}}),

where we recall from Definition
4.44
that
\mathrm{PF}(V_{X_{l}})
stands for the set of pseudoforests on
V_{X_{l}}
. Fix
T_{l}^{a}\in\mathsf{T}^{X_{l}}
and
F_{l}\in\mathrm{PF}(V_{X_{l}})
. Let
E\in\mathrm{Eul}_{\mathrm{c}}^{X_{l}}
be such that
\mathcal{T}^{X_{l}}(\cdot,E)=T_{l}^{a}
and
\mathcal{F}^{X_{l}}(\cdot,E)=F_{l}
. We have

\prod_{ij\in E}(C_{0}a_{ij}^{\mathrm{abs}})=\prod_{ij\in T_{l}^{a}\cup F_{l}}(C_{0}a_{ij}^{\mathrm{abs}})\prod_{ij\in E\setminus(T_{l}^{a}\cup F_{l})}(C_{0}a_{ij}^{\mathrm{abs}}).

Since
T_{l}^{a}\cup F_{l}
is minimally 2-edge-connected relative to
X_{l}
(since
T_{l}^{a}\cup F_{l}=\mathrm{Peeled}_{X_{l}}(E)
, where
\mathrm{Peeled}_{X}(E)
is as in Definition
4.34
), we have
|T_{l}^{a}\cup F_{l}|\leq 2(|V_{X_{l}}|-1)
. Hence,

\prod_{ij\in E}(C_{0}a_{ij}^{\mathrm{abs}})\leq C_{0}^{2|V_{X_{l}}|-1}\prod_{ij\in T_{l}^{a}\cup F_{l}}a_{ij}^{\mathrm{abs}}\prod_{ij\in E\setminus(T_{l}^{a}\cup F_{l})}(C_{0}a_{ij}^{\mathrm{abs}}).

By Corollary
4.43
, there exists a constant
C>0
depending on
\beta
such that

(4.100)

\prod_{ij\in T_{l}^{a}\cup F_{l}}a_{ij}^{\mathrm{abs}}\leq C^{|V_{X_{l}}|}\prod_{S\in X_{l},S\neq\hat{S}_{l}}r_{S}^{2}\sum_{T\in B(\cdot,T_{l}^{a})}\prod_{ij\in T}\Bigr(\frac{1}{d_{ij}^{2}}\mathbf{1}_{d_{ij}\leq 16\varepsilon_{0}\bar{R}_{\beta,\lambda}}\mathbf{1}_{\mathcal{B}_{ij}^{c}}\Bigr)\prod_{ij\in F_{l}}g_{ij},

where
g_{ij}
is as in (
4.66
) and
\hat{S}_{l}
is as in (
4.96
).
Therefore,

\sum_{F_{l}\in\mathrm{PF}(V_{X_{l}})}\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}E\in\mathrm{Eul}_{\mathrm{c}}^{X_{l}}:\\
\mathcal{T}^{X_{l}}(\cdot,E)=T_{l}^{a}\\
\\
{\mathcal{F}}^{X_{l}}(\cdot,E)={F}_{l}\end{subarray}}\prod_{ij\in E}(C_{0}a_{ij}^{\mathrm{abs}})\leq C^{|V_{X_{l}}|}\prod_{S\in X_{l},S\neq\hat{S}_{l}}r_{S}^{2}\sum_{T\in B(\cdot,T_{l}^{a})}\prod_{ij\in T}\Bigr(\frac{1}{d_{ij}^{2}}\mathbf{1}_{d_{ij}\leq 16\varepsilon_{0}\bar{R}_{\beta,\lambda}}\mathbf{1}_{\mathcal{B}_{ij}^{c}}\Bigr)\\
\times\left(\sum_{F_{l}\in\mathrm{PF}(V_{X_{l}})}\prod_{ij\in F_{l}}g_{ij}\right)\sum_{E^{\prime}\subset\mathcal{E}^{\mathrm{inter}}(X_{l})}\prod_{ij\in E^{\prime}}C_{0}a_{ij}^{\mathrm{abs}}.

Using
1+C_{0}a_{ij}^{\mathrm{abs}}\leq e^{C_{0}a_{ij}^{\mathrm{abs}}}
, we get

(4.101)

\sum_{F_{l}\in\mathrm{PF}(V_{X_{l}})}\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}E\in\mathrm{Eul}_{\mathrm{c}}^{X_{l}}:\\
\mathcal{T}^{X_{l}}(\cdot,E)=T_{l}^{a}\\
\\
{\mathcal{F}}^{X_{l}}(\cdot,E)={F}_{l}\end{subarray}}\prod_{ij\in E}(C_{0}a_{ij}^{\mathrm{abs}})\leq C^{|V_{X_{l}}|}\prod_{S\in X_{l},S\neq\hat{S}_{l}}r_{S}^{2}\sum_{T\in B(\cdot,T_{l}^{a})}\prod_{ij\in T}\Bigr(\frac{1}{d_{ij}^{2}}\mathbf{1}_{d_{ij}\leq 16\varepsilon_{0}\bar{R}_{\beta,\lambda}}\mathbf{1}_{\mathcal{B}_{ij}^{c}}\Bigr)\\
\times\left(\sum_{F_{l}\in\mathrm{PF}(V_{X_{l}})}\prod_{ij\in F_{l}}g_{ij}\right)\prod_{ij\in\mathcal{E}^{\mathrm{inter}}(X_{l})}e^{C_{0}a_{ij}^{\mathrm{abs}}}.

By (
4.70
),

(4.102)

\sum_{F_{l}\in\mathrm{PF}(V_{X_{l}})}\prod_{ij\in F_{l}}g_{ij}\leq\prod_{i\in V_{X_{l}}}\left(1+\sum_{j\in V_{X_{l}}:j\neq i}g_{ij}\right).

Finally, using (
4.72
),

\displaystyle\sum_{T_{l}^{a}\in\mathsf{T}^{X_{l}}}\sum_{T\in B(\cdot,T_{l}^{a})}\prod_{ij\in T}\Bigr(\frac{1}{d_{ij}^{2}}\mathbf{1}_{d_{ij}\leq 16\varepsilon_{0}\bar{R}_{\beta,\lambda}}\mathbf{1}_{\mathcal{B}_{ij}^{c}}\Bigr)\leq\sum_{T\in\mathsf{T}^{X_{l}}}|\{T^{\prime}\in\mathsf{T}^{X_{l}}:T\in B(\cdot,T^{\prime})\}\prod_{ij\in T}\Bigr(\frac{1}{d_{ij}^{2}}\mathbf{1}_{d_{ij}\leq 2\varepsilon_{0}\bar{R}_{\beta,\lambda}}\mathbf{1}_{\mathcal{B}_{ij}^{c}}\Bigr)

(4.103)

\displaystyle\qquad\qquad\qquad\leq C^{|V_{X_{l}}|}\prod_{i\in V_{X_{l}}}\log_{M}\Bigr(\frac{16\varepsilon_{0}\bar{R}_{\beta,\lambda}}{r_{i}}\Bigr)\sum_{T\in\mathsf{T}^{X_{l}}}\prod_{ij\in T}\Bigr(\frac{1}{d_{ij}^{2}}\mathbf{1}_{d_{ij}\leq 16\varepsilon_{0}\bar{R}_{\beta,\lambda}}\mathbf{1}_{\mathcal{B}_{ij}^{c}}\Bigr).

Combining (
4.101
), (
4.102
), (
4.103
) and summing over
T_{l}^{a}
, there exists
C>0
depending only on
\beta
such that

(4.104)

\sum_{E\in\mathrm{Eul}_{\mathrm{c}}^{X_{l}}}\prod_{ij\in E}(C_{0}a_{ij}^{\mathrm{abs}})\leq C^{|V_{X_{l}}|}\prod_{S\in X_{l}:S\neq\hat{S}_{l}}r_{S}^{2}\sum_{T_{l}^{a}\in\mathsf{T}^{X_{l}}}\Bigr(\prod_{ij\in T_{l}^{a}}\frac{1}{d_{ij}^{2}}\mathbf{1}_{d_{ij}\leq 16\varepsilon_{0}\bar{R}_{\beta,\lambda}}\mathbf{1}_{\mathcal{B}_{ij}^{c}}\Bigr)\\
\times\prod_{ij\in\mathcal{E}^{\mathrm{inter}}(X_{l})}e^{C_{0}a_{ij}^{\mathrm{abs}}}\prod_{i\in V_{X_{l}}}\Big(1+\sum_{j\in V_{X_{l}}:j\neq i}g_{ij}\mathbf{1}_{\mathcal{B}_{ij}^{c}}\Big)\prod_{i\in V_{X_{l}}}\log_{M}\Bigr(\frac{16\varepsilon_{0}\bar{R}_{\beta,\lambda}}{r_{i}}\Bigr).

Taking the product over
l
gives the desired result.
∎

We now address the last two types of terms appearing in the function
H_{X}
from (
4.98
). This analysis will rely on geometric properties of the configurations.

Lemma 4.51

(Control of the interactions)
.

Let
\beta\in(2,\infty)
and
p(\beta)
be as Definition
2.7
. Let
X
be a partition of
[N]
such that for every
S\in X
, one has
|S|\leq p(\beta)
. Define the event

\mathcal{D}=\bigcap_{i\in V_{X}}\{r_{i}\leq\varepsilon_{0}\bar{R}_{\beta,\lambda}\}\cap\bigcap_{ij\in\mathcal{E}^{\mathrm{inter}}(X)}\mathcal{B}_{ij}^{c}.

Then, there exists a constant
C>0
depending on
p(\beta)
such that on the event
\mathcal{D}
, for every
i\in V_{X}
,

(4.105)

\sum_{j\in V_{X}:ij\in\mathcal{E}^{\mathrm{inter}}(X)}a_{ij}^{\mathrm{abs}}\mathbf{1}_{r_{j}\geq r_{i}}\leq\frac{C}{M}\left(\log\Bigr(\frac{16\varepsilon_{0}\bar{R}_{\beta,\lambda}}{r_{i}}\Bigr)+1\right).

Moreover, with
g_{ij}
being as in (
4.66
), there exists a constant
C>0
depending on
p(\beta)
such that on the event
\mathcal{D}
, for every
i\in V_{X}
,

(4.106)

\sum_{j\in V_{X}:j\neq i}g_{ij}\mathbf{1}_{r_{j}\geq r_{i}}\leq C\Bigr(\frac{1}{M^{2}}\log^{2}\Bigr(\frac{16\varepsilon_{0}\bar{R}_{\beta,\lambda}}{r_{i}}\Bigr)+1\Bigr).

Proof.

Denote
p\coloneqq p(\beta)
.

Fix
i\in V_{X}
. Let us write

\sum_{j\in V_{X}:ij\in\mathcal{E}^{\mathrm{inter}}(X)}a_{ij}^{\mathrm{abs}}\leq C(A_{1}+A_{2}).

where

(4.107)

A_{1}\coloneqq\sum_{j\in V_{X},j\neq i}\frac{r_{i}r_{j}}{d_{ij}^{2}}\mathbf{1}_{r_{j}<d_{ij}<16\varepsilon_{0}\bar{R}_{\beta,\lambda}}\mathbf{1}_{\mathcal{B}_{ij}^{c}}\mathbf{1}_{r_{j}\geq r_{i}},

(4.108)

A_{2}\coloneqq\sum_{j\in V_{X},j\neq i}\frac{r_{i}}{d_{ij}}\mathbf{1}_{d_{ij}\leq r_{j}}\mathbf{1}_{\mathcal{B}_{ij}^{c}}\mathbf{1}_{r_{j}\geq r_{i}}.

Let us first consider the sum in (
4.107
). Recall that
\mathcal{B}_{ij}^{c}
is the event where
d_{ij}\geq M\min(\max(r_{i},\lambda),\max(r_{j},\lambda))
. Therefore,

(4.109)

A_{1}\leq\sum_{j\in V_{X},j\neq i}\frac{r_{i}r_{j}}{d_{ij}^{2}}\mathbf{1}_{\max(r_{j},Mr_{i})<d_{ij}<16\varepsilon_{0}\bar{R}_{\beta,\lambda}}\mathbf{1}_{r_{j}\geq r_{i}}.

Let us introduce

(4.110)
