\sum_{j\in I_{\mathrm{bad}}}|v_{i,j,+}^{Z}|\leq C\sum_{j\in I_{\mathrm{bad}}}\frac{r_{i}}{d_{i,j}^{+}}\mathbf{1}_{M\lambda\leq d_{i,\mathrm{bad}}\leq d_{i,j}^{+}\leq 2\tau_{j}^{+,Z}}.

As in the proof of Lemma
4.51
, let us define for all
t\geq 0
,

(5.27)

\mathcal{E}_{i}(t)\coloneqq\Bigr\{j\in V_{\mathrm{bad}}:d_{i,\mathrm{bad}}\leq d_{i,j}^{+}\leq 2\tau_{j}^{+,Z},d_{ij}^{+}\in(t,2t)\Bigr\}\quad\text{and}\quad\mathcal{N}_{i}(t)\coloneqq|\mathcal{E}_{i}(t)|.

By (
5.26
),

(5.28)

\sum_{j\in I_{\mathrm{bad}}}|v_{i,j,+}^{Z}|\leq Cr_{i}\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}j\in V_{\mathrm{bad}}:\\
d_{i,\mathrm{bad}}\leq d^{+}_{ij}\leq 2\tau_{j}^{+,Z}\end{subarray}}\int_{\tfrac{1}{2}d_{i,\mathrm{bad}}}^{2\varepsilon_{0}\bar{R}_{\beta,\lambda}}\frac{1}{t^{2}}\mathbf{1}_{d_{i,j}^{+}\in(t,2t)}\mathrm{d}t\leq Cr_{i}\int_{\tfrac{1}{2}d_{i,\mathrm{bad}}}^{2\varepsilon_{0}\bar{R}_{\beta,\lambda}}\frac{1}{t^{2}}\mathcal{N}_{i}(t)\mathrm{d}t.

Notice that

\mathcal{E}_{i}(t)\subset\Bigr\{j\in V_{\mathrm{bad}}:x_{j}\in B(x_{i},2t),\tau_{j}^{+,Z}\geq\max(\tfrac{t}{2},\lambda)\Bigr\}\cup\Bigr\{j\in V_{\mathrm{bad}}:x_{j}\in B(y_{i},2t),\tau_{j}^{+,Z}\geq\max(\tfrac{t}{2},\lambda)\Bigr\}.

Therefore, by (
5.15
), there exists a constant
C>0
depending on
q(\beta)
such that
\mathcal{N}_{i}(t)\leq C
. Hence, inserting into (
5.28
), there exists a constant
C>0
depending on
q(\beta)
such that

(5.29)

\sum_{j\in I_{\mathrm{bad}}}|v_{i,j,+}^{Z}|\leq C\frac{r_{i}}{d_{i,\mathrm{bad}}}.

A similar bound can be proven for the
v_{i,j,-}^{Z}
, hence (
5.24
).
∎

Lemma 5.12

(Control of bad-bad interactions)
.

There exists a constant
C>0
depending on
q(\beta)
such that on the event
\cap_{i,j\in V_{\mathrm{bad}}:i\neq j}\mathcal{A}_{ij}
,

(5.30)

\sum_{i,j\in V_{\mathrm{bad}}:i<j}v_{ij}^{Z}\geq-C|V_{\mathrm{bad}}|.

Proof.

We begin by writing

\sum_{i,j\in I_{\mathrm{bad}}:i<j}v_{ij}^{Z}=\sum_{i\neq j\in I_{\mathrm{bad}}}v_{ij}^{Z}\mathbf{1}_{r_{j}\geq r_{i}}.

Fix
i\in I_{\mathrm{bad}}
. First, decompose the sum of the interactions as follows:

(5.31)

\sum_{j\in I_{\mathrm{bad}}:j\neq i}v_{ij}^{Z}\mathbf{1}_{r_{j}\geq r_{i}}=\sum_{j\in I_{\mathrm{bad}}:j\neq i}v_{i,j,+}^{Z}\mathbf{1}_{r_{j}\geq r_{i}}+\sum_{j\in I_{\mathrm{bad}}:j\neq i}v_{i,j,-}^{Z}\mathbf{1}_{r_{j}\geq r_{i}}.

We bound the first term, the second one is analogous. First, decompose the sum of the
v_{i,j,+}^{Z}
into

(5.32)

\sum_{j\in I_{\mathrm{bad}}:j\neq i}v_{i,j,+}^{Z}\mathbf{1}_{r_{j}\geq r_{i}}=\sum_{j\in I_{\mathrm{bad}}:j\neq i}v_{i,j,+}^{Z}\mathbf{1}_{r_{j}\geq r_{i}}\mathbf{1}_{d_{i,j}^{+}\geq r_{i}}\mathbf{1}_{\tau_{j}^{+,Z}=\lambda}+\sum_{j\in I_{\mathrm{bad}}:i\neq j}v_{i,j,+}^{Z}\mathbf{1}_{d_{i,j}^{+}<r_{i}}\mathbf{1}_{r_{j}\geq r_{i}}\\
+\sum_{j\in I_{\mathrm{bad}}:j\neq i}v_{i,j,+}^{Z}\mathbf{1}_{d_{i,j}^{+}\geq\max(r_{i},\lambda)}\mathbf{1}_{\tau_{j}^{+,Z}>\lambda}\mathbf{1}_{r_{j}\geq r_{i}}+\sum_{j\in I_{\mathrm{bad}}:j\neq i}v_{i,j,+}^{Z}\mathbf{1}_{r_{i}\leq d_{i,j}^{+}<\lambda}\mathbf{1}_{\tau_{j}^{+,Z}>\lambda}\mathbf{1}_{r_{j}\geq r_{i}}.

Step 1: the case
\tau_{j}^{+,Z}=\lambda
and
d_{i,j}^{+}\geq r_{i}

By Newton’s theorem, if

\Bigr(\mathsf{g}_{\lambda}-\mathsf{g}*\delta_{0}^{(\tau_{i}^{+,Z})}*\delta_{0}^{(\lambda)}\Bigr)(x_{i}-x_{j})\neq 0,

then

|x_{i}-x_{j}|\leq\tau_{i}^{+,Z}+\lambda.

Moreover, since
\mathsf{g}_{\lambda}=\mathsf{g}*\delta_{0}^{(\lambda)}*\delta_{0}^{(\lambda)}
, then if
\Bigr(\mathsf{g}_{\lambda}-\mathsf{g}*\delta_{0}^{(\tau_{i}^{+,Z})}*\delta_{0}^{(\lambda)}\Bigr)(x_{i}-x_{j})\neq 0
then
\tau_{i}^{+,Z}>\lambda
, which implies
\tau_{i}^{+,Z}\leq\frac{r_{i}}{4}
, hence
r_{i}\geq 4\lambda
. Thus, if
\Bigr(\mathsf{g}_{\lambda}-\mathsf{g}*\delta_{0}^{(\tau_{i}^{+,Z})}*\delta_{0}^{(\lambda)}\Bigr)(x_{i}-x_{j})\neq 0
and if in addition
d_{i,j}^{+}\geq r_{i}
, then
r_{i}\geq 4\lambda
and

r_{i}\leq d_{i,j}^{+}\leq|x_{i}-x_{j}|\leq\frac{r_{i}}{4}+\lambda,

which implies
r_{i}\leq\frac{4}{3}\lambda
and
r_{i}\geq 4\lambda
. Clearly, this is impossible. Thus,

\sum_{j\in I_{\mathrm{bad}}:j\neq i}\Bigr(\mathsf{g}_{\lambda}-\mathsf{g}*\delta_{0}^{(\tau_{i}^{+,Z})}*\delta_{0}^{(\lambda)}\Bigr)(x_{i}-x_{j})\mathbf{1}_{d_{i,j}^{+}\geq r_{i}}\mathbf{1}_{\tau_{j}^{+,Z}=\lambda}=0.

Arguing similarly for the interaction of
y_{i}
with the
x_{j}
, we obtain

(5.33)

\sum_{j\in I_{\mathrm{bad}}:j\neq i}v_{i,j,+}^{Z}\mathbf{1}_{d_{i,j}^{+}\geq r_{i}}\mathbf{1}_{\tau_{j}^{+,Z}=\lambda}=0.

Step 2: control for
d_{i,j}^{+}<r_{i}

First, by Newton’s theorem, if
|x_{j}-y_{i}|>\tau_{i}^{-,Z}+\tau_{j}^{+,Z}
, then

\Bigr(\mathsf{g}_{\lambda}-\mathsf{g}*\delta_{0}^{(\tau_{i}^{-,Z})}*\delta_{0}^{(\tau_{j}^{+,Z})}\Bigr)(x_{j}-y_{i})=0.

Therefore,

v_{i,j,+}^{Z}\mathbf{1}_{|x_{j}-y_{i}|>\tau_{i}^{-,Z}+\tau_{j}^{+,Z}}=\Bigr(\mathsf{g}_{\lambda}-\mathsf{g}*\delta_{0}^{(\tau_{i}^{+,Z})}*\delta_{0}^{(\tau_{j}^{+,Z})}\Bigr)(x_{i}-x_{j})\mathbf{1}_{|x_{j}-y_{i}|>\tau_{i}^{-,Z}+\tau_{j}^{+,Z}}.

Since
\tau_{i}^{+,Z}\geq\lambda
and
\tau_{j}^{+,Z}\geq\lambda
, we deduce from the monotonicity property of Lemma
3.2
that

(5.34)

\Bigr(\mathsf{g}_{\lambda}-\mathsf{g}*\delta_{0}^{(\tau_{i}^{+,Z})}*\delta_{0}^{(\tau_{j}^{+,Z})}\Bigr)(x_{i}-x_{j})\geq 0.

Hence, combining the above two displays yields

(5.35)

v_{i,j,+}^{Z}\mathbf{1}_{|x_{j}-y_{i}|>\tau_{i}^{-,Z}+\tau_{j}^{+,Z}}\geq 0.

Now suppose that
|x_{j}-y_{i}|\leq\tau_{i}^{-,Z}+\tau_{j}^{+,Z}
. On the event
\mathcal{A}_{ij}
, by compatibility of the matching (see Lemma
4.5
), we have

|x_{j}-y_{i}|\geq r_{i}
.
It follows that on the event
\mathcal{A}_{ij}
, if
d_{i,j}^{+}\leq r_{i}\leq r_{j}
,

(5.36)

r_{i}\leq|x_{j}-y_{i}|\leq\tau_{i}^{-,Z}+\tau_{j}^{+,Z}\leq\max(\tfrac{1}{4}r_{i},\lambda)+\tau_{j}^{+,Z}.

Thus, on the event
\mathcal{A}_{ij}
, if
v_{i,j,+}^{Z}\leq 0
and
d_{i,j}^{+}\leq r_{i}
, then

(5.37)

\tau_{j}^{+,Z}\geq\max(\tfrac{r_{i}}{4},\lambda),

which gives in particular

d_{i,j}^{+}\leq 2\tau_{j}^{+,Z}.

Suppose that
\tau_{j}^{+,Z}=\lambda
. Then, since the interaction between
x_{i}
and
x_{j}
is always non-negative by (
5.34
), if
v_{i,j,+}^{Z}<0
, then

(5.38)

\Bigr(\mathsf{g}_{\lambda}-\mathsf{g}*\delta_{0}^{(\tau_{i}^{-,Z})}*\delta_{0}^{(\lambda))}\Bigr)(y_{i}-x_{j})\neq 0,

which implies that
\tau_{i}^{-,Z}\neq\lambda
, hence
r_{i}>4\lambda
. But (
5.38
) also implies that
r_{i}\leq|y_{i}-x_{j}|\leq\frac{r_{i}}{4}+\lambda
, which gives
r_{i}\leq\frac{4}{3}\lambda
. These two conditions are therefore incompatible. Thus,

(5.39)

\sum_{j\in I_{\mathrm{bad}}:j\neq i}v_{i,j,+}^{Z}\mathbf{1}_{d_{ij}\leq r_{i}}\mathbf{1}_{\tau_{j}^{+,Z}=\lambda}\geq 0.

It remains to treat the case where
\tau_{j}^{+,Z}>\lambda
. By (
5.35
) and (
5.37
),

\sum_{j\in I_{\mathrm{bad}}:j\neq i}v_{i,j,+}^{Z}\mathbf{1}_{d_{i,j}^{+}<r_{i}}\mathbf{1}_{\tau_{j}^{+,Z}>\lambda}\mathbf{1}_{r_{j}\geq r_{i}}\geq-C\sum_{j\in V_{\mathrm{bad}}:j\neq i}\mathbf{1}_{\tau_{j}^{+,Z}\geq\tfrac{r_{i}}{4}}\mathbf{1}_{\tau_{j}^{+,Z}>\lambda}\mathbf{1}_{d_{i,j}^{+}\leq\max(2\tau_{j}^{+,Z},r_{i})}.

By Lemma
5.9
, item (5), there exists a constant
C>0
depending on
q(\beta)
such that

\sum_{j\in V_{\mathrm{bad}}:j\neq i}\mathbf{1}_{\tau_{j}^{+,Z}\geq\tfrac{r_{i}}{4}}\mathbf{1}_{\tau_{j}^{+,Z}>\lambda}\mathbf{1}_{d_{i,j}^{+}\leq\max(2\tau_{j}^{+,Z},r_{i})}\mathbf{1}_{r_{j}\geq r_{i}}\leq C.

Thus, there exists a constant
C>0
depending on
q(\beta)
such that

(5.40)

\sum_{j\in I_{\mathrm{bad}}:i\neq j}v_{i,j,+}^{Z}\mathbf{1}_{d_{ij}<r_{i}}\mathbf{1}_{\tau_{j}^{Z,+}>\lambda}\mathbf{1}_{r_{j}\geq r_{i}}\geq-C.

By (
5.33
) and (
5.39
), there exists a constant
C>0
such that

(5.41)

\sum_{j\in I_{\mathrm{bad}}:i\neq j}v_{i,j,+}^{Z}\mathbf{1}_{d_{i,j^{+}}<r_{i}}\mathbf{1}_{r_{j}\geq r_{i}}\geq-C.

Step 3: control for
d_{i,j}^{+}\geq\max(r_{i},\lambda)
and
\tau_{j}^{+,Z}>\lambda

By Newton’s theorem, if
v_{i,j,+}^{Z}\neq 0
, then
d_{i,j}^{+}\leq\tau_{j}^{+,Z}+\max(\tau_{i}^{+,Z},\tau_{i}^{-,Z})
. Moreover, by the construction of the radii,

\max(\tau_{i}^{+,Z},\tau_{i}^{-,Z})\leq\max(\tfrac{1}{4}r_{i},\lambda).

Hence, if
v_{i,j,+}^{Z}\neq 0
and
d_{i,j}^{+}\geq r_{i}
, then
r_{i}\leq d_{i,j}^{+}\leq\tau_{j}^{+}+\max(\frac{1}{4}r_{i},\lambda)
and thus

\tau_{j}^{+,Z}\geq\frac{r_{i}}{2}\mathbf{1}_{r_{i}\geq 4\lambda}+\max(r_{i}-\lambda,\lambda)\mathbf{1}_{r_{i}\leq 4\lambda}.

In particular,
\tau_{j}^{+,Z}\geq\max(\frac{r_{i}}{4},\lambda)\geq\max(\tau_{i}^{+,Z},\tau_{i}^{-,Z})
and

d_{i,j}^{+}\leq 2\tau_{j}^{+,Z}.

Moreover, by (
3.44
) in Lemma
3.9
, we have

|v_{i,j,+}^{Z}|\leq C\left(\frac{r_{i}}{\max(d_{i,j}^{+},\lambda)}+\frac{r_{i}}{\tau_{j}^{+,Z}}+\frac{\max(\tau_{i}^{+,Z},\tau_{i}^{-,Z})^{2}}{(\tau_{j}^{+,Z})^{2}}\right)\mathbf{1}_{d_{i,j}^{+}}\leq\max(\tau_{i}^{+,Z},\tau_{i}^{-,Z})+\tau_{j}^{+,Z}.

Using that
\tau_{j}^{+,Z}\geq\frac{1}{2}d_{i,j}^{+}
, that
\tau_{i}^{\pm,Z}\leq\max(\frac{r_{i}}{4},\lambda)
, we deduce that

|v_{i,j,+}^{Z}|\leq C\frac{\max(r_{i},\lambda)}{d_{i,j}^{+}}.

It follows that

\sum_{j\in I_{\mathrm{bad}}:j\neq i}|v_{i,j,+}^{Z}|\mathbf{1}_{d_{i,j}^{+}\geq\max(r_{i},\lambda),\tau_{j}^{+,Z}>\lambda}\mathbf{1}_{r_{j}\geq r_{i}}\leq C\sum_{j\in I_{\mathrm{bad}}:j\neq i}\frac{\max(r_{i},\lambda)}{d_{i,j}^{+}}\mathbf{1}_{\max(r_{i},\lambda)\leq d_{i,j}^{+}\leq 2\tau_{j}^{+,Z},\tau_{j}^{+,Z}>\lambda}.

Define for all
t\geq 0
,

\mathcal{E}_{i}(t)\coloneqq\Bigr\{j\in V_{\mathrm{bad}}:\max(r_{i},\lambda)\leq d_{i,j}^{+}\leq 2\tau_{j}^{+,Z},\tau_{j}^{+,Z}>\lambda,d_{ij}^{+}\in(t,2t)\Bigr\}\quad\text{and}\quad\mathcal{N}_{i}(t)\coloneqq|\mathcal{E}_{i}(t)|.

We get

\sum_{j\in I_{\mathrm{bad}}:j\neq i}\frac{\max(r_{i},\lambda)}{d_{ij}^{+}}\mathbf{1}_{\max(r_{i},\lambda)\leq d_{ij}^{+}\leq 2\tau_{j}^{+,Z},\tau_{j}^{+,Z}>\lambda}\leq C\max(r_{i},\lambda)\int_{\tfrac{1}{2}\max(r_{i},\lambda)}^{2\varepsilon_{0}\bar{R}_{\beta,\lambda}}\frac{1}{t^{2}}\mathcal{N}_{i}(t)\mathrm{d}t.

By Lemma
5.9
, item (5), there exists a constant
C>0
depending on
q(\beta)
such that
\mathcal{N}_{i}(t)\leq C
. Hence, there exists
C>0
depending on
q(\beta)
such that

(5.42)

\sum_{j\in I_{\mathrm{bad}}:j\neq i}|v_{i,j,+}^{Z}|\mathbf{1}_{d_{i,j}^{+}\geq\max(r_{i},\lambda)}\mathbf{1}_{\tau_{j}^{+,Z}>\lambda}\mathbf{1}_{r_{j}\geq r_{i}}\leq C.

Step 4: control for
r_{i}\leq d_{i,j}^{+}<\lambda
and
\tau_{j}^{+,Z}>\lambda
.

In that case, since for
i\in I_{\mathrm{bad}}
we have
\lambda\leq\tau_{i}^{\pm,Z}\leq\max(\frac{1}{4}r_{i},\lambda)
(see Remark
5.10
), we have
\tau_{i}^{\pm,Z}=\lambda
. Inserting this,
r_{i}\leq d_{i,j}^{+}<\lambda
and
\tau_{j}^{+,Z}>\lambda
into (
3.44
), we find that
|v_{i,j,+}^{Z}|\leq C
and thus

\sum_{j\in I_{\mathrm{bad}}:j\neq i}|v_{i,j,+}^{Z}|\mathbf{1}_{r_{i}\leq d_{i,j}^{+}<\lambda}\mathbf{1}_{\tau_{j}^{+,Z}>\lambda}\mathbf{1}_{r_{j}\geq r_{i}}\leq C\sum_{j\in I_{\mathrm{bad}}:j\neq i}\mathbf{1}_{r_{i}\leq d_{i,j}^{+}<\lambda}\mathbf{1}_{\tau_{j}^{+,Z}>\lambda}\mathbf{1}_{r_{j}\geq r_{i}}.

Then, applying Lemma
5.9
item (5) with
t=\lambda
and
A\geq 2
yields that the sum on the right-hand side is bounded by a constant
C>0
depending on
q(\beta)
hence

(5.43)

\sum_{j\in I_{\mathrm{bad}}:j\neq i}|v_{i,j,+}^{Z}|\mathbf{1}_{d_{i,j}^{+}\geq\max(r_{i},\lambda)}\mathbf{1}_{\tau_{j}^{+,Z}>\lambda}\mathbf{1}_{r_{j}\geq r_{i}}\leq C.

Step 5: conclusion

Combining (
5.33
), (
5.41
), (
5.42
) and (
5.43
) gives the existence of a constant
C>0
depending on
q(\beta)
such that

\sum_{j\in I_{\mathrm{bad}}:j\neq i}v_{i,j,+}^{Z}\mathbf{1}_{r_{j}\geq r_{i}}\geq-C.

Proceeding similarly for the
v_{i,j,-}^{Z}
proves the result by using the decomposition (
5.31
).

∎

Let us finally record here the main energy lower bound that we will use.
Let
I_{1}\coloneqq I_{\mathrm{good}}\cup\{i\in I_{\mathrm{bad}},\frac{1}{4}r_{i}<\lambda\}
, and
I_{2}\coloneqq I_{\mathrm{bad}}\cap\{i\in I_{\mathrm{bad}},\frac{1}{4}r_{i}\geq\lambda\}
. Then
[N]=I_{1}\sqcup I_{2}
and by Remark
5.10
, the sets
I_{1}
and
I_{2}
satisfy the assumptions of the energy lower bound of Proposition
3.3
. Thus, we know that there exists
L>0
, depending on
\beta
, such that, if
\sigma_{N}=\mathrm{Id}
,

\mathsf{F}_{\lambda}(\vec{X}_{N},\vec{Y}_{N})\geq-\sum_{i\in I_{1}}\mathsf{g}_{\lambda}(x_{i}-y_{i})-\frac{1}{2}\sum_{i\in I_{2}}(\mathsf{g}(\tau_{i}^{+,Z})+\mathsf{g}(\tau_{i}^{-,Z}))+\sum_{i<j}v_{ij}^{Z}-\frac{L}{\beta}\sum_{i\in I_{1}}\Bigr(\frac{|x_{i}-y_{i}|}{\tau_{i}^{+,Z}}\Bigr)^{2}-\frac{L}{\beta}|I_{2}|,

where the interactions
v_{ij}^{Z}
and the radii
\tau_{i}^{Z}
are given by Definition
5.8
.
By definition of
I_{1}
and
I_{2}
and the
\tau_{i}^{\pm}
’s, we can easily rearrange and absorb terms to obtain that

(5.44)

\mathsf{F}_{\lambda}(\vec{X}_{N},\vec{Y}_{N})\geq-\sum_{i\in I_{\mathrm{good}}}\mathsf{g}_{\lambda}(x_{i}-y_{i})-\frac{1}{2}\sum_{i\in I_{\mathrm{bad}}}(\mathsf{g}(\tau_{i}^{+,Z})+\mathsf{g}(\tau_{i}^{-,Z}))+\sum_{i<j}v_{ij}^{Z}\\
-\frac{L}{\beta}\sum_{i\in I_{\mathrm{good}}}\Bigr(\frac{|x_{i}-y_{i}|}{\tau_{i}^{+,Z}}\Bigr)^{2}-\frac{L}{\beta}|V_{\mathrm{bad}}|.

5.3.
Definitions: hierarchical model and activities with frozen bad points

We now introduce the hierarchical multipole model that we will be using in this section.

Definition 5.13
.

Let
V_{\mathrm{bad}}\subset[N]
and
Z\in(\Lambda^{2})^{|V_{\mathrm{bad}}|}
. Let
X
be a subpartition of
[N]
such that
V_{X}\cap V_{\mathrm{bad}}=\emptyset
. Let us define

(5.45)

I_{\mathrm{good}}^{Z}\coloneqq\bigcap_{j\in I_{\mathrm{bad}}^{1}\cup I_{\mathrm{bad}}^{2}}\{i\in[N]:d_{ij}>M\max(r_{i},\lambda)\}\cap\bigcap_{j\in I_{\mathrm{bad}}^{3}}\{i\in[N]:d_{ij}>M\max(\min(r_{i},r_{j}),\lambda)\}.

Note that
I_{\mathrm{good}}\subset I_{\mathrm{good}}^{Z}
. We let
\mathsf{P}_{X}^{+,\varepsilon_{0},Z}
be the probability measure

(5.46)

\mathrm{d}\mathsf{P}^{+,\varepsilon_{0},Z}_{X}\propto\prod_{S\in X}\Bigr(\mathbf{1}_{\mathcal{B}_{S}}\prod_{i,j\in S:i<j}e^{-\beta v^{Z}_{ij}}\mathbf{1}_{\mathcal{A}_{ij}}\Bigr)\\
\times\prod_{i\in V_{X}}\Bigr(e^{\beta\mathsf{g}_{\lambda}(x_{i}-y_{i})+L(\frac{x_{i}-y_{i}}{\tau_{i}^{Z}})^{2}}\mathbf{1}_{|x_{i}-y_{i}|\leq\varepsilon_{0}\bar{R}_{\beta,\lambda}}\mathbf{1}_{i\in I_{\mathrm{good}}^{Z}}\Bigr)\prod_{i\in V_{X}}\mathrm{d}x_{i}\mathrm{d}y_{i}

where
L
is the constant in (
5.44
)
and
\mathsf{M}_{\varepsilon_{0}}^{+,Z}(X)
be the normalization constant

(5.47)

\mathsf{M}_{\varepsilon_{0}}^{+,Z}(X)\coloneqq\prod_{S\in X}\mathbb{E}_{(\mu_{\beta,\lambda,\varepsilon_{0}})^{\otimes|S|}}\left[\mathbf{1}_{\mathcal{B}_{S}}\prod_{i,j\in S:i<j}e^{-\beta v^{Z}_{ij}}\mathbf{1}_{\mathcal{A}_{ij}}\prod_{i\in S}\mathbf{1}_{i\in I_{\mathrm{good}}^{Z}}\right].

Definition 5.14
.

Let
V_{\mathrm{bad}}\subset[N]
and
Z\in(\Lambda^{2})^{|V_{\mathrm{bad}}|}
. For every
i\in[N]\setminus V_{\mathrm{bad}}
, we let

f_{i\infty}^{v^{Z}}\coloneqq\prod_{j\in V_{\mathrm{bad}}}e^{-\beta v_{ij}^{Z}}-1.

Definition 5.15
.

Let
V_{\mathrm{bad}}\subset[N]
and set
V_{\mathrm{good}}\coloneqq[N]\setminus V_{\mathrm{bad}}
. Let
Z\in(\Lambda^{2})^{|V_{\mathrm{bad}}|}
.

We define
\mathsf{K}_{\varepsilon_{0}}^{+,Z}:\mathbf{\Pi}_{\mathrm{sub}}(V_{\mathrm{good}})\to\mathbb{R}
to be given for every
X\in\mathbf{\Pi}_{\mathrm{sub}}(V_{\mathrm{good}})
by

\mathsf{K}_{\varepsilon_{0}}^{+,Z}(X)\coloneqq\sum_{V^{\infty}\subset V_{X}}\sum_{n=0}^{\infty}\frac{1}{n!}\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}X_{1},\ldots,X_{n}\subset X\\
\mathrm{disjoint}\end{subarray}}\sum_{E_{1}\in\mathsf{E}^{X_{1}}}\cdots\sum_{E_{n}\in\mathsf{E}^{X_{n}}}\sum_{F\in\mathsf{E}^{\mathrm{Coa}_{X}(X_{1},\ldots,X_{n})}}\\
\mathbb{E}_{\mathsf{P}_{X}^{+,\varepsilon_{0},Z}}\left[\prod_{ij\in E_{1}\cup\cdots\cup E_{n}}f^{v^{Z}}_{ij}\prod_{ij\in\cup_{l}\mathcal{E}^{\mathrm{inter}}(X_{l})}\mathbf{1}_{\mathcal{B}_{ij}^{c}}\prod_{ij\in F}(-\mathbf{1}_{\mathcal{B}_{ij}})\prod_{i\in V^{\infty}}f_{i\infty}^{v^{Z}}\right],

where
\mathcal{E}^{\mathrm{inter}}(X)
is as in Definition
4.8
,
\mathsf{E}^{X}
as in Definition
4.9
,
\mathrm{Coa}_{X}(X_{1},\ldots,X_{n})
as in (
4.6
),
f_{ij}^{v^{Z}}
as in (
4.13
), and
f_{i\infty}^{v^{Z}}
as in (
5.14
).

Notice that for
|X|\in\{0,1\}
, we have
\mathsf{K}_{\varepsilon_{0}}^{+,Z}(X)=0
.

5.4.
Isolating good points and perturbative expansion around the hierarchical model

In this section, we rewrite the partition function of good points, defined in Definition
5.5
, as a cluster expansion series. This process largely follows the steps of Section
4.6
.

Lemma 5.16
.

Let
\beta\in(2,\infty)
and
p(\beta)
be as in Definition
2.7
. Let
\mathcal{N}_{1},\ldots,\mathcal{N}_{p(\beta)}
be the number of multipoles of size
1,\ldots,p(\beta)
in
I_{\mathrm{good}}
. Let
n_{1},\ldots,n_{p(\beta)}\geq 0

satisfying for every
k\in\{2,\dots,p(\beta)\}

(5.48)

n_{k}\leq\varepsilon_{0}^{-\alpha(\beta)}\lambda^{2(k-1)}N,

where we recall that
\alpha(\beta)
is as in (
4.21
).
Set
N^{\prime}=n_{1}+2n_{2}+\cdots+p(\beta)n_{p(\beta)}
. Define

(5.49)

\mathcal{A}\coloneqq\{\mathcal{N}_{1}=n_{1},\ldots,\mathcal{N}_{p(\beta)}=n_{p(\beta)},|I_{\mathrm{good}}|=N^{\prime}\}.

Let
V_{\mathrm{bad}}\subset[N]
of cardinality
N-N^{\prime}
and set
V_{\mathrm{good}}\coloneqq[N]\setminus V_{\mathrm{bad}}
.
Let
\pi
be a partition of
V_{\mathrm{good}}
such that for every
k=1,\ldots,p(\beta)
,

|\{S\in\pi:|S|=k\}|=n_{k}

and such that for every
k>p(\beta)
,

|\{S\in\pi:|S|=k\}|=0.

Recall
C_{\beta,\lambda,\varepsilon_{0}}
from (
4.8
),
\mathsf{M}_{\varepsilon_{0}}^{+,Z}
from Definition
5.13
and
\mathsf{K}_{\varepsilon_{0}}^{+,Z}
from (
5.15
).

Then, we have

(5.50)

(NC_{\beta,\lambda,\varepsilon_{0}})^{-N}\int_{\mathcal{A}\cap\{\sigma_{N}=\mathrm{Id}\}}e^{-\beta\mathsf{F}_{\lambda}}\leq\binom{N}{N^{\prime}}\frac{(N^{\prime})!}{1^{n_{1}}(2!)^{n_{2}}\cdots(p(\beta)!)^{n_{p(\beta)}}n_{1}!\cdots n_{p(\beta)}!}\frac{1}{(NC_{\beta,\lambda,\varepsilon_{0}})^{|V_{\mathrm{bad}}|}}\\
\times\int_{(\Lambda^{2})^{|V_{\mathrm{bad}}|}}\mathbf{1}_{\{I_{\mathrm{bad}}=V_{\mathrm{bad}}\}}W(Z)\prod_{i,j\in V_{\mathrm{bad}}:i<j}(e^{-\beta v^{Z}_{ij}}\mathbf{1}_{\mathcal{A}_{ij}})\prod_{i\in V_{\mathrm{bad}}}\frac{1}{(\tau_{i}^{+,Z})^{\frac{\beta}{2}}}\frac{1}{(\tau_{i}^{-,Z})^{\frac{\beta}{2}}}\prod_{S\in\pi}\mathsf{M}_{\varepsilon_{0}}^{+,Z}(S)\mathrm{d}Z,

where

(5.51)

W:Z\in(\Lambda^{2})^{|V_{\mathrm{bad}}|}\mapsto\sum_{n=0}^{\infty}\frac{1}{n!}\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}X_{1},\ldots,X_{n}\in\mathcal{P}(\pi)\\
\mathrm{disjoint}\end{subarray}}\mathsf{K}_{\varepsilon_{0}}^{+,Z}(X_{1})\cdots\mathsf{K}_{\varepsilon_{0}}^{+,Z}(X_{n}).

Proof.

Denote
p\coloneqq p(\beta)
.

Step 1: starting point

Recall
I_{\mathrm{good}}
and
I_{\mathrm{bad}}
defined in Definition
5.5
. For every
V\subset[N]
, we let
I_{\mathrm{bad}}^{V}
be the set of bad points when computed only among
(x_{i},y_{i})_{i\in V}
.

Let
\Pi_{\mathrm{mult}}^{I_{\mathrm{good}}}
be the partition of
I_{\mathrm{good}}
into multipoles (see Definition
2.2
). Recall that by definition,
I_{\mathrm{good}}\subset[N]\setminus I_{\mathrm{bad}}^{2}
and therefore every
S\in\Pi_{\mathrm{mult}}^{V_{\mathrm{good}}}
such that
S\cap I_{\mathrm{good}}\neq\emptyset
satisfies
S\subset I_{\mathrm{good}}
and
|S|\leq p
. Hence
|I_{\mathrm{good}}|=\mathcal{N}_{1}+2\mathcal{N}_{2}+\ldots+p\mathcal{N}_{p}
and for
\mathcal{A}
to be nonempty, we need
N^{\prime}=n_{1}+2n_{2}+\cdots+pn_{p}
.

Step 2: fixing dipoles, good dipoles and good multipoles

We wish to give an upper bound on
\int_{\mathcal{A}}e^{-\beta\mathsf{F}_{\lambda}}
. First, since particles are undistinguishable, one may write

\int_{\mathcal{A}}e^{-\beta\mathsf{F}_{\lambda}}=N!\int_{\mathcal{A}\cap\{\sigma_{N}=\mathrm{Id}\}}e^{-\beta\mathsf{F}_{\lambda}}.

One may write

(5.52)

\int_{\mathcal{A}\cap\{\sigma_{N}=\mathrm{Id}\}}e^{-\beta\mathsf{F}_{\lambda}}=\binom{N}{N^{\prime}}\int_{\mathcal{A}\cap\{\sigma_{N}=\mathrm{Id},I_{\mathrm{bad}}=V_{\mathrm{bad}}\}}e^{-\beta\mathsf{F}_{\lambda}}.

By (
4.25
),

(5.53)

\int_{\mathcal{A}\cap\{\sigma_{N}=\mathrm{Id},I_{\mathrm{bad}}=V_{\mathrm{bad}}\}}e^{-\beta\mathsf{F}_{\lambda}}=\frac{(N^{\prime})!}{1^{n_{1}}(2!)^{n_{2}}\cdots(p!)^{n_{p}}n_{1}!\cdots n_{p}!}\int_{\{\sigma_{N}=\mathrm{Id},I_{\mathrm{bad}}=V_{\mathrm{bad}},\Pi_{\mathrm{mult}}^{V_{\mathrm{good}}}=\pi\}}e^{-\beta\mathsf{F}_{\lambda}}.

Step 3: rewriting the event
\{\sigma_{N}=\mathrm{Id},I_{\mathrm{bad}}=V_{\mathrm{bad}},\Pi_{\mathrm{mult}}^{V_{\mathrm{good}}}=\pi\}
as an intersection of simpler events

Recalling the notation
I_{\mathrm{good}}^{Z}
from (
5.45
), in view of the definition of
I_{\mathrm{bad}}
and of (
5.48
), by Remark
5.6
, item (2),
we have

(5.54)

\{\sigma_{N}=\mathrm{Id}\}\cap\{I_{\mathrm{bad}}=V_{\mathrm{bad}}\}=\{\sigma_{N}=\mathrm{Id}\}\cap\{I_{\mathrm{bad}}^{V_{\mathrm{bad}}}=V_{\mathrm{bad}}\}\\
\cap\bigcap_{i\in V_{\mathrm{good}}}\Bigr(\{|x_{i}-y_{i}|\leq\varepsilon_{0}\bar{R}_{\beta,\lambda}\}\cap\{|[i]^{\Pi_{\mathrm{mult}}^{V_{\mathrm{good}}}|}\leq p\}\Bigr)\cap\bigcap_{i\in V_{\mathrm{good}}}\{i\in I_{\mathrm{good}}^{Z}\}.

Moreover,

(5.55)

\{\sigma_{N}=\mathrm{Id}\}\cap\{\Pi_{\mathrm{mult}}^{V_{\mathrm{good}}}=\pi\}=\{\sigma_{N}=\mathrm{Id}\}\cap\bigcap_{S\in\pi}\mathcal{B}_{S}\bigcap_{ij\in\mathcal{E}^{\mathrm{inter}}(\pi)}\mathcal{B}_{ij}^{c}.

Also recall that

(5.56)

\{\sigma_{N}=\mathrm{Id}\}=\bigcap_{ij:i<j}\mathcal{A}_{ij}.

.

Step 4: isolating good points

Combining (
5.54
) – (
5.56
), (
5.52
), (
5.53
) and (
5.44
), we obtain that for some
C>0
depending only on
\lambda
,

\int_{\mathcal{A}\cap\{\sigma_{N}=\mathrm{Id}\}}e^{-\beta\mathsf{F}_{\lambda}}\leq e^{C(N-N^{\prime})}\binom{N}{N^{\prime}}\frac{(N^{\prime})!}{1^{n_{1}}(2!)^{n_{2}}\cdots(p!)^{n_{p}}n_{1}!\cdots n_{p}!}\\
\times\int_{(\Lambda^{2})^{|V_{\mathrm{bad}}|}}\mathbf{1}_{I_{\mathrm{bad}}^{V_{\mathrm{bad}}}=V_{\mathrm{bad}}}\prod_{i,j\in V_{\mathrm{bad}}:i<j}(e^{-\beta v_{ij}^{Z}}\mathbf{1}_{\mathcal{A}_{ij}})W^{\prime}(Z)\prod_{i\in V_{\mathrm{bad}}}\frac{1}{(\tau_{i}^{+,Z})^{\frac{\beta}{2}}}\frac{1}{(\tau_{i}^{-,Z})^{\frac{\beta}{2}}}\mathrm{d}Z,

where for every bad point configuration
Z\in(\Lambda^{2})^{|V_{\mathrm{bad}}|}
,

W^{\prime}(Z)\coloneqq\int_{\Lambda^{2(N-|V_{\mathrm{bad}}|)}}\left(\prod_{i\in V_{\mathrm{bad}},j\in V_{\mathrm{good}}}e^{-\beta v_{ij}^{Z}}\mathbf{1}_{\mathcal{B}_{ij}^{c}\cap\mathcal{A}_{ij}}\right)\prod_{ij\in\mathcal{E}^{\mathrm{inter}}(\pi)}e^{-\beta v_{ij}^{Z}}\mathbf{1}_{\mathcal{B}_{ij}^{c}\cap\mathcal{A}_{ij}}\\
\times\prod_{S\in\pi}\left(\mathbf{1}_{\mathcal{B}_{S}}\prod_{i,j\in S:i<j}e^{-\beta v_{ij}^{Z}}\mathbf{1}_{\mathcal{A}_{ij}}\right)\prod_{i\in V_{\mathrm{good}}}\left(\mathbf{1}_{|x_{i}-y_{i}|\leq\varepsilon_{0}\bar{R}_{\beta,\lambda}}\mathbf{1}_{|[i]^{\Pi_{\mathrm{mult}}^{V_{\mathrm{good}}}}|\leq p}\mathbf{1}_{i\in I_{\mathrm{good}}^{Z}}\right)\\
\times\prod_{i\in V_{\mathrm{good}}}\left(e^{\beta\mathsf{g}_{\lambda}(x_{i}-y_{i})+L(\frac{|x_{i}-y_{i}|}{\tau_{i}^{Z}})^{2}}\right)\prod_{i\in V_{\mathrm{good}}}\mathrm{d}x_{i}\mathrm{d}y_{i}.

where we recall the notation
\mathcal{E}^{\mathrm{inter}}(\pi)
from Definition
4.9
.
On the event
\mathcal{B}_{ij}^{c}\cap\{r_{i}<r_{j}\}
, we have

y_{j}\notin B(x_{i},r_{i}),

and therefore since
r_{i}<r_{j}
, the point
x_{i}
is assigned to
y_{i}
when performing the matching between
x_{i},y_{i}
and
x_{j},y_{j}
only. Reversing the roles of
i
and
j
, this shows that

\mathcal{B}_{ij}^{c}\subset\mathcal{A}_{ij}.

Therefore,

W^{\prime}(Z)=\int_{\Lambda^{2(N-|V_{\mathrm{bad}}|)}}\left(\prod_{i\in V_{\mathrm{bad}},j\in V_{\mathrm{good}}}e^{-\beta v_{ij}^{Z}}\mathbf{1}_{\mathcal{B}_{ij}^{c}}\right)\prod_{ij\in\mathcal{E}^{\mathrm{inter}}(\pi)}e^{-\beta v_{ij}^{Z}}\mathbf{1}_{\mathcal{B}_{ij}^{c}\cap\mathcal{A}_{ij}}\prod_{S\in\pi}\left(\mathbf{1}_{\mathcal{B}_{S}}\prod_{i,j\in S:i<j}e^{-\beta v_{ij}^{Z}}\mathbf{1}_{\mathcal{A}_{ij}}\right)\\
\times\prod_{i\in V_{\mathrm{good}}}\left(\mathbf{1}_{|x_{i}-y_{i}|\leq\varepsilon_{0}\bar{R}_{\beta,\lambda}}\mathbf{1}_{\Bigr|[i]^{\Pi_{\mathrm{mult}}^{V_{\mathrm{good}}}}\Bigr|\leq p}\mathbf{1}_{i\in I_{\mathrm{good}}^{Z}}\right)\prod_{i\in V_{\mathrm{good}}}\left(e^{\beta\mathsf{g}_{\lambda}(x_{i}-y_{i})+L(\frac{|x_{i}-y_{i}|}{\tau_{i}^{Z}})^{2}}\right)\prod_{i\in V_{\mathrm{good}}}\mathrm{d}x_{i}\mathrm{d}y_{i}.

Since we are restricted to the event where
\Pi_{\mathrm{mult}}^{V_{\mathrm{good}}}=\pi
and since
\pi
has no elements of cardinality strictly larger than
p
, one may simplify
W^{\prime}(Z)
into

W^{\prime}(Z)\coloneqq\int_{\Lambda^{2(N-|V_{\mathrm{bad}}|)}}\left(\prod_{i\in V_{\mathrm{bad}},j\in V_{\mathrm{good}}}e^{-\beta v_{ij}^{Z}}\right)\prod_{ij\in\mathcal{E}^{\mathrm{inter}}(\pi)}e^{-\beta v_{ij}^{Z}}\mathbf{1}_{\mathcal{B}_{ij}^{c}\cap\mathcal{A}_{ij}}\prod_{S\in\pi}\left(\mathbf{1}_{\mathcal{B}_{S}}\prod_{i,j\in S:i<j}e^{-\beta v_{ij}^{Z}}\mathbf{1}_{\mathcal{A}_{ij}}\right)\\
\times\prod_{i\in V_{\mathrm{good}}}\left(e^{\beta\mathsf{g}_{\lambda}(x_{i}-y_{i})+L(\frac{|x_{i}-y_{i}|}{\tau_{i}^{Z}})^{2}}\mathbf{1}_{|x_{i}-y_{i}|\leq\varepsilon_{0}\bar{R}_{\beta,\lambda}}\mathbf{1}_{i\in I_{\mathrm{good}}^{Z}}\right)\prod_{i\in V_{\mathrm{good}}}\mathrm{d}x_{i}\mathrm{d}y_{i}.

Step 5: rewriting
W^{\prime}(Z)
as a cluster expansion series

As in Section
4.6
, we expand the interaction between dipoles in distinct multipoles, the terms
\mathbf{1}_{\mathcal{B}_{ij}^{c}}
, and the interaction between good and bad points.

Recalling
f_{ij}^{v^{Z}}
from Definition
4.13
, and
f_{i\infty}^{v^{Z}}
from Definition
5.14
, one can write

W^{\prime}(Z)=\sum_{E\subset\mathcal{E}^{\mathrm{inter}}(\pi)}\sum_{V^{\infty}\subset V_{\mathrm{good}}}I(E,V^{\infty}),

where

I(E,V^{\infty})\coloneqq\int_{\Lambda^{2(N-|V_{\mathrm{bad}}|)}}\prod_{ij\in E}f_{ij}^{v^{Z}}\prod_{i\in V^{\infty}}f_{i\infty}^{v^{Z}}\prod_{S\in\pi}\left(\mathbf{1}_{\mathcal{B}_{S}}\prod_{i,j\in S:i<j}e^{-\beta v_{ij}^{Z}}\mathbf{1}_{\mathcal{A}_{ij}}\right)\\
\times\prod_{ij\in\mathcal{E}^{\mathrm{inter}}(\pi)}\mathbf{1}_{\mathcal{B}_{ij}^{c}}\prod_{i\in V_{\mathrm{good}}}\left(e^{\beta\mathsf{g}_{\lambda}(x_{i}-y_{i})+L(\frac{|x_{i}-y_{i}|}{\tau_{i}^{Z}})^{2}}\mathbf{1}_{|x_{i}-y_{i}|\leq\varepsilon_{0}\bar{R}_{\beta,\lambda}}\mathbf{1}_{i\in I_{\mathrm{good}}^{Z}}\right)\prod_{i\in V_{\mathrm{good}}}\mathrm{d}x_{i}\mathrm{d}y_{i}.

Resumming according to the connected components relative to
\pi
of the graph
(V_{\mathrm{good}},E)
gives

W^{\prime}(Z)=\sum_{n=0}^{\infty}\frac{1}{n!}\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}X_{1},\ldots,X_{n}\subset\pi\\
\mathrm{disjoint}\end{subarray}}\sum_{E_{1}\in\mathsf{E}^{X_{1}}}\cdots\sum_{E_{n}\in\mathsf{E}^{X_{n}}}\sum_{V_{1}^{\infty}\subset V_{X_{1}}}\cdots\sum_{V_{n}^{\infty}\subset V_{X_{n}}}I(\cup_{l=1}^{n}E_{l},\cup_{l=1}^{n}V_{l}^{\infty}).

Next, we use

\prod_{ij\in\mathcal{E}^{\mathrm{inter}}(\pi)}\mathbf{1}_{\mathcal{B}_{ij}^{c}}=\prod_{ij\in\cup_{l}\mathcal{E}^{\mathrm{inter}}(X_{l})}\mathbf{1}_{\mathcal{B}_{ij}^{c}}\prod_{ij\in\mathrm{Coa}_{\pi}(X_{1},\ldots,X_{n})}\mathbf{1}_{\mathcal{B}_{ij}^{c}}.

For every
ij\in\mathrm{Coa}_{\pi}(X_{1},\ldots,X_{n})
, we write
\mathbf{1}_{\mathcal{B}_{ij}^{c}}=1-\mathbf{1}_{\mathcal{B}_{ij}}
to obtain

\prod_{ij\in\mathrm{Coa}_{\pi}(X_{1},\ldots,X_{n})}\mathbf{1}_{\mathcal{B}_{ij}^{c}}=\sum_{F\subset\mathrm{Coa}_{\pi}(X_{1},\ldots,X_{n})}\prod_{ij\in F}(-\mathbf{1}_{\mathcal{B}_{ij}}).

This gives

W^{\prime}(Z)\\
=\sum_{n=0}^{\infty}\frac{1}{n!}\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}X_{1},\ldots,X_{n}\subset\pi\\
\mathrm{disjoint}\end{subarray}}\sum_{E_{1}\in\mathsf{E}^{X_{1}}}\cdots\sum_{E_{n}\in\mathsf{E}^{X_{n}}}\sum_{V_{1}^{\infty}\subset V_{X_{1}}}\cdots\sum_{V_{n}^{\infty}\subset V_{X_{n}}}\sum_{F\subset\mathrm{Coa}_{\pi}(X_{1},\ldots,X_{n})}I_{1}(\cup_{l=1}^{n}E_{l},\cup_{l=1}^{n}V_{l}^{\infty},F),

where, denoting by
X_{1},\ldots,X_{n}
the connected components relative to
\pi
of
(V_{\mathrm{good}},E)
with at least two multipoles,

I_{1}(E,V^{\infty},F)\coloneqq\int_{\Lambda^{2(N-|V_{\mathrm{bad}}|)}}\prod_{ij\in E}f_{ij}^{v^{Z}}\prod_{i\in V^{\infty}}f_{i\infty}^{v^{Z}}\prod_{S\in\pi}\Bigr(\mathbf{1}_{\mathcal{B}_{S}}\prod_{i,j\in S:i<j}e^{-\beta v_{ij}^{Z}}\mathbf{1}_{\mathcal{A}_{ij}}\Bigr)\prod_{ij\in\cup_{l=1}^{n}\mathcal{E}^{\mathrm{inter}}(X_{l})}\mathbf{1}_{\mathcal{B}_{ij}^{c}}\\
\times\prod_{ij\in F}(-\mathbf{1}_{\mathcal{B}_{ij}})\prod_{i\in V_{\mathrm{good}}}\left(e^{\beta\mathsf{g}_{\lambda}(x_{i}-y_{i})+L(\frac{|x_{i}-y_{i}|}{\tau_{i}^{Z}})^{2}}\mathbf{1}_{|x_{i}-y_{i}|\leq\varepsilon_{0}\bar{R}_{\beta,\lambda}}\mathbf{1}_{i\in I_{\mathrm{good}}^{Z}}\right)\prod_{i\in V_{\mathrm{good}}}\mathrm{d}x_{i}\mathrm{d}y_{i}.

Next, resumming according to the connected components relative to
\pi
of the graph
(V_{\mathrm{good}},E\cup F)
, we obtain

W^{\prime}(Z)=\sum_{n=0}^{\infty}\frac{1}{n!}\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}X_{1},\ldots,X_{n}\subset\pi\\
\mathrm{disjoint}\end{subarray}}\int_{\Lambda^{2(N-|V_{\mathrm{bad}}|)}}\mathcal{U}(X_{1})\cdots\mathcal{U}(X_{n})\prod_{S\in\pi}\Bigr(\mathbf{1}_{\mathcal{B}_{S}}\prod_{i,j\in S:i<j}e^{-\beta v_{ij}^{Z}}\mathbf{1}_{\mathcal{A}_{ij}}\Bigr)\\
\times\prod_{i\in V_{\mathrm{good}}}\Bigr(e^{\beta\mathsf{g}_{\lambda}(x_{i}-y_{i})+L(\frac{|x_{i}-y_{i}|}{\tau_{i}^{Z}})^{2}}\mathbf{1}_{|x_{i}-y_{i}|\leq\varepsilon_{0}\bar{R}_{\beta,\lambda}}\mathbf{1}_{i\in I_{\mathrm{good}}^{Z}}\Bigr)\prod_{i\in V_{\mathrm{good}}}\mathrm{d}x_{i}\mathrm{d}y_{i},

where for every
X\subset\pi
,

\mathcal{U}(X)=\sum_{V^{\infty}\subset V_{X}}\sum_{k=0}^{\infty}\frac{1}{k!}\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}Y_{1},\ldots,Y_{k}\subset X\\
\mathrm{disjoint}\end{subarray}}\sum_{E_{1}\in\mathsf{E}^{Y_{1}}}\cdots\sum_{E_{k}\in\mathsf{E}^{Y_{k}}}\sum_{F\in\mathsf{E}^{\mathrm{Coa}_{X}(Y_{1},\ldots,Y_{k})}}\prod_{ij\in\cup_{l=1}^{k}E_{l}}f_{ij}^{v^{Z}}\prod_{i\in V^{\infty}}f_{i\infty}^{v^{Z}}\prod_{ij\in F}(-\mathbf{1}_{\mathcal{B}_{ij}})\\
\times\prod_{ij\in\cup_{l=1}^{k}\mathcal{E}^{\mathrm{inter}}(Y_{l})}\mathbf{1}_{\mathcal{B}_{ij}^{c}}.

Therefore, dividing by the normalization constant
\mathsf{M}_{\varepsilon_{0}}^{+,Z}(\pi)
introduced in Definition
5.13
and using independence over distinct connected components gives

W(Z)=\frac{W^{\prime}(Z)}{\mathsf{M}_{\varepsilon_{0}}^{+,Z}(\pi)}=\sum_{n=0}^{\infty}\frac{1}{n!}\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}X_{1},\ldots,X_{n}\in\mathcal{P}(\pi)\\
\mathrm{disjoint}\end{subarray}}\mathsf{K}_{\varepsilon_{0}}^{+,Z}(X_{1})\cdots\mathsf{K}_{\varepsilon_{0}}^{+,Z}(X_{n}),

where
\mathsf{K}_{\varepsilon_{0}}^{+,Z}
is as in Definition
5.15
.
∎

5.5.
Statement of the main activity controls

We now state the analogue of Propositions
4.19
and
4.20
.

Proposition 5.17

(Control on the activity of bounded size clusters with frozen bad points)
.

Let
\beta\in(2,+\infty)
and
p(\beta)
be as in Definition
2.7
. Let
\varepsilon_{0}\in(0,1)
.
Let
V_{\mathrm{bad}}\subset[N]
and set
V_{\mathrm{good}}=[N]\setminus V_{\mathrm{bad}}
. Let
Z\in(\Lambda^{2})^{|V_{\mathrm{bad}}|}
. Let
M
be as in Definition
2.2
. Let
S\subset[N]
be such that
|S|\leq p(\beta)
and
X
be a subpartition of
V_{\mathrm{good}}
.

There exists
C>0
depending only on
\beta
,
M
,
\varepsilon_{0}
and
|S|
such that

(5.57)

\mathsf{M}_{\varepsilon_{0}}^{+,Z}(S)\geq\frac{1}{CN^{|S|-1}}\gamma_{\beta,\lambda,|S|},

(5.58)

\mathsf{M}_{\varepsilon_{0}}^{+,Z}(S)\leq\frac{C}{N^{|S|-1}}\gamma_{\beta,\lambda,|S|}.

Moreover, there exists
C>0
depending only on
\beta
,
M
,
\varepsilon_{0}
and
|V_{X}|
such that

(5.59)

|\mathsf{K}_{\varepsilon_{0}}^{+,Z}(X)|\leq\frac{C}{N^{|V_{X}|-1}\mathsf{M}^{0}_{\infty}(X)}\gamma_{\beta,\lambda,|V_{X}|}.

Proposition 5.18

(Expansion errors with frozen bad points)
.

Let
\beta\in(2,+\infty)
,
p(\beta)
be as in Definition
2.7
and
\gamma_{\beta,\lambda,n}
be as in Definition
4.18
. Let
\varepsilon_{0}\in(0,1)
.
Let
V_{\mathrm{bad}}\subset[N]
and set
V_{\mathrm{good}}=[N]\setminus V_{\mathrm{bad}}
. Let
Z\in(\Lambda^{2})^{|V_{\mathrm{bad}}|}
. Let
M
be as in Definition
2.2
. Let
S\subset[N]
be such that
|S|\leq p(\beta)
and
X
be a subpartition of
V_{\mathrm{good}}
such that
|V_{X}|\leq p(\beta)
.

Then, there exists
C>0
depending on
\beta
,
M
,
\varepsilon_{0}
and
|S|
such that

(5.60)

|\mathsf{M}_{\varepsilon_{0}}^{+,Z}(S)-\mathsf{M}^{0}_{\varepsilon_{0}}(S)|\leq\frac{C}{N^{|S|-1}}\Bigr(\bar{R}_{\beta,\lambda}^{-2}+\frac{|V_{\mathrm{bad}}|}{N}\Bigr).

Moreover, there exists
C>0
depending on
\beta
,
M
,
\varepsilon_{0}
and
|V_{X}|
such that

(5.61)

|\mathsf{K}_{\varepsilon_{0}}^{+,Z}(X)-\mathsf{K}_{\varepsilon_{0}}^{0}(X)|\leq\frac{C}{N^{|V_{X}|-1}\mathsf{M}^{0}_{\varepsilon_{0}}(X)}\Bigr(\bar{R}_{\beta,\lambda}^{-2}+\frac{|V_{\mathrm{bad}}|}{N}\Bigr).

Propositions
5.17
and
5.18
are proved in Section
5.9
.

In the next proposition, we expand the logarithm of the function
W(Z)
appearing in Lemma
5.16
, see (
5.51
). We fix a configuration of bad points and show that the series of the
\mathsf{K}_{\varepsilon_{0}}^{+,Z}(X)
is absolutely convergent.

Proposition 5.19

(Absolute convergence of the cluster expansion series with frozen bad points)
.

Let
\beta\in(2,+\infty)
and
p(\beta)
be as in Definition
2.7
. Let
\varepsilon_{0}\in(0,1)
.
Let
V_{\mathrm{bad}}\subset[N]
and set
V_{\mathrm{good}}=[N]\setminus V_{\mathrm{bad}}
. Let
Z\in(\Lambda^{2})^{|V_{\mathrm{bad}}|}
. Let
M
be as in Definition
2.2
. Let
\pi
be a partition of
V_{\mathrm{good}}
such that for every
S\in\pi
, one has
|S|\leq p(\beta)
. Assume that for every
k\in\{2,\ldots,p(\beta)\}
,

n_{k}\coloneqq|\{S\in\pi:|S|=k\}|
satisfies (
5.48
).

For
M
large enough with respect to
\beta
and
p(\beta)
,
\varepsilon_{0}
small enough with respect to
\beta
,
p(\beta)
and
M
, and
\lambda
small enough, there exists
C>0
depending only on
\beta,M,p(\beta)
and
\varepsilon_{0}
such that

(5.62)

\Bigr|\sum_{X\in\mathcal{P}(\pi)}\mathsf{K}_{\varepsilon_{0}}^{+,Z}(X)\mathbf{1}_{\{|V_{X}|>p(\beta)\}}\Bigr|\leq C(N\delta_{\beta,\lambda}+|V_{\mathrm{bad}}|),

in particular, the series
\sum_{X\in\mathcal{P}(\pi)}\mathsf{K}_{\varepsilon_{0}}^{+,Z}(X)
is absolutely convergent.

Moreover, for
M
large enough with respect to
\beta
and
p(\beta)
,
\varepsilon_{0}
small enough with respect to
\beta
and
M
, and
\lambda
small enough, there exists
C>0
depending only on
\beta,M,p(\beta)
and
\varepsilon_{0}
such that

(5.63)

\sum_{n=1}^{\infty}\frac{1}{n!}\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}X_{1},\ldots,X_{n}\in\mathcal{P}(\pi):\\
\mathrm{connected}\end{subarray}}|\mathsf{K}_{\varepsilon_{0}}^{+,Z}(X_{1})\cdots\mathsf{K}_{\varepsilon_{0}}^{+,Z}(X_{n})\mathrm{I}(G(X_{1},\ldots,X_{n}))|\mathbf{1}_{|V_{X_{1}\cup\cdots\cup X_{n}}|>p(\beta)}\leq C(N\delta_{\beta,\lambda}+|V_{\mathrm{bad}}|).

The proof is a variation on the proof of Proposition
4.23
and is provided in Section
5.9
.

5.6.
Core-forest decomposition

In this section we bound the activity
\mathsf{K}_{\varepsilon_{0}}^{+,Z}(X)
of a subpartition

X
of
[N]
. Following Definition
4.24
, decompose each Mayer bond into

f_{ij}^{v^{Z}}=a_{ij}^{v^{Z}}+b_{ij}^{v^{Z}}.

We begin by splitting
f_{i\infty}^{v^{Z}}
(Definition
5.14
), the Mayer bond between good and bad points, into its odd and even parts:

Definition 5.20
.

Let
V_{\mathrm{bad}}\subset[N]
and
Z\in(\Lambda^{2})^{|V_{\mathrm{bad}}|}
. For every
i\in[N]\setminus V_{\mathrm{bad}}
, we split
f_{i\infty}^{v^{Z}}
into
a_{i\infty}^{v^{Z}}+b_{i\infty}^{v^{Z}}
where

a_{i\infty}^{v^{Z}}\coloneqq-\sum_{k\,\mathrm{odd}\,}\frac{\beta^{k}}{k!}\left(\sum_{j\in V_{\mathrm{bad}}}v_{ij}^{Z}\right)^{k}\quad\mathrm{and}\,\quad b_{i\infty}^{v^{Z}}\coloneqq\sum_{k\,\mathrm{even},\,k\neq 0}\frac{\beta^{k}}{k!}\left(\sum_{j\in V_{\mathrm{bad}}}v_{ij}^{Z}\right)^{k}.

Remark 5.21
.

In view of Lemma
5.11
, on the event
I_{\mathrm{bad}}=V_{\mathrm{bad}}
, using (
5.9
) we may bound

(5.64)

|a_{i\infty}^{v^{Z}}|\leq C\frac{r_{i}}{d_{i,\mathrm{bad}}},\qquad|b_{i\infty}^{v^{Z}}|\leq C\frac{r_{i}^{2}}{d_{i,\mathrm{bad}}^{2}}

for some constant
C>0
depending on
\beta
and
q(\beta)
.

Also if
i,j\in I_{\mathrm{good}}
, in view of (
3.48
) in Lemma
3.9
, and using that
\tau_{i}^{+,Z}=\tau_{i}^{-,Z}\in[\max(\frac{1}{4}r_{i},\lambda),\varepsilon_{0}\bar{R}_{\beta,\lambda}]
for
i\in I_{\mathrm{good}}
, we have

(5.65)

|a_{ij}^{v^{Z}}\mathbf{1}_{\mathcal{B}_{ij}^{c}}|\leq Ca_{ij}^{\mathrm{abs}},\quad|b_{ij}^{v^{Z}}\mathbf{1}_{\mathcal{B}_{ij}^{c}}|\leq Cb_{ij}^{\mathrm{abs}}

where
a_{ij}^{\mathrm{abs}},b_{ij}^{\mathrm{abs}}
are as in Definition
4.25
, and
C>0
depends only on
\beta
.

Recall that for the lower bound, Corollary
4.30
shows that when the odd weights
a_{ij}^{\tilde{v}}
are used, only Eulerian graphs relative to
X
contribute. In the present setting, some vertices can interact with bad points; let
V^{\infty}
denote this set. We will show that the only graphs with odd internal weights that contribute are the following:

Definition 5.22

(Eulerian graphs with exceptional vertices)
.

Let
X
be a subpartition of
[N]
. Let
V^{\infty}_{1},V^{\infty}_{2}\subset V_{X}
be such that
V^{\infty}_{1}\cap V^{\infty}_{2}=\emptyset
. We say that
E\in\mathrm{Eul}^{X}(V^{\infty}_{1},V^{\infty}_{2})
if

(1)

E\subset\mathcal{E}^{\mathrm{inter}}(X)
,

(2)

for every
S\in X
,

\deg_{E}(S)+\sum_{i\in S}\mathbf{1}_{i\in V_{1}^{\infty}}\quad\mathrm{is}\,\mathrm{even}.

Moreover, we say that
E\in\mathrm{Eul}_{\mathrm{c}}^{X}(V_{1}^{\infty},V_{2}^{\infty})
if
E\in\mathrm{Eul}^{X}(V^{\infty}_{1},V^{\infty}_{2})
and if
E
is connected relative to
X
, i.e. we set

\mathrm{Eul}_{\mathrm{c}}^{X}(V^{\infty}_{1},V_{2}^{\infty})\coloneqq\mathrm{Eul}^{X}(V_{1}^{\infty},V_{2}^{\infty})\cap\mathsf{E}^{X}.

By using the parity argument of Lemma
4.28
, we obtain the following:

Corollary 5.23
.

Let
X
be a subpartition of
[N]
.

We have

\mathsf{K}_{\varepsilon_{0}}^{+,Z}(X)=\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}V_{1}^{\infty},V_{2}^{\infty}\subset V_{X}:\\
V_{1}^{\infty}\cap V_{2}^{\infty}=\emptyset\end{subarray}}\sum_{n=1}^{\infty}\frac{1}{n!}\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}X_{1},\ldots,X_{n}\subset X\\
\mathrm{disjoint}\end{subarray}}\prod_{l=1}^{n}\Biggr(\sum_{E_{l,1}\in\mathrm{Eul}^{X_{l}}(V_{X_{l}}\cap V_{1}^{\infty},V_{X_{l}}\cap V_{2}^{\infty})}\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}E_{l,2}:E_{l,1}\cup E_{l,2}\in\mathsf{E}^{X_{l}}\\
E_{l,1}\cap E_{l,2}=\emptyset\end{subarray}}\Biggr)\\
\sum_{F\in\mathsf{E}^{\mathrm{Coa}_{X}(X_{1},\ldots,X_{n})}}\mathbb{E}_{\mathsf{P}_{X}^{+,\varepsilon_{0},Z}}\left[\prod_{l=1}^{n}\left(\prod_{ij\in E_{l,1}}a^{v^{Z}}_{ij}\prod_{ij\in E_{l,2}}b^{v^{Z}}_{ij}\right)\prod_{ij\in\cup_{l}\mathcal{E}^{\mathrm{inter}}(X_{l})}\mathbf{1}_{\mathcal{B}_{ij}^{c}}\prod_{ij\in F}(-\mathbf{1}_{\mathcal{B}_{ij}})\prod_{i\in V_{1}^{\infty}}a_{i\infty}^{v^{Z}}\prod_{i\in V_{2}^{\infty}}b_{i\infty}^{v^{Z}}\right].

We now weaken the condition
E\in\mathrm{Eul}^{X}(V_{1}^{\infty},V_{2}^{\infty})
. Note that
E\in\mathrm{Eul}_{\mathrm{c}}^{X}(V_{1}^{\infty},V_{2}^{\infty})
does not guarantee that
E
is 2-edge-connected relative to
X
, in contrast to the case
V_{1}^{\infty}\cup V_{2}^{\infty}=\emptyset
discussed in Lemma
4.33
.

Recall the definition of the 2-core of a graph as introduced in
[
Sei83
]
and related notions.

Definition 5.24

(Core decomposition of a graph)
.

Let
G=(V,E)
be a finite connected graph.

(1)

\bm{2}
-core.

The
2
-core
of
G
, written
C_{2}(G)
,
is the unique maximal
induced
subgraph
whose minimum degree among all vertices (denoted
\delta
) is at least
2
:

C_{2}(G)=\max\bigl\{H\subseteq G\text{ induced}:\delta(H)\geq 2\bigr\}.

It can be found by repeatedly deleting every vertex whose

current
degree is
<2
(that is,
0
or
1
)
together with all incident edges, until no such vertex remains.
The resulting subgraph is connected.

(2)

Pendant trees.

The complement
G\setminus C_{2}(G)
is a forest made of disjoint trees attached to
C_{2}(G)
. These components are called
pendant trees
.
Thus

G=C_{2}(G)\cup\bigl(\text{disjoint union of pendant trees}\bigr).

(3)

Bridge-blocks.

A
bridge-block
is any maximal
2
-edge-connected
subgraph of
C_{2}(G)
.
Equivalently, it is a maximal subgraph of
C_{2}(G)

containing no
bridge
–an edge whose deletion
disconnects
C_{2}(G)
.
(Note that a singleton can be a bridge-block.)

(4)

Bridge-tree.

Let
\mathcal{B}
denote the family of bridge-blocks of
C_{2}(G)
and write
\mathcal{E}_{\mathrm{br}}
for the set of bridges of
C_{2}(G)
. Then

V\bigl(C_{2}(G)\bigr)=\bigcup_{B\in\mathcal{B}}V(B),\qquad E\bigl(C_{2}(G)\bigr)=\Bigl(\bigcup_{B\in\mathcal{B}}E(B)\Bigr)\cup\mathcal{E}_{\mathrm{br}},

so the vertex-sets of distinct blocks are disjoint, the edges of the blocks are pairwise disjoint, and every edge that is not in a block is a bridge. Contracting each block
B\in\mathcal{B}
to a single vertex and
retaining every edge in
\mathcal{E}_{\mathrm{br}}
produces a tree, called the
bridge-tree
of
G
. Its vertices correspond one-to-one with the bridge-blocks, and its edges correspond to the bridges that join them.

In other words, setting
Y\coloneqq\{V(B):B\in\mathcal{B}\}
, we have

(V_{X},\mathcal{E}_{\mathrm{br}})\quad\text{is a tree relative to $Y$.}

Figure 10
.
In red, the edges of the pendant trees. In blue and black the edges of the
2
-core. In blue the edges of the bridge tree linking the three 2-edge-connected blocks.

We now extend Definition
5.24
to the same notion but “relative to a partition”.

Definition 5.25

(Core decomposition of a graph relative to a partition)
.

Let
X
be a subpartition of
[N]
, let
E\in\mathsf{E}^{X}
and set
G\coloneqq(V_{X},E)
. For every
S\in X
, recall
\deg_{E}(S)\coloneqq\sum_{i\in S}\deg_{E}(i)
.

(1)

\bm{2}
-core.

The
2
-core
of
G
relative to
X
, written
C_{2}(G,X)
,
is the unique maximal subgraph
induced
by
V_{X^{\prime}}
for
X^{\prime}\subset X
such that each
S\in X^{\prime}
has degree at least 2. It can be found by repeatedly deleting every
S\in X
whose

current
degree is
<2
(that is,
0
or
1
) together with all incident edges, until no such
S
remains.

Letting
X^{\prime}
be the set of
S\in X
incident to some edge in
C_{2}(G,X)
, we have that
C_{2}(G,X)
is connected relative to
X^{\prime}
.

(2)

Pendant trees.

The complement
G\setminus C_{2}(G,X)
is a forest made of disjoint trees relative to
X
attached to
C_{2}(G,X)
. These components are called
pendant trees
.
Thus

G=C_{2}(G,X)\cup\bigl(\text{disjoint union of pendant trees relative to $X$}\bigr).

(3)

Bridge-blocks.

A
bridge-block
is any maximal
2
-edge-connected subgraph relative to
X
of
C_{2}(G,X)
.

(4)

Bridge-tree.

Let
\mathcal{B}
denote the family of bridge-blocks of
C_{2}(G,X)
and write
\mathcal{E}_{\mathrm{br}}
for the set of bridges of
C_{2}(G,X)
in
\mathcal{E}^{\mathrm{inter}}(X)
. Then,

V\bigl(C_{2}(G,X)\bigr)=\bigcup_{B\in\mathcal{B}}V(B),\qquad E\bigl(C_{2}(G,X)\bigr)=\Bigl(\bigcup_{B\in\mathcal{B}}E(B)\Bigr)\cup\mathcal{E}_{\mathrm{br}}.

Setting
Y\coloneqq\{V(B):B\in\mathcal{B}\}
, we have

(V_{X},\mathcal{E}_{\mathrm{br}})\quad\text{is a tree relative to $Y$}.

We now simplify the condition that
E\in\mathrm{Eul}_{\mathrm{c}}^{X}(V_{1}^{\infty},V_{2}^{\infty})
. Recall from Corollary
5.23
that
V_{1}^{\infty}
and
V_{2}^{\infty}
stand for the sets of points
i\in V_{X}
interacting with bad points with respective weight
a_{i\infty}^{v^{Z}}
and
b_{i\infty}^{v^{Z}}
.

Lemma 5.26

(Graph structure)
.

Let
X
be a subpartition of
[N]
and
V_{1}^{\infty},V_{2}^{\infty}\subset V_{X}
be disjoint. Set
V^{\infty}\coloneqq V_{1}^{\infty}\cup V_{2}^{\infty}
. Suppose that
V^{\infty}\neq\emptyset
and let

X^{\infty}=\{[i]^{X}:i\in V^{\infty}\}.

Let
E\in\mathrm{Eul}_{\mathrm{c}}^{X}(V_{1}^{\infty},V_{2}^{\infty})
and set
G\coloneqq(V_{X},E)
. Let
G_{1},\ldots,G_{n}
be the bridge-blocks and
\mathcal{E}_{\mathrm{br}}
be the bridges of
G
relative to
X
. For every
i\in[n]
, let
B_{i}
be the set of multipoles adjacent to some edge in
G_{i}
or to some edge in a pendant tree attached to
V(G_{i})
. Let
Y\coloneqq\{V(G_{i}):1\leq i\leq n\}
.

Then, the following holds:

(1)

Let
S
be a leaf of one of the pendant trees, i.e. let
S\in X
be such that
\deg_{E}(S)=1
. Then,
S\in X^{\infty}
.

(2)

Suppose that the tree
(V_{X},\mathcal{E}_{\mathrm{br}})/Y
is not reduced to a point. Let
l\in[n]
be such that
V(G_{l})
is a leaf of
(V_{X},\mathcal{E}_{\mathrm{br}})/Y
. Then,

B_{l}\cap X^{\infty}\neq\emptyset.

Proof.

By the definition of
\mathrm{Eul}_{\mathrm{c}}^{X}(V_{1}^{\infty},V_{2}^{\infty})
, since the degree of
S
in
(V_{X},E)
relative to
X
is odd, we must have
S\in X^{\infty}
(all multipoles not touching
V^{\infty}
have even degree).

Let us now prove (2). The argument is the same as in the proof of Lemma
4.33
. Let
E_{l}
be the set of edges in
E
adjacent to some
S\in B_{l}
. Let
a\in B_{l}
and
b\notin B_{l}
be such that
ab\in E
. Suppose by contradiction that

B_{l}\cap X^{\infty}=\emptyset.

Since
E\in\mathrm{Eul}^{X}(V^{\infty}_{1},V^{\infty}_{2})
, we therefore get that the part
[a]^{X}
has even degree relative to
X
in the graph
(V_{B_{l}},E_{l}\cup\{ab\})
. Hence,
[a]^{X}
has odd degree relative to
X
in the graph
(V_{B_{l}},E_{l})
. By the handshaking lemma, there exists an even number of
S\in B_{l}
with odd degree relative to
X
in the graph
(V_{B_{l}},E_{l})
. Therefore, there must be another
S^{\prime}\neq S
in
B_{l}
with odd degree relative to
X
in the graph
(V_{B_{l}},E_{l})
. Thus,
S^{\prime}
also has odd degree relative to
X
in the graph
(V_{X},E)
, which is a contradiction since
E\in\mathrm{Eul}^{X}(V^{\infty}_{1},V^{\infty}_{2})
and
X^{\infty}\cap B_{l}=\emptyset.

∎

As in Definition
4.34
, we peel each 2-edge-connected block down to a graph that is minimally 2-edge-connected relative to
X
. This reduction leaves the resulting graph with only
O\bigl(|V_{X}|\bigr)
edges, ensuring that our activity estimates remain well controlled. By Lemma
4.50
, the edges removed during peeling simply migrate to the interaction term in the exponential term of (
4.98
).

Definition 5.27

(Peeling into a minimal skeleton)
.

Let
X
be a subpartition of
V_{\mathrm{good}}
,
E\in\mathsf{E}^{X}
and
G\coloneqq(V_{X},E)
. Let
G_{1},\ldots,G_{n}
be the bridge-blocks,
P_{1},\ldots,P_{k}
be the pendant trees and
\mathcal{E}_{\mathrm{br}}
be the bridges of
G
relative to
X
. For every
i\in[n]
, let
G_{i}^{\prime}\subset G_{i}
be a spanning 2-edge-connected relative to
X
subgraph of
G_{i}
(if multiple exist, select one according to the lexicographical order). We let

\mathrm{Peeled}_{X}(E)\coloneqq\bigcup_{i=1}^{n}E(G_{i}^{\prime})\cup\bigcup_{i=1}^{k}E(P_{i})\cup\mathcal{E}_{\mathrm{br}}.

We say that
\mathrm{Peeled}_{X}(E)
is the “minimal skeleton of
E
relative to
X
”. If
\mathrm{Peeled}_{X}(E)=X
, we say that
E
is a minimal skeleton relative to
X
.

From every
minimally 2-edge-connected
block of the reduced skeleton we now
extract a spanning tree using the algorithm described in Definition
4.39
. Adding the pendant trees together with the
collection of bridges then produces a spanning tree of the whole graph relative to
X
.

Definition 5.28

(Peeling of a minimal skeleton)
.

Let
X
be a subpartition of
V_{\mathrm{good}}
and let
E\in\mathsf{E}^{X}
be a minimal skeleton. Let
G\coloneqq(V_{X},E)
. Let
G_{1},\ldots,G_{n}
be the bridge-blocks,
P_{1},\ldots,P_{k}
be the pendant trees, and
\mathcal{E}_{\mathrm{br}}
be the bridges of
G
relative to
X
. Recall
\mathcal{T}^{X}
and
\mathcal{F}^{X}
from Definition
4.40
.

We define

\mathcal{T}^{X}(\cdot,E)\coloneqq\bigcup_{i=1}^{n}\mathcal{T}^{X}(\cdot,E(G_{i}))\cup\bigcup_{i=1}^{k}E(P_{i})\cup\mathcal{E}_{\mathrm{br}},\quad\text{and}\quad{\mathcal{F}}_{+}^{X}(\cdot,E)\coloneqq\bigcup_{i=1}^{n}{\mathcal{F}}^{X}(\cdot,E(G_{i})).

5.7.
Study of the
2
-edge connected blocks and their pendant trees

By Lemma
5.26
, the subgraphs with odd internal weights can be reduced to a
2
-core with pendant trees, whose leaves are linked to bad points.

For later use, we need a nice way to decompose a tree into disjoint-edge paths starting at the leaves and ending at branching points or at the root. We use the heavy-light decomposition algorithm
[
ST83
,
HT84
]
.

Definition 5.29

(Heavy-light decomposition)
.

Let
T=(V,E)
be a finite rooted tree with root
r
.

For every internal vertex
u
of
T
, choose one child
c
whose subtree is largest (breaking ties arbitrarily) and declare the edge
(u,c)

heavy
;
all other child edges of
u
are
light
.
Starting at a leaf
i
distinct from the root, move upward along heavy edges
until you meet
the first light edge (or reach
r
); call the parent endpoint of that light edge
\mathrm{loc}_{T}(i)
.
Because heavy edges form disjoint root-directed chains, each terminating
at a light edge or at
r
, the map

\mathrm{loc}_{T}:\mathrm{Leaves}(T)\setminus\{r\}\longrightarrow V,\qquad\mathrm{loc}_{T}(i)\coloneqq\text{ancestor endpoint of light-edge at end of $i$'s heavy chain},

is well defined. Note that if the leaf
i
is not in a heavy chain, i.e. adjacent to a light edge, then
\mathrm{loc}_{T}(i)
is just the ancestor of the leaf, i.e. the other endpoint of the edge.

Figure 11
.
Left: rooted tree
T
with root
r
; heavy edges are black, light edges grey. Right: decomposition
T
into edge disjoint paths. Alternating blue, red and green colors delineate different paths. The head of each colored path starting at a leaf
v
is the vertex
\mathrm{loc}_{T}(v)
, which is also the parent of the endpoint of the heavy chain starting at
v
.

Lemma 5.30
.

Let
T=(V,E)
be a finite tree with root
r
. Let
\mathrm{loc}_{T}
be as in Definition
5.29
. For every
i\in\mathrm{Leaves}(T)\setminus\{r\}
, let
P(i,\mathrm{loc}_{T}(i))
stand for the set of edges in
T
between
i
and
\mathrm{loc}_{T}(i)
. Then,

(5.66)

E=\bigsqcup_{i\in\mathrm{Leaves}(T)\setminus\{r\}}P(i,\mathrm{loc}_{T}(i)).

In particular,

(5.67)

\sum_{i\in\mathrm{Leaves}(T)\setminus\{r\}}\mathrm{dist}_{T}(i,\mathrm{loc}_{T}(i))=|V|-1.

Moreover, for every
i\in V_{X}\setminus\{r\}
,

(5.68)

|\{l\in\mathrm{Leaves}(T)\setminus\{r\}:\mathrm{loc}_{T}(l)=i\}|=\deg_{T}(i)-2

and

(5.69)

|\{l\in\mathrm{Leaves}(T)\setminus\{r\}:\mathrm{loc}_{T}(l)=r\}|=\deg_{T}(r).

The proof is standard and is, therefore, omitted.

We now study the product of the odd weights over pendant trees. Recall that by Lemma
5.26
item (1), every leaf
i
of the pendant trees interacts with bad points, hence comes with a weight
a_{i\infty}^{v^{Z}}
or
b_{i\infty}^{v^{Z}}
. By (
5.64
), we can bound this term by
C\frac{r_{i}}{d_{i,\mathrm{bad}}}
. We therefore need to estimate the following:

Lemma 5.31
.

Let
X
be a subpartition of
V_{\mathrm{good}}
such that for every
S\in X
, we have
|S|\leq p(\beta)
. Let
P\in\mathsf{T}^{X}
be a rooted tree with root
a\in V_{X}
. Recall
d_{i,\mathrm{bad}}
from (
5.3
). There exists a constant
C>0
depending on
\beta
,
M
and
p(\beta)
such that

\left(\prod_{ij\in P}|a_{ij}^{v^{Z}}|\mathbf{1}_{\mathcal{B}_{ij}^{c}}\right)\left(\prod_{i\in\mathrm{Leaves}(P)\setminus\{a\}}\frac{r_{i}}{d_{i,\mathrm{bad}}}\right)\mathbf{1}_{V_{X}\subset I_{\mathrm{good}}}\mathbf{1}_{\Pi_{\mathrm{mult}}^{V_{X}}=X}\\
\leq C^{|P|}\left(\prod_{S\in X:S\neq[a]^{X}}r_{S}^{2}\right)\left(\prod_{ij\in P}\frac{1}{d_{ij}^{2}}\mathbf{1}_{\min(r_{i},r_{j})\leq d_{ij}\leq 16\varepsilon_{0}\bar{R}_{\beta,\lambda}}\right)\Bigr(\frac{r_{a}}{d_{[a]^{X},\mathrm{bad}}}\Bigr).

Let us explain the proof of the lemma. Start with the toy case
P=(12)
and declare
1
as the root. One should think of
P
as rooted at
1
in the 2-core.

By Lemma
3.9
, we have

|a_{12}^{v^{Z}}|\leq C\frac{r_{1}r_{2}}{\max(d_{12},\lambda)\max(d_{12},r_{1},r_{2})}\mathbf{1}_{d_{12}\leq 2\varepsilon_{0}\bar{R}_{\beta,\lambda}}\leq Ca_{ij}^{\mathrm{abs}}.

Therefore,

(5.70)

|a_{12}^{v^{Z}}|\frac{r_{2}}{d_{2,\mathrm{bad}}}\leq C\frac{r_{2}^{2}}{d_{12}\max(d_{12},r_{1},r_{2})}\mathbf{1}_{d_{12}\leq 2\varepsilon_{0}\bar{R}_{\beta,\lambda}}\frac{r_{1}}{d_{2,\mathrm{bad}}}.

Since the tree
12
is rooted in the 2-core at
1
, the term
r_{1}^{2}
will already appear in the peeling of the 2-core. Hence, to have a purely quadratic estimate, our bound on
|a_{12}^{v^{Z}}||f_{2\infty}^{v^{Z}}|
should not contain the term
r_{1}
. The key fact is that, if
a_{12}^{v^{Z}}\neq 0
, then
v_{12}^{Z}\neq 0
and therefore, by the estimate (
5.13
) of Lemma
5.9
,

d_{2,\mathrm{bad}}\geq\frac{1}{4}d_{1,\mathrm{bad}}.

Moreover, by (
5.9
) in Lemma
5.9
, since
1
is a good point, we have
r_{1}\leq d_{1,\mathrm{bad}}
.
Inserting these two facts into (
5.70
), we obtain

|a_{12}^{v^{Z}}|\frac{r_{2}}{d_{2,\mathrm{bad}}}\leq C\frac{r_{2}^{2}}{d_{12}\max(d_{12},r_{1},r_{2})}\mathbf{1}_{d_{12}\leq 2\varepsilon_{0}\bar{R}_{\beta,\lambda}}.

For a general tree
P\in\mathsf{T}^{X}
, we apply the assignment map (Definition
5.29
) to split
P
into edge-disjoint paths, each running from a leaf
l
distinct from the root to its assigned branching vertex (or the root)
\mathrm{loc}_{P}(l)
.
On such a path, the term
d_{l,\mathrm{bad}}^{-1}
that accompanies the leaf is sufficient to absorb the radius of the endpoint, because

d_{l,\mathrm{bad}}^{-1}\leq 4^{\mathrm{dist}_{P}(l,\mathrm{loc}_{P}(l))}\,d_{\mathrm{loc}_{P}(l),\mathrm{bad}}^{-1}.

Replacing
d_{l,\mathrm{bad}}^{-1}
by
d_{\mathrm{loc}_{P}(l),\mathrm{bad}}^{-1}
therefore costs at most the factor

4^{\mathrm{dist}_{P}(l,\mathrm{loc}_{P}(l))}
. Summing these distances over all leaf–to–root paths gives a total exponent equal to
|P|-1
.

Consequently, every extra radius introduced at a branching vertex (or at the root) is canceled by the corresponding leaf denominator, at the harmless overall cost
C^{|P|}
.

Proof.

We work on the event where
V_{X}\subset I_{\mathrm{good}}
,
\Pi_{\mathrm{mult}}^{V_{X}}=X
and where

\prod_{ij\in P}|a_{ij}^{v^{Z}}|\neq 0,

otherwise the bound is clear.

Step 1: heavy-light decomposition

Let
\mathrm{loc}_{(V_{X},P)/X}
be the map of Definition
5.29
for the tree
(V_{X},P)/X
, rooted at
[a]^{X}
. For every
w\in\mathrm{Leaves}(P)\setminus\{a\}
, we let
\mathrm{loc}(w)
be the unique
i\in V_{X}
such that

[i]^{X}=\mathrm{loc}_{(V_{X},P)/X}([w]^{X})

and such that the simple path in the quotient from
[w]^{X}
to
[i]^{X}
lifts into a path with endpoints
w
,
i
.

For every
w\in\mathrm{Leaves}(P)\setminus\{a\}
, let
P(w,\mathrm{loc}(w))
stand for the set of edges between
w
and
\mathrm{loc}(w)
in
P
.
Recall that by Lemma
5.30
,

P=\bigsqcup_{w\in\mathrm{Leaves}(P)\setminus\{a\}}P(w,\mathrm{loc}(w)).

Thus,

(5.71)

\left(\prod_{ij\in P}|a_{ij}^{v^{Z}}|\mathbf{1}_{\mathcal{B}_{ij}^{c}}\right)\left(\prod_{i\in\mathrm{Leaves}(P)\setminus\{a\}}\frac{r_{i}}{d_{i,\mathrm{bad}}}\right)=\prod_{w\in\mathrm{Leaves}(P)\setminus\{a\}}\left(\left(\prod_{ij\in P(w,\mathrm{loc}(w))}|a_{ij}^{v^{Z}}|\mathbf{1}_{\mathcal{B}_{ij}^{c}}\right)\frac{r_{w}}{d_{w,\mathrm{bad}}}\right).

Step 2: equilibrating degrees in each path

Fix
w\in\mathrm{Leaves}(P)\setminus\{a\}
and let
x\coloneqq\mathrm{loc}(w)
. By Remark
5.21
, (
5.65
), we have

\prod_{ij\in P(w,x)}|a_{ij}^{v^{Z}}\mathbf{1}_{\mathcal{B}_{ij}^{c}}|\leq C^{|P(w,x)|}\prod_{ij\in P(w,x)}\frac{r_{i}r_{j}}{d_{ij}\max(d_{ij},r_{i},r_{j})}\mathbf{1}_{d_{ij}\leq 16\varepsilon_{0}\bar{R}_{\beta,\lambda}}.

Notice that

\prod_{ij\in P(w,x)}r_{i}r_{j}\leq r_{w}r_{x}\prod_{S\in\mathrm{Res}(X,P(w,x))\setminus\{[w]^{X},[x]^{X}\}}r_{S}^{2},

where we recall from Definition
4.8
that for every
E\subset\mathcal{E}^{\mathrm{inter}}(X)
,
\mathrm{Res}(X,E)
stands for the set of
S\in X
adjacent to some edge in
E
. Therefore, combining the above displays, we have

(5.72)

\left(\prod_{ij\in P(w,x)}|a_{ij}^{v^{Z}}|\mathbf{1}_{\mathcal{B}_{ij}^{c}}\right)\frac{r_{w}}{d_{w,\mathrm{bad}}}\\
\leq C\left(\prod_{S\in\mathrm{Res}(X,P(w,x))\setminus\{[x]^{X}\}}r_{S}^{2}\right)\left(\prod_{ij\in P(w,x)}\frac{\mathbf{1}_{d_{ij}\leq 16\varepsilon_{0}\bar{R}_{\beta,\lambda}}}{d_{ij}\max(d_{ij},r_{i},r_{j})}\right)\frac{r_{x}}{d_{w,\mathrm{bad}}}.

By Lemma
5.9
, estimate (
5.14
), there exists
C>0
depending on
M
and
p(\beta)
such that

d_{x,\mathrm{bad}}\leq C^{|P(w,x)|}d_{w,\mathrm{bad}}.

Therefore, inserting this into (
5.72
), we get that there exists a constant
C>0
depending on
\beta
,
M
and
p(\beta)
such that

(5.73)

\left(\prod_{ij\in P(w,x)}|a_{ij}^{v^{Z}}|\mathbf{1}_{\mathcal{B}_{ij}^{c}}\right)\frac{r_{w}}{d_{w,\mathrm{bad}}}\\
\leq C^{|P(w,x)|}\left(\prod_{S\in\mathrm{Res}(X,P(w,x))\setminus\{[x]^{X}\}}r_{S}^{2}\right)\left(\prod_{ij\in P(w,x)}\frac{\mathbf{1}_{d_{ij}\leq 16\varepsilon_{0}\bar{R}_{\beta,\lambda}}}{d_{ij}\max(d_{ij},r_{i},r_{j})}\right)\frac{r_{x}}{d_{x,\mathrm{bad}}}.

Step 3: conclusion

Applying (
5.73
) to every
w\in\mathrm{Leaves}(P)\setminus\{a\}
and using the heavy-light decomposition (
5.71
) gives

(5.74)

\left(\prod_{ij\in P}|a_{ij}^{v^{Z}}|\mathbf{1}_{\mathcal{B}_{ij}^{c}}\right)\left(\prod_{i\in\mathrm{Leaves}(P)\setminus\{a\}}\frac{r_{i}}{d_{i,\mathrm{bad}}}\right)\mathbf{1}_{V_{X}\subset I_{\mathrm{good}}}\leq C^{|P|}\left(\prod_{S\in X:S\neq[a]}r_{S}^{2}\right)\\
\times\left(\prod_{ij\in P}\frac{1}{d_{ij}^{2}}\mathbf{1}_{\max(\min(r_{i},r_{j}),\lambda)\leq d_{ij}\leq 16\varepsilon_{0}\bar{R}_{\beta,\lambda}}\right)\prod_{x\in V_{X}}\Bigr(\frac{r_{x}}{d_{x,\mathrm{bad}}}\Bigr)^{|\{w\in\mathrm{Leaves}(P)\setminus\{a\}:\mathrm{loc}(w)=x\}|},

where we re-added the constraint
d_{ij}\geq\max(\min(r_{i},r_{j}),\lambda)
in view of the fact that
i
and
j
cannot be in the same multipole. By (
5.9
), for every
x\in I_{\mathrm{good}}
, we have
d_{x,\mathrm{bad}}\geq r_{x}
. Moreover, note that by Lemma
5.30
, there exists at least one
w\in\mathrm{Leaves}(P)\setminus\{a\}
such that
\mathrm{loc}(w)=a
. Therefore,

\prod_{x\in V_{X}}\Bigr(\frac{r_{x}}{d_{x,\mathrm{bad}}}\Bigr)^{|\{w\in\mathrm{Leaves}(P)\setminus\{a\}:\mathrm{loc}(w)=x\}|}\leq C^{|P|}\frac{r_{a}}{d_{a,\mathrm{bad}}}.

Inserting this into (
5.74
) yields the desired result.
∎

We now bound the product of the weights over the 2-edge-connected blocks and their pendant trees.

Lemma 5.32
.

Let
X
be a subpartition of
[V_{\mathrm{good}}]
such that for every
S\in X
, we have
|S|\leq p(\beta)
. Let
V^{\infty}_{1},V^{\infty}_{2}\subset V_{X}
be disjoint. Let
V^{\infty}\coloneqq V_{1}^{\infty}\cup V_{2}^{\infty}
and let
E\in\mathrm{Eul}_{\mathrm{c}}^{X}(V^{\infty}_{1},V^{\infty}_{2})
be a minimal skeleton (see Definition
5.27
) relative to
X
. Set
G\coloneqq(V_{X},E)
.

Let
G_{1},\ldots,G_{n}
be the bridge-blocks,
P_{1},\ldots,P_{k}
be the pendant trees, and
\mathcal{E}_{\mathrm{br}}
be the bridges of
G
relative to
X
. For every
l\in[n]
, let
G_{l}^{\prime}
be the union of
G_{l}
and the pendant trees attached to it. For every
l\in[n]
, let
B_{l}
be the set of multipoles adjacent to some edge in
G_{l}
and
B_{l}^{\prime}
be the set of multipoles adjacent to some edge in
G_{l}^{\prime}
. Let
Y\coloneqq\{V(G_{l}):1\leq l\leq n\}
.

For every
l=1,\ldots,n
, let
\hat{S}_{l}
be the index of the largest
r_{S}
,
S\in B_{l}
. Let
T\coloneqq\mathcal{T}_{+}^{X}(\cdot,E)
and
F\coloneqq\vec{\mathcal{F}}_{+}^{X}(\cdot,E)
be as in Definition
5.28
. Let
g_{ij}
be as in Definition
4.66
.

If
V(G_{l}^{\prime})\cap V^{\infty}=\emptyset
, then there exists
C>0
depending on
\beta,M
and
p(\beta)
such that

(5.75)

\left(\prod_{ij\in E(G_{l}^{\prime})}|a_{ij}^{v^{Z}}|\mathbf{1}_{\mathcal{B}_{ij}^{c}}\right)\left(\prod_{i\in V(G_{l}^{\prime})\cap V^{\infty}}\frac{r_{i}}{d_{i,\mathrm{bad}}}\right)\mathbf{1}_{V_{X}\subset I_{\mathrm{good}}}\mathbf{1}_{\Pi_{\mathrm{mult}}^{V_{X}}=X}\\
\leq C^{|V(G_{l}^{\prime})|}\left(\prod_{S\in B_{l}^{\prime}:S\neq\hat{S}_{l}}r_{S}^{2}\right)\left(\prod_{ij\in E(G_{l}^{\prime})\cap T}\frac{1}{d_{ij}^{2}}\mathbf{1}_{d_{ij}\leq 16\varepsilon_{0}\bar{R}_{\beta,\lambda}}\right)\prod_{ij\in E(G_{l})\cap F}g_{ij}.

If
V(G_{l}^{\prime})\cap V^{\infty}\neq\emptyset
, then there exists
C>0
depending on
\beta,M
and
p(\beta)
such that

(5.76)

\left(\prod_{ij\in E(G_{l}^{\prime})}|a_{ij}^{v^{Z}}|\mathbf{1}_{\mathcal{B}_{ij}^{c}}\right)\left(\prod_{i\in V(G_{l}^{\prime})\cap V^{\infty}}\frac{r_{i}}{d_{i,\mathrm{bad}}}\right)\mathbf{1}_{V_{X}\subset I_{\mathrm{good}}}\mathbf{1}_{\Pi_{\mathrm{mult}}^{V_{X}}=X}\\
\leq C^{|V(G_{l}^{\prime})|}\left(\prod_{S\in B_{l}^{\prime}:S\neq\hat{S}_{l}}r_{S}^{2}\right)\left(\prod_{ij\in E(G_{l}^{\prime})\cap T}\frac{1}{d_{ij}^{2}}\mathbf{1}_{d_{ij}\leq 16\varepsilon_{0}\bar{R}_{\beta,\lambda}}\right)\frac{r_{\hat{S}_{l}}}{d_{\hat{S}_{l},\mathrm{bad}}}\prod_{ij\in E(G_{l})\cap F}g_{ij}.

Proof.

We work on the event where
V_{X}\subset I_{\mathrm{good}}
,
\Pi_{\mathrm{mult}}^{V_{X}}=X
and where

\prod_{ij\in E(G_{l}^{\prime})}|a_{ij}^{v^{Z}}|\neq 0.

For each pendant tree
P
, we denote by
\mathrm{Root}(P)
the unique vertex
at which
P
is attached to the 2-core.

Step 1: the case
V(G_{l}^{\prime})\cap V^{\infty}=\emptyset

By Lemma
5.26
, the parts
S\in X
of degree
1
in
(V_{X},E)
are intersecting
V^{\infty}
. Thus, we deduce that
G_{l}
has no pendant tree, hence
G_{l}^{\prime}=G_{l}
. Since
G_{l}
is minimally 2-edge-connected relative to
X
(which implies
|E(G_{l})|\leq 2(|V_{X_{l}}|-1)
), we deduce from (
5.65
) and Corollary
4.43
that

(5.77)

\prod_{ij\in E(G_{l})}|a_{ij}^{v^{Z}}|\mathbf{1}_{\mathcal{B}_{ij}^{c}}\leq C^{|V(G_{l})|}\left(\prod_{S\in B_{l}:S\neq\hat{S}_{l}}r_{S}^{2}\right)\left(\prod_{ij\in E(G_{l})\cap T}\frac{1}{d_{ij}^{2}}\mathbf{1}_{d_{ij}\leq 16\varepsilon_{0}\bar{R}_{\beta,\lambda}}\right)\prod_{ij\in E(G_{l})\cap F}g_{ij},

which proves the result.

Step 2: the case
V(G_{l}^{\prime})\cap V^{\infty}\neq\emptyset

Let us denote by
I_{l}
the set of
i\in\{1,\ldots,k\}
such that
P_{i}
is attached to
G_{l}
, so that
G_{l}^{\prime}=G_{l}\cup\cup_{i\in I_{l}}P_{i}
.

Assume first that
I_{l}=\emptyset
, i.e.
G_{l}^{\prime}=G_{l}
.

Let
i_{0}\in V(G_{l}^{\prime})\cap V^{\infty}
. By the estimate (
5.9
) of Lemma
5.9
, for every
i\in I_{\mathrm{good}}
, we have
\max(r_{i},\lambda)\leq d_{i,\mathrm{bad}}
. Thus, we deduce that

(5.78)

\prod_{i\in V(G_{l}^{\prime})\cap V^{\infty}}\frac{r_{i}}{d_{i,\mathrm{bad}}}\leq C^{|V(G_{l}^{\prime})|}\frac{r_{i_{0}}}{d_{i_{0},\mathrm{bad}}}.

We next use
r_{i_{0}}\leq r_{\hat{S}_{l}}
. By the estimate (
5.14
) of Lemma
5.9
, there exists a constant
C>0
depending on
M
and
p(\beta)
such that

d_{\hat{S}_{l},\mathrm{bad}}\leq C^{|V(G_{l})|}d_{i_{0},\mathrm{bad}}.

Inserting these into (
5.78
), there exists a constant
C>0
depending on
M
and
p(\beta)
such that

\prod_{i\in V(G_{l})\cap V^{\infty}}\frac{r_{i}}{d_{i,\mathrm{bad}}}\leq C^{|V(G_{l})|}\frac{r_{\hat{S}_{l}}}{d_{\hat{S}_{l},\mathrm{bad}}}.

Combining this with (
5.77
) gives (
5.76
) in the case
V(G_{l}^{\prime})\cap V^{\infty}\neq\emptyset
and
I_{l}=\emptyset.

Now assume that
I_{l}\neq\emptyset
. Fix some
r\in I_{l}
. By Lemma
5.26
, the leaves of
P_{r}
are in
X^{\infty}
. Thus, by Lemma
5.31
, there exists
C>0
depending on
\beta
,
M
and
p(\beta)
such that

(5.79)

\prod_{ij\in E(P_{r})}|a_{ij}^{v^{Z}}|\mathbf{1}_{\mathcal{B}_{ij}^{c}}\prod_{i\in V(P_{r})\cap V^{\infty}}\frac{r_{i}}{d_{i,\mathrm{bad}}}\leq C^{|V(P_{r})|}\frac{r_{\mathrm{Root}(P_{r})}}{d_{\mathrm{Root}(P_{r}),\mathrm{bad}}}\prod_{\begin{subarray}{c}S\in\mathrm{Res}(X,E(P_{r})):\\
S\neq\mathrm{Root}(P_{r})\end{subarray}}r_{S}^{2}\\
\times\prod_{ij\in E(P_{r})\cap T}\frac{1}{d_{ij}^{2}}\mathbf{1}_{d_{ij}\leq 16\varepsilon_{0}\bar{R}_{\beta,\lambda}},

where we recall that for every set
E\subset\mathcal{E}^{\mathrm{inter}}(X)
,
\mathrm{Res}(X,E)
stands for the set of
S\in X
adjacent to some edge in
E
.

For the other pendant trees, we use a rougher estimate: using Lemma
5.31
and (
5.10
), we get that there exists
C>0
depending on
\beta,M
and
p(\beta)
such that for every
u\in I_{l}\setminus\{r\}
,

(5.80)

\prod_{ij\in E(P_{u})}|a_{ij}^{v^{Z}}|\mathbf{1}_{\mathcal{B}_{ij}^{c}}\prod_{i\in V(P_{u})\cap V^{\infty}}\frac{r_{i}}{d_{i,\mathrm{bad}}}\leq C^{|V(P_{u})|}\prod_{\begin{subarray}{c}S\in\mathrm{Res}(X,E(P_{u})):\\
S\neq\mathrm{Root}(P_{u})\end{subarray}}r_{S}^{2}\prod_{ij\in E(P_{u})\cap T}\frac{1}{d_{ij}^{2}}\mathbf{1}_{d_{ij}\leq 16\varepsilon_{0}\bar{R}_{\beta,\lambda}},

where we used that by (
5.9
), for every
a\in V_{X}
,
r_{a}\leq d_{a,\mathrm{bad}}
. Thus, combining (
5.77
) for the 2-edge-connected part, (
5.79
) and (
5.80
), we get that there exists
C>0
depending on
\beta,M
and
p(\beta)
such that

\left(\prod_{ij\in E(G_{l}^{\prime})}|a_{ij}^{v^{Z}}|\mathbf{1}_{\mathcal{B}_{ij}^{c}}\right)\left(\prod_{i\in V(G_{l}^{\prime})\cap V^{\infty}}\frac{r_{i}}{d_{i,\mathrm{bad}}}\right)\\
\leq C^{|V(G_{l}^{\prime})|}\frac{r_{\mathrm{Root}(P_{r})}}{d_{\mathrm{Root}(P_{r}),\mathrm{bad}}}\prod_{S\in B_{l}^{\prime}:S\neq\hat{S}_{l}}r_{S}^{2}\prod_{ij\in E(G_{l}^{\prime})\cap T}\frac{1}{d_{ij}^{2}}\mathbf{1}_{d_{ij}\leq 16\varepsilon_{0}\bar{R}_{\beta,\lambda}}\prod_{ij\in E(G_{l})\cap F}g_{ij}.

By (
5.14
), there exists
C>0
depending on
M
and
p(\beta)
such that

d_{\hat{S}_{l},\mathrm{bad}}\leq C^{|V(G_{l})|}d_{\mathrm{Root}(P_{r}),\mathrm{bad}},

Using this and
r_{\mathrm{Root}(P_{r})}\leq r_{\hat{S}_{l}}
, this concludes the proof of (
5.76
).
∎

5.8.
Proof of the quadratic estimate

We can now prove the quadratic estimate on the product of the odd weights for minimal skeletons.

Corollary 5.33

(Quadratic estimate for minimal skeletons)
.

Let
X
be a subpartition of
[V_{\mathrm{good}}]
such that for every
S\in X
, we have
|S|\leq p(\beta)
. Let
V_{1}^{\infty},V_{2}^{\infty}\subset V_{X}
be disjoint. Suppose that
V^{\infty}\coloneqq V_{1}^{\infty}\cup V_{2}^{\infty}\neq\emptyset
. Let
E\in\mathrm{Eul}_{\mathrm{c}}^{X}(V^{\infty}_{1},V^{\infty}_{2})
be a minimal skeleton relative to
X
. Let
T\coloneqq\mathcal{T}_{+}^{X}(\cdot,E)
and
F\coloneqq\vec{\mathcal{F}}_{+}^{X}(\cdot,E)
be as in Definition
5.28
. Let
g_{ij}
be as in Definition
4.66
,
a_{i\infty}^{v^{Z}}
and
b_{i\infty}^{v^{Z}}
be as in Definition
5.20
. Fix an arbitrary
i_{0}\in V_{X}
.

There exists
C>0
depending on
\beta,M
and
p(\beta)
such that

(5.81)

\left(\prod_{ij\in E}|a_{ij}^{v^{Z}}|\mathbf{1}_{\mathcal{B}_{ij}^{c}}\right)\left(\prod_{i\in V_{1}^{\infty}}|a_{i\infty}^{v^{Z}}|\right)\left(\prod_{i\in V_{2}^{\infty}}|b_{i\infty}^{v^{Z}}|\right)\mathbf{1}_{V_{X}\subset I_{\mathrm{good}}}\mathbf{1}_{\Pi_{\mathrm{mult}}^{V_{X}}=X}\leq C^{|V_{X}|}\left(\prod_{S\in X}r_{S}^{2}\right)\\
\times\left(\prod_{ij\in T}\frac{1}{d_{ij}^{2}}\mathbf{1}_{\min(r_{i},r_{j})\leq d_{ij}\leq 16\varepsilon_{0}\bar{R}_{\beta,\lambda}}\right)\frac{1}{d_{i_{0},\mathrm{bad}}^{2}}\prod_{ij\in F}g_{ij}.

In particular, letting
\hat{S}
be the
S\in X
such that
r_{S}
is maximal, there exists
C>0
depending on
\beta,M
and
p(\beta)
such that

(5.82)

\left(\prod_{ij\in E}|a_{ij}^{v^{Z}}|\mathbf{1}_{\mathcal{B}_{ij}^{c}}\right)\left(\prod_{i\in V_{1}^{\infty}}|a_{i\infty}^{v^{Z}}|\right)\left(\prod_{i\in V_{2}^{\infty}}|b_{i\infty}^{v^{Z}}|\right)\mathbf{1}_{V_{X}\subset I_{\mathrm{good}}}\mathbf{1}_{\Pi_{\mathrm{mult}}^{V_{X}}=X}\leq C^{|V_{X}|}\left(\prod_{S\in X:S\neq\hat{S}}r_{S}^{2}\right)\\
\times\left(\prod_{ij\in T}\frac{1}{d_{ij}^{2}}\mathbf{1}_{\min(r_{i},r_{j})\leq d_{ij}\leq 16\varepsilon_{0}\bar{R}_{\beta,\lambda}}\right)\prod_{ij\in F}g_{ij}.

Suppose that
(V_{X},E)
has bridge-blocks
G_{1},\ldots,G_{n}
. Consider the tree
T^{\prime}
formed by the bridges of
(V_{X},E)
relative to
X
as a tree over
[n]
. Again, we will use the terms
d_{\hat{S}_{l},\mathrm{bad}}^{-1}
in (
5.76
) to cancel the extra radii at the branching points or the root of
T^{\prime}
. As in Lemma
5.31
, we use the geometric estimate (
5.13
) to replace
d_{\hat{S}_{l},\mathrm{bad}}^{-1}
by a well-chosen
d_{S,\mathrm{bad}}^{-1}
up to a cost equal to
C^{\mathrm{dist}_{T^{\prime}}(\hat{S}_{l},S)}
.

Particular care should be taken to show that the total replacement cost is
e^{O(|V_{X}|)}
. (This prevents us from working directly on the quotient graph and the vertices
\hat{S}_{l}
, hence the intricate proof.)

Proof.

We work on the event where
V_{X}\subset I_{\mathrm{good}}
,
\Pi_{\mathrm{mult}}^{V_{X}}=X
and where

\prod_{ij\in E}|a_{ij}^{v^{Z}}|\neq 0.

Let
G_{1},\ldots,G_{n}
be the bridge-blocks,
P_{1},\ldots,P_{k}
be the pendant trees, and
\mathcal{E}_{\mathrm{br}}
be the bridges of
G
relative to
X
.

Step 1: the case
n=1

We claim that if
|V_{1}^{\infty}|=1
, then
|V_{2}^{\infty}|\neq 0
.

Indeed, suppose by contradiction that
|V_{1}^{\infty}|=1
and
|V_{2}^{\infty}|=0
and let
S_{0}\in X
be such that
S_{0}\cap V_{1}^{\infty}\neq\emptyset
. Since
E\in\mathrm{Eul}_{\mathrm{c}}^{X}(V^{\infty}_{1},V^{\infty}_{2})
, by Definition
5.22
we have that
\deg_{E}(S_{0})
is odd. Thus,
S_{0}
is the only
S\in X
such that
\deg_{E}(S)
is odd, which is impossible, since by the handshaking lemma, the number of such
S\in X
is even. We deduce that (provided
V^{\infty}\neq\emptyset)

|V_{1}^{\infty}|\geq 2
or
|V_{2}^{\infty}|\geq 1
.

Suppose first that
|V_{1}^{\infty}|\geq 2
and let
i_{1},i_{2}\in V^{\infty}
be such that
i_{1}\neq i_{2}
. Since
E
is minimally 2-edge-connected relative to
X
, notice that
|E|\leq 2(|V_{X}|-1)
. Hence, by Remark
5.21
and Corollary
4.43
, there exists
C>0
depending on
\beta
such that

\left(\prod_{ij\in E}|a_{ij}^{v^{Z}}|\mathbf{1}_{\mathcal{B}_{ij}^{c}}\right)\left(\prod_{i\in V_{1}^{\infty}}|a_{i\infty}^{v^{Z}}|\right)\left(\prod_{i\in V_{2}^{\infty}}|b_{i\infty}^{v^{Z}}|\right)\mathbf{1}_{V_{X}\subset I_{\mathrm{good}}}\leq C^{|V_{X}|}\left(\prod_{S\in X:S\neq\hat{S}}r_{S}^{2}\right)\\
\times\left(\prod_{ij\in T}\frac{1}{d_{ij}^{2}}\mathbf{1}_{\min(r_{i},r_{j})\leq d_{ij}\leq 16\varepsilon_{0}\bar{R}_{\beta,\lambda}}\right)\left(\prod_{ij\in F}g_{ij}\right)|a^{v^{Z}}_{i_{1}\infty}||a^{v^{Z}}_{i_{2}\infty}|.

Notice that we have used the fact that for every
i\in V_{X}\setminus\{i_{1},i_{2}\}
, by (
5.64
) and (
5.9
), there exists
C>0
depending on
\beta
such that
|a^{v^{Z}}_{i\infty}|\leq C
and
|b^{v^{Z}}_{i\infty}|\leq C
. Using Remark
5.21
again, there exists
C>0
depending on
\beta
such that

\left(\prod_{ij\in E}|a_{ij}^{v^{Z}}|\mathbf{1}_{\mathcal{B}_{ij}^{c}}\right)\left(\prod_{i\in V_{1}^{\infty}}|a_{i\infty}^{v^{Z}}|\right)\left(\prod_{i\in V_{2}^{\infty}}|b_{i\infty}^{v^{Z}}|\right)\mathbf{1}_{V_{X}\subset I_{\mathrm{good}}}\leq C^{|V_{X}|}\left(\prod_{S\in X:S\neq\hat{S}}r_{S}^{2}\right)\\
\times\left(\prod_{ij\in T}\frac{1}{d_{ij}^{2}}\mathbf{1}_{\min(r_{i},r_{j})\leq d_{ij}\leq 16\varepsilon_{0}\bar{R}_{\beta,\lambda}}\right)\left(\prod_{ij\in F}g_{ij}\right)\frac{r_{\hat{S}}^{2}}{d_{i_{1},\mathrm{bad}}d_{i_{2},\mathrm{bad}}}.

Using (
5.13
) in Lemma
5.9
, we get that there exists
C>0
depending on
M
and
p(\beta)
such that

\frac{1}{d_{[i_{1}]^{X},\mathrm{bad}}d_{[i_{2}]^{X},\mathrm{bad}}}\leq C^{|V_{X}|}\frac{1}{d_{i_{0},\mathrm{bad}}^{2}}.

This proves (
5.81
) in the case
|V_{1}^{\infty}|\geq 2
.

Suppose now that
V_{2}^{\infty}\neq\emptyset
and let
i\in V_{2}^{\infty}
. By Lemma
5.11
, there exists
C>0
depending on
\beta
such that

|b_{i\infty}^{v^{Z}}|\leq C\frac{r_{i}^{2}}{d_{i,\mathrm{bad}}^{2}}.

Thus, using (
5.13
), we get that there exists
C>0
depending on
\beta
,
M
and
p(\eta)
such that

|b_{i\infty}^{v^{Z}}|\leq C^{|V_{X}|}\frac{r_{\hat{S}}^{2}}{d_{i_{0},\mathrm{bad}}^{2}}.

Together with Corollary
4.43
, this concludes the proof in the case where
n=1
.

In the rest of the proof, we assume that
n\geq 2
.

Step 2: heavy-light decomposition

For every
l\in[n]
, let
G_{l}^{\prime}
be the union of
G_{l}
and the pendant trees attached to it. For every
l\in[n]
, let
B_{l}
be the set of multipoles adjacent to some edge in
G_{l}
and
B_{l}^{\prime}
be the set of multipoles adjacent to some edge in
G_{l}^{\prime}
. Let
Y\coloneqq\{V(G_{l}):1\leq l\leq n\}
.

Consider the quotient graph
T^{\prime}\coloneqq(V_{X},\mathcal{E}_{\mathrm{br}})/Y
viewed as a tree on
[n]
, with arbitrary root
a\in[n]
. Let
\mathrm{loc}_{T^{\prime}}
be the allocation map of Definition
5.29
. We define

X^{*}\coloneqq\bigcup_{l\in\mathrm{Leaves}(T^{\prime})\setminus\{a\}}\{S\in B_{l}:\deg_{\mathcal{E}_{\mathrm{br}}}(S)=1\}.

Let
l\in\mathrm{Leaves}(T^{\prime})\setminus\{a\}
and let
u
be the unique neighbor of
\mathrm{loc}_{T^{\prime}}(l)
on the path from
l
to
\mathrm{loc}_{T^{\prime}}(l)
in
T^{\prime}
. Let
S
be the unique element in
X^{*}\cap B_{l}
. We denote by
\mathrm{loc}(S)
the unique
S^{\prime}\in B_{\mathrm{loc}(l)}
such that there exists
S^{\prime\prime}\in B_{u}
and an edge in
\mathcal{E}_{\mathrm{br}}
adjacent to
S^{\prime\prime}
and
S^{\prime}
(see the figure below).

Figure 12
.
Left and center: the quotient graph
T^{\prime}
rooted at
a
and its heavy-light decomposition. Right: the graph
(V_{X},\mathcal{E}_{\mathrm{br}})/X
. Each square represents a block
B_{l}
; each vertex a multipole
S\in B_{l}
adjacent to some edge in
\mathcal{E}_{\mathrm{br}}
. The nodes
S_{1},S_{2},\dots
along the bottom are elements of
X^{*}
, and the circles stand for
\mathrm{loc}(S_{1})
and
\mathrm{loc}(S_{2})
.

Step 3: bounding the weights on bridges

By Remark
5.21
, estimate (
5.65
), there exists
C>0
depending on
\beta
such that

(5.83)

\begin{split}\prod_{ij\in\mathcal{E}_{\mathrm{br}}}|a_{ij}^{v^{Z}}|\mathbf{1}_{\mathcal{B}_{ij}^{c}}&\leq C^{|\mathcal{E}_{\mathrm{br}}|}\prod_{i\in V_{X}}r_{i}^{\deg_{\mathcal{E}_{\mathrm{br}}}(i)}\prod_{ij\in\mathcal{E}_{\mathrm{br}}}\frac{1}{d_{ij}\max(d_{ij},r_{i},r_{j})}\\
&\leq C^{|\mathcal{E}_{\mathrm{br}}|}\prod_{S\in X}r_{S}^{\deg_{\mathcal{E}_{\mathrm{br}}}(S)}\prod_{ij\in\mathcal{E}_{\mathrm{br}}}\frac{1}{d_{ij}^{2}}.\end{split}

We next use that by Lemma
5.11
, there exists
C>0
depending on
\beta
such that

(5.84)

\left(\prod_{i\in V_{1}^{\infty}}|a_{i\infty}^{v^{Z}}|\right)\left(\prod_{i\in V_{2}^{\infty}}|b_{i\infty}^{v^{Z}}|\right)\leq C^{|V_{X}|}\prod_{i\in V^{\infty}}\frac{r_{i}}{d_{i,\mathrm{bad}}}.

Combining (
5.83
) and (
5.84
) with Lemma
5.32
, there exists
C>0
depending on
\beta,M
and
p(\beta)
such that

\left(\prod_{ij\in E}|a_{ij}^{v^{Z}}|\mathbf{1}_{\mathcal{B}_{ij}^{c}}\right)\left(\prod_{i\in V_{1}^{\infty}}|a_{i\infty}^{v^{Z}}|\right)\left(\prod_{i\in V_{2}^{\infty}}|b_{i\infty}^{v^{Z}}|\right)\leq C^{|V_{X}|}\left(\prod_{S\in X\setminus\cup_{l}\{\hat{S}_{l}\}}r_{S}^{2}\right)\left(\prod_{S\in X}r_{S}^{\deg_{\mathcal{E}_{\mathrm{br}}}(S)}\right)\\
\times\prod_{l\in[n]:V(G_{l}^{\prime})\cap V^{\infty}\neq\emptyset}\frac{r_{\hat{S}_{l}}}{d_{\hat{S}_{l},\mathrm{bad}}}\prod_{ij\in T}\frac{1}{d_{ij}^{2}}\mathbf{1}_{d_{ij}\leq 16\varepsilon_{0}\bar{R}_{\beta,\lambda}}\prod_{ij\in F}g_{ij},

where we recall that for every
l\in[n]
,
\hat{S}_{l}
is the index
S
in
B_{l}
such that
r_{S}
is maximal.

By Lemma
5.26
, if
l
is a leaf of
T^{\prime}
, then
V(G_{l}^{\prime})\cap V^{\infty}\neq\emptyset
. Using this and (
5.9
), we obtain

\left(\prod_{ij\in E}|a_{ij}^{v^{Z}}|\mathbf{1}_{\mathcal{B}_{ij}^{c}}\right)\left(\prod_{i\in V_{1}^{\infty}}|a_{i\infty}^{v^{Z}}|\right)\left(\prod_{i\in V_{2}^{\infty}}|b_{i\infty}^{v^{Z}}|\right)\leq C^{|V_{X}|}\left(\prod_{S\in X\setminus\cup_{l}\{\hat{S}_{l}\}}r_{S}^{2}\right)\prod_{S\in X}r_{S}^{\deg_{\mathcal{E}_{\mathrm{br}}}(S)}\frac{r_{\hat{S}_{a}}}{d_{\hat{S}_{a},\mathrm{bad}}}\\
\times\prod_{l\in\mathrm{Leaves}(T^{\prime})\setminus\{a\}}\frac{r_{\hat{S}_{l}}}{d_{\hat{S}_{l},\mathrm{bad}}}\prod_{ij\in T}\frac{1}{d_{ij}^{2}}\mathbf{1}_{d_{ij}\leq 16\varepsilon_{0}\bar{R}_{\beta,\lambda}}\prod_{ij\in F}g_{ij}.

Hence, using (
5.14
) again to replace
d_{\hat{S}_{l},\mathrm{bad}}
by
d_{S,\mathrm{bad}}
with
S
the unique element in
B_{l}\cap X^{*}
, we find that there exists
C>0
depending on
\beta,M
and
p(\beta)
such that

\left(\prod_{ij\in E}|a_{ij}^{v^{Z}}|\mathbf{1}_{\mathcal{B}_{ij}^{c}}\right)\left(\prod_{i\in V_{1}^{\infty}}|a_{i\infty}^{v^{Z}}|\right)\left(\prod_{i\in V_{2}^{\infty}}|b_{i\infty}^{v^{Z}}|\right)\leq C^{|V_{X}|}\left(\prod_{S\in X\setminus\cup_{l}\{\hat{S}_{l}\}}r_{S}^{2}\right)\prod_{S\in X}r_{S}^{\deg_{\mathcal{E}_{\mathrm{br}}}(S)}\frac{r_{\hat{S}_{a}}}{d_{\hat{S}_{a},\mathrm{bad}}}\\
\times\prod_{l\in\mathrm{Leaves}(T^{\prime})\setminus\{a\}}r_{\hat{S}_{l}}\prod_{S\in X^{*}}\frac{1}{d_{S,\mathrm{bad}}}\prod_{ij\in T}\frac{1}{d_{ij}^{2}}\mathbf{1}_{d_{ij}\leq 16\varepsilon_{0}\bar{R}_{\beta,\lambda}}\prod_{ij\in F}g_{ij}.

Notice that

\prod_{S\in X}r_{S}^{\deg_{\mathcal{E}_{\mathrm{br}}}(S)}=\left(\prod_{l\neq a:\deg_{T^{\prime}}(l)\leq 2}\prod_{S\in B_{l}}r_{S}^{\deg_{\mathcal{E}_{\mathrm{br}}}(S)}\right)\left(\prod_{\begin{subarray}{c}l:\deg_{T^{\prime}}(l)>2\\
\mathrm{or}\,l=a\end{subarray}}\prod_{S\in B_{l}}r_{S}^{\deg_{\mathcal{E}_{\mathrm{br}}}(S)}\right).

Moreover, by the definition of
\hat{S}_{l}
,

\prod_{l\neq a:\deg_{T^{\prime}}(l)\leq 2}\prod_{S\in B_{l}}r_{S}^{\deg_{\mathcal{E}_{\mathrm{br}}}(S)}\leq\prod_{l\neq a:\deg_{T^{\prime}}(l)=1}r_{\hat{S}_{l}}\prod_{l\neq a:\deg_{T^{\prime}}(l)=2}r_{\hat{S}_{l}}^{2}.

Therefore, combining the above two displays, we get

\prod_{S\in X}r_{S}^{\deg_{\mathcal{E}_{\mathrm{br}}}(S)}\prod_{l\in\mathrm{Leaves}(T^{\prime})\setminus\{a\}}r_{\hat{S}_{l}}\leq\prod_{l\neq a:\deg_{T^{\prime}}(l)\leq 2}r_{\hat{S}_{l}}^{2}\left(\prod_{\begin{subarray}{c}l:\deg_{T^{\prime}}(l)>2\\
\mathrm{or}\,l=a\end{subarray}}\prod_{S\in B_{l}}r_{S}^{\deg_{\mathcal{E}_{\mathrm{br}}}(S)}\right).

Thus, there exists
C>0
depending on
\beta,M
and
p(\beta)
such that

(5.85)

\left(\prod_{ij\in E}|a_{ij}^{v^{Z}}|\mathbf{1}_{\mathcal{B}_{ij}^{c}}\right)\left(\prod_{i\in V_{1}^{\infty}}|a_{i\infty}^{v^{Z}}|\right)\left(\prod_{i\in V_{2}^{\infty}}|b_{i\infty}^{v^{Z}}|\right)\leq C^{|V_{X}|}\left(\prod_{S\in X\setminus\cup_{l}\{\hat{S}_{l}\}}r_{S}^{2}\right)\prod_{l\neq a:\deg_{T^{\prime}}(l)\leq 2}r_{\hat{S}_{l}}^{2}\\
\times\left(\prod_{\begin{subarray}{c}l:\deg_{T^{\prime}}(l)>2\\
\mathrm{or}\,l=a\end{subarray}}\prod_{S\in B_{l}}r_{S}^{\deg_{\mathcal{E}_{\mathrm{br}}}(S)}\right)\frac{r_{\hat{S}_{a}}}{d_{\hat{S}_{a},\mathrm{bad}}}\prod_{S^{\prime}\in X^{*}}\frac{1}{d_{S^{\prime},\mathrm{bad}}}\prod_{ij\in T}\frac{1}{d_{ij}^{2}}\mathbf{1}_{d_{ij}\leq 16\varepsilon_{0}\bar{R}_{\beta,\lambda}}\prod_{ij\in F}g_{ij}.

Step 4: compensation of high degree multipoles by distances to bad points

We now bound

\frac{r_{\hat{S}_{a}}}{d_{\hat{S}_{a},\mathrm{bad}}}\left(\prod_{\begin{subarray}{c}l:\deg_{T^{\prime}}(l)>2\\
\mathrm{or}\,l=a\end{subarray}}\prod_{S\in B_{l}}r_{S}^{\deg_{\mathcal{E}_{\mathrm{br}}}(S)}\right)\prod_{S^{\prime}\in X^{*}}\frac{1}{d_{S^{\prime},\mathrm{bad}}}.

Let us write

\prod_{S^{\prime}\in X^{*}}\frac{1}{d_{S^{\prime},\mathrm{bad}}}=\prod_{S\in X}\prod_{\begin{subarray}{c}S^{\prime}\in X^{*}:\\
\mathrm{loc}(S^{\prime})=S\end{subarray}}\frac{1}{d_{S^{\prime},\mathrm{bad}}}.

By (
5.14
), there exists
C>0
depending on
M
and
p(\beta)
such that

d_{S,\mathrm{bad}}\leq C^{\mathrm{dist}_{T}(S,S^{\prime})}d_{S^{\prime},\mathrm{bad}},

where we recall that
T\coloneqq\mathcal{T}^{+}(\cdot,E)
and where
\mathrm{dist}_{T}(S,S^{\prime})
is the length of the path in
T
joining
S
and
S^{\prime}
. Therefore,

\prod_{S^{\prime}\in X^{*}}\frac{1}{d_{S^{\prime},\mathrm{bad}}}\leq C^{\sum_{S^{\prime}\in X^{*}}\mathrm{dist}_{T}(S^{\prime},\mathrm{loc}(S^{\prime}))}\prod_{S\in X}\Bigr(\frac{1}{d_{S,\mathrm{bad}}}\Bigr)^{|\{S^{\prime}\in X^{*}:\mathrm{loc}(S^{\prime})=S\}|}.

Using (
5.67
) for the quotient graph, the paths of the heavy-light decomposition are edge-disjoint and therefore

\sum_{S^{\prime}\in X^{*}}\mathrm{dist}_{T}(S^{\prime},\mathrm{loc}(S^{\prime}))\leq|V_{X}|-1.

Thus, there exists
C>0
depending on
M
and
p(\beta)
such that

(5.86)

\prod_{S^{\prime}\in X^{*}}\frac{1}{d_{S^{\prime},\mathrm{bad}}}\leq C^{|V_{X}|}\prod_{S\in X}\Bigr(\frac{1}{d_{S,\mathrm{bad}}}\Bigr)^{|\{S^{\prime}\in X^{*}:\mathrm{loc}(S^{\prime})=S\}|}.

Let
l\in[n]
be such that
l\neq a
. For every
S\in X
, denote by
\mathrm{Child}_{\mathcal{E}_{\mathrm{br}}}(S)
the set of children of
S
in
\mathcal{E}_{\mathrm{br}}
.
In the tree
(V_{X},\mathcal{E}_{\mathrm{br}})/X
, each block
B_{l}
has a unique vertex
S_{l}
incident to the parent edge (i.e., the edge on the path from that vertex to the root). For this vertex,

\deg_{\mathcal{E}_{\mathrm{br}}}(S_{l})=|\mathrm{Child}_{\mathcal{E}_{\mathrm{br}}}(S_{l})|+1,

whereas for every
S\in B_{l}\setminus\{S_{l}\}
,

\deg_{\mathcal{E}_{\mathrm{br}}}(S)=|\mathrm{Child}_{\mathcal{E}_{\mathrm{br}}}(S)|.

Consequently,

(5.87)
