\prod_{S\in B_{l}}r_{S}^{\deg_{\mathcal{E}_{\mathrm{br}}}(S)}\leq r_{\hat{S}_{l}}\prod_{S\in B_{l}}r_{S}^{|\mathrm{Child}_{\mathcal{E}_{\mathrm{br}}}(S)|}.

By the construction of Definition
5.29
, exactly one edge to the children of
l
is heavy. Let
S_{l}^{\prime}
be the unique
S\in B_{l}
adjacent to the heavy edge of the children of
l
(see Figure
12
and notice that we can have
S_{l}=S_{l}^{\prime}
). Observe that for this vertex,

|\{S^{\prime}\in X^{*}:\mathrm{loc}(S^{\prime})=S_{l}^{\prime}\}|=|\mathrm{Child}_{\mathcal{E}_{\mathrm{br}}}(S_{l}^{\prime})|-1,

whereas for every
S\in B_{l}\setminus\{S_{l}^{\prime}\}
,

|\{S^{\prime}\in X^{*}:\mathrm{loc}(S^{\prime})=S\}|=|\mathrm{Child}_{\mathcal{E}_{\mathrm{br}}}(S)|.

Therefore, combining the above two displays,

(5.88)

\prod_{S\in B_{l}}r_{S}^{|\mathrm{Child}_{\mathcal{E}_{\mathrm{br}}}|}\leq r_{\hat{S}_{l}}\prod_{S\in B_{l}}r_{S}^{|\{S^{\prime}\in X^{*}:\mathrm{loc}(S^{\prime})=S\}|}.

Combining (
5.87
), (
5.88
) and (
5.10
), we obtain the existence of
C>0
depending on
\beta,M
and
p(\beta)
such that

(5.89)

\prod_{S\in B_{l}}\left(r_{S}^{\deg_{\mathcal{E}_{\mathrm{br}}}(S)}\prod_{S^{\prime}\in X^{*}}\Bigr(\frac{1}{d_{S,\mathrm{bad}}}\Bigr)^{|\{S^{\prime}\in X^{*}:\mathrm{loc}(S^{\prime})=S|\}}\right)\leq r_{\hat{S}_{l}}^{2}.

It remains to consider the vertices in
B_{a}
. For every
S\in B_{a}
, we have

(5.90)

|\{S^{\prime}\in X^{*}:\mathrm{loc}(S^{\prime})=S\}|=\deg_{\mathcal{E}_{\mathrm{br}}}(S).

Fix some
S_{0}\in B_{a}
of non-zero degree in
\mathcal{E}_{\mathrm{br}}
. Using (
5.90
) and (
5.9
), we get

(5.91)

\prod_{S\in B_{a}}\left(r_{S}^{\deg_{\mathcal{E}_{\mathrm{br}}}(S)}\prod_{S^{\prime}\in X^{*}}\Bigr(\frac{1}{d_{S,\mathrm{bad}}}\Bigr)^{|\{S^{\prime}\in X^{*}:\mathrm{loc}(S^{\prime})=S|\}}\right)\leq\frac{r_{S_{0}}}{d_{S_{0},\mathrm{bad}}}.

Hence, using (
5.13
), we deduce that there exists
C>0
depending on
M
and
p(\beta)
such that

(5.92)

\prod_{S\in B_{a}}\left(r_{S}^{\deg_{\mathcal{E}_{\mathrm{br}}}(S)}\prod_{S^{\prime}\in X^{*}}\Bigr(\frac{1}{d_{S,\mathrm{bad}}}\Bigr)^{|\{S^{\prime}\in X^{*}:\mathrm{loc}(S^{\prime})=S|\}}\right)\leq C^{|V_{X}|}\frac{r_{\hat{S}_{a}}}{d_{\hat{S}_{a},\mathrm{bad}}}.

Thus, taking the product of (
5.89
) for
l
such
\deg_{T^{\prime}}(l)>2
and
l\neq a
, multiplying by
(
5.92
) and then by
\frac{r_{\hat{S}_{a}}}{d_{\hat{S}_{a},\mathrm{bad}}}
gives the existence of
C>0
depending on
M
and
p(\beta)
such that

(5.93)

\frac{r_{\hat{S}_{a}}}{d_{\hat{S}_{a},\mathrm{bad}}}\left(\prod_{\begin{subarray}{c}l:\deg_{T^{\prime}}(l)>2\\
\mathrm{or}\,l=a\end{subarray}}\prod_{S\in B_{l}}r_{S}^{\deg_{\mathcal{E}_{\mathrm{br}}}(S)}\right)\prod_{S^{\prime}\in X^{*}}\frac{1}{d_{S^{\prime},\mathrm{bad}}}\leq C^{|V_{X}|}\left(\prod_{\begin{subarray}{c}l:\deg_{T^{\prime}}(l)>2\\
\mathrm{or}\,l=a\end{subarray}}r_{\hat{S}_{l}}^{2}\right)\frac{1}{d_{\hat{S}_{a},\mathrm{bad}}^{2}}.

Step 5: conclusion

Notice that by using (
5.13
), one can replace
d_{\hat{S}_{a},\mathrm{bad}}
by
d_{i_{0},\mathrm{bad}}
up to a multiplicative constant
C^{|V_{X}|}
, with
C
depending on
M
and
p(\beta)
. Inserting (
5.93
) into (
5.85
) gives the desired result.
∎

5.9.
Proof of the bounded-cluster results with frozen bad points

Proof of Proposition
5.17
.

Denote
k\coloneqq|V_{X}|
. Taking
w=v^{Z}
in Lemma
A.3
proves the estimates (
5.57
) and (
5.58
).

Let us now prove (
5.59
). By Corollary
5.23
,

\mathsf{K}_{\varepsilon_{0}}^{+,Z}(X)=\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}V_{1}^{\infty},V_{2}^{\infty}\subset V_{X}:\\
V_{1}^{\infty}\cap V_{2}^{\infty}=\emptyset\end{subarray}}\sum_{K=1}^{\infty}\frac{1}{K!}\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}Y_{1},\ldots,Y_{K}\subset X\\
\mathrm{disjoint}\end{subarray}}\prod_{l=1}^{K}\Biggr(\sum_{E_{l,1}\in\mathrm{Eul}^{Y_{l}}(V_{Y_{l}}\cap V_{1}^{\infty},V_{Y_{l}}\cap V_{2}^{\infty})}\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}E_{l,2}:E_{l,1}\cup E_{l,2}\in\mathsf{E}^{Y_{l}}\\
E_{l,1}\cap E_{l,2}=\emptyset\end{subarray}}\Biggr)\\
\sum_{F\in\mathsf{E}^{\mathrm{Coa}_{X}(Y_{1},\ldots,Y_{K})}}\mathbb{E}_{\mathsf{P}_{X}^{+,\varepsilon_{0},Z}}\left[\prod_{l=1}^{K}\left(\prod_{ij\in E_{l,1}}a^{v^{Z}}_{ij}\prod_{ij\in E_{l,2}}b^{v^{Z}}_{ij}\right)\prod_{ij\in\cup_{l}\mathcal{E}^{\mathrm{inter}}(Y_{l})}\mathbf{1}_{\mathcal{B}_{ij}^{c}}\prod_{ij\in F}(-\mathbf{1}_{\mathcal{B}_{ij}})\prod_{i\in V_{1}^{\infty}}a_{i\infty}^{v^{Z}}\prod_{i\in V_{2}^{\infty}}b_{i\infty}^{v^{Z}}\right].

We first use that by Lemma
3.9
,

\mathbf{1}_{\mathcal{B}_{ij}}+|b_{ij}^{v^{Z}}|\mathbf{1}_{\mathcal{B}_{ij}^{c}}\leq b_{ij}^{\mathrm{abs}},

where
b_{ij}^{\mathrm{abs}}
is as in Definition
4.25
.

Let
X_{1},\ldots,X_{n}
be the connected components relative to
X
with at least two multipoles of the graph
(V_{X},\cup_{l=1}^{K}E_{l,1})
. One may extract from
(V_{X},\cup_{l=1}^{K}E_{l,2}\cup F)
a tree
T^{b}\in\mathsf{T}^{\mathrm{Coa}_{X}(X_{1},\ldots,X_{n})}
. Therefore, there exists
C>0
depending on
\beta,M
and
k
such that

|\mathsf{K}_{\varepsilon_{0}}^{+,Z}(X)|\leq C\max_{n}\max_{\begin{subarray}{c}V_{1}^{\infty},V_{2}^{\infty}\subset V_{X}\\
\mathrm{disjoint}\end{subarray}}\max_{\begin{subarray}{c}X_{1},\ldots,X_{n}\subset X\\
\mathrm{disjoint}\end{subarray}}\prod_{l=1}^{n}\max_{E_{l}\in\mathrm{Eul}^{X_{l}}(V_{1}^{\infty}\cap V_{X_{l}},V_{2}^{\infty}\cap V_{X_{l}})}\max_{T^{b}\in\mathsf{T}^{\mathrm{Coa}_{X}(X_{1},\ldots,X_{n})}}\\
\mathbb{E}_{\mathsf{P}_{X}^{+,\varepsilon_{0},Z}}\left[\prod_{l=1}^{n}\prod_{ij\in E_{l}}(|a_{ij}^{v^{Z}}|\mathbf{1}_{\mathcal{B}_{ij}^{c}})\prod_{ij\in T^{b}}b_{ij}^{\mathrm{abs}}\prod_{i\in V_{1}^{\infty}}|a_{i\infty}^{v^{Z}}|\prod_{i\in V_{2}^{\infty}}|b_{i\infty}^{v^{Z}}|\right].

Fix
l\in\{1,\ldots,n\}
and denote by
\hat{S}_{l}
the
S\in X_{l}
such that
r_{S}
is maximal. Let also
T_{l}\coloneqq\mathcal{T}^{X}_{+}(E_{l})
and
F_{l}\coloneqq\mathcal{F}^{X}_{+}(E_{l})
be as in Definition
5.28
. By Remark
5.21
, Corollary
4.43
or Corollary
5.33
(
5.82
) (according to whether
V^{\infty}=\emptyset
or not) there exists
C>0
depending on
\beta
,
M
and
k
such that

\prod_{ij\in E_{l}}|a_{ij}^{v^{Z}}|\mathbf{1}_{\mathcal{B}_{ij}^{c}}\prod_{i\in V_{1}^{\infty}\cap V_{X_{l}}}|a_{i\infty}^{v^{Z}}|\prod_{i\in V_{2}^{\infty}\cap V_{X_{l}}}|b_{i\infty}^{v^{Z}}|\leq C\prod_{S\in X_{l}:S\neq\hat{S}_{l}}r_{S}^{2}\prod_{ij\in T_{l}}\frac{1}{d_{ij}^{2}}\mathbf{1}_{d_{ij}\leq 16\varepsilon_{0}R_{\lambda}}\mathbf{1}_{\mathcal{B}_{ij}^{c}}\prod_{ij\in F_{l}}g_{ij}.

Thus, using the estimate (
4.71
) of Lemma
4.45
, we deduce that there exists a constant
C>0
depending on
\beta
,
M
and
k
such that

|K_{\varepsilon_{0}}^{+,Z}(X)|\leq\frac{C}{\mathsf{M}_{\varepsilon_{0}}^{+,Z}(X)(NC_{\beta,\lambda,\varepsilon_{0}})^{k}}\max_{n}\max_{\begin{subarray}{c}X_{1},\ldots,X_{n}\subset X\\
\mathrm{disjoint}\end{subarray}}\max_{T_{1}^{a}\in\mathsf{T}^{X_{1}},\ldots,T_{n}^{a}\in\mathsf{T}^{X_{n}}}\max_{T^{b}\in\mathsf{T}^{\mathrm{Coa}_{X}(X_{1},\ldots,X_{n})}}\\
\int_{\Lambda^{2k}}\left(\prod_{l=1}^{n}\prod_{S\in X_{l}:S\neq\hat{S}_{l}}r_{S}^{2}\right)\prod_{ij\in T^{b}}b_{ij}^{\mathrm{abs}}\prod_{ij\in T^{a}}\frac{1}{d_{ij}^{2}}\mathbf{1}_{\mathcal{B}_{ij}^{c}}\mathbf{1}_{d_{ij}\leq 16\varepsilon_{0}R_{\lambda}}\min\Bigr(\frac{\max_{i\in V_{X}}r_{i}^{2}}{\max_{e\in\cup_{l}T_{l}^{a}}d_{e}},1\Bigr)^{2}\prod_{S\in X}\mathbf{1}_{\mathcal{B}_{S}}\\
\times\prod_{i\in V_{X}}e^{\beta\mathsf{g}_{\lambda}(r_{i})}\mathrm{d}\vec{r}_{i}.

Thus, inserting the estimate (
A.20
) of Lemma
A.4
in the appendix, we deduce that there exists a constant
C>0
depending on
\beta
,
M
and
k
such that

(5.94)

|K_{\varepsilon_{0}}^{+,Z}(X)|\leq\frac{C}{\mathsf{M}_{\varepsilon_{0}}^{+,Z}(X)(NC_{\beta,\lambda,\varepsilon_{0}})^{k}}N\lambda^{(2-\beta)k}\gamma_{\beta,\lambda,k}.

By the estimates (
A.6
) of Lemma
A.3
, we know that there exists a constant
C>0
depending on
\beta,M
and
k
such that

(5.95)

\mathsf{M}_{\varepsilon_{0}}^{+,Z}(X)\geq C^{-1}\mathsf{M}^{0}_{\infty}(X).

Therefore, combining (
5.94
) with (
5.95
) and (
4.10
), we conclude that there exists
C>0
depending on
\beta,M
and
k
such that

(5.96)

|\mathsf{K}_{\varepsilon_{0}}^{+,Z}(X)|\leq\frac{CN^{1-k}}{\mathsf{M}^{0}_{\infty}(X)}\gamma_{\beta,\lambda,k}

This concludes the proof of the proposition.
∎

Proof of Proposition
5.18
.

Let us first prove (
5.60
). Define

I_{1}(S)=\int_{(\Lambda^{2})^{|S|}}\prod_{i\in S}\mathbf{1}_{i\in I_{\mathrm{good}}^{Z}}\mathbf{1}_{\mathcal{B}_{S}}\prod_{i,j\in S:i<j}e^{-\beta v^{Z}_{ij}}\mathbf{1}_{\mathcal{A}_{ij}}\prod_{i\in S}e^{L\frac{|x_{i}-y_{i}|^{2}}{(\tau_{i}^{Z})^{2}}}\prod_{i\in S}e^{\beta\mathsf{g}_{\lambda}(x_{i}-y_{i})}\mathbf{1}_{|x_{i}-y_{i}|\leq\varepsilon_{0}\bar{R}_{\beta,\lambda}}\mathrm{d}x_{i}\mathrm{d}y_{i}.

and

I_{1}^{\prime}(S)=\int_{(\Lambda^{2})^{|S|}}\mathbf{1}_{\mathcal{B}_{S}}\prod_{i,j\in S:i<j}e^{-\beta v_{ij}}\mathbf{1}_{\mathcal{A}_{ij}}\prod_{i\in S}e^{\beta\mathsf{g}_{\lambda}(x_{i}-y_{i})}\mathrm{d}x_{i}\mathrm{d}y_{i}.

Notice that

\mathsf{M}_{\varepsilon_{0}}^{+,Z}(S)=\frac{1}{(NC_{\beta,\lambda,\varepsilon_{0}})^{|S|}}I_{1}(S)

and

\mathsf{M}^{0}_{\infty}(S)=\frac{1}{(NC_{\beta,\lambda})^{|S|}}I_{1}^{\prime}(S).

By Lemma
A.6
, there exists
C>0
depending on
\beta
,
M
,
\varepsilon_{0}
and
|S|
such that

|I_{1}(S)-I_{1}^{\prime}(S)|\leq C\lambda^{(2-\beta)|S|}(N\bar{R}_{\beta,\lambda}^{-2}+|V_{\mathrm{bad}}|).

Therefore, proceeding as in (
4.76
), we get the existence of
C>0
depending on
\beta
,
M
,
\varepsilon_{0}
, and
|S|
such that

|\mathsf{M}_{\varepsilon_{0}}^{+,Z}(S)-\mathsf{M}^{0}_{\infty}(S)|\leq\frac{C}{N^{|S|}}(N\bar{R}_{\beta,\lambda}^{-2}+|V_{\mathrm{bad}}|).

We now turn to the proof of (
5.61
). Denote
k\coloneqq|V_{X}|
. Recall
\mathsf{K}_{\varepsilon_{0}}^{+,Z}
from Definition
5.15
.

Let
V^{\infty}\subset V_{X}
be such that
V^{\infty}\neq\emptyset
. We claim that there exists
C>0
depending on
\beta,M,\varepsilon_{0}
and
k
such that

(5.97)

\max_{K}\max_{\begin{subarray}{c}Y_{1},\ldots,Y_{K}\subset X\\
\mathrm{disjoint}\end{subarray}}\prod_{l=1}^{K}\max_{E_{l}\in\mathsf{E}^{Y_{l}}}\max_{F\in\mathsf{E}^{\mathrm{Coa}_{X}(Y_{1},\ldots,Y_{K})}}\\
\left|\mathbb{E}_{\mathsf{P}_{X}^{+,\varepsilon_{0},Z}}\left[\prod_{ij\in E_{1}\cup\cdots\cup E_{K}}f^{v^{Z}}_{ij}\prod_{ij\in\cup_{l}\mathcal{E}^{\mathrm{inter}}(Y_{l})}\mathbf{1}_{\mathcal{B}_{ij}^{c}}\prod_{ij\in F}(-\mathbf{1}_{\mathcal{B}_{ij}})\prod_{i\in V^{\infty}}f_{i\infty}^{v^{Z}}\right]\right|\leq\frac{C}{\mathsf{M}_{\varepsilon_{0}}^{+,Z}(X)N^{k}}(N\bar{R}_{\beta,\lambda}^{-2}+|V_{\mathrm{bad}}|).

Indeed, expanding each Mayer bond into an odd part and an even part, using Corollary
5.33
(
5.81
) (taking out
r_{\hat{S}}^{2}=\max r_{i}^{2}
from the rhs product) and the fact that
\mathbf{1}_{\mathcal{B}_{ij}}+|b_{ij}^{v^{Z}}|\mathbf{1}_{\mathcal{B}_{ij}^{c}}\leq b_{ij}^{\mathrm{abs}}
, we obtain the existence of
C>0
depending on
\beta,M,\varepsilon_{0}
and
k
such that

\max_{K}\max_{\begin{subarray}{c}Y_{1},\ldots,Y_{K}\subset X\\
\mathrm{disjoint}\end{subarray}}\prod_{l=1}^{K}\max_{E_{l}\in\mathsf{E}^{Y_{l}}}\max_{F\in\mathsf{E}^{\mathrm{Coa}_{X}(Y_{1},\ldots,Y_{K})}}\\
\left|\mathbb{E}_{\mathsf{P}_{X}^{+,\varepsilon_{0},Z}}\left[\prod_{ij\in E_{1}\cup\cdots\cup E_{K}}f^{v^{Z}}_{ij}\prod_{ij\in\cup_{l}\mathcal{E}^{\mathrm{inter}}(Y_{l})}\mathbf{1}_{\mathcal{B}_{ij}^{c}}\prod_{ij\in F}(-\mathbf{1}_{\mathcal{B}_{ij}})\prod_{i\in V^{\infty}}f_{i\infty}^{v^{Z}}\right]\right|\\
\leq\frac{C}{\mathsf{M}_{\varepsilon_{0}}^{+,Z}(X)(NC_{\beta,\lambda,\varepsilon_{0}})^{k}}\max_{n}\max_{\begin{subarray}{c}X_{1},\ldots,X_{n}\subset X\\
\mathrm{disjoint}\end{subarray}}\max_{T_{1}^{a}\in\mathsf{T}^{X_{1}},\ldots,T_{n}^{a}\in\mathsf{T}^{X_{n}}}\max_{T^{b}\in\mathsf{T}^{\mathrm{Coa}_{X}(X_{1},\ldots,X_{n})}}\\
\int_{\Lambda^{2k}}\min\Bigg(\frac{\max_{i\in V_{X}}r_{i}^{2}}{\min_{i\in V_{X}}d_{i,\mathrm{bad}}^{2}},1\Bigg)\left(\prod_{l=1}^{n}\prod_{S\in X_{l}:S\neq\hat{S}_{l}}r_{S}^{2}\right)\prod_{ij\in T^{b}}b_{ij}^{\mathrm{abs}}\prod_{ij\in T^{a}}\mathbf{1}_{\min(r_{i},r_{j})\leq d_{ij}\leq 16\varepsilon_{0}R_{\lambda}}\\
\times\min\Bigr(\frac{\max_{i\in V_{X}}r_{i}}{\max_{e\in\cup_{l}T_{l}^{a}}d_{e}},1\Bigr)^{2}\prod_{S\in X}\mathbf{1}_{\mathcal{B}_{S}}\prod_{i\in V_{X}}e^{\beta\mathsf{g}_{\lambda}(r_{i})}\mathbf{1}_{r_{i}\leq\varepsilon_{0}\bar{R}_{\beta,\lambda}}\mathrm{d}\vec{r}_{i}.

Thus, using the estimate (
A.21
) from Lemma
A.4
to bound the above integral, we deduce that (
5.97
) holds.

In view of (
5.97
), we can reduce to the situation where
V^{\infty}=\emptyset
up to small errors, and obtain

(5.98)

\mathsf{K}_{\varepsilon_{0}}^{+,Z}(X)-\mathsf{K}_{\infty}^{0}(X)=\sum_{n=1}^{\infty}\frac{1}{n!}\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}X_{1},\ldots,X_{n}\subset X\\
\mathrm{disjoint}\end{subarray}}\sum_{E_{1}\in\mathsf{E}^{X_{1}}}\cdots\sum_{E_{n}\in\mathsf{E}^{X_{n}}}\sum_{F\in\mathsf{E}^{\mathrm{Coa}_{X}(X_{1},\ldots,X_{n})}}\\
\left(\mathbb{E}_{\mathsf{P}_{X}^{+,\varepsilon_{0},Z}}\left[\prod_{ij\in E}f_{ij}^{v^{Z}}\prod_{ij\in\cup_{l}\mathcal{E}^{\mathrm{inter}}(X_{l})}\mathbf{1}_{\mathcal{B}_{ij}^{c}}\prod_{ij\in F}(-\mathbf{1}_{\mathcal{B}_{ij}})\right]-\mathbb{E}_{\mathsf{P}_{X}^{0,\infty}}\left[\prod_{ij\in E}f_{ij}^{v}\prod_{ij\in\cup_{l}\mathcal{E}^{\mathrm{inter}}(X_{l})}\mathbf{1}_{\mathcal{B}_{ij}^{c}}\prod_{ij\in F}(-\mathbf{1}_{\mathcal{B}_{ij}})\right]\right)\\
+O_{\beta,M,\varepsilon_{0},k}\left(\frac{1}{\mathsf{M}_{\varepsilon_{0}}^{+,Z}(X)N^{k}}(N\bar{R}_{\beta,\lambda}^{-2}+|V_{\mathrm{bad}}|)\right).

Fix
X_{1},\ldots,X_{n}\subset X
disjoint and
E_{1}\in\mathsf{E}^{X_{1}},\ldots,E_{n}\in\mathsf{E}^{X_{n}}
and
F\in\mathsf{E}^{\mathrm{Coa}_{X}(X_{1},\dots,X_{n})}
. Denote

I_{2}(E_{1},\ldots,E_{n},F)=\int_{(\Lambda^{2})^{k}}\prod_{i\in V_{X}}\mathbf{1}_{i\in I_{\mathrm{good}}^{Z}}\prod_{ij\in\cup_{l}E_{l}}f_{ij}^{v^{Z}}\prod_{ij\in F}(-\mathbf{1}_{\mathcal{B}_{ij}})\prod_{ij\in\cup_{l=1}^{n}\mathcal{E}^{\mathrm{inter}}(X_{l})}\mathbf{1}_{\mathcal{B}_{ij}^{c}}\\
\times\left(\prod_{S\in X}\mathbf{1}_{\mathcal{B}_{S}}\prod_{ij\in S:i<j}e^{-\beta v^{Z}_{ij}}\right)\prod_{i\in V_{X}}e^{L\frac{|x_{i}-y_{i}|^{2}}{(\tau_{i}^{Z})^{2}}}\prod_{i\in V_{X}}e^{\beta\mathsf{g}_{\lambda}(x_{i}-y_{i})}\mathbf{1}_{|x_{i}-y_{i}|\leq\varepsilon_{0}\bar{R}_{\beta,\lambda}}\mathrm{d}x_{i}\mathrm{d}y_{i}

and

I_{2}^{\prime}(E_{1},\ldots,E_{n},F)=\int_{(\Lambda^{2})^{k}}\prod_{ij\in\cup_{l}E_{l}}f_{ij}^{v}\prod_{ij\in F}(-\mathbf{1}_{\mathcal{B}_{ij}})\prod_{ij\in\cup_{l=1}^{n}\mathcal{E}^{\mathrm{inter}}(X_{l})}\mathbf{1}_{\mathcal{B}_{ij}^{c}}\\
\times\left(\prod_{S\in X}\mathbf{1}_{\mathcal{B}_{S}}\prod_{ij\in S:i<j}e^{-\beta v_{ij}}\right)\prod_{i\in V_{X}}e^{\beta\mathsf{g}_{\lambda}(x_{i}-y_{i})}\mathrm{d}x_{i}\mathrm{d}y_{i}.

We have

\mathbb{E}_{\mathsf{P}_{X}^{+,\varepsilon_{0},Z}}\left[\prod_{ij\in E}f_{ij}^{v^{Z}}\prod_{ij\in\cup_{l}\mathcal{E}^{\mathrm{inter}}(X_{l})}\mathbf{1}_{\mathcal{B}_{ij}^{c}}\prod_{ij\in F}(-\mathbf{1}_{\mathcal{B}_{ij}})\right]=\frac{1}{\mathsf{M}_{\varepsilon_{0}}^{+,Z}(X)(NC_{\beta,\lambda,\varepsilon_{0}})^{k}}I_{2}(E_{1},\ldots,E_{n},F)

and

\mathbb{E}_{\mathsf{P}_{X}^{0,\infty}}\left[\prod_{ij\in E}f_{ij}^{v}\prod_{ij\in\cup_{l}\mathcal{E}^{\mathrm{inter}}(X_{l})}\mathbf{1}_{\mathcal{B}_{ij}^{c}}\prod_{ij\in F}(-\mathbf{1}_{\mathcal{B}_{ij}})\right]=\frac{1}{\mathsf{M}_{\infty}^{0}(X)(NC_{\beta,\lambda})^{k}}I_{2}^{\prime}(E_{1},\ldots,E_{n},F).

Hence,

\left|\mathbb{E}_{\mathsf{P}_{X}^{+,\varepsilon_{0},Z}}\left[\prod_{ij\in E}f_{ij}^{v^{Z}}\prod_{ij\in\cup_{l}\mathcal{E}^{\mathrm{inter}}(X_{l})}\mathbf{1}_{\mathcal{B}_{ij}^{c}}\prod_{ij\in F}(-\mathbf{1}_{\mathcal{B}_{ij}})\right]-\mathbb{E}_{\mathsf{P}_{X}^{0,\infty}}\left[\prod_{ij\in E}f_{ij}^{v}\prod_{ij\in\cup_{l}\mathcal{E}^{\mathrm{inter}}(X_{l})}\mathbf{1}_{\mathcal{B}_{ij}^{c}}\prod_{ij\in F}(-\mathbf{1}_{\mathcal{B}_{ij}})\right]\right|\\
\leq\frac{1}{(NC_{\beta,\lambda,\varepsilon_{0}})^{k}\mathsf{M}_{\varepsilon_{0}}^{+,Z}(X)}\Bigr|I_{2}(E_{1},\ldots,E_{n},F)-I_{2}^{\prime}(E_{1},\ldots,E_{n},F)\Bigr|\\
+{|I_{2}^{\prime}(E_{1},\ldots,E_{n},F)|}\times\left|\frac{1}{(NC_{\beta,\lambda,\varepsilon_{0}})^{k}\mathsf{M}_{\varepsilon_{0}}^{+,Z}(X)}-\frac{1}{(NC_{\beta,\lambda})^{k}\mathsf{M}^{0}_{\infty}(X)}\right|.

By Lemma
A.7
, there exists
C>0
depending on
\beta,M,k
and
\varepsilon_{0}
such that

\Bigr|I_{2}(E_{1},\ldots,E_{n},F)-I_{2}^{\prime}(E_{1},\ldots,E_{n},F)\Bigr|\leq C(N\bar{R}_{\beta,\lambda}^{-2}+|V_{\mathrm{bad}}|)\lambda^{(2-\beta)k}.

Therefore, proceeding as in (
4.82
), we get the existence of
C>0
depending on
\beta,M,k
and
\varepsilon_{0}
such that

\left|\mathbb{E}_{\mathsf{P}_{X}^{+,\varepsilon_{0},Z}}\left[\prod_{ij\in E}f_{ij}^{v^{Z}}\prod_{ij\in\cup_{l}\mathcal{E}^{\mathrm{inter}}(X_{l})}\mathbf{1}_{\mathcal{B}_{ij}^{c}}\prod_{ij\in F}(-\mathbf{1}_{\mathcal{B}_{ij}})\right]-\mathbb{E}_{\mathsf{P}_{X}^{0,\infty}}\left[\prod_{ij\in E}f_{ij}^{v}\prod_{ij\in\cup_{l}\mathcal{E}^{\mathrm{inter}}(X_{l})}\mathbf{1}_{\mathcal{B}_{ij}^{c}}\prod_{ij\in F}(-\mathbf{1}_{\mathcal{B}_{ij}})\right]\right|\\
\leq\frac{CN^{1-k}}{\mathsf{M}^{0}_{\infty}(X)}\Bigr(\bar{R}_{\beta,\lambda}^{-2}+\frac{|V_{\mathrm{bad}}|}{N}\Bigr).

By (
5.98
), this concludes the proof of (
5.61
).
∎

5.10.
Absolute convergence of the cluster series with frozen bad points

We turn to the proof of Proposition
5.19
. We first state the analogue of Lemma
4.49
.

Lemma 5.34
.

Let
V_{\mathrm{bad}}\subset[N]
,
V_{\mathrm{good}}=[N]\setminus V_{\mathrm{bad}}
and
X
be a subpartition of
V_{\mathrm{good}}
such that for every
S\in X
, one has
|S|\leq p(\beta)
. Let
a_{ij}^{\mathrm{abs}}
and
b_{ij}^{\mathrm{abs}}
be as in Definition
4.25
.
Then, there exists
C_{0}>0
depending on
\beta
,
p(\beta)
and
M
such that

(5.99)

|\mathsf{K}_{\varepsilon_{0}}^{+,Z}(X)|\leq C_{0}^{|V_{X}|}\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}V_{1}^{\infty},V_{2}^{\infty}\subset V_{X}\\
\mathrm{disjoint}\end{subarray}}\sum_{n=0}^{\infty}\frac{1}{n!}\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}X_{1},\ldots,X_{n}\subset X\\
\mathrm{disjoint}\end{subarray}}\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}T^{b}\in\mathsf{T}^{\mathrm{Coa}_{X}(X_{1},\ldots,X_{n})}\end{subarray}}\\
\mathbb{E}_{\mathsf{P}_{X}^{+,\varepsilon_{0},Z}}\left[\mathcal{L}_{C_{0}}^{+}(X_{1},V_{1}^{\infty},V_{2}^{\infty})\cdots\mathcal{L}_{C_{0}}^{+}(X_{n},V_{1}^{\infty},V_{2}^{\infty})\prod_{ij\in T^{b}}b_{ij}^{\mathrm{abs}}\right],

where for every subpartition
X^{\prime}
of
[N]
,

(5.100)

\mathcal{L}_{C_{0}}^{+}(X^{\prime},V_{1}^{\infty},V_{2}^{\infty})\coloneqq\sum_{k=0}^{\infty}\frac{1}{k!}\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}X_{1}^{\prime},\ldots,X_{k}^{\prime}\subset X^{\prime}\\
\mathrm{disjoint}\end{subarray}}\sum_{E_{1}\in\mathrm{Eul}_{\mathrm{c}}^{X_{1}^{\prime}}(V_{X_{1}^{\prime}}\cap V_{1}^{\infty},V_{X_{1}^{\prime}}\cap V_{2}^{\infty})}\ldots\sum_{E_{k}\in\mathrm{Eul}_{\mathrm{c}}^{X_{k}^{\prime}}(V_{X_{k}^{\prime}}\cap V_{1}^{\infty},V_{X_{k}^{\prime}}\cap V_{2}^{\infty})}\\
\sum_{\tilde{T}^{b}\in\mathsf{T}^{\mathrm{Coa}_{X^{\prime}}(X_{1}^{\prime},\ldots,X_{k}^{\prime})}}\prod_{i\in V_{X^{\prime}}\cap V_{1}^{\infty}}|a_{i\infty}^{v^{Z}}|\prod_{i\in V_{X^{\prime}}\cap V_{2}^{\infty}}|b_{i\infty}^{v^{Z}}|\prod_{ij\in E_{1}\cup\cdots\cup E_{k}}a_{ij}^{\mathrm{abs}}\prod_{ij\in\tilde{T}^{b}}b_{ij}^{\mathrm{abs}}\prod_{ij\in\mathcal{E}^{\mathrm{inter}}(X^{\prime})}e^{C_{0}a_{ij}^{\mathrm{abs}}}\\
\times\prod_{ij\in\mathcal{E}^{\mathrm{inter}}(X^{\prime})}\mathbf{1}_{\mathcal{B}_{ij}^{c}}.

The proof uses Corollary
5.23
and is entirely similar to the proof of Lemma
4.49
.

We now sum over Eulerian graphs as in Lemma
4.50
to give a bound on the quantity
\mathcal{L}_{C_{0}}^{+}(X,V_{1}^{\infty},V_{2}^{\infty})
defined in (
5.100
).

Lemma 5.35
.

Let
V_{\mathrm{bad}}\subset[N]
,
V_{\mathrm{good}}=[N]\setminus V_{\mathrm{bad}}
and
X
be a subpartition of
V_{\mathrm{good}}
such that for every
S\in X
, one has
|S|\leq p(\beta)
. Let
X_{1},\ldots,X_{n}\subset X
be disjoint. Let
V_{1}^{\infty},V_{2}^{\infty}\subset V_{X}
be disjoint. Let
k\coloneqq|V_{X}|
. There exists a constant
C>0
depending on
\beta
,
p(\beta)
and
M
such that

\mathcal{L}_{C_{0}}^{+}(X,V_{1}^{\infty},V_{2}^{\infty})\leq e^{C|V_{X}|}\sum_{n=1}^{\infty}\frac{1}{n!}\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}X_{1},\ldots,X_{n}\subset X\\
\mathrm{disjoint}\end{subarray}}H_{X}(X_{1},\ldots,X_{n}),

where
H_{X}(X_{1},\ldots,X_{n})
is as in (
4.98
).

Proof.

The argument parallels that of Lemma
4.50
.
Insert the quadratic estimate from Corollary
5.33
(
5.82
) when

V_{1}^{\infty}\cup V_{2}^{\infty}\neq\emptyset
, and from
Corollary
4.43
when
V_{1}^{\infty}\cup V_{2}^{\infty}=\emptyset
;
this yields the desired result.
∎

Combining Lemma
5.34
, Lemma
5.35
and Lemma
4.51
and the multipole partition function estimates from Propositions
4.19
and
5.17
, we obtain the analogue of Lemma
4.52
:

Lemma 5.36
.

Let
V_{\mathrm{bad}}\subset[N]
,
V_{\mathrm{good}}\coloneqq[N]\setminus V_{\mathrm{bad}}
and
Z\in(\Lambda^{2})^{|V_{\mathrm{bad}}|}
. Let
X
be a subpartition of
V_{\mathrm{good}}
such that for every
S\in X
, one has
|S|\leq p(\beta)
. Then, there exists
C>0
depending only on
\beta
and
M
and
C_{0}>0
depending only on
\beta
such that

(5.101)

|\mathsf{K}_{\varepsilon_{0}}^{+,Z}(X)|\leq\frac{e^{C|V_{X}|}}{(N\lambda^{(2-\beta)})^{|V_{X}|}\mathsf{M}_{\varepsilon_{0}}^{0}(X)}\sum_{n=1}^{\infty}\frac{1}{n!}\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}X_{1},\ldots,X_{n}\subset X\\
\mathrm{disjoint}\end{subarray}}\prod_{l=1}^{n}\sum_{T_{l}^{a}\in\mathsf{T}^{X_{l}}}\sum_{T^{b}\in\mathsf{T}^{\mathrm{Coa}_{X}(X_{1},\ldots,X_{n})}}\mathcal{J}_{C_{0}}(\cup_{l=1}^{n}T_{l}^{a},T^{b}),

where for every
T_{1}^{a}\in\mathsf{T}^{X_{1}},\ldots,T_{n}^{a}\in\mathsf{T}^{X_{n}}
,
T^{b}\in\mathsf{T}^{\mathrm{Coa}_{X}(X_{1},\ldots,X_{n})}
,
\mathcal{J}_{C_{0}}(\cup_{l=1}^{n}T_{l}^{a},T^{b})
is as in (
4.114
).

We finally prove the absolute convergence result of Proposition
5.19
.

Proof of Proposition
5.19
.

By Lemma
5.36
, the activity
\mathsf{K}_{\varepsilon_{0}}^{+,Z}(X)
satisfies the exact same bound as
\mathsf{K}_{\varepsilon_{0}}^{-}(X)
in Lemma
4.52
. Therefore, since the proofs of Lemma
4.54
and Proposition
4.23
only depend on this final bound and on the estimate on bounded clusters already established in Proposition
5.17
, this concludes the proof of Proposition
5.19
.
∎

5.11.
Reduction to a system of non interacting bad points

Recall Lemma
5.16
.
In Proposition
5.19
, we have proved that the cluster series (
5.63
) is absolutely convergent, which implies by Lemma
4.6
that for any bad point configuration
Z\in(\Lambda^{2})^{|V_{\mathrm{bad}}|}
, the quantity
W(Z)
is positive and

\log W(Z)=\sum_{n=1}^{\infty}\frac{1}{n!}\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}X_{1},\ldots,X_{n}\in\mathcal{P}(\pi):\\
\mathrm{connected}\end{subarray}}\mathsf{K}_{\varepsilon_{0}}^{+,Z}(X_{1})\cdots\mathsf{K}_{\varepsilon_{0}}^{+,Z}(X_{n})\mathrm{I}(G(X_{1},\ldots,X_{n})).

Moreover, by the estimate (
5.63
) of Proposition
5.19
, one can restrict the sum to clusters of cardinality smaller than
p(\beta)
, and by Proposition
5.18
, one can replace
\mathsf{K}_{\varepsilon_{0}}^{+,Z}
by
\mathsf{K}_{\varepsilon_{0}}^{0}
and we will show that

(5.102)

\log W(Z)=\sum_{n=1}^{\infty}\frac{1}{n!}\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}X_{1},\ldots,X_{n}\in\mathcal{P}(\pi):\\
\mathrm{connected}\end{subarray}}\mathsf{K}_{\varepsilon_{0}}^{0}(X_{1})\cdots\mathsf{K}_{\varepsilon_{0}}^{0}(X_{n})\mathrm{I}(G(X_{1},\ldots,X_{n}))\mathbf{1}_{|V_{X_{1}\cup\ldots\cup X_{n}}|\leq p(\beta)}\\
+O(N\delta_{\beta,\lambda}+|V_{\mathrm{bad}}|).

Note that the right-hand side of (
5.102
) depends only on the bad points configuration
Z
through the cardinality of
V_{\mathrm{bad}}
. Moreover, by Lemma
5.12
, there exists a constant
C>0
depending on
\beta
and
p(\beta)
such that

(5.103)

\prod_{i,j\in V_{\mathrm{bad}}:i<j}e^{-\beta v_{ij}^{Z}}\mathbf{1}_{\mathcal{A}_{ij}}\leq e^{C|V_{\mathrm{bad}}|}.

It therefore remains to control the bad points partition function defined by

(5.104)

\mathsf{Z}_{\mathrm{bad},N-N^{\prime}}\coloneqq\int_{(\Lambda^{2})^{|V_{\mathrm{bad}}|}}\mathbf{1}_{\{I_{\mathrm{bad}}=V_{\mathrm{bad}}\}}\prod_{i,j\in V_{\mathrm{bad}}:i<j}\mathbf{1}_{\mathcal{A}_{ij}}\prod_{i\in V_{\mathrm{bad}}}\frac{1}{(\tau_{i}^{+,Z})^{\frac{\beta}{2}}}\frac{1}{(\tau_{i}^{-,Z})^{\frac{\beta}{2}}}\prod_{i\in V_{\mathrm{bad}}}\mathrm{d}x_{i}\mathrm{d}y_{i}.

The main result will be:

Proposition 5.37
.

Let
\beta\in(2,\infty)
. Let
\mathsf{Z}_{\mathrm{bad},N-N^{\prime}}
be as in (
5.104
). Let
t\geq 1
.

Then, taking
\lambda
and
\varepsilon_{0}
small enough with respect to
\beta,M
and
p(\beta)
, there exists a constant
C>0
depending on
\beta
,
M
,
\varepsilon_{0}
,
p(\beta)
and
t
such that

\binom{N}{N-N^{\prime}}\frac{\mathsf{Z}_{\mathrm{bad},N-N^{\prime}}}{(NC_{\beta,\lambda,\varepsilon_{0}})^{N-N^{\prime}}}e^{t(N-N^{\prime})}\leq e^{CN\delta_{\beta,\lambda}}.

As a first step,
for every
N_{1},N_{2},N_{3}\geq 0
such that
N_{1}+N_{2}+N_{3}=N-N^{\prime}
, define

(5.105)

\mathsf{Z}_{\mathrm{bad},N_{1},N_{2},N_{3}}\coloneqq\int_{(\Lambda^{2})^{|V_{\mathrm{bad}}|}}\prod_{i=1}^{3}\mathbf{1}_{\{I_{\mathrm{bad}}^{i}=V_{\mathrm{bad}}^{i}\}}\prod_{i,j\in V_{\mathrm{bad}}:i<j}\mathbf{1}_{\mathcal{A}_{ij}}\prod_{i\in V_{\mathrm{bad}}}\frac{1}{(\tau_{i}^{+,Z})^{\frac{\beta}{2}}}\frac{1}{(\tau_{i}^{-,Z})^{\frac{\beta}{2}}}\prod_{i\in V_{\mathrm{bad}}}\mathrm{d}x_{i}\mathrm{d}y_{i},

where
V_{\mathrm{bad}}^{1},V_{\mathrm{bad}}^{2}
,
V_{\mathrm{bad}}^{3}\subset[N]
are arbitrary disjoint sets of cardinality
N_{1},N_{2},N_{3}
.

We have

(5.106)

\mathsf{Z}_{\mathrm{bad},N-N^{\prime}}\leq\max_{\begin{subarray}{c}N_{1},N_{2},N_{3}:\\
N_{1}+N_{2}+N_{3}=N^{\prime}\end{subarray}}\frac{(N-N^{\prime})!}{N_{1}!N_{2}!N_{3}!}\mathsf{Z}_{\mathrm{bad},N_{1},N_{2},N_{3}},

which reduces to bounding
\mathsf{Z}_{\mathrm{bad},N_{1},N_{2},N_{3}}
.

5.12.
Reduction to bad points of type 1 and 2

We begin by controlling bad points of type 3 by bad points of types 1 and 2.

Lemma 5.38
.

Let
\mathsf{Z}_{\mathrm{bad},N_{1},N_{2},N_{3}}
be as in (
5.105
).
There exists a constant
C>0
depending on
\beta
,
M
, and
p(\beta)
such that for all
t\geq 1
,

\max_{N_{3}}\left(\Bigr(\frac{N}{N_{3}}\Bigr)^{N_{3}}\frac{1}{(NC_{\beta,\lambda,\varepsilon_{0}})^{N_{3}}}\mathsf{Z}_{\mathrm{bad},N_{1},N_{2},N_{3}}e^{tN_{3}}\right)\leq e^{e^{Ct}(N_{1}+N_{2})}\mathsf{Z}_{\mathrm{bad},N_{1}}^{(1)}\mathsf{Z}_{\mathrm{bad},N_{2}}^{(2)},

where

(5.107)

\mathsf{Z}_{\mathrm{bad},N_{1}}^{(1)}\coloneqq\int_{(\Lambda^{2})^{N_{1}}}\mathbf{1}_{I_{\mathrm{bad}}^{1}=V_{\mathrm{bad}}^{1}}\prod_{i,j\in V_{\mathrm{bad}}^{1}:i<j}\mathbf{1}_{\mathcal{A}_{ij}}\left(\prod_{i\in V_{\mathrm{bad}}^{1}}\frac{1}{(\tau_{i}^{+,Z})^{\frac{\beta}{2}}(\tau_{i}^{-,Z})^{\frac{\beta}{2}}}\right)\prod_{i\in V_{\mathrm{bad}}^{1}}\mathrm{d}x_{i}\mathrm{d}y_{i}

and

(5.108)

\mathsf{Z}_{\mathrm{bad},N_{2}}^{(2)}\coloneqq\int_{(\Lambda^{2})^{N_{2}}}\mathbf{1}_{I_{\mathrm{bad}}^{2}=V_{\mathrm{bad}}^{2}}\prod_{i,j\in V_{\mathrm{bad}}^{2}:i<j}\mathbf{1}_{\mathcal{A}_{ij}}\prod_{i\in V_{\mathrm{bad}}^{2}}e^{\beta\mathsf{g}_{\lambda}(x_{i}-y_{i})}\mathrm{d}x_{i}\mathrm{d}y_{i}.

Proof.

Step 1: fixing
J_{\mathrm{bad}}^{3}

Recall (
5.105
).
Since dipoles in
V_{\mathrm{bad}}\setminus(I_{\mathrm{bad}}^{1}\cup I_{\mathrm{bad}}^{2})
are in multipoles of cardinality smaller than
p(\beta)
, choosing one vertex in
J_{\mathrm{bad}}^{3}
(that is, having an internal bad dipole) per multipole, there exists
\tilde{V}_{\mathrm{bad}}^{3}\subset J_{\mathrm{bad}}^{3}
such that

\tilde{V}_{\mathrm{bad}}^{3}\subset V_{\mathrm{bad}}^{3},\qquad|\tilde{V}_{\mathrm{bad}}^{3}|\geq\frac{1}{p(\beta)}|V_{\mathrm{bad}}^{3}|.

Therefore,

\mathsf{Z}_{\mathrm{bad},N_{1},N_{2},N_{3}}\leq\sum_{\tilde{N}_{3}=\lfloor\frac{1}{p(\beta)}N_{3}\rfloor}^{N_{3}}\binom{N_{3}}{\tilde{N}_{3}}\max_{\tilde{V}_{\mathrm{bad}}^{3}\subset V_{\mathrm{bad}}^{3}:|\tilde{V}_{\mathrm{bad}}^{3}|=\tilde{N}_{3}}I(N_{1},N_{2},N_{3},\tilde{N}_{3}),

where

I(N_{1},N_{2},N_{3},\tilde{N}_{3})\coloneqq\int_{(\Lambda^{2})^{|V_{\mathrm{bad}}|}}\mathbf{1}_{I_{\mathrm{bad}}^{1}=V_{\mathrm{bad}}^{1},I_{\mathrm{bad}}^{2}=V_{\mathrm{bad}}^{2}}\mathbf{1}_{J_{\mathrm{bad}}^{3}=\tilde{V}_{\mathrm{bad}}^{3}}\mathbf{1}_{I_{\mathrm{bad}}^{3}=V_{\mathrm{bad}}^{3}}\prod_{i,j\in V_{\mathrm{bad}}:i<j}\mathbf{1}_{\mathcal{A}_{ij}}\\
\times\prod_{i\in V_{\mathrm{bad}}}\frac{1}{(\tau_{i}^{+,Z})^{\frac{\beta}{2}}}\frac{1}{(\tau_{i}^{-,Z})^{\frac{\beta}{2}}}\prod_{i\in V_{\mathrm{bad}}}\mathrm{d}x_{i}\mathrm{d}y_{i}.

Recall that, from Definition
5.2
and Remark
5.10
, for every
i\in V_{\mathrm{bad}}^{2}\cup V_{\mathrm{bad}}^{3}
, we have

\tau_{i}^{+,Z}=\tau_{i}^{-,Z}=\max(\tfrac{1}{4}r_{i},\lambda).

Therefore, there exists a constant
C>0
depending on
\beta
such that

I(N_{1},N_{2},N_{3},\tilde{N}_{3})\leq C^{N_{2}+N_{3}}\int_{(\Lambda^{2})^{|V_{\mathrm{bad}}|}}\mathbf{1}_{I_{\mathrm{bad}}^{1}=V_{\mathrm{bad}}^{1},I_{\mathrm{bad}}^{2}=V_{\mathrm{bad}}^{2}}\mathbf{1}_{J_{\mathrm{bad}}^{3}=\tilde{V}_{\mathrm{bad}}^{3}}\mathbf{1}_{I_{\mathrm{bad}}^{3}=V_{\mathrm{bad}}^{3}}\prod_{i,j\in V_{\mathrm{bad}}:i<j}\mathbf{1}_{\mathcal{A}_{ij}}\\
\times\prod_{i\in V_{\mathrm{bad}}^{2}\cup V_{\mathrm{bad}}^{3}}e^{\beta\mathsf{g}_{\lambda}(x_{i}-y_{i})}\prod_{i\in V_{\mathrm{bad}}^{1}}\frac{1}{(\tau_{i}^{+,Z})^{\frac{\beta}{2}}}\frac{1}{(\tau_{i}^{-,Z})^{\frac{\beta}{2}}}\prod_{i\in V_{\mathrm{bad}}}\mathrm{d}x_{i}\mathrm{d}y_{i}.

Step 2: integrating variables in
V_{\mathrm{bad}}^{3}\setminus J_{\mathrm{bad}}^{3}

Recall that if
i\in I_{\mathrm{bad}}^{3}\setminus J_{\mathrm{bad}}^{3}
, then
r_{i}\leq\varepsilon_{0}\bar{R}_{\beta,\lambda}
(otherwise
i
would be in
I_{\mathrm{bad}}^{1}
). Moreover, if
i\in I_{\mathrm{bad}}^{3}\setminus J_{\mathrm{bad}}^{3}
, it is in the same multipole as some
j\in J^{3}_{\mathrm{bad}}
, i.e. there exists
j\in J_{\mathrm{bad}}^{3}
such that

d_{ij}\leq M\max(\min(r_{i},r_{j}),\lambda)\leq M\max(r_{i},\lambda).

Hence, if
i\in I_{\mathrm{bad}}^{3}\setminus J_{\mathrm{bad}}^{3}
, then

x_{i}\in\bigcup_{j\in J_{\mathrm{bad}}^{3}}\Bigr(B(x_{j},2M\max(r_{i},\lambda))\cup B(y_{j},2M\max(r_{i},\lambda))\Bigr).

Performing the change of variables
(x_{i},y_{i})\mapsto(x_{i},\vec{r}_{i})=(x_{i},y_{i}-x_{i})
and integrating out the variables
x_{i}
for
i\in V_{\mathrm{bad}}^{3}\setminus\tilde{V}_{\mathrm{bad}}^{3}
, we get that there exists
C>0
depending on
\beta
and
M
such that

I(N_{1},N_{2},N_{3},\tilde{N}_{3})\leq C^{N_{2}+N_{3}}\int\mathbf{1}_{I_{\mathrm{bad}}^{1}=V_{\mathrm{bad}}^{1},I_{\mathrm{bad}}^{2}=V_{\mathrm{bad}}^{2},J_{\mathrm{bad}}^{3}=\tilde{V}_{\mathrm{bad}}^{3}}\prod_{i\in V_{\mathrm{bad}}^{3}\setminus\tilde{V}_{\mathrm{bad}}^{3}}\Bigr(\max(r_{i},\lambda)^{2-\beta}\tilde{N}_{3}\Bigr)\\
\times\prod_{i,j\in V_{\mathrm{bad}}^{1}\cup V_{\mathrm{bad}}^{2}\cup\tilde{V}_{\mathrm{bad}}^{3}:i<j}\mathbf{1}_{\mathcal{A}_{ij}}\left(\prod_{i\in V_{\mathrm{bad}}^{2}\cup\tilde{V}_{\mathrm{bad}}^{3}}e^{\beta\mathsf{g}_{\lambda}(x_{i}-y_{i})}\right)\left(\prod_{i\in V_{\mathrm{bad}}^{3}\setminus\tilde{V}_{\mathrm{bad}}^{3}}e^{\beta\mathsf{g}_{\lambda}(r_{i})}\right)\prod_{i\in V_{\mathrm{bad}}^{1}}\frac{1}{(\tau_{i}^{+,Z})^{\frac{\beta}{2}}}\frac{1}{(\tau_{i}^{-,Z})^{\frac{\beta}{2}}}\\
\prod_{i\in V_{\mathrm{bad}}^{1}\cup V_{\mathrm{bad}}^{2}\cup\tilde{V}_{\mathrm{bad}}^{3}}\mathrm{d}x_{i}\mathrm{d}y_{i}\prod_{i\in V_{\mathrm{bad}}^{3}\setminus\tilde{V}_{\mathrm{bad}}^{3}}\mathrm{d}\vec{r}_{i}.

\bullet
Suppose that
\beta\in(2,4)
. Then, there exists
C>0
depending on
\beta
and
M
such that

I(N_{1},N_{2},N_{3},\tilde{N}_{3})\leq C^{N_{2}+N_{3}}(R_{\beta,\lambda}^{4-\beta}\tilde{N}_{3})^{N_{3}-\tilde{N}_{3}}J(N_{1},N_{2},\tilde{N}_{3}),

where

J(N_{1},N_{2},\tilde{N}_{3})\coloneqq\int_{(\Lambda^{2})^{N_{1}+N_{2}+\tilde{N}_{3}}}\mathbf{1}_{I_{\mathrm{bad}}^{1}=V_{\mathrm{bad}}^{1},I_{\mathrm{bad}}^{2}=V_{\mathrm{bad}}^{2}}\mathbf{1}_{J_{\mathrm{bad}}^{3}=\tilde{V}_{\mathrm{bad}}^{3}}\prod_{i,j\in V_{\mathrm{bad}}^{1}\cup V_{\mathrm{bad}}^{2}\cup\tilde{V}_{\mathrm{bad}}^{3}:i<j}\mathbf{1}_{\mathcal{A}_{ij}}\\
\times\prod_{i\in V_{\mathrm{bad}}^{2}\cup\tilde{V}_{\mathrm{bad}}^{3}}e^{\beta\mathsf{g}_{\lambda}(x_{i}-y_{i})}\prod_{i\in V_{\mathrm{bad}}^{1}}\frac{1}{(\tau_{i}^{+,Z})^{\frac{\beta}{2}}}\frac{1}{(\tau_{i}^{-,Z})^{\frac{\beta}{2}}}\prod_{i\in V_{\mathrm{bad}}^{1}\cup V_{\mathrm{bad}}^{2}\cup\tilde{V}_{\mathrm{bad}}^{3}}\mathrm{d}x_{i}\mathrm{d}y_{i}.

Hence, using (
4.10
) and the fact that
R_{\beta,\lambda}^{4-\beta}=\lambda^{2-\beta}
, we obtain the existence of
C>0
depending on
\beta
and
M
such that

\frac{1}{(NC_{\beta,\lambda,\varepsilon_{0}})^{N_{3}-\tilde{N}_{3}}}I(N_{1},N_{2},N_{3},\tilde{N}_{3})\leq C^{N_{2}+N_{3}}\Bigr(\frac{N_{3}}{N}\Bigr)^{N_{3}-\tilde{N}_{3}}J(N_{1},N_{2},\tilde{N}_{3}).

\bullet
Suppose that
\beta>4
. Then, there exists
C>0
depending on
\beta
and
M
such that

I(N_{1},N_{2},N_{3},\tilde{N}_{3})\leq C^{N_{2}+N_{3}}(\lambda^{4-\beta}\tilde{N}_{3})^{N_{3}-\tilde{N}_{3}}J(N_{1},N_{2},\tilde{N}_{3}).

Therefore, there exists
C>0
depending on
\beta
and
M
such that

\frac{1}{(NC_{\beta,\lambda,\varepsilon_{0}})^{N_{3}-\tilde{N}_{3}}}I(N_{1},N_{2},N_{3},\tilde{N}_{3})\leq C^{N_{2}+N_{3}}\Bigr(\frac{N_{3}}{N}\Bigr)^{N_{3}-\tilde{N}_{3}}\lambda^{2(N_{3}-\tilde{N}_{3})}J(N_{1},N_{2},\tilde{N}_{3}).

\bullet
Suppose that
\beta=4
. Then, there exists a constant
C>0
depending on
p(\beta)
and
M
such that

I(N_{1},N_{2},N_{3},\tilde{N}_{3})\leq C^{N_{2}+N_{3}}|\log\lambda|^{N_{3}-\tilde{N}_{3}}J(N_{1},N_{2},\tilde{N}_{3}).

Therefore, there exists
C>0
depending on
p(\beta)
such that

\frac{1}{(NC_{\beta,\lambda,\varepsilon_{0}})^{N_{3}-\tilde{N}_{3}}}I(N_{1},N_{2},N_{3},\tilde{N}_{3})\leq C^{N_{2}+N_{3}}\Bigr(\frac{N_{3}}{N}\Bigr)^{N_{3}-\tilde{N}_{3}}(\lambda^{2}|\log\lambda|)^{N_{3}-\tilde{N}_{3}}J(N_{1},N_{2},\tilde{N}_{3}).

Therefore, for every
\beta>2
, there exists
C>0
depending on
\beta,M
and
p(\beta)
such that

(5.109)

\frac{1}{(NC_{\beta,\lambda,\varepsilon_{0}})^{N_{3}-\tilde{N}_{3}}}I(N_{1},N_{2},N_{3},\tilde{N}_{3})\leq C^{N_{2}+N_{3}}\Bigr(\frac{N_{3}}{N}\Bigr)^{N_{3}-\tilde{N}_{3}}J(N_{1},N_{2},\tilde{N}_{3}).

Step 3: integrating variables in
J_{\mathrm{bad}}^{3}

By Definition
5.4
, if
i\in J_{\mathrm{bad}}^{3}
, then
r_{i}\leq\varepsilon_{0}\bar{R}_{\beta,\lambda}
and there exists
j\in I_{\mathrm{bad}}^{1}\cup I_{\mathrm{bad}}^{2}
such that
d_{ij}\leq M\max(r_{i},\lambda)
. Therefore, if
i\in J_{\mathrm{bad}}^{3}
, then

x_{i}\in\bigcup_{j\in I_{\mathrm{bad}}^{1}\cup I_{\mathrm{bad}}^{2}}\Bigr(B(x_{j},2M\max(r_{i},\lambda))\cup B(x_{j},2M\max(r_{i},\lambda))\Bigr).

Performing the change of variables
(x_{i},y_{i})\mapsto(x_{i},\vec{r}_{i})=(x_{i},y_{i}-x_{i})
and integrating out the variables
x_{i}
for
i\in\tilde{V}_{\mathrm{bad}}^{3}
thus gives the existence of
C>0
depending on
\beta
and
M
such that

J(N_{1},N_{2},\tilde{N}_{3})\leq C^{\tilde{N}_{3}}\int\mathbf{1}_{I_{\mathrm{bad}}^{1}=V_{\mathrm{bad}}^{1},I_{\mathrm{bad}}^{2}=V_{\mathrm{bad}}^{2}}\prod_{i\in\tilde{V}_{\mathrm{bad}}^{3}}\Bigr(\max(r_{i},\lambda)^{2-\beta}(N_{1}+N_{2})\Bigr)\prod_{i,j\in V_{\mathrm{bad}}^{1}\cup V_{\mathrm{bad}}^{2}:i<j}\mathbf{1}_{\mathcal{A}_{ij}}\\
\times\left(\prod_{i\in V_{\mathrm{bad}}^{2}}e^{\beta\mathsf{g}_{\lambda}(x_{i}-y_{i})}\right)\left(\prod_{i\in\tilde{V}_{\mathrm{bad}}^{3}}e^{\beta\mathsf{g}_{\lambda}(r_{i})}\right)\prod_{i\in V_{\mathrm{bad}}^{1}}\frac{1}{(\tau_{i}^{+,Z})^{\frac{\beta}{2}}}\frac{1}{(\tau_{i}^{-,Z})^{\frac{\beta}{2}}}\prod_{i\in V_{\mathrm{bad}}^{1}\cup V_{\mathrm{bad}}^{2}}\mathrm{d}x_{i}\mathrm{d}y_{i}\prod_{i\in\tilde{V}_{\mathrm{bad}}^{3}}\mathrm{d}\vec{r}_{i}.

\bullet
Suppose that
\beta\in(2,4)
. Then,

J(N_{1},N_{2},\tilde{N}_{3})\leq C^{\tilde{N}_{3}}\Bigr(R_{\beta,\lambda}^{4-\beta}(N_{1}+N_{2})\Bigr)^{\tilde{N}_{3}}\mathsf{Z}_{\mathrm{bad},N_{1}}^{(1)}\mathsf{Z}_{\mathrm{bad},N_{2}}^{(2)},

where
\mathsf{Z}_{\mathrm{bad}}
are as in (
5.107
), (
5.108
).

Thus, since
C_{\beta,\lambda,\varepsilon_{0}}\geq\frac{1}{C}\lambda^{2-\beta}=\frac{1}{C}R_{\beta,\lambda}^{4-\beta}
, we get that

\frac{1}{(NC_{\beta,\lambda,\varepsilon_{0}})^{\tilde{N}_{3}}}J(N_{1},N_{2},\tilde{N}_{3})\leq\Bigr(C\frac{N_{1}+N_{2}}{N}\Bigr)^{\tilde{N}_{3}}\mathsf{Z}_{\mathrm{bad},N_{1}}^{(1)}\mathsf{Z}_{\mathrm{bad},N_{2}}^{(2)}.

\bullet
Suppose that
\beta>4
. Then, there exists
C>0
depending on
\beta
such that

\frac{1}{(NC_{\beta,\lambda,\varepsilon_{0}})^{\tilde{N}_{3}}}J(N_{1},N_{2},\tilde{N}_{3})\leq\Bigr(C\frac{N_{1}+N_{2}}{N}\Bigr)^{\tilde{N}_{3}}\lambda^{2\tilde{N}_{3}}\mathsf{Z}_{\mathrm{bad},N_{1}}^{(1)}\mathsf{Z}_{\mathrm{bad},N_{2}}^{(2)}.

\bullet
Suppose that
\beta=4
. Then, there exists
C>0
depending on
p(\beta)
such that

\frac{1}{(NC_{\beta,\lambda,\varepsilon_{0}})^{\tilde{N}_{3}}}J(N_{1},N_{2},\tilde{N}_{3})\leq\Bigr(C\frac{N_{1}+N_{2}}{N}\Bigr)^{\tilde{N}_{3}}(\lambda^{2}|\log\lambda|)^{\tilde{N}_{3}}\mathsf{Z}_{\mathrm{bad},N_{1}}^{(1)}\mathsf{Z}_{\mathrm{bad},N_{2}}^{(2)}.

Thus, for every
\beta>2
, we deduce that there exists
C>0
depending on
\beta,M
and
p(\beta)
such that

(5.110)

\frac{1}{(NC_{\beta,\lambda,\varepsilon_{0}})^{\tilde{N}_{3}}}J(N_{1},N_{2},\tilde{N}_{3})\leq\Bigr(C\frac{N_{1}+N_{2}}{N}\Bigr)^{\tilde{N}_{3}}\mathsf{Z}_{\mathrm{bad},N_{1}}^{(1)}\mathsf{Z}_{\mathrm{bad},N_{2}}^{(2)}.

Step 4: summing over
\tilde{N}_{3}

Combining (
5.109
) and (
5.110
), we get that there exists
C>0
depending on
\beta,M
and
p(\beta)
such that

\displaystyle\Bigr(\frac{N}{N_{3}}\Bigr)^{N_{3}}\frac{1}{(NC_{\beta,\lambda,\varepsilon_{0}})^{N_{3}}}\mathsf{Z}_{\mathrm{bad},N_{1},N_{2},N_{3}}

\displaystyle\leq e^{C(N_{1}+N_{2}+N_{3})}\sum_{\tilde{N}_{3}=\lfloor\frac{1}{p(\beta)}N_{3}\rfloor}^{N_{3}}\binom{N_{3}}{N_{3}^{\prime}}\Bigr(\frac{N_{1}+N_{2}}{N}\Bigr)^{\tilde{N}_{3}}\Bigr(\frac{N_{3}}{N}\Bigr)^{N_{3}-\tilde{N}_{3}}\Bigr(\frac{N}{N_{3}}\Bigr)^{N_{3}}\mathsf{Z}_{\mathrm{bad},N_{1}}^{(1)}\mathsf{Z}_{\mathrm{bad},N_{2}}^{(2)}

\displaystyle=e^{C(N_{1}+N_{2}+N_{3})}\sum_{\tilde{N}_{3}=\lfloor\frac{1}{p(\beta)}N_{3}\rfloor}^{N_{3}}\binom{N_{3}}{N_{3}^{\prime}}\Bigr(\frac{N_{1}+N_{2}}{N}\Bigr)^{\tilde{N}_{3}}\Bigr(\frac{N}{N_{3}}\Bigr)^{\tilde{N}_{3}}\mathsf{Z}_{\mathrm{bad},N_{1}}^{(1)}\mathsf{Z}_{\mathrm{bad},N_{2}}^{(2)}.

Since
\tilde{N}_{3}\leq N_{3}\leq p(\beta)\tilde{N}_{3}
, there exists a constant
C>0
depending on
p(\beta)
such that

\binom{N_{3}}{\tilde{N}_{3}}\leq e^{CN_{3}}\leq e^{Cp(\beta)\tilde{N}_{3}}.

Therefore, there exists
C>0
depending on
\beta,M
and
p(\beta)
such that for all
t\geq 1
,

\begin{split}\Bigr(\frac{N}{N_{3}}\Bigr)^{N_{3}}\frac{1}{(NC_{\beta,\lambda,\varepsilon_{0}})^{N_{3}}}\mathsf{Z}_{\mathrm{bad},N_{1},N_{2},N_{3}}e^{tN_{3}}&\leq e^{C(N_{1}+N_{2})}\sum_{\tilde{N}_{3}=1}^{N}e^{Ct\tilde{N}_{3}}\Bigr(\frac{N_{1}+N_{2}}{N}\Bigr)^{\tilde{N}_{3}}\Bigr(\frac{N}{\tilde{N}_{3}}\Bigr)^{\tilde{N}_{3}}\mathsf{Z}_{\mathrm{bad},N_{1}}^{(1)}\mathsf{Z}_{\mathrm{bad},N_{2}}^{(2)}\\
&\leq e^{C(N_{1}+N_{2})}\sum_{\tilde{N}_{3}=1}^{N}\frac{e^{Ct\tilde{N}_{3}}}{\tilde{N}_{3}!}\Bigr(\frac{N_{1}+N_{2}}{N}\Bigr)^{\tilde{N}_{3}}N^{\tilde{N_{3}}}\mathsf{Z}_{\mathrm{bad},N_{1}}^{(1)}\mathsf{Z}_{\mathrm{bad},N_{2}}^{(2)}.\end{split}

Thus, summing over
\tilde{N_{3}}
, we recognize the exponential function, and deduce that there exists
C>0
depending on
\beta,M
and
p(\beta)
such that

\max_{N_{3}}\left(\Bigr(\frac{N}{N_{3}}\Bigr)^{N_{3}}\frac{1}{(NC_{\beta,\lambda,\varepsilon_{0}})^{N_{3}}}\mathsf{Z}_{\mathrm{bad},N_{1},N_{2},N_{3}}e^{tN_{3}}\right)\leq e^{e^{Ct}(N_{1}+N_{2})}\mathsf{Z}_{\mathrm{bad},N_{1}}^{(1)}\mathsf{Z}_{\mathrm{bad},N_{2}}^{(2)},

as claimed.
∎

5.13.
Control on bad points of type 1

Next, we control the number of bad points of type 1.

Lemma 5.39
.

Let
\beta\in(2,\infty)
and let
q(\beta)
be as in Definition
5.1
. Let
\mathsf{Z}_{\mathrm{bad},N_{1}}^{(1)}
be as in (
5.107
). Then, there exists a constant
C>0
depending on
\beta
,
\varepsilon_{0}
, and
p(\beta)
such that for all
t\geq 1
,

\max_{N_{1}}\left(\Bigr(\frac{N}{N_{1}}\Bigr)^{N_{1}}\frac{1}{(NC_{\beta,\lambda,\varepsilon_{0}})^{N_{1}}}\mathsf{Z}_{\mathrm{bad},N_{1}}^{(1)}e^{tN_{1}}\right)\leq e^{e^{Ct}N\delta_{\beta,\lambda}}.

Proof.

Step 1: removing the pairing

First, by Lemma
4.3
,

\prod_{i,j\in V_{\mathrm{bad}}^{1}:i\neq j}\mathbf{1}_{\mathcal{A}_{ij}}=\mathbf{1}_{\{\sigma_{N_{1}}=\mathrm{Id}\}},

where
\sigma_{N_{1}}
is the stable matching over
V_{\mathrm{bad}}^{1}
introduced in Definition
4.1
. Hence, since there are
N_{1}!
permutations of
V_{\mathrm{bad}}^{1}
, and since particles are indistinguishable,

(5.111)

\mathsf{Z}_{\mathrm{bad},N_{1}}^{(1)}=\frac{1}{N_{1}!}\int\mathbf{1}_{I_{\mathrm{bad}}^{1}=V_{\mathrm{bad}}^{1}}\prod_{i\in V_{\mathrm{bad}}^{1}}\frac{1}{(\tau_{i}^{+,Z})^{\frac{\beta}{2}}}\prod_{i\in V_{\mathrm{bad}}^{1}}\frac{1}{(\tau_{i}^{-,Z})^{\frac{\beta}{2}}}\prod_{i\in V_{\mathrm{bad}}^{1}}\mathrm{d}x_{i}\mathrm{d}y_{i}.

Step 2: re-clustering

Recall the random subpartition
\mathrm{Clus}_{N_{1}}^{+}
of
[N]
defined in the clustering algorithm of Definition
5.1
. With an abuse of notation, we view
\mathrm{Clus}_{N_{1}}^{+}
as a partition of
V_{\mathrm{bad}}^{1}
. Recall
J_{\mathrm{bad}}^{1,+}
and
J_{\mathrm{bad}}^{1,-}
from Definition
5.2
. Note that we can restrict to the event of full measure where the distances
|x_{i}-x_{j}|
and
|y_{i}-y_{j}|
are all distinct which ensures that at disappearance parameter, clusters are either of cardinality
1
or of cardinality
q(\beta)+1
.
Given
S\in\mathrm{Clus}_{N_{1}}^{+}
, we thus have three cases:

(1)

|S|=q(\beta)+1
, which implies that
S\subset J_{\mathrm{bad}}^{1,+}
. We call such a cluster overcrowded.

(2)

S=\{i\}
for some
i\in I_{\mathrm{bad}}^{1}
such that
\tau_{i}^{+,Z}\geq\varepsilon_{0}\bar{R}_{\beta,\lambda}
, which implies
S\subset J_{\mathrm{bad}}^{1,+}
,

(3)

S=\{i\}
for some
i\in J_{\mathrm{bad}}^{1,-}\setminus J_{\mathrm{bad}}^{1,+}
, which is equivalent to
\tau_{i}^{+,Z}=\max(\tfrac{1}{4}r_{i},\lambda)
. We say that
x_{i}
is “good”.

The same holds for negative clusters.

Notice that if
i\in I_{\mathrm{bad}}^{1}
is such that
\tau_{i}^{+,Z}\geq\varepsilon_{0}\bar{R}_{\beta,\lambda}
, then
r_{i}\geq\varepsilon_{0}\bar{R}_{\beta,\lambda}
and therefore
y_{i}
cannot be good:

(5.112)

\tau_{i}^{+,Z}\geq\varepsilon_{0}\bar{R}_{\beta,\lambda}\quad\Longrightarrow\quad i\in J_{\mathrm{bad}}^{1,-}.

Moreover,
x_{i}
and its partner
y_{\sigma_{N_{1}}(i)}
cannot both be good; otherwise, the dipole would not lie in
I_{\mathrm{bad}}^{1}
:

(5.113)

\{i\in I_{\mathrm{bad}}\setminus J_{\mathrm{bad}}^{1,+}:\sigma_{N_{1}}(i)\in I_{\mathrm{bad}}^{1}\setminus J_{\mathrm{bad}}^{1,-}\}=\emptyset.

Now, we enlarge each overcrowded cluster of same-sign particles by adding partners that are good. For every
S\in\mathrm{Clus}_{N_{1}}^{+}
such that
|S|=q(\beta)+1
, we let
\mathrm{GoodP}^{-}(S)
denote the set of good negative partners,

\mathrm{GoodP}^{-}(S)\coloneqq\left\{i\in S:\sigma_{N_{1}}(i)\in I_{\mathrm{bad}}^{1}\setminus J_{\mathrm{bad}}^{1,-}\right\}.

Similarly, for every
S\in\mathrm{Clus}_{N_{1}}^{-}
, we let
\mathrm{GoodP}^{+}(S)
denote the set of good positive partners,

\mathrm{GoodP}^{+}(S)\coloneqq\left\{i\in S:\sigma_{N_{1}}^{-1}(i)\in I_{\mathrm{bad}}^{1}\setminus J_{\mathrm{bad}}^{1,+}\right\}.

In view of (
5.112
) and (
5.113
), we can partition the points into

(5.114)

\bigcup_{i\in I_{\mathrm{bad}}^{1}}\{x_{i},y_{i}\}=\bigcup_{S\in\mathrm{Clus}^{+}_{N_{1}}:|S|=q(\beta)+1}\left\{\{x_{i}:i\in S\}\cup\{y_{\sigma_{N_{1}}(i)}:i\in\mathrm{GoodP}^{-}(S)\}\right\}\cup\bigcup_{S\in\mathrm{Clus}^{+}_{N_{1}}:|S|=1}\{x_{i}:i\in S\}\\
\cup\bigcup_{S\in\mathrm{Clus}^{-}_{N_{1}}:|S|=q(\beta)+1}\left\{\{y_{i}:i\in S\}\cup\{x_{\sigma_{N_{1}}^{-1}(i)}:i\in\mathrm{GoodP}^{+}(S)\}\right\}\cup\bigcup_{S\in\mathrm{Clus}^{+}_{N_{1}}:|S|=1}\{y_{i}:i\in S\}.

Denote by
\tilde{N}_{1}^{+}
the cardinality of
J_{\mathrm{bad}}^{1,+}
and by
\tilde{N}_{1}^{-}
the cardinality of
J_{\mathrm{bad}}^{1,-}
, and split the partition function according to the values of
\tilde{N}_{1}^{+}
and
\tilde{N}_{1}^{-}
: by (
5.111
),

(5.115)

\mathsf{Z}_{\mathrm{bad},N_{1}}^{(1)}=\frac{1}{N_{1}!}\sum_{\tilde{N}_{1}^{+}\leq N_{1},\tilde{N}_{1}^{-}\leq N_{1}}\binom{N_{1}}{\tilde{N}_{1}^{+}}\binom{N_{1}}{\tilde{N}_{1}^{-}}I(\tilde{N}_{1}^{+},\tilde{N}_{1}^{-}),

where

I(\tilde{N}_{1}^{+},\tilde{N}_{1}^{-})\coloneqq\int_{(\Lambda^{2})^{N_{1}}}\mathbf{1}_{J_{\mathrm{bad}}^{1,+}=\tilde{V}_{\mathrm{bad}}^{1,+}}\mathbf{1}_{J_{\mathrm{bad}}^{1,-}=\tilde{V}_{\mathrm{bad}}^{1,-}}\mathbf{1}_{I_{\mathrm{bad}}^{1}=V_{\mathrm{bad}}^{1}}\prod_{i\in V_{\mathrm{bad}}^{1}}\frac{1}{(\tau_{i}^{+,Z})^{\frac{\beta}{2}}}\prod_{i\in V_{\mathrm{bad}}^{1}}\frac{1}{(\tau_{i}^{-,Z})^{\frac{\beta}{2}}}\prod_{i\in V_{\mathrm{bad}}^{1}}\mathrm{d}x_{i}\mathrm{d}y_{i},

where
\tilde{V}_{\mathrm{bad}}^{1,+}\subset V_{\mathrm{bad}}^{1}
and
\tilde{V}_{\mathrm{bad}}^{1,-}\subset V_{\mathrm{bad}}^{1}
are arbitrary disjoint sets such that
|\tilde{V}_{\mathrm{bad}}^{1,+}|=\tilde{N}_{1}^{+}
and
|\tilde{V}_{\mathrm{bad}}^{1,-}|=\tilde{N}_{1}^{-}
.

Step 3: assigning a partner to good charges

We now restrict to the event

I_{\mathrm{bad}}^{1}=V_{\mathrm{bad}}^{1},J_{\mathrm{bad}}^{1,+}=\tilde{V}_{\mathrm{bad}}^{1,+},J_{\mathrm{bad}}^{1,-}=\tilde{V}_{\mathrm{bad}}^{1,-}
.
Recall that by (
5.113
), if
i\in V_{\mathrm{bad}}^{1}\setminus\tilde{V}_{\mathrm{bad}}^{1,+}
, then
\sigma_{N_{1}}(i)\in\tilde{V}_{\mathrm{bad}}^{1,-}
. Moreover, there are clearly fewer than
N_{1}
choices for the value of
\sigma_{N_{1}}(i)
. Similarly, if
i\in V_{\mathrm{bad}}^{1}\setminus\tilde{V}_{\mathrm{bad}}^{1,-}
, then
\sigma^{-1}_{N_{1}}(i)\in\tilde{V}_{\mathrm{bad}}^{1,+}
, with fewer than
N_{1}
choices for
\sigma^{-1}_{N_{1}}(i)
. Thus,

(5.116)

I(\tilde{N}_{1}^{+},\tilde{N}_{1}^{-})\leq N_{1}^{N_{1}-\tilde{N}_{1}^{+}}N_{1}^{N_{1}-\tilde{N}_{1}^{-}}\max_{f^{+},f^{-}}I(\tilde{N}_{1}^{+},\tilde{N}_{1}^{-},f^{+},f^{-}),

where the maximum is over injective maps

f^{+}:V_{\mathrm{bad}}^{1}\setminus\tilde{V}_{\mathrm{bad}}^{1,-}\to V_{\mathrm{bad}}^{1,+}
and
f^{-}:V_{\mathrm{bad}}^{1}\setminus\tilde{V}_{\mathrm{bad}}^{1,+}\to V_{\mathrm{bad}}^{1,-}
, and where

I(\tilde{N}_{1}^{+},\tilde{N}_{1}^{-},f^{+},f^{-})\coloneqq\int_{(\Lambda^{2})^{N_{1}}}\mathbf{1}_{J_{\mathrm{bad}}^{1,+}=\tilde{V}_{\mathrm{bad}}^{1,+}}\mathbf{1}_{J_{\mathrm{bad}}^{1,-}=\tilde{V}_{\mathrm{bad}}^{1,-}}\mathbf{1}_{I_{\mathrm{bad}}^{1}=V_{\mathrm{bad}}^{1}}\prod_{i\in V_{\mathrm{bad}}^{1}\setminus\tilde{V}_{\mathrm{bad}}^{1,+}}\mathbf{1}_{\sigma_{N_{1}}(i)=f^{-}(i)}\\
\times\prod_{i\in V_{\mathrm{bad}}^{1}\setminus\tilde{V}_{\mathrm{bad}}^{1,-}}\mathbf{1}_{\sigma_{N_{1}}^{-1}(i)=f^{+}(i)}\prod_{i\in V_{\mathrm{bad}}^{1}}\frac{1}{(\tau_{i}^{+,Z})^{\frac{\beta}{2}}}\prod_{i\in V_{\mathrm{bad}}^{1}}\frac{1}{(\tau_{i}^{-,Z})^{\frac{\beta}{2}}}\prod_{i\in V_{\mathrm{bad}}^{1}}\mathrm{d}x_{i}\mathrm{d}y_{i},

Fix two injective maps
f^{+}:V_{\mathrm{bad}}^{1}\setminus\tilde{V}_{\mathrm{bad}}^{1,-}\to V_{\mathrm{bad}}^{1,+}
and
f^{-}:V_{\mathrm{bad}}^{1}\setminus\tilde{V}_{\mathrm{bad}}^{1,+}\to V_{\mathrm{bad}}^{1,-}
.

Step 4: splitting according to the partition of
\tilde{V}_{\mathrm{bad}}^{1,+}
and
\tilde{V}_{\mathrm{bad}}^{1,-}

Let us denote

\widetilde{\mathrm{Clus}}^{+}\coloneqq\{S\in\mathrm{Clus}_{N_{1}}^{+}:S\subset\tilde{V}_{\mathrm{bad}}^{1,+}\}\quad\mathrm{and}\quad\widetilde{\mathrm{Clus}}^{-}\coloneqq\{S\in\mathrm{Clus}_{N_{1}}^{+}:S\subset\tilde{V}_{\mathrm{bad}}^{1,-}\}.

We will denote by
k_{0}^{+}
the cardinality of
\widetilde{\mathrm{Clus}}^{+}
and by
k_{0}^{-}
the cardinality of
\widetilde{\mathrm{Clus}}^{-}
.

For every
V\subset[N]
and
k_{0}\geq 1
, let
\mathbf{\Pi}^{k_{0}}(V)
be the set of partitions
\pi
of
V
with
k_{0}
components such that for every
S\in\pi
,
|S|=q(\beta)+1
or
|S|=1
. We have

(5.117)

I(\tilde{N}_{1}^{+},\tilde{N}_{1}^{-},f^{+},f^{-})=\sum_{k_{0}^{+},k_{0}^{-}}\sum_{\pi^{+}\in\mathbf{\Pi}^{k_{0}^{+}}(\tilde{V}_{\mathrm{bad}}^{1,+})}\sum_{\pi^{-}\in\mathbf{\Pi}^{k_{0}^{-}}(\tilde{V}_{\mathrm{bad}}^{1,-})}I(\tilde{N}_{1}^{+},\tilde{N}_{1}^{-},f^{+},f^{-},\pi^{+},\pi^{-}),

where

I(\tilde{N}_{1}^{+},\tilde{N}_{1}^{-},f^{+},f^{-},\pi^{+},\pi^{-})\coloneqq\int_{(\Lambda^{2})^{N_{1}}}\mathbf{1}_{J_{\mathrm{bad}}^{1,+}=\tilde{V}_{\mathrm{bad}}^{1,+}}\mathbf{1}_{J_{\mathrm{bad}}^{1,-}=\tilde{V}_{\mathrm{bad}}^{1,-}}\mathbf{1}_{I_{\mathrm{bad}}^{1}=V_{\mathrm{bad}}^{1}}\mathbf{1}_{\widetilde{\mathrm{Clus}}^{+}=\pi^{+}}\mathbf{1}_{\widetilde{\mathrm{Clus}}^{-}=\pi^{-}}\\
\times\prod_{i\in V_{\mathrm{bad}}^{1}\setminus\tilde{V}_{\mathrm{bad}}^{1,+}}\mathbf{1}_{\sigma_{N_{1}}(i)=f^{-}(i)}\prod_{i\in V_{\mathrm{bad}}^{1}\setminus\tilde{V}_{\mathrm{bad}}^{1,-}}\mathbf{1}_{\sigma_{N_{1}}^{-1}(i)=f^{+}(i)}\prod_{i\in V_{\mathrm{bad}}^{1}}\frac{1}{(\tau_{i}^{+,Z})^{\frac{\beta}{2}}}\prod_{i\in V_{\mathrm{bad}}^{1}}\frac{1}{(\tau_{i}^{-,Z})^{\frac{\beta}{2}}}\prod_{i\in V_{\mathrm{bad}}^{1}}\mathrm{d}x_{i}\mathrm{d}y_{i},

Step 5: integration

Denote
D
as the set of dyadic scales from
\lambda
to
\varepsilon_{0}\bar{R}_{\beta,\lambda}:

D=\Bigr\{\lambda 2^{k}:0\leq k\leq 1+\Bigr\lfloor\log_{2}\Bigr(\frac{\varepsilon_{0}\bar{R}_{\beta,\lambda}}{\lambda}\Bigr)\Bigr\rfloor\Bigr\}.

For every
S\in\widetilde{\mathrm{Clus}}^{+}
, fix some
\iota_{S}^{+}\in S

and for every
S\in\widetilde{\mathrm{Clus}}^{-}
, fix some
\iota_{S}^{-}\in S
. For every sequence of scales
(d_{S}^{+})_{S\in\widetilde{\mathrm{Clus}}^{+}}
and
(d_{S}^{-})_{S\in\widetilde{\mathrm{Clus}}^{-}}
taking values in
D
, set

\displaystyle J(\tilde{N}_{1}^{+},\tilde{N}_{1}^{-},f^{+},f^{-},\pi^{+},\pi^{-},(d_{S}^{+}),(d_{S}^{-}))

\displaystyle\coloneqq\int_{(\Lambda^{2})^{N_{1}}}\prod_{S\in\widetilde{\mathrm{Clus}}^{+}:|S|=q(\beta)+1}\left(\prod_{i\in S}\mathbf{1}_{|x_{i}-x_{\iota_{S}}|\leq 4d_{S}^{+}}\frac{1}{(d_{S}^{+})^{\frac{\beta}{2}|S|}}\prod_{i\in\mathrm{GoodP}^{-}(S)}\frac{\mathbf{1}_{|x_{i}-y_{f^{-}(i)}|\leq\varepsilon_{0}\bar{R}_{\beta,\lambda}}}{\max(|x_{i}-y_{f^{-}(i)}|,\lambda)^{\frac{\beta}{2}}}\right)

\displaystyle\times\prod_{S\in\widetilde{\mathrm{Clus}}^{-}:|S|=q(\beta)+1}\left(\prod_{i\in S}\mathbf{1}_{|y_{i}-y_{\iota_{S}}|\leq 4d_{S}^{-}}\frac{1}{(d_{S}^{-})^{\frac{\beta}{2}|S|}}\prod_{i\in\mathrm{GoodP}^{+}(S)}\frac{\mathbf{1}_{|y_{i}-x_{f^{+}(i)}|\leq\varepsilon_{0}\bar{R}_{\beta,\lambda}{\varepsilon_{0},q(\beta)}}}{\max(|y_{i}-x_{f^{+}(i)}|,\lambda)^{\frac{\beta}{2}}}\right)

\displaystyle\times\prod_{S\in\widetilde{\mathrm{Clus}}^{+}:|S|=1}(\varepsilon_{0}\bar{R}_{\beta,\lambda})^{-\frac{\beta}{2}}\prod_{S\in\widetilde{\mathrm{Clus}}^{-}:|S|=1}(\varepsilon_{0}\bar{R}_{\beta,\lambda})^{-\frac{\beta}{2}}\prod_{i\in V_{\mathrm{bad}}^{1}}\mathrm{d}x_{i}\mathrm{d}y_{i}.

Observe that for every
S\in\widetilde{\mathrm{Clus}}^{+}
,

(5.118)

\mathbf{1}_{\tau_{\iota_{S}}^{+,Z}\in[d_{S}^{+},2d_{S}^{+})}\leq\prod_{i\in S}\mathbf{1}_{|x_{i}-x_{\iota_{S}}|\leq 4d_{S}^{+}}.

Moreover, by the construction of the clustering algorithm, the radii
\tau_{i}^{+,Z}
are equal within each cluster, and the same holds for the
\tau_{i}^{-,Z}
. Thus, splitting the phase space according to the dyadic scale of
\tau_{\iota_{S}^{+}}^{+,Z}
and
\tau_{\iota_{S}^{-}}^{-,Z}
in each cluster and using (
5.118
), we get

I(\tilde{N}_{1}^{+},\tilde{N}_{1}^{-},f^{+},f^{-},\pi^{+},\pi^{-})\leq\prod_{S\in\widetilde{\mathrm{Clus}}^{+}}\sum_{d_{S}^{+}\in D}\prod_{S\in\widetilde{\mathrm{Clus}}^{-}}\sum_{d_{S}^{-}\in D}J(\tilde{N}_{1}^{+},\tilde{N}_{1}^{-},f^{+},f^{-},\pi^{+},\pi^{-},(d_{S}^{+}),(d_{S}^{-})).

Fix
(d_{S}^{+})_{S\in\widetilde{\mathrm{Clus}}^{+}}
and
(d_{S}^{-})_{S\in\widetilde{\mathrm{Clus}}^{-}}
.

\bullet
Suppose that
\beta\in(2,4)
. Integrating for each cluster over all indices except one (which is either
x_{\iota_{S}}
or
y_{\iota_{S}}
) and then integrating out that last variable over the whole domain
\Lambda
, one obtains that there exists
C>0
depending on
\beta
and
q(\beta)
such that

\displaystyle J(\tilde{N}_{1}^{+},\tilde{N}_{1}^{-},f^{+},f^{-},\pi^{+},\pi^{-},(d_{S}^{+}),(d_{S}^{-}))

\displaystyle\leq(CN)^{k_{0}^{+}+k_{0}^{-}}\prod_{\begin{subarray}{c}S\in\widetilde{\mathrm{Clus}}^{+}:\\
|S|=q(\beta)+1\end{subarray}}(d_{S}^{+})^{(2-\frac{\beta}{2})|S|-2}(\varepsilon_{0}R_{\beta,\lambda})^{(2-\frac{\beta}{2})|\mathrm{GoodP}^{-}(S)|}

\displaystyle\times\prod_{\begin{subarray}{c}S\in\widetilde{\mathrm{Clus}}^{-}:\\
|S|=q(\beta)+1\end{subarray}}(d_{S}^{-})^{(2-\frac{\beta}{2})|S|-2}(\varepsilon_{0}R_{\beta,\lambda})^{(2-\frac{\beta}{2})|\mathrm{GoodP}^{+}(S)|}\prod_{\begin{subarray}{c}S\in\widetilde{\mathrm{Clus}}^{+}:\\
|S|=1\end{subarray}}(\varepsilon_{0}R_{\beta,\lambda})^{-\frac{\beta}{2}}\prod_{\begin{subarray}{c}S\in\widetilde{\mathrm{Clus}}^{-}:\\
|S|=1\end{subarray}}(\varepsilon_{0}R_{\beta,\lambda})^{-\frac{\beta}{2}}.

By definition (
5.1
), we have
(2-\frac{\beta}{2})(q(\beta)+1)-2=1>0
, and therefore, summing over
d_{S}^{+}
and
d_{S}^{-}
in
D
gives

I(\tilde{N}_{1}^{+},\tilde{N}_{1}^{-},f^{+},f^{-},\pi^{+},\pi^{-})\leq(CN)^{k_{0}^{+}+k_{0}^{-}}\prod_{\begin{subarray}{c}S\in\widetilde{\mathrm{Clus}}^{+}:\\
|S|=q(\beta)+1\end{subarray}}(\varepsilon_{0}R_{\beta,\lambda})^{(2-\frac{\beta}{2})|S|-2}(\varepsilon_{0}R_{\beta,\lambda})^{(2-\frac{\beta}{2})|\mathrm{GoodP}^{-}(S)|}\\
\times\prod_{\begin{subarray}{c}S\in\widetilde{\mathrm{Clus}}^{-}:\\
|S|=q(\beta)+1\end{subarray}}(\varepsilon_{0}R_{\beta,\lambda})^{(2-\frac{\beta}{2})|S|-2}(\varepsilon_{0}R_{\beta,\lambda})^{(2-\frac{\beta}{2})|\mathrm{GoodP}^{+}(S)|}\prod_{\begin{subarray}{c}S\in\widetilde{\mathrm{Clus}}^{+}:\\
|S|=1\end{subarray}}(\varepsilon_{0}R_{\beta,\lambda})^{(2-\frac{\beta}{2})-2}\prod_{\begin{subarray}{c}S\in\widetilde{\mathrm{Clus}}^{-}:\\
|S|=1\end{subarray}}(\varepsilon_{0}R_{\beta,\lambda})^{(2-\frac{\beta}{2})-2}.

Using (
5.114
) to count the number of points, one can check that

I(\tilde{N}_{1}^{+},\tilde{N}_{1}^{-},f^{+},f^{-},\pi^{+},\pi^{-})\leq(CN)^{k_{0}^{+}+k_{0}^{-}}(\varepsilon_{0}R_{\beta,\lambda})^{(4-\beta)N_{1}}(\varepsilon_{0}R_{\beta,\lambda})^{-2(k_{0}^{+}+k_{0}^{-})}.

Recalling that
R_{\lambda}^{4-\beta}=\lambda^{2-\beta}
, we find that there exists
C>0
depending on
\beta,q(\beta)
and
\varepsilon_{0}
such that

(5.119)

I(\tilde{N}_{1}^{+},\tilde{N}_{1}^{-},f^{+},f^{-},\pi^{+},\pi^{-})\leq(CN)^{k_{0}^{+}+k_{0}^{-}}\lambda^{(2-\beta)N_{1}}R_{\beta,\lambda}^{-2(k_{0}^{+}+k_{0}^{-})}.

\bullet
Suppose that
\beta\geq 4
. We have

I(\tilde{N}_{1}^{+},\tilde{N}_{1}^{-},f^{+},f^{-},\pi^{+},\pi^{-})\leq(CN)^{k_{0}^{+}+k_{0}^{-}}\prod_{\begin{subarray}{c}S\in\widetilde{\mathrm{Clus}}^{+}:\\
|S|=q(\beta)+1\end{subarray}}\lambda^{(2-\frac{\beta}{2})|S|-2}\lambda^{(2-\frac{\beta}{2})|\mathrm{GoodP}^{-}(S)|}\\
\times\prod_{\begin{subarray}{c}S\in\widetilde{\mathrm{Clus}}^{-}:\\
|S|=q(\beta)+1\end{subarray}}\lambda^{(2-\frac{\beta}{2})|S|-2}\lambda^{(2-\frac{\beta}{2})|\mathrm{GoodP}^{+}(S)|}\prod_{\begin{subarray}{c}S\in\widetilde{\mathrm{Clus}}^{+}:\\
|S|=1\end{subarray}}(\varepsilon_{0}\bar{R}_{\beta,\lambda})^{-\frac{\beta}{2}}\prod_{\begin{subarray}{c}S\in\widetilde{\mathrm{Clus}}^{-}:\\
|S|=1\end{subarray}}(\varepsilon_{0}\bar{R}_{\beta,\lambda})^{-\frac{\beta}{2}}.

Notice that by definition (
2.17
)

(\varepsilon_{0}\bar{R}_{\beta,\lambda})^{-\frac{\beta}{2}}\leq\lambda^{2-\beta}\varepsilon_{0}^{-\frac{\beta}{2}}\lambda^{\frac{\beta}{2}p_{0}-2+\beta},

and also, since
\beta\geq 4
and
p_{0}\geq 1
, we have
\frac{\beta}{2}p_{0}-2+\beta\geq 2p_{0}
. Thus, since
\lambda\leq 1
,

(\varepsilon_{0}\bar{R}_{\beta,\lambda})^{-\frac{\beta}{2}}\leq\lambda^{2-\beta}\varepsilon_{0}^{-\frac{\beta}{2}}\lambda^{2p_{0}}.

It follows that there exists
C>0
depending on
\beta
,
p_{0}
, and
\varepsilon_{0}
such that

(5.120)

I(\tilde{N}_{1}^{+},\tilde{N}_{1}^{-},f^{+},f^{-},\pi^{+},\pi^{-})\leq(CN)^{k_{0}^{+}+k_{0}^{-}}\lambda^{(2-\beta)N_{1}}\lambda^{2p_{0}(k_{0}^{+}+k_{0}^{-})}.

Assembling (
5.119
) and (
5.120
) gives the existence of
C>0
depending on
\beta
,
q(\beta)
, and
\varepsilon_{0}
such that

(5.121)

I(\tilde{N}_{1}^{+},\tilde{N}_{1}^{-},f^{+},f^{-},\pi^{+},\pi^{-})\leq(CN)^{k_{0}^{+}+k_{0}^{-}}\lambda^{(2-\beta)N_{1}}\delta_{\beta,\lambda}^{k_{0}^{+}+k_{0}^{-}},

where
\delta_{\beta,\lambda}
is as in (
2.34
).

Step 6: conclusion

Observe that there exists a constant
C>0
depending on
q(\beta)
such that

|\mathbf{\Pi}^{k_{0}^{+}}(\tilde{V}_{\mathrm{bad}}^{1,+})|\leq C^{k_{0}^{+}}\frac{(\tilde{N}_{1}^{+})^{\tilde{N}_{1}^{+}}}{k_{0}^{+}!}\quad\mathrm{and}\,\quad|\mathbf{\Pi}^{k_{0}^{-}}(\tilde{V}_{\mathrm{bad}}^{1,-})|\leq C^{k_{0}^{+}}\frac{(\tilde{N}_{1}^{-})^{\tilde{N}_{1}^{-}}}{k_{0}^{-}!}.

Therefore, combining the above two displays with (
5.115
), (
5.116
), (
5.117
) and (
5.121
), we obtain

\mathsf{Z}_{\mathrm{bad},N_{1}}^{1}\leq\frac{1}{N_{1}!}\sum_{\tilde{N}_{1}^{+}\leq N_{1},\tilde{N}_{1}^{-}\leq N_{1}}\binom{N_{1}}{\tilde{N}_{1}^{+}}\binom{N_{1}}{\tilde{N}_{1}^{-}}N_{1}^{N_{1}-\tilde{N}_{1}^{+}}N_{1}^{N_{1}-\tilde{N}_{1}^{-}}N^{k_{0}^{+}+k_{0}^{-}}\lambda^{(2-\beta)N_{1}}\\
\times\delta_{\beta,\lambda}^{k_{0}^{+}+k_{0}^{-}}\frac{(\tilde{N}_{1}^{+})^{\tilde{N}_{1}^{+}}}{k_{0}^{+}!}\frac{(\tilde{N}_{1}^{-})^{\tilde{N}_{1}^{-}}}{k_{0}^{-}!}.

By (
4.122
),

\binom{N_{1}}{\tilde{N}_{1}^{+}}\binom{N_{1}}{\tilde{N}_{1}^{-}}(\tilde{N}_{1}^{+})^{\tilde{N}_{1}^{+}}(\tilde{N}_{1}^{-})^{\tilde{N}_{1}^{-}}\leq\Bigr(\frac{CN_{1}}{\tilde{N}_{1}^{+}}\Bigr)^{\tilde{N}_{1}^{+}}\Bigr(\frac{CN_{1}}{\tilde{N}_{1}^{-}}\Bigr)^{\tilde{N}_{1}^{-}}(\tilde{N}_{1}^{+})^{\tilde{N}_{1}^{+}}(\tilde{N}_{1}^{-})^{\tilde{N}_{1}^{-}}\leq(CN_{1})^{\tilde{N}_{1}^{+}+\tilde{N}_{1}^{-}}.

Hence,

\binom{N_{1}}{\tilde{N}_{1}^{+}}\binom{N_{1}}{\tilde{N}_{1}^{-}}(\tilde{N}_{1}^{+})^{\tilde{N}_{1}^{+}}(\tilde{N}_{1}^{-})^{\tilde{N}_{1}^{-}}N_{1}^{N_{1}-\tilde{N}_{1}^{+}}N_{1}^{N_{1}-\tilde{N}_{1}^{-}}\leq(CN_{1})^{2N_{1}}.

It follows that

\mathsf{Z}_{\mathrm{bad},N_{1}}^{(1)}\leq\frac{1}{N_{1}!}\sum_{\tilde{N}_{1}^{+}\leq N_{1},\tilde{N}_{1}^{-}\leq N_{1}}(CN_{1})^{2N_{1}}\lambda^{(2-\beta)N_{1}}\delta_{\beta,\lambda}^{k_{0}^{+}+k_{0}^{-}}\frac{N^{k_{0}^{+}}}{k_{0}^{+}!}\frac{N^{k_{0}^{-}}}{k_{0}^{-}!}.

By Stirling’s formula again,

\mathsf{Z}_{\mathrm{bad},N_{1}}^{(1)}\leq\sum_{\tilde{N}_{1}^{+}\leq N_{1},\tilde{N}_{1}^{-}\leq N_{1}}(CN_{1})^{N_{1}}\frac{N^{k_{0}^{+}}}{k_{0}^{+}!}\frac{N^{k_{0}^{-}}}{k_{0}^{-}!}\lambda^{(2-\beta)N_{1}}\delta_{\beta,\lambda}^{k_{0}^{+}+k_{0}^{-}}.

Then, using
C_{\beta,\lambda,\varepsilon_{0}}\geq\frac{1}{C}\lambda^{2-\beta}
from (
4.10
), we get that there exists
C>0
depending on
\beta
,
q(\beta)
, and
\varepsilon_{0}
such that for all
t\geq 1
,

\Bigr(\frac{N}{N_{1}}\Bigr)^{N_{1}}\frac{\mathsf{Z}_{\mathrm{bad},N_{1}}^{(1)}}{(NC_{\beta,\lambda,\varepsilon_{0}})^{N_{1}}}e^{tN_{1}}\leq\sum_{k_{0}^{+},k_{0}^{-}}e^{Ct(k_{0}^{+}+k_{0}^{-})}\delta_{\beta,\lambda}^{k_{0}^{+}+k_{0}^{-}}\frac{N^{k_{0}^{+}}}{k_{0}^{+}!}\frac{N^{k_{0}^{-}}}{k_{0}^{-}!}=e^{2e^{Ct}N\delta_{\beta,\lambda}},

proving the lemma.
∎

5.14.
Control of bad points of type 2

Recalling Definition
5.3
, we write

(5.122)

\mathsf{Z}_{\mathrm{bad},N_{2}}^{(2)}\leq\frac{N_{2}!}{N_{2}^{\prime}!N_{1}^{\prime\prime}!\cdots N_{p(\beta)}^{\prime\prime}!}\max_{N_{2}^{\prime},(N_{k}^{\prime\prime})_{k\in[p(\beta)]}}\mathsf{Z}_{\mathrm{bad},N_{2^{\prime}}}^{(2,1)}\prod_{k=1}^{p(\beta)}\mathsf{Z}_{\mathrm{bad},N_{k}^{\prime\prime}}^{(2,2,k)},

where

(5.123)

\mathsf{Z}_{\mathrm{bad},N_{2}^{\prime}}^{(2,1)}\coloneqq\int_{(\Lambda^{2})^{N_{2}^{\prime}}}\mathbf{1}_{I_{\mathrm{bad}}^{2,1}=V_{\mathrm{bad}}^{2,1}}\prod_{i,j\in V_{\mathrm{bad}}^{2,1}:i<j}\mathbf{1}_{\mathcal{A}_{ij}}\prod_{i\in V_{\mathrm{bad}}^{2,1}}e^{\beta\mathsf{g}_{\lambda}(x_{i}-y_{i})}\mathrm{d}x_{i}\mathrm{d}y_{i}

and for every
k\in[p(\beta)]
,

(5.124)

\mathsf{Z}_{\mathrm{bad},N_{k}^{\prime\prime}}^{(2,2,k)}\coloneqq\int_{(\Lambda^{2})^{N_{k}^{\prime\prime}}}\mathbf{1}_{I_{\mathrm{bad}}^{2,2,k}=V_{\mathrm{bad}}^{2,2,k}}\prod_{i,j\in V_{\mathrm{bad}}^{2,2,k}:i<j}\mathbf{1}_{\mathcal{A}_{ij}}\prod_{i\in V_{\mathrm{bad}}^{2,2,k}}e^{\beta\mathsf{g}_{\lambda}(x_{i}-y_{i})}\mathrm{d}x_{i}\mathrm{d}y_{i},

for some arbitrary disjoint sets
V_{\mathrm{bad}}^{2,1},V_{\mathrm{bad}}^{2,2,1},\ldots,V_{\mathrm{bad}}^{2,2,p(\beta)}
of cardinality
N_{2}^{\prime}
,
N_{1}^{\prime\prime},\ldots,N_{p(\beta)}^{\prime\prime}
.

We begin by bad points of type
(2,1)
, which are those in multipoles of cardinality larger than
p(\beta)
.

Lemma 5.40
.

Let
\beta\in(2,\infty)
and let
p(\beta)
be as in Definition
2.7
. Let
\mathsf{Z}_{\mathrm{bad},N_{2}}^{(2,1)}
be as in (
5.123
). Let
t\geq 1
.

(1)

Suppose that
\beta\in(2,4)
. There exists
\tilde{\varepsilon}_{0}
depending on
\beta,M
and
t
such that for all
\varepsilon_{0}\in(0,\tilde{\varepsilon}_{0})
, there exists a constant
C>0
depending on
\beta,M
and
\varepsilon_{0}
such that

\max_{N_{2}}\left(\Bigr(\frac{N}{N_{2}}\Bigr)^{N_{2}}\frac{\mathsf{Z}_{\mathrm{bad},N_{2}}^{(2,1)}}{(NC_{\beta,\lambda,\varepsilon_{0}})^{N_{2}}}e^{tN_{2}}\right)\leq e^{Ce^{t}NR_{\beta,\lambda}^{-2}}.

(2)

Suppose that
\beta\geq 4
. There exists
\tilde{\lambda}
depending on
\beta,M,p_{0}
and
t
such that for all
\lambda\in(0,\tilde{\lambda})
, there exists
C>0
depending on
\beta,M
and
p_{0}
such that

\max_{N_{2}}\left(\Bigr(\frac{N}{N_{2}}\Bigr)^{N_{2}}\frac{\mathsf{Z}_{\mathrm{bad},N_{2}}^{(2,1)}}{(NC_{\beta,\lambda,\varepsilon_{0}})^{N_{2}}}e^{tN_{2}}\right)\leq e^{Ce^{t}N\lambda^{2p_{0}}}.

Proof.

Let
\mathbf{\Pi}^{\prime}(V_{\mathrm{bad}}^{2,1})
be the set of partitions
\pi
of
V_{\mathrm{bad}}^{2,1}
such that for every
S\in\pi
, we have
|S|>p(\beta)
. One can write

(5.125)

\mathsf{Z}_{\mathrm{bad},N_{2}}^{(2,1)}=\sum_{\pi\in\mathbf{\Pi}^{\prime}(V_{\mathrm{bad}}^{2,1})}\mathcal{J}(\pi),

where

\mathcal{J}(\pi)\coloneqq\int_{(\Lambda^{2})^{N_{2}}}\mathbf{1}_{\{I_{\mathrm{bad}}^{2,1}=V_{\mathrm{bad}}^{2,1}\}}\mathbf{1}_{\Pi_{\mathrm{mult}}=\pi}\prod_{i,j\in V_{\mathrm{bad}}^{2,1}:i<j}\mathbf{1}_{\mathcal{A}_{ij}}\prod_{i\in V_{\mathrm{bad}}^{2,1}}e^{\beta\mathsf{g}_{\lambda}(x_{i}-y_{i})}\mathrm{d}x_{i}\mathrm{d}y_{i}.

Fix
\pi\in\mathbf{\Pi}^{\prime}(V_{\mathrm{bad}}^{2,1})
. Recall that
\mathcal{T}_{c}(S)
stands for the set of collections of edges
E
on
S
such that
(S,E)
is a tree. Also, by a union bound,

\mathbf{1}_{\mathcal{B}_{S}}\leq\sum_{T_{S}\in\mathcal{T}_{c}(S)}\prod_{ij\in T_{S}}\mathbf{1}_{\mathcal{B}_{ij}}\leq\left(\max_{T_{S}\in\mathcal{T}_{c}(S)}\prod_{ij\in T_{S}}\mathbf{1}_{\mathcal{B}_{ij}}\right)|S|^{|S|-2},

where we have used that by Cayley’s formula, the number of trees on
S
equals
|S|^{|S|-2}
.

For every
S\in\pi
, choosing
\iota_{S}\in S
as the index of the largest
r_{i}
for
i\in S
, giving
|S|
choices, one has

(5.126)

\mathcal{J}(\pi)\leq e^{CN_{2}}\prod_{S\in\pi}|S|^{|S|-2}|S|\max_{(T_{S}),(\iota_{S})}\mathcal{J}^{\prime}(\pi,(T_{S}),(\iota_{S})),

where

\mathcal{J}^{\prime}(\pi,(T_{S}),(\iota_{S}))=\int_{(\Lambda^{2})^{N_{2}}}\prod_{S\in\pi}\left(\prod_{ij\in T_{S}}\mathbf{1}_{\mathcal{B}_{ij}}\prod_{i\in S}\mathbf{1}_{|x_{i}-y_{i}|\leq r_{\iota_{S}}\leq\varepsilon_{0}\bar{R}_{\beta,\lambda}}e^{\beta\mathsf{g}_{\lambda}(x_{i}-y_{i})}\right)\prod_{i\in V_{\mathrm{bad}}}\mathrm{d}x_{i}\mathrm{d}y_{i}.

For every
S\in\pi
, fix a tree
T_{S}
on
S
and an index
\iota_{S}
in
S
.
Recall that
\mathcal{B}_{ij}=\{d_{ij}\leq M\min(\max(r_{i},\lambda),\max(r_{j},\lambda))\}
and
d_{ij}=\mathrm{dist}(\{x_{i},y_{i}\},\{x_{j},y_{j}\})
. For every
ij\in\cup_{S}T_{S}
, we split the phase space according to the variables achieving
d_{ij}
. We let

\begin{split}\mathcal{D}_{ij}^{1}&=\{(x_{q},y_{q})_{q\in S}:d_{ij}=|x_{i}-x_{j}|\},\\
\mathcal{D}_{ij}^{2}&=\{(x_{q},y_{q})_{q\in S}:d_{ij}=|x_{i}-y_{j}|\},\\
\mathcal{D}_{ij}^{3}&=\{(x_{q},y_{q})_{q\in S}:d_{ij}=|y_{i}-x_{j}|\},\\
\mathcal{D}_{ij}^{4}&=\{(x_{q},y_{q})_{q\in S}:d_{ij}=|y_{i}-y_{j}|\}.\end{split}

Define

\mathcal{J}^{\prime\prime}(\pi,(T_{S}^{l})_{l\in[4],S\in X},(\iota_{S}))\\
\coloneqq\int_{(\Lambda^{2})^{N_{2}}}\prod_{S\in\pi}\left(\prod_{l=1}^{4}\prod_{ij\in T_{S}^{l}}\mathbf{1}_{\mathcal{B}_{ij}}\mathbf{1}_{\mathcal{D}_{ij}^{l}}\prod_{i\in S}\mathbf{1}_{|x_{i}-y_{i}|\leq r_{\iota_{S}}\leq\varepsilon_{0}\bar{R}_{\beta,\lambda}}e^{\beta\mathsf{g}_{\lambda}(x_{i}-y_{i})}\right)\prod_{i\in V_{\mathrm{bad}}}\mathrm{d}x_{i}\mathrm{d}y_{i}.

Notice that

(5.127)

\mathcal{J}^{\prime}(\pi,(T_{S}),(\iota_{S}))=\sum_{(T_{S}^{1}),(T_{S}^{2}),(T_{S}^{3}),(T_{S}^{4})}\mathcal{J}^{\prime\prime}(\pi,(T_{S}^{l})_{l\in[4],S\in X},(\iota_{S})),

where the sum is over sequences of trees such that for every
S\in\pi
,
T_{S}^{1},T_{S}^{2},T_{S}^{3},T_{S}^{4}
are disjoint and
T_{S}^{1}\cup T_{S}^{2}\cup T_{S}^{3}\cup T_{S}^{4}=T_{S}
. Let us fix
T_{S}^{1},T_{S}^{2},T_{S}^{3},T_{S}^{4}
accordingly. Define the variables

z_{ij}=\begin{cases}x_{i}-x_{j}&\text{if $ij\in T_{S}^{1}$}\\
x_{i}-y_{j}&\text{if $ij\in T_{S}^{2}$}\\
y_{i}-x_{j}&\text{if $ij\in T_{S}^{3}$}\\
y_{i}-y_{j}&\text{if $ij\in T_{S}^{4}$}.\end{cases}

Then, perform the change of variables

(x_{i},y_{i})_{i\in V_{\mathrm{bad}}^{2,1}}\mapsto((x_{\iota_{S}})_{S\in X},(z_{ij})_{\in\cup_{S\in\pi}T_{S}},(\vec{r}_{i})_{i\in V_{\mathrm{bad}}^{2,1}}).

Integrating out the variables
(x_{\iota_{S}})_{S\in X}
yields

\mathcal{J}^{\prime\prime}(\pi,(T_{S}^{l})_{l\in[4],S\in X},(\iota_{S}))\leq N^{|\pi|}\prod_{S\in\pi}\int_{(\Lambda^{2})^{N_{2}}}\prod_{ij\in T_{S}}\mathbf{1}_{z_{ij}\leq M\min(\max(r_{i},\lambda),\max(r_{j},\lambda))}\\
\times\left(\prod_{i\in S}\mathbf{1}_{r_{i}\leq r_{\iota_{S}}\leq\varepsilon_{0}\bar{R}_{\beta,\lambda}}e^{\beta\mathsf{g}_{\lambda}(r_{i})}\right)\prod_{ij\in T_{S}}\mathrm{d}z_{ij}\prod_{i\in S}\mathrm{d}\vec{r}_{i}.

Integrating out the variables
z_{ij}^{\prime}
yields

\mathcal{J}^{\prime\prime}(\pi,(T_{S}^{l})_{l\in[4],S\in X},(\iota_{S}))\leq(CM^{2})^{N_{2}}N^{|\pi|}\prod_{S\in\pi}\int_{(\Lambda^{2})^{N_{2}}}\prod_{ij\in T_{S}}\min(\max(r_{i},\lambda),\max(r_{j},\lambda))^{2}\\
\times\left(\prod_{i\in S}\mathbf{1}_{r_{i}\leq r_{\iota_{S}}\leq\varepsilon_{0}\bar{R}_{\beta,\lambda}}e^{\beta\mathsf{g}_{\lambda}(r_{i})}\right)\prod_{i\in S}\mathrm{d}\vec{r}_{i}.

By Lemma
A.1
, on the event where
r_{\iota_{S}}=\max_{i\in S}r_{i}
,

\prod_{ij\in T_{S}}\min(\max(r_{i},\lambda),\max(r_{j},\lambda))^{2}\leq\prod_{i\in S:i\neq\iota_{S}}\max(r_{i},\lambda)^{2}.

Therefore, integrating out the variables
\vec{r}_{i}
,
i\neq\iota_{S}
and performing a polar change of coordinates gives

(5.128)

\mathcal{J}^{\prime\prime}(\pi,(T_{S}^{l})_{l\in[4],S\in X},(\iota_{S}))\leq(CM^{2})^{N_{2}}N^{|\pi|}\prod_{S\in\pi}\int_{0}^{\varepsilon_{0}\bar{R}_{\beta,\lambda}}\max(r,\lambda)^{(4-\beta)|S|-3}\mathrm{d}r.

\bullet
Suppose
\beta\in(2,4)
. In this case, recall that
\bar{R}_{\beta,\lambda}=R_{\beta,\lambda}
. By assumption, for every
S\in\pi
,
|S|>p(\beta)
with
p(\beta)=p^{*}(\beta)
. Hence, for
\beta\in(\beta_{p(\beta)},\beta_{p(\beta)+1})
, (recall (
1.3
)) we have
(4-\beta)|S|-3>-1
and therefore

\mathcal{J}^{\prime\prime}(\pi,(T_{S}^{l})_{l\in[4],S\in X},(\iota_{S}))\leq(CM^{2})^{|S|}N^{|\pi|}(\varepsilon_{0}R_{\beta,\lambda})^{(4-\beta)|S|-2}.

Therefore, recalling that
R_{\beta,\lambda}^{4-\beta}=\lambda^{2-\beta}
, we get

(5.129)

\mathcal{J}^{\prime\prime}(\pi,(T_{S}^{l})_{l\in[4],S\in X},(\iota_{S}))\leq(CM^{2})^{|S|}N^{|\pi|}\prod_{S\in\pi}\lambda^{(2-\beta)|S|}\varepsilon_{0}^{(4-\beta)|S|}(\varepsilon_{0}R_{\beta,\lambda})^{-2}.

\bullet
Suppose that
\beta\geq 4
. Then,
(4-\beta)|S|-3\leq-3<-1
. Therefore, by (
5.128
), we have

(5.130)

\mathcal{J}^{\prime\prime}(\pi,(T_{S}^{l})_{l\in[4],S\in X},(\iota_{S}))\leq(CM^{2})^{N_{2}}N^{|\pi|}\prod_{S\in\pi}\lambda^{(4-\beta)|S|-2}=(CM^{2})^{N_{2}}N^{|\pi|}\prod_{S\in\pi}\lambda^{(2-\beta)|S|}\lambda^{2(|S|-1)}.

For every
m\geq 0
, let us define

f_{\beta}(m)=\begin{cases}\varepsilon_{0}^{(4-\beta)m}(\varepsilon_{0}R_{\beta,\lambda})^{-2}&\text{if $\beta\in(2,4)$}\\
\lambda^{2(m-1)}&\text{if $\beta\geq 4$}.\end{cases}

One can regroup the cases (
5.129
) and (
5.130
) into

\mathcal{J}^{\prime\prime}(\pi,(T_{S}^{l})_{l\in[4],S\in X},(\iota_{S}))\leq(CM^{2})^{N_{2}}N^{|\pi|}\prod_{S\in\pi}\lambda^{(2-\beta)|S|}f_{\beta}(|S|).

Since
C_{\beta,\lambda,\varepsilon_{0}}\geq\frac{1}{C}\lambda^{2-\beta}
, we deduce using (
5.126
) and (
5.127
) that

\frac{1}{C_{\beta,\lambda,\varepsilon_{0}}^{N_{2}}}\mathcal{J}(\pi)\leq(CM^{2})^{N_{2}}N^{|\pi|}\prod_{S\in\pi}|S|!|S|f_{\beta}(|S|).

We now sum this over
\pi\in\mathbf{\Pi}^{\prime}(V_{\mathrm{bad}}^{2,1})
. We first sum according to the number of components
k_{0}
of the partition, then divide by
k_{0}!
since blocks are indistinguishable and then according to the sizes of the blocks. Given
k_{0}
labeled blocks, the number of ways to assign
m_{i}
elements in the block
i
for every
i=1,\ldots,k_{0}
is given by
\frac{N_{2}!}{m_{1}!\ldots m_{k_{0}}!}
. Thus,

(5.131)

\sum_{\pi\in\mathbf{\Pi}^{\prime}(V_{\mathrm{bad}}^{2,1})}\frac{1}{C_{\beta,\lambda,\varepsilon_{0}}^{N_{2}}}\mathcal{J}(\pi)=\sum_{k_{0}=1}^{N_{2}}\sum_{\pi\in\mathbf{\Pi}^{\prime}(V_{\mathrm{bad}}^{2,1}):|\pi|=k_{0}}\frac{1}{C_{\beta,\lambda,\varepsilon_{0}}^{N_{2}}}\mathcal{J}(\pi)\\
\leq(CM^{2})^{N_{2}}\sum_{k_{0}=1}^{N_{2}}\frac{1}{k_{0}!}\sum_{m_{1}+\cdots+m_{k_{0}}=N_{2}:m_{i}>p(\beta)}\frac{N_{2}!}{m_{1}!\cdots m_{k_{0}}!}N^{k_{0}}\prod_{i=1}^{k_{0}}m_{i}!m_{i}f_{\beta}(m_{i}).

Therefore

\Bigr(\frac{N}{N_{2}}\Bigr)^{N_{2}}\frac{\mathsf{Z}_{\mathrm{bad},N_{2}}^{(2,1)}}{(NC_{\beta,\lambda,\varepsilon_{0}})^{N_{2}}}\\
\leq(CM^{2})^{N_{2}}\Bigr(\frac{N}{N_{2}}\Bigr)^{N_{2}}\frac{1}{N^{N_{2}}}\sum_{k_{0}=1}^{N_{2}}\frac{N_{2}!}{k_{0}!}N^{k_{0}}\sum_{m_{1}+\cdots+m_{k_{0}}=N_{2}:m_{i}>p(\beta)}\prod_{i=1}^{k_{0}}\Bigr(m_{i}f_{\beta}(m_{i})\Bigr).

Applying Stirling’s formula to
N_{2}!
, we get

\Bigr(\frac{N}{N_{2}}\Bigr)^{N_{2}}\frac{\mathsf{Z}_{\mathrm{bad},N_{2}}^{(2,1)}}{(NC_{\beta,\lambda,\varepsilon_{0}})^{N_{2}}}\leq(CM^{2})^{N_{2}}\sum_{k_{0}=1}^{N_{2}}\frac{N^{k_{0}}}{k_{0}!}\sum_{m_{1}+\cdots+m_{k_{0}}=N_{2}:m_{i}>p(\beta)}\prod_{i=1}^{k_{0}}m_{i}f_{\beta}(m_{i}).

By the above display, there exists a constant
C>0
depending on
\beta
and
M
such that for all
t\geq 1
,

\begin{split}\max_{N_{2}}\Bigr(\frac{N}{N_{2}}\Bigr)^{N_{2}}\frac{\mathsf{Z}_{\mathrm{bad},N_{2}}^{(2,1)}}{(NC_{\beta,\lambda,\varepsilon_{0}})^{N_{2}}}e^{tN_{2}}&\leq\sum_{N_{2}=0}^{N}\binom{N}{N_{2}}\frac{\mathsf{Z}_{\mathrm{bad},N_{2}}^{(2,1)}}{(NC_{\beta,\lambda,\varepsilon_{0}})^{N_{2}}}e^{tN_{2}}\\
&\leq\sum_{N_{2}=0}^{N}\sum_{k_{0}=1}^{N_{2}}\frac{N^{k_{0}}}{k_{0}!}\sum_{m_{1}+\cdots+m_{k_{0}}=N_{2}:m_{i}>p(\beta)}\prod_{i=1}^{k_{0}}\Bigr(m_{i}f_{\beta}(m_{i})C^{m_{i}}e^{tm_{i}}\Bigr)\\
&\leq\sum_{k_{0}=1}^{N}\frac{N^{k_{0}}}{k_{0}!}\left(\sum_{m>p(\beta)}mf_{\beta}(m)C^{m}e^{tm}\right)^{k_{0}}.\end{split}

\bullet
Suppose that
\beta\in(2,4)
. Recall that
f_{\beta}(m)=\varepsilon_{0}^{(4-\beta)|m|}(\varepsilon_{0}R_{\beta,\lambda}^{-2})
. We get that there exists
\tilde{\varepsilon}_{0}
depending on
\beta,M
and
t
such that for all
\varepsilon_{0}\in(0,\tilde{\varepsilon}_{0})
, there exists
C^{\prime}>0
depending on
\beta
,
M
,
\varepsilon_{0}
and
t
such that

\sum_{m>p(\beta)}mf_{\beta}(m)C^{m}e^{tm}\leq C^{\prime}R_{\beta,\lambda}^{-2}.

Therefore, for
\varepsilon_{0}\in(0,\tilde{\varepsilon}_{0})
there exists a constant
C>0
depending on
\beta
,
M
,
\varepsilon_{0}
, and
t
such that

\max_{N_{2}}\Bigr(\frac{N}{N_{2}}\Bigr)^{N_{2}}\frac{\mathsf{Z}_{\mathrm{bad},N_{2}}^{(2,1)}}{(NC_{\beta,\lambda,\varepsilon_{0}})^{N_{2}}}e^{tN_{2}}\leq\sum_{k_{0}=1}^{N}\frac{N^{k_{0}}}{k_{0}!}(CR_{\beta,\lambda}^{-2})^{k_{0}}\leq e^{CNR_{\beta,\lambda}^{-2}},

which concludes the proof in the case
\beta\in(2,4)
.

\bullet
Suppose that
\beta\geq 4
. Recall that
f_{\beta}(m)=\lambda^{2(m-1)}
. Then, taking
\lambda
small enough with respect to
\beta
,
M
,
t
and
p_{0}
, we get that there exists
C>0
depending on
\beta
,
M
,
t
and
p_{0}
such that

\max_{N_{2}}\Bigr(\frac{N}{N_{2}}\Bigr)^{N_{2}}\frac{\mathsf{Z}_{\mathrm{bad},N_{2}}^{(2,1)}}{(NC_{\beta,\lambda,\varepsilon_{0}})^{N_{2}}}e^{tN_{2}}\leq\sum_{k_{0}=1}^{N}\frac{N^{k_{0}}}{k_{0}!}(C\lambda^{2p(\beta)})^{k_{0}}\leq e^{NC\lambda^{2p(\beta)}}=e^{NC\lambda^{2p_{0}}}.

∎

Next, we give a control on bad points of type
(2,2)
, which are those in multipoles of cardinality
k\in\{1,\ldots,p(\beta)\}
such that
\mathcal{N}_{k}
is above the authorized threshold.

Lemma 5.41
.

Let
k\in\{1,\ldots,p(\beta)\}
and let
\mathsf{Z}_{\mathrm{bad},N^{\prime\prime}_{k}}^{(2,2,k)}
be as in (
5.124
). Then, there exists a constant
C>0
depending on
\beta,p(\beta)
and
M
such that

\Bigr(\frac{N}{N^{\prime\prime}_{k}}\Bigr)^{N_{k}^{\prime\prime}}\frac{\mathsf{Z}_{\mathrm{bad},N_{k}^{\prime\prime}}^{(2,2,k)}}{(NC_{\beta,\lambda,\varepsilon_{0}})^{N_{k}^{\prime\prime}}}\leq C^{N_{k}^{\prime\prime}}\Bigr(\frac{N\lambda^{2(k-1)}}{N_{k}^{\prime\prime}}\Bigr)^{N_{k}^{\prime\prime}}.

The argument is similar to the one of the preceding lemma, so we record only the estimates. Partition the index set
[kN_{k}^{\prime\prime}]
into
N_{k}^{\prime\prime}
blocks of size
k
(“
k
-poles”). The number of such partitions is

\frac{(kN_{k}^{\prime\prime})!}{(k!)^{N_{k}^{\prime\prime}}N_{k}^{\prime\prime}!}\leq C^{N_{k}^{\prime\prime}}(N_{k}^{\prime\prime})^{(k-1)N_{k}^{\prime\prime}}.

Each
2k
-pole contributes, after integration, a factor
N\,\lambda^{(4-\beta)k-2}
. Normalizing by
(NC_{\beta,\lambda,\varepsilon_{0}})^{N_{k}^{\prime\prime}}
therefore yields

\frac{\mathsf{Z}_{\mathrm{bad},N_{k}^{\prime\prime}}^{(2,2,k)}}{(NC_{\beta,\lambda,\varepsilon_{0}})^{N_{k}^{\prime\prime}}}\leq C^{N_{k}^{\prime\prime}}\,\lambda^{2(k-1)N_{k}^{\prime\prime}},

which is the desired bound.

Combining (
5.106
), (
5.122
), Lemma
5.38
, Lemma
5.39
, Lemma
5.40
and Lemma
5.41
concludes the proof of Proposition
5.37
.

5.15.
Proof of the upper bound

Proposition 5.42

(Upper bound)
.

Let
\beta\in(2,+\infty)
and
p_{0}\in\mathbb{N}^{*}
. Recall
p(\beta)
from Definition
2.7
.

Let
\mathcal{N}_{1},\ldots,\mathcal{N}_{p(\beta)}
be the number of multipoles of cardinality
1,\ldots,p(\beta)
in
I_{\mathrm{good}}
. Let
n_{1},\ldots,n_{p(\beta)}\in\mathbb{N}
. Define

\mathcal{A}\coloneqq\{\mathcal{N}_{1}=n_{1},\ldots,\mathcal{N}_{p(\beta)}=n_{p(\beta)}\}.

Let
\mathcal{I}_{\beta,p(\beta),\lambda}
be as in Definition
2.14
. Suppose that for every
k\in\{2,\ldots,p(\beta)\}
, (
5.48
) holds.
Then, for
\varepsilon_{0}
small enough and for every
t\geq 1
, we have

\log\int_{\mathcal{A}}e^{t|I_{\mathrm{bad}}|}e^{-\beta\mathsf{F}_{\lambda}}\mathbf{1}_{\{\sigma_{N}=\mathrm{Id}\}}\mathrm{d}\vec{X}_{N}\mathrm{d}\vec{Y}_{N}\leq N\log N+N(2-\beta)\log\lambda+N\log\mathcal{Z}_{\beta}-N\mathcal{I}_{\beta,p(\beta),\lambda}(\tfrac{n_{1}}{N},\ldots,\tfrac{n_{p(\beta)}}{N})\\
+O_{\beta,M,p,\varepsilon_{0},t}(N\delta_{\beta,\lambda}).

Proof.

Denote
p=p(\beta)
. Let
N^{\prime}\coloneqq n_{1}+2n_{2}+\cdots+pn_{p}
and let
V_{\mathrm{good}}\subset[N]
be an arbitrary set of cardinality
N^{\prime}
. Set
V_{\mathrm{bad}}\coloneqq[N]\setminus V_{\mathrm{good}}
. Let
\pi
be a partition of
V_{\mathrm{good}}
with
n_{i}
parts of cardinality
i
for every
i\in[p]
.
Recall Lemma
5.16
.

Step 1: expanding the multipole terms

Combining Proposition
5.18
and Lemma
4.22
, we obtain that for every
S\subset\pi
,

\mathsf{M}_{\varepsilon_{0}}^{+,Z}(S)=\frac{1}{N^{|S|-1}}\Bigr(\mathsf{m}_{\beta,\lambda}(|S|)+O_{\beta,M,p,\varepsilon_{0}}\Bigr(\bar{R}_{\beta,\lambda}^{-2}+\frac{|V_{\mathrm{bad}}|}{N}\Bigr)\Bigr).

Therefore, since
\mathsf{m}_{\beta,\lambda}(1)=1
and
\mathsf{M}_{\varepsilon_{0}}^{+,Z}(S)
when
|S|=1
, we have

\prod_{S\in\pi}\mathsf{M}_{\varepsilon_{0}}^{+,Z}(S)=\frac{N^{n_{1}+\ldots+n_{p(\beta)}}}{N^{N^{\prime}}}\prod_{k=2}^{p}\mathsf{m}_{\beta,\lambda}(k)^{n_{k}}\prod_{k=2}^{p}e^{\frac{n_{k}^{\prime}}{\mathsf{m}_{\beta,\lambda(k)}}O_{\beta,M,p,\varepsilon_{0}}(\bar{R}_{\beta,\lambda}^{-2}+\frac{|V_{\mathrm{bad}}|}{N})}.

Recall that by Proposition
4.19
, there exists a constant
C>0
depending on
\beta
,
M
and
p
such that

\mathsf{m}_{\beta,\lambda}(k)\geq\frac{1}{C}\lambda^{2(k-1)}.

Using this and the assumption (
5.48
), we deduce that

\prod_{S\in\pi}\mathsf{M}_{\varepsilon_{0}}^{+,Z}(S)=\frac{N^{n_{1}+\ldots+n_{p}}}{N^{N^{\prime}}}\left(\prod_{k=2}^{p}\mathsf{m}_{\beta,\lambda}(k)^{n_{k}}\right)e^{O_{\beta,M,p,\varepsilon_{0}}(N\bar{R}_{\beta,\lambda}^{-2}+|V_{\mathrm{bad}}|)}.

Inserting this into (
5.50
), we deduce that

(5.132)

\frac{1}{(NC_{\beta,\lambda,\varepsilon_{0}})^{N}}\int_{\mathcal{A}}\mathbf{1}_{\sigma_{N}=\mathrm{Id}}e^{-\beta\mathsf{F}_{\lambda}}\leq T_{1}T_{2}e^{O_{\beta,M,p,\varepsilon_{0}}(N\bar{R}_{\beta,\lambda}^{-2}+|V_{\mathrm{bad}}|)},

where

T_{1}\coloneqq\frac{(N^{\prime})!}{1^{n_{1}}(2!)^{n_{2}}\cdots(p!)^{n_{p}}n_{1}!\cdots n_{p}!}\frac{N^{n_{1}+\cdots+n_{p}}}{N^{N^{\prime}}}\prod_{k=2}^{p}\mathsf{m}_{\beta,\lambda}(k)^{n_{k}}

and

(5.133)

T_{2}\coloneqq\binom{N}{N^{\prime}}\frac{1}{(NC_{\beta,\lambda,\varepsilon_{0}})^{|V_{\mathrm{bad}}|}}\int_{(\Lambda^{2})^{|V_{\mathrm{bad}}|}}\prod_{i,j\in V_{\mathrm{bad}}:i<j}e^{-\beta v^{Z}_{ij}}\mathbf{1}_{\mathcal{A}_{ij}}W(Z)\mathbf{1}_{I_{\mathrm{bad}}=V_{\mathrm{bad}}}\\
\times\prod_{i\in V_{\mathrm{bad}}}\frac{1}{(\tau_{i}^{+,Z})^{\frac{\beta}{2}}}\frac{1}{(\tau_{i}^{-,Z})^{\frac{\beta}{2}}}\mathrm{d}Z.

By Stirling’s formula,

\log\left(\frac{(N^{\prime})!}{n_{1}!\cdots n_{p}!}\frac{N^{n_{1}+\cdots+n_{p}}}{N^{N^{\prime}}}\right)=\log\left(\Bigr(\frac{N^{\prime}}{N}\Bigr)^{N^{\prime}}e^{-N^{\prime}}\prod_{k=1}^{p}\Bigr(\frac{N}{n_{k}}\Bigr)^{n_{k}}e^{n_{k}}\right)+O(\log N).

Using that
N^{\prime}=N-|V_{\mathrm{bad}}|
, it follows that

(5.134)

\begin{split}T_{1}&=\Bigr(\frac{N^{\prime}}{N}\Bigr)^{N^{\prime}}e^{-N^{\prime}}\left(\prod_{k=1}^{p}\Bigr(\frac{N}{n_{k}k!}\Bigr)^{n_{k}}e^{n_{k}}\prod_{k=2}^{p}\mathsf{m}_{\beta,\lambda}(k)^{n_{k}}\right)e^{O(\log N)}\\
&=e^{-N}\left(\prod_{k=1}^{p}\Bigr(\frac{N}{n_{k}k!}\Bigr)^{n_{k}}e^{n_{k}}\prod_{k=2}^{p}\mathsf{m}_{\beta,\lambda}(k)^{n_{k}}\right)e^{O(\log N+|V_{\mathrm{bad}}|)}.\end{split}

Step 2: cluster expansion

By Proposition
5.19
and Lemma
4.6
, for
\varepsilon_{0}
small enough, we have

\log W(Z)=\sum_{n=1}^{\infty}\frac{1}{n!}\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}X_{1},\ldots,X_{n}\in\mathcal{P}(\pi)\\
\mathrm{connected}\end{subarray}}\left(\prod_{i=1}^{n}\mathbf{1}_{|V_{X_{i}}|\leq p}\mathsf{K}_{\varepsilon_{0}}^{+,Z}(X_{i})\right)\mathrm{I}(G(X_{1},\ldots,X_{n}))+O_{\beta,M,p,\varepsilon_{0}}\left(N\delta_{\beta,\lambda}+|V_{\mathrm{bad}}|\right).

Furthermore, by Proposition
5.18
,

\sum_{n=1}^{\infty}\frac{1}{n!}\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}X_{1},\ldots,X_{n}\in\mathcal{P}(\pi)\\
\mathrm{connected}\end{subarray}}\left(\prod_{i=1}^{n}\mathbf{1}_{|V_{X_{i}}|\leq p}\mathsf{K}_{\varepsilon_{0}}^{+,Z}(X_{i})\right)\mathrm{I}(G(X_{1},\ldots,X_{n}))\\
=\sum_{n=1}^{\infty}\frac{1}{n!}\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}X_{1},\ldots,X_{n}\in\mathcal{P}(\pi)\\
\mathrm{connected}\end{subarray}}\left(\prod_{i=1}^{n}\mathbf{1}_{|V_{X_{i}}|\leq p}\mathsf{K}_{\varepsilon_{0}}^{0}(X_{i})\right)\mathrm{I}(G(X_{1},\ldots,X_{n}))+O_{\beta,M,p,\varepsilon_{0}}\left(N\delta_{\beta,\lambda}+|V_{\mathrm{bad}}|\right).

Therefore, combining the above two displays gives

\log W(Z)=\sum_{n=1}^{\infty}\frac{1}{n!}\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}X_{1},\ldots,X_{n}\in\mathcal{P}(\pi)\\
\mathrm{connected}\end{subarray}}\left(\prod_{i=1}^{n}\mathbf{1}_{|V_{X_{i}}|\leq p}\mathsf{K}_{\varepsilon_{0}}^{0}(X_{i})\right)\mathrm{I}(G(X_{1},\ldots,X_{n}))+O_{\beta,M,p,\varepsilon_{0}}\left(N\delta_{\beta,\lambda}+|V_{\mathrm{bad}}|\right).

By the estimate (
5.61
) of Proposition
5.18
, one can replace
\mathsf{K}_{\varepsilon_{0}}^{0}
by
\mathsf{K}_{\infty}^{0}
up to a well-controlled error term. By Corollary
4.56
,
\mathsf{K}_{\infty}^{0}
can be replaced by
\mathsf{K}_{\beta,\lambda}^{\mathrm{mult}}
. Hence, applying (
4.158
), we then get that for
\varepsilon_{0}
small enough,

(5.135)
