\log W(Z)=S_{N}+O_{\beta,M,p,\varepsilon_{0}}\left(N\delta_{\beta,\lambda}+|V_{\mathrm{bad}}|\right)+o(N),

where

S_{N}=-N\sum_{n=1}^{\infty}\frac{(-1)^{n}}{n!}\sum_{m_{1},\ldots,m_{p}\in\mathbb{N}}\prod_{i=1}^{p}\frac{\gamma_{i}^{m_{i}}}{m_{i}!}\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}(X_{1},\ldots,X_{n})\\
\in\mathrm{Htrees}_{n}(Y(m_{1},\ldots,m_{p}))\\
X_{1}\cup\cdots\cup X_{n}=Y(m_{1},\ldots,m_{p})\end{subarray}}\prod_{i=1}^{p}\mathsf{k}_{\beta,\lambda}^{\mathrm{mult}}((\#_{k}X_{i})_{k})\mathbf{1}_{|V_{X_{i}}|\leq p},

where we recall that
Y(m_{1},\ldots,m_{p})
is as in Definition
2.13
. Inserting this into (
5.133
) and using (
5.106
) gives

(5.136)

T_{2}=e^{S_{N}+O_{\beta,M,p,\varepsilon_{0}}\left(N\delta_{\beta,\lambda}+|V_{\mathrm{bad}}|\right)}\\
\times\binom{N}{N^{\prime}}\frac{1}{(NC_{\beta,\lambda,\varepsilon_{0}})^{|V_{\mathrm{bad}}|}}\int_{(\Lambda^{2})^{|V_{\mathrm{bad}}|}}\prod_{i,j\in V_{\mathrm{bad}}:i<j}\mathbf{1}_{\mathcal{A}_{ij}}\mathbf{1}_{I_{\mathrm{bad}}=V_{\mathrm{bad}}}e^{-\beta v_{ij}^{Z}}\prod_{i\in V_{\mathrm{bad}}}\frac{1}{(\tau_{i}^{+,Z})^{\frac{\beta}{2}}}\frac{1}{(\tau_{i}^{-,Z})^{\frac{\beta}{2}}}\mathrm{d}x_{i}\mathrm{d}y_{i}.

Step 3: conclusion

Combining (
5.132
), (
5.134
), (
5.136
) and Definition
2.14
, we obtain

\frac{1}{(NC_{\beta,\lambda,\varepsilon_{0}})^{N}}\int_{\mathcal{A}}\mathbf{1}_{\sigma_{N}=\mathrm{Id}}e^{-\beta\mathsf{F}_{\lambda}}\leq e^{-N\mathcal{I}_{\beta,p,\lambda}(\frac{n_{1}}{N},\ldots,\frac{n_{p}}{N})+O_{\beta,M,p,\varepsilon_{0}}\left(N\delta_{\beta,\lambda}+|V_{\mathrm{bad}}|\right)}\\
\times\binom{N}{N^{\prime}}\frac{1}{(NC_{\beta,\lambda,\varepsilon_{0}})^{|V_{\mathrm{bad}}|}}\int_{(\Lambda^{2})^{|V_{\mathrm{bad}}|}}\prod_{i,j\in V_{\mathrm{bad}}:i<j}\mathbf{1}_{\mathcal{A}_{ij}}\mathbf{1}_{I_{\mathrm{bad}}=V_{\mathrm{bad}}}e^{-\beta v_{ij}^{Z}}\prod_{i\in V_{\mathrm{bad}}}\frac{1}{(\tau_{i}^{+,Z})^{\frac{\beta}{2}}}\frac{1}{(\tau_{i}^{-,Z})^{\frac{\beta}{2}}}\mathrm{d}x_{i}\mathrm{d}y_{i}.

Hence, for all
t\geq 1
, there exists a constant
C>0
depending on
\beta,M,p
and
\varepsilon_{0}
such that

\frac{1}{(NC_{\beta,\lambda,\varepsilon_{0}})^{N}}\int_{\mathcal{A}}\mathbf{1}_{\sigma_{N}=\mathrm{Id}}e^{t|I_{\mathrm{bad}}|}e^{-\beta\mathsf{F}_{\lambda}}\leq e^{-NI_{\beta,p,\lambda}(\frac{n_{1}}{N},\ldots,\frac{n_{p}}{N})}e^{O_{\beta,M,p,\varepsilon_{0}}(N\delta_{\beta,\lambda})}\\
\times\binom{N}{N^{\prime}}\frac{e^{(t+C)|V_{\mathrm{bad}}|}}{(NC_{\beta,\lambda,\varepsilon_{0}})^{|V_{\mathrm{bad}}|}}\int_{(\Lambda^{2})^{|V_{\mathrm{bad}}|}}\prod_{i,j\in V_{\mathrm{bad}}:i<j}\mathbf{1}_{\mathcal{A}_{ij}}\mathbf{1}_{I_{\mathrm{bad}}=V_{\mathrm{bad}}}e^{-\beta v_{ij}^{Z}}\prod_{i\in V_{\mathrm{bad}}}\frac{1}{(\tau_{i}^{+,Z})^{\frac{\beta}{2}}}\frac{1}{(\tau_{i}^{-,Z})^{\frac{\beta}{2}}}\mathrm{d}x_{i}\mathrm{d}y_{i}.

Thus, in view of (
5.103
) and (
5.104
), applying the Laplace transform control of Proposition
5.37
, we conclude that for all
t\geq 1
,

(5.137)

\frac{1}{(NC_{\beta,\lambda,\varepsilon_{0}})^{N}}\int_{\mathcal{A}}\mathbf{1}_{\sigma_{N}=\mathrm{Id}}e^{t|I_{\mathrm{bad}}|}e^{-\beta\mathsf{F}_{\lambda}}\leq e^{-N\mathcal{I}_{\beta,p,\lambda}(\frac{n_{1}}{N},\ldots,\frac{n_{p}}{N})+O_{\beta,M,p,\varepsilon_{0},t}\left(N\delta_{\beta,\lambda}\right)}.

∎

5.16.
Proof of the multipole distribution theorem

Proof of Theorem
2
.

Denote
p\coloneqq p(\beta)
. Recall the notation (
2.34
).
Denote
\mathcal{N}_{1}^{\mathrm{good}},\ldots,\mathcal{N}_{p}^{\mathrm{good}}
the number of multipoles of cardinality
1,\ldots,p
in
I_{\mathrm{good}}
.
The proof of item (
2.45
) is straightforward. By Lemma
4.22
, under the assumption of (
2.45
), the limits
\mathsf{m}_{\beta,\lambda}(k)
and
\mathsf{k}_{\beta,\lambda}^{\mathrm{mult}}(\{\#_{m}X_{i}\}_{m})
exist. Moreover, inserting the estimates of Proposition
4.19
to control the numerator and the denominator of the quotients defining both limiting activities yields (
2.44
) and (
2.45
).

Step 1: control of the number of bad points

Let us write

\mathbb{P}_{N,\beta}^{\lambda}(|I_{\mathrm{bad}}|=k)\leq\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}n_{1},\ldots,n_{p}:\\
n_{1}+2n_{2}+\cdots+pn_{p}=N-k\end{subarray}}\mathbb{P}_{N,\beta}^{\lambda}(|I_{\mathrm{bad}}|=k,\forall i\in[p],\mathcal{N}_{i}^{\mathrm{good}}=n_{i}).

Hence,

(5.138)

\mathbb{P}_{N,\beta}^{\lambda}(|I_{\mathrm{bad}}|=k)\leq N^{p}\max_{\begin{subarray}{c}n_{1},\ldots,n_{p}:\\
n_{1}+2n_{2}+\cdots+pn_{p}=N-k\end{subarray}}\mathbb{P}_{N,\beta}^{\lambda}(|I_{\mathrm{bad}}|=k,\forall i\in[p],\mathcal{N}_{i}^{\mathrm{good}}=n_{i}).

By Markov’s inequality,

(5.139)

\begin{split}\mathbb{P}_{N,\beta}^{\lambda}(|I_{\mathrm{bad}}|=k,\forall i\in[p],\mathcal{N}_{i}^{\mathrm{good}}=n_{i})&\leq e^{-k}\mathbb{E}_{\mathbb{P}_{N,\beta}^{\lambda}}\left[e^{|I_{\mathrm{bad}}|}\prod_{i=1}^{p}\mathbf{1}_{\mathcal{N}_{i}^{\mathrm{good}}=n_{i}}\right]\\
&=e^{-k}\frac{1}{Z_{N,\beta}^{\lambda}}\int e^{|I_{\mathrm{bad}}|}e^{-\beta\mathsf{F}_{\lambda}}\prod_{i=1}^{p}\mathbf{1}_{\mathcal{N}_{i}^{\mathrm{good}}=n_{i}}\mathrm{d}\vec{X}_{N}\mathrm{d}\vec{Y}_{N}.\end{split}

By Proposition
4.57
,

(5.140)

\log Z_{N,\beta}^{\lambda}\geq\log N!+N\log N+N\log(\lambda^{2-\beta}\mathcal{Z}_{\beta})-N\inf_{\triangle_{p,0}}\mathcal{I}_{\beta,p,\lambda}+O_{\beta,M,p,\varepsilon_{0}}(N\delta_{\beta,\lambda}).

Moreover, by Proposition
5.42
,

\log\int e^{|I_{\mathrm{bad}}|}e^{-\beta\mathsf{F}_{\lambda}}\prod_{i=1}^{p}\mathbf{1}_{\mathcal{N}_{i}^{\mathrm{good}}=n_{i}}\mathrm{d}\vec{X}_{N}\mathrm{d}\vec{Y}_{N}=\log N!+N\log N+N\log(\lambda^{2-\beta}\mathcal{Z}_{\beta})-N\mathcal{I}_{\beta,p,\lambda}(\tfrac{n_{1}}{N},\ldots,\tfrac{n_{p}}{N})\\
+O_{\beta,M,p,\varepsilon_{0}}(N\delta_{\beta,\lambda}).

Inserting into (
5.139
) and using (
5.138
), we deduce that

(5.141)

\log\mathbb{P}_{N,\beta}^{\lambda}(|I_{\mathrm{bad}}|=k)\leq p\log N-k-N\left(\inf_{\triangle_{p,\frac{k}{N}}}\mathcal{I}_{\beta,p,\lambda}-\inf_{\triangle_{p,0}}\mathcal{I}_{\beta,p,\lambda}\right)+O_{\beta,M,p,\varepsilon_{0}}(N\delta_{\beta,\lambda}).

Let us study how
\inf_{\triangle_{p,x}}\mathcal{I}_{\beta,p,\lambda}
depends on
x
. Denote for shorthand

\tilde{m}_{i}\coloneqq\frac{\mathsf{m}_{\beta,\lambda}(i)}{i!}

We split
\mathcal{I}_{\beta,p,\lambda}
into
f+h
with

f:(\gamma_{1},\ldots,\gamma_{p})\in\cup_{x\in[0,1]}\triangle_{p,x}\mapsto\sum_{i=1}^{p}\gamma_{i}(\log\gamma_{i}-\log\tilde{m}_{i})+1-\sum_{i=1}^{p}\gamma_{i}

and

h:(\gamma_{1},\ldots,\gamma_{p})\in\cup_{x\in[0,1]}\triangle_{p,x}\\
\mapsto\sum_{n=1}^{\infty}\frac{(-1)^{n}}{n!}\sum_{m_{1},\ldots,m_{p}\in\mathbb{N}}\prod_{i=1}^{p}\frac{\gamma_{i}^{m_{i}}}{m_{i}!}\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}(X_{1},\ldots,X_{n})\\
\in\mathrm{Htrees}_{n}(Y(m_{1},\ldots,m_{p}))\\
X_{1}\cup\cdots\cup X_{n}=Y(m_{1},\ldots,m_{p})\end{subarray}}\prod_{i=1}^{p}\mathsf{k}_{\beta,\lambda}^{\mathrm{mult}}((\#_{k}X_{i})_{k})\mathbf{1}_{|V_{X_{i}}|\leq p}.

Notice that by
h
is a polynomial and that by (
2.45
), the nonzero contributions to the sum are
O(\lambda^{2})
. Therefore, there exists
C>0
depending on
\beta
,
M
and
p
such that for every
x\in[0,1]
and every
(\gamma_{1},\ldots,\gamma_{p})\in\triangle_{p,0}
, we have

|h(\gamma_{1},\ldots,\gamma_{p})-h(\gamma_{1}(1-x),\ldots,\gamma_{p}(1-x))|\leq Cx\lambda^{2}.

Hence, there exists
C>0
depending on
\beta
,
M
and
p
such that for every
x\in[0,1]
,

(5.142)

\left|\inf_{\triangle_{p,x}}\mathcal{I}_{\beta,p,\lambda}-\inf_{\triangle_{p,0}}\mathcal{I}_{\beta,p,\lambda}\right|\leq\left|\inf_{\triangle_{p,x}}f-\inf_{\triangle_{p,0}}f\right|+Cx\lambda^{2}.

Next, one can easily check that the minimizer over
\triangle_{p,x}
of
f
is given by

\gamma_{i}^{x}=(1-x)\frac{\tilde{m}_{i}}{\sum_{i=1}^{p}\tilde{m}_{i}}\quad\text{for every $i=1,\ldots,p$}.

Moreover,

\inf_{\triangle_{p,x}}f=f(\gamma_{1}^{x},\ldots,\gamma_{p}^{x})=(1-x)\log(1-x)-(1-x)\log\sum_{i=1}^{p}\tilde{m}_{i}+x.

Thus,

\inf_{\triangle_{p,x}}f-\inf_{\triangle_{p,0}}f=((1-x)\log(1-x)+x)+x\log\sum_{i=1}^{p}\tilde{m}_{i}.

Since
\tilde{m}_{1}=1
and by (
2.44
), we have

\log\sum_{i=1}^{p}\tilde{m}_{i}=O_{\beta,p,M}(\lambda^{2}).

Moreover, notice that
(1-x)\log(1-x)+x\geq 0
on
[0,1]
. Hence, there exists
C>0
depending on
\beta,p
and
M
such that for every
x\in[0,1]
,

\inf_{\triangle_{p,x}}f-\inf_{\triangle_{p,0}}f\geq-Cx\lambda^{2}.

Combining this with (
5.142
) we obtain

(5.143)

\inf_{\triangle_{p,x}}\mathcal{I}_{\beta,p,\lambda}\geq\inf_{\triangle_{p,0}}\mathcal{I}_{\beta,p,\lambda}+O_{\beta,M,p}(x\lambda^{2}).

Inserting (
5.143
) into (
5.141
) gives

\log\mathbb{P}_{N,\beta}^{\lambda}(|I_{\mathrm{bad}}|=k)\leq-k(1+O_{\beta,M,p}(\lambda^{2}))+O_{\beta,M,p,\varepsilon_{0}}(N\delta_{\beta,\lambda}).

Hence,

(5.144)

\log\mathbb{P}_{N,\beta}^{\lambda}(|I_{\mathrm{bad}}|=k)\leq-\frac{k}{2}+O_{\beta,M,p,\varepsilon_{0}}(N\delta_{\beta,\lambda}).

Using that
0\leq N-\mathcal{N}_{1}+2\mathcal{N}_{2}+\cdots+p\mathcal{N}_{p}\leq|I_{\mathrm{bad}}|
and that
|\{i\in[N]:|x_{i}-y_{\sigma_{N}(i)}|\geq R_{\beta,\lambda}\}|\leq|I_{\mathrm{bad}}|
, this concludes the proof of (
2.47
) and (
2.48
). Moreover, since for every
i\in\{2,\ldots,p\}
,

\mathcal{N}_{i}\mathbf{1}_{\mathcal{N}_{i}>\varepsilon_{0}^{-\alpha(\beta)}N}\leq|I_{\mathrm{bad}}|,

this shows that

(5.145)

\mathbb{P}_{N,\beta}^{\lambda}(\mathcal{N}_{i}>\varepsilon_{0}^{-\alpha(\beta)}N)\leq e^{-N\varepsilon_{0}^{-\alpha(\beta)}(1+O_{\beta,M,p,\varepsilon_{0}}(\lambda^{2}))+O_{\beta,M,p,\varepsilon_{0}}(N\delta_{\beta,\lambda})}.

Step 2: expansion of the partition function

We write

Z_{N,\beta}^{\lambda}=\sum_{k=0}^{N}\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}n_{1},\ldots,n_{p}:\\
n_{1}+2n_{2}+\cdots+pn_{p}=N-k\end{subarray}}\int e^{-\beta\mathsf{F}_{\lambda}}\mathbf{1}_{|I_{\mathrm{bad}}|=k}\prod_{i=1}^{p}\mathbf{1}_{\mathcal{N}_{i}^{\mathrm{good}}=n_{i}}\mathrm{d}\vec{X}_{N}\mathrm{d}\vec{Y}_{N}.

Proceeding as in Step 1, we get

\log Z_{N,\beta}^{\lambda}\leq(p+1)\log N+\log N!+N\log N+N\log(\lambda^{2-\beta}\mathcal{Z}_{\beta})+\max_{x\in[0,1]}(-Nx+O_{\beta,M,p,\varepsilon_{0}}(Nx\lambda^{2}))\\
-N\inf_{\triangle_{p,0}}\mathcal{I}_{\beta,p,\lambda}+O_{\beta,M,p,\varepsilon_{0}}(N\delta_{\beta,\lambda}).

Thus,

\log Z_{N,\beta}^{\lambda}\leq\log N!+N\log N+N\log(\lambda^{2-\beta}\mathcal{Z}_{\beta})-N\inf_{\triangle_{p,0}}\mathcal{I}_{\beta,p,\lambda}+O_{\beta,M,p,\varepsilon_{0}}(N\delta_{\beta,\lambda}).

Combining this with (
5.140
) gives equality, i.e. proves (
2.46
).

Step 3: proof of the large deviations bounds

For every
\gamma\in\triangle_{p,0}
and
r>0
, denote

\bar{B}_{\infty}(\gamma,r)\coloneqq\{(\gamma^{\prime}_{1},\ldots,\gamma^{\prime}_{p})\in(\mathbb{R}^{+})^{p}:\forall i\in[p],|\gamma_{i}^{\prime}-\gamma_{i}|\leq r\}.

Let
\gamma\in\triangle_{p,0}
be such that for every
i\in[p]
,
\gamma_{i}\leq C_{0}\lambda^{2(i-1)}
. Recall that
\mathcal{N}_{1},\ldots,\mathcal{N}_{p}
stand for the numbers of multipoles in
[N]
of cardinality
1,\ldots,p
. Clearly, for every
i\in[p]
,

\mathcal{N}_{i}^{\mathrm{good}}\leq\mathcal{N}_{i}\leq\mathcal{N}_{i}^{\mathrm{good}}+|I_{\mathrm{bad}}|.

Therefore,

(5.146)

\begin{split}\mathbb{P}_{N,\beta}^{\lambda}\left(\forall i\in[p],|\mathcal{N}_{i}-N\gamma_{i}|\leq C_{0}N\delta_{\beta,\lambda}\right)&\leq\frac{1}{Z_{N,\beta}^{\lambda}}\sum_{k=0}^{N}\int\mathbf{1}_{|I_{\mathrm{bad}}|=k}\mathbf{1}_{|\mathcal{N}_{i}^{\mathrm{good}}-N\gamma_{i}|\leq C_{0}N\delta_{\beta,\lambda}+k}e^{-\beta\mathsf{F}_{\lambda}}\mathrm{d}\vec{X}_{N}\mathrm{d}\vec{Y}_{N}\\
&\leq\frac{1}{Z_{N,\beta}^{\lambda}}\sum_{k=0}^{N}e^{-k}\int e^{|I_{\mathrm{bad}}|}\mathbf{1}_{|\mathcal{N}_{i}^{\mathrm{good}}-N\gamma_{i}|\leq C_{0}N\delta_{\beta,\lambda}+k}e^{-\beta\mathsf{F}_{\lambda}}\mathrm{d}\vec{X}_{N}\mathrm{d}\vec{Y}_{N}.\end{split}

Applying Proposition
5.42
(which we can thanks to the assumption on
\gamma_{i}
), we therefore get

\log\mathbb{P}_{N,\beta}^{\lambda}\left(\forall i\in[p],|\mathcal{N}_{i}-N\gamma_{i}|\leq C_{0}N\delta_{\beta,\lambda}\right)\leq-\log Z_{N,\beta}^{\lambda}+\log N!+N\log N+N\log(\lambda^{2-\beta}\mathcal{Z}_{\beta})\\
-N\inf_{x\in[0,1]}\left(x+\inf_{\bar{B}_{\infty}(\gamma,C_{0}\delta_{\beta,\lambda}+x)}\mathcal{I}_{\beta,p,\lambda}\right)+O_{\beta,M,p,\varepsilon_{0}}(N\delta_{\beta,\lambda}).

Proceeding as in Step 1 shows that

\inf_{\bar{B}_{\infty}(\gamma,C_{0}\delta_{\beta,\lambda}+x)}\mathcal{I}_{\beta,p,\lambda}\geq\mathcal{I}_{\beta,p,\lambda}(\gamma)+O_{\beta,M,p,\varepsilon_{0}}((C_{0}\delta_{\beta,\lambda}+x)\lambda^{2}).

Therefore, using (
2.46
) and (
5.146
), we get

(5.147)

\log\mathbb{P}_{N,\beta}^{\lambda}\left(\forall i\in[p],|\mathcal{N}_{i}-N\gamma_{i}|\leq C_{0}N\delta_{\beta,\lambda}\right)\leq-N\left(\mathcal{I}_{\beta,p,\lambda}(\gamma)-\inf_{\triangle_{p,0}}\mathcal{I}_{\beta,p,\lambda}\right)+O_{\beta,M,p,\varepsilon_{0}}(N\delta_{\beta,\lambda}).

Fix
n_{1},\ldots,n_{p}
such that
n_{1}+2n_{2}+\cdots+pn_{p}=N
and such that for every
i\in[p]
,
|n_{i}-N\gamma_{i}|\leq p
(which is possible since
\gamma\in\triangle_{p,0}
). We use

\mathbb{P}_{N,\beta}^{\lambda}\left(\forall i\in[p],|\mathcal{N}_{i}-N\gamma_{i}|\leq C_{0}N\delta_{\beta,\lambda}\right)\geq\mathbb{P}_{N,\beta}^{\lambda}(\forall i\in[p],\mathcal{N}_{i}^{\mathrm{good}}=n_{i}).

Notice that by the definition of good points, since
N-(n_{1}+2n_{2}+\cdots+pn_{p})=0
,

\bigcap_{i\in[p]}\{\mathcal{N}_{i}^{\mathrm{good}}=n_{i}\}=\bigcap_{i\in[p]}\{\mathcal{N}_{i}=n_{i}\}\cap\bigcap_{i\in[N]}\{r_{i}\leq\varepsilon_{0}R_{\beta,\lambda}\}.

Therefore, we obtain from Proposition
4.57
and (
2.46
) that

\log\mathbb{P}_{N,\beta}^{\lambda}(\forall i\in[p],\mathcal{N}_{i}^{\mathrm{good}}=n_{i})\geq-N\left(\mathcal{I}_{\beta,p,\lambda}(\gamma)-\inf_{\triangle_{p,0}}\mathcal{I}_{\beta,p,\lambda}\right)+O_{\beta,M,p,\varepsilon_{0}}(N\delta_{\beta,\lambda}).

Combining this with (
5.147
) concludes the proof of (
2.49
).

Step 4: study of the minimizer

Let us next study the rate function
\mathcal{I}_{\beta,p,\lambda}

from Definition
2.14
.
By (
2.45
) and the fact that there are only a finite number of terms in the second sum, there exists
C>0
depending on
\beta,M
and
p
such that for every
i,j\in[p]
such that
i\neq j
,

\Bigr|\partial^{2}_{\gamma_{i}\gamma_{j}}\mathcal{I}_{\beta,p,\lambda}(\gamma_{1},\ldots,\gamma_{p})-\frac{1}{\gamma_{i}}\mathbf{1}_{i=j}\Bigr|\leq C\lambda^{2}.

Thus, we deduce that for
\lambda
small enough, the function
\mathcal{I}_{\beta,p,\lambda}
is strictly convex. Moreover, for
\lambda
small enough,

(5.148)

\nabla^{2}I_{\beta,p,\lambda}\geq\frac{1}{2}\mathrm{Id}.

Since
\triangle_{p,0}
is a convex set, we deduce that
\mathcal{I}_{\beta,p,\lambda}|_{\triangle_{p,0}}
is strictly convex. Hence, it admits a unique minimizer
\gamma^{*}
. By optimization and the estimate (
2.45
), there exists
\mu\in\mathbb{R}
such that for every
k\in[p]
,

\log\gamma_{k}^{*}=\mu+\log\tilde{m}_{k}+\alpha_{k},

where
|\alpha_{k}|\leq C\lambda^{2}
, for some
C>0
depending on
\beta
,
p
and
M
. Hence, for every
k\in[p]
,

\gamma_{k}^{*}=\frac{\tilde{m}_{k}e^{\alpha_{k}}}{\sum_{i=1}^{p}\tilde{m}_{i}e^{\alpha_{i}}}.

Using that
\tilde{m}_{1}=1
, we thus conclude that there exists
C>0
depending on
\beta,p
and
M
such that for every
i\in\{2,\ldots,p\}
,

\frac{1}{C}\lambda^{2(i-1)}\leq\gamma_{i}^{*}\leq C\lambda^{2(i-1)}.

This proves (
2.50
).

Step 5: proof of (
2.51
)

By (
2.49
),

\log\mathbb{P}_{N,\beta}^{\lambda}\left(\mathcal{N}_{i}\leq\frac{1}{C_{0}}\lambda^{2(i-1)}\right)\leq-N\left(\inf_{\gamma\in\triangle_{p,0}:\gamma_{i}\leq\frac{1}{C_{0}}\lambda^{2(i-1)}}\mathcal{I}_{\beta,p,\lambda}-\inf_{\triangle_{p,0}}\mathcal{I}_{\beta,p,\lambda}\right)+O(N\delta_{\beta,\lambda}).

Thus, by (
5.148
) and (
2.50
), we get that for
C_{0}
large enough, there exists
C>0
depending on
\beta
,
p
and
M
such that

(5.149)

\log\mathbb{P}_{N,\beta}^{\lambda}\left(\mathcal{N}_{i}\leq\frac{1}{C_{0}}\lambda^{2(i-1)}\right)\leq-NC\lambda^{2(i-1)}+O(N\delta_{\beta,\lambda}).

Similarly, for
C_{0}
large enough and
\varepsilon_{0}
small enough, there exists
C>0
depending on
\beta
,
p
and
M
such that

\log\mathbb{P}_{N,\beta}^{\lambda}(\mathcal{N}_{i}\geq C_{0}\lambda^{2(i-1)},\gamma_{i}\leq\varepsilon_{0}^{-\alpha(\beta)})\leq-NC\lambda^{2(i-1)}+O(N\delta_{\beta,\lambda}).

Together with the tail estimate (
5.145
), this shows (by taking
\varepsilon_{0}
small enough) that for
C_{0}
large enough, there exists
C>0
depending on
\beta
,
p
and
M
such that

\log\mathbb{P}_{N,\beta}^{\lambda}(\mathcal{N}_{i}\geq C_{0}\lambda^{2(i-1)})\leq-NC\lambda^{2(i-1)}+O(N\delta_{\beta,\lambda}).

Together with (
5.149
), this concludes the proof of (
2.51
).
∎

5.17.
Reduction to small clusters in the multipole expansion

We now turn to the proof of Theorem
1
.

Our aim is to prove that

(5.150)

\log\frac{1}{(NC_{\beta,\lambda,\varepsilon_{0}})^{N}}\int_{\sigma_{N}=\mathrm{Id}}e^{-\beta\mathsf{F}_{\lambda}(\vec{X}_{N},\vec{Y}_{N})}\mathrm{d}\vec{X}_{N}\mathrm{d}\vec{Y}_{N}=\sum_{n=0}^{\infty}\frac{1}{n!}\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}V_{1},\ldots,V_{n}\subset[N]\\
\forall i,|V_{i}|\leq p(\beta)\\
\mathrm{disjoint}\end{subarray}}\mathsf{K}_{\beta,\lambda}^{\mathrm{dip}}(V_{1})\cdots\mathsf{K}_{\beta,\lambda}^{\mathrm{dip}}(V_{n})\\
+O(N\delta_{\beta,\lambda}).

We first perform the multipole-based cluster expansion of Section
4.6
but show that we can reduce to connected graphs and multipoles that have total size
\leq p(\beta)
, up to a well-controlled error.

Definition 5.43
.

Let
p\in\mathbb{N}^{*}
.

(1)

We let
G_{p}([N])
be the set of edges
E
on
[N]
such that the connected components of
([N],E)
all have cardinality less than
p
.

(2)

For every
\pi\in\mathbf{\Pi}([N])
, we let
G_{p}([N],\pi)
be the set of edges
E\subset\mathcal{E}^{\mathrm{inter}}(\pi)
on
[N]
such that connected components relative to
\pi
(see Definition
4.9
) of
([N],E)
all have cardinality less than
p
.

(3)

We denote by
\mathbf{\Pi}_{p}([N])
the set of partitions of
[N]
such that every element is of cardinality less than
p
.

For every partition
\pi
of
[N]
and
\varepsilon_{0}\in(0,1)
, let us define

(5.151)

Z_{\varepsilon_{0}}(\pi)\coloneqq\sum_{E\in G_{p}([N],\pi)}\int_{(\Lambda^{2})^{N}}\prod_{ij\in E}f_{ij}^{v}\prod_{ij\in\mathcal{E}^{\mathrm{inter}}(\pi)}\mathbf{1}_{\mathcal{B}_{ij}^{c}}\prod_{S\in\pi}\mathbf{1}_{\mathcal{B}_{S}}\prod_{ij\in\mathcal{E}^{\mathrm{intra}}(\pi)}e^{-\beta v_{ij}}\mathbf{1}_{\mathcal{A}_{ij}}\\
\times\prod_{i=1}^{N}e^{\beta\mathsf{g}_{\lambda}(x_{i}-y_{i})}\mathbf{1}_{|x_{i}-y_{i}|\leq\varepsilon_{0}\bar{R}_{\beta,\lambda}}\mathrm{d}x_{i}\mathrm{d}y_{i}.

Proposition 5.44
.

Let
\varepsilon_{0}\in(0,1)
. Recall
Z_{\varepsilon_{0}}(\pi)
from (
5.151
). Recall
p(\beta)
from Definition
2.7
. We have

(5.152)

\log\int_{\sigma_{N}=\mathrm{Id}}e^{-\beta\mathsf{F}_{\lambda}(\vec{X}_{N},\vec{Y}_{N})}\mathrm{d}\vec{X}_{N}\mathrm{d}\vec{Y}_{N}=\log\sum_{\pi\in\mathbf{\Pi}_{p(\beta)}([N])}Z_{\varepsilon_{0}}(\pi)+O_{\beta,p(\beta),M,\varepsilon_{0}}\left(N\delta_{\beta,\lambda}\right).

Proof.

Denote
p=p(\beta)
.
As a direct corollary of Proposition
4.57
and Proposition
5.42
, we obtain

(5.153)

\log\int_{\sigma_{N}=\mathrm{Id}}e^{-\beta\mathsf{F}_{\lambda}(\vec{X}_{N},\vec{Y}_{N})}\mathrm{d}\vec{X}_{N}\mathrm{d}\vec{Y}_{N}=N\log N+N\log(\lambda^{2-\beta}\mathcal{Z}_{\beta})-N\inf\mathcal{I}_{\beta,p,\lambda}+O_{\beta,p,M,\varepsilon_{0}}\left(N\delta_{\beta,\lambda}\right).

We now prove that

(5.154)

\log\sum_{\pi\in\mathbf{\Pi}_{p}([N])}Z_{\varepsilon_{0}}(\pi)=N\log N+N\log(\lambda^{2-\beta}\mathcal{Z}_{\beta})-N\inf I_{\beta,p,\lambda}+O_{\beta,p,M,\varepsilon_{0}}\left(N\delta_{\beta,\lambda}\right),

which will conclude the proof of the proposition. Let
\mathbf{\Pi}(n_{1},\ldots,n_{p})([N])
be the set of partitions of
[N]
with
n_{i}
elements of cardinality
i
for every
i=1,\ldots,p
. Fix
n_{1},\ldots,n_{p}\geq 0
such that
n_{1}+2n_{2}+\cdots+pn_{p}=N
and let
\pi\in\mathbf{\Pi}(n_{1},\ldots,n_{p})([N])
. By (
4.25
),

\sum_{\pi^{\prime}\in\mathbf{\Pi}(n_{1},\ldots,n_{p})([N])}Z_{\varepsilon_{0}}(\pi^{\prime})=\frac{N!}{1^{n_{1}}(2!)^{n_{2}}\cdots(p!)^{n_{p}}n_{1}!\cdots n_{p}!}Z_{\varepsilon_{0}}(\pi).

Recall the activities
\mathsf{M}_{\varepsilon_{0}}^{0}
from Definition
4.12
and
\mathsf{K}_{\varepsilon_{0}}^{0}
from Definition
4.14
. Proceeding as in the proof of Lemma
4.16
, we get

(5.155)

Z_{\varepsilon_{0}}(\pi)=(NC_{\beta,\lambda,\varepsilon_{0}})^{N}\mathsf{M}_{\varepsilon_{0}}^{0}(\pi)\sum_{n=0}^{\infty}\frac{1}{n!}\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}X_{1},\ldots,X_{n}\subset\pi:\\
\forall i,|V_{X_{i}}|\leq p\\
\mathrm{disjoint}\end{subarray}}\mathsf{K}_{\varepsilon_{0}}^{0}(X_{1})\cdots\mathsf{K}_{\varepsilon_{0}}^{0}(X_{n}).

For every
k=1,\ldots,p
, set
\gamma_{k}\coloneqq\frac{n_{k}}{N}
. Arguing as in (
4.148
), we have

\log\sum_{\pi^{\prime}\in\mathbf{\Pi}(n_{1},\ldots,n_{p})([N])}Z_{\varepsilon_{0}}(\pi^{\prime})=N\log(NC_{\beta,\lambda,\varepsilon_{0}})-N\sum_{k=1}^{p}\gamma_{k}(\log k!+\log\gamma_{k}-1-\log\mathsf{m}_{\beta,\lambda}(k))\\
+\log\sum_{n=0}^{\infty}\frac{1}{n!}\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}X_{1},\ldots,X_{n}\subset\pi:\\
\forall i,|V_{X_{i}}|\leq p\\
\mathrm{disjoint}\end{subarray}}\mathsf{K}_{\varepsilon_{0}}^{0}(X_{1})\cdots\mathsf{K}_{\varepsilon_{0}}^{0}(X_{n})+O_{\beta,p,M,\varepsilon_{0}}\left(N\delta_{\beta,\lambda}\right).

By the definition of
\mathcal{I}_{\beta,p,\lambda}
(see Definition
2.14
), it therefore remains to show that

(5.156)

\log\sum_{n=0}^{+\infty}\frac{1}{n!}\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}X_{1},\ldots,X_{n}\in\mathcal{P}(\pi)\\
\mathrm{disjoint}\end{subarray}}\mathsf{K}_{\varepsilon_{0}}^{0}(X_{1})\cdots\mathsf{K}_{\varepsilon_{0}}^{0}(X_{n})\\
=-N\sum_{n=1}^{\infty}\frac{(-1)^{n}}{n!}\sum_{m_{1},\ldots,m_{p}\in\mathbb{N}}\prod_{i=1}^{p}\frac{\gamma_{i}^{m_{i}}}{m_{i}!}\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}(X_{1},\ldots,X_{n})\\
\in\mathrm{Htrees}_{n}(Y(m_{1},\ldots,m_{p}))\\
X_{1}\cup\cdots\cup X_{n}=Y(m_{1},\ldots,m_{p})\end{subarray}}\prod_{i=1}^{p}\mathsf{k}_{\beta,\lambda}^{\mathrm{mult}}((\#_{k}X_{i})_{k})\mathbf{1}_{|V_{X_{i}}|\leq p}\\
+O_{\beta,p,M,\varepsilon_{0}}\left(N\delta_{\beta,\lambda}\right).

Since the clusters in (
5.155
) are of bounded cardinality, the absolute convergence of the cluster series

\sum_{n=1}^{\infty}\frac{1}{n!}\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}X_{1},\ldots,X_{n}\subset\pi:\\
\forall i,|V_{X_{i}}|\leq p\\
\mathrm{connected}\end{subarray}}\mathsf{K}_{\varepsilon_{0}}^{0}(X_{1})\cdots\mathsf{K}_{\varepsilon_{0}}^{0}(X_{n})\mathrm{I}(G(X_{1},\ldots,X_{n}))

is clear. Hence, applying Lemma
4.6
, we have

\log\sum_{n=0}^{\infty}\frac{1}{n!}\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}X_{1},\ldots,X_{n}\subset\pi:\\
\forall i,|V_{X_{i}}|\leq p\\
\mathrm{disjoint}\end{subarray}}\mathsf{K}_{\varepsilon_{0}}^{0}(X_{1})\cdots\mathsf{K}_{\varepsilon_{0}}^{0}(X_{n})=\sum_{n=1}^{\infty}\frac{1}{n!}\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}X_{1},\ldots,X_{n}\subset\pi:\\
\forall i,|V_{X_{i}}|\leq p\\
\mathrm{connected}\end{subarray}}\mathsf{K}_{\varepsilon_{0}}^{0}(X_{1})\cdots\mathsf{K}_{\varepsilon_{0}}^{0}(X_{n})\mathrm{I}(G(X_{1},\ldots,X_{n})).

Using Proposition
4.20
to replace
\mathsf{K}_{\varepsilon_{0}}^{0}
by
\mathsf{K}_{\infty}^{0}
up to a small error and Corollary
4.56
to replace
\mathsf{K}_{\infty}^{0}
by
\mathsf{K}_{\beta,\lambda}^{\mathrm{mult}}
, we thus deduce from estimate (
4.158
) that

\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}X_{1},\ldots,X_{n}\in\mathcal{P}(\pi)\\
\mathrm{connected}\end{subarray}}\mathsf{K}_{\varepsilon_{0}}^{0}(X_{1})\mathbf{1}_{|V_{X_{1}}|\leq p}\cdots\mathsf{K}_{\varepsilon_{0}}^{0}(X_{n})\mathbf{1}_{|V_{X_{n}}|\leq p}\mathrm{I}(G(X_{1},\ldots,X_{n}))\\
=N(-1)^{n-1}\sum_{m_{1},\ldots,m_{p}\in\mathbb{N}}\prod_{i=1}^{p}\frac{\gamma_{i}^{m_{i}}}{m_{i}!}\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}(X_{1},\ldots,X_{n})\\
\in\mathrm{Htrees}_{n}(Y(m_{1},\ldots,m_{p}))\\
X_{1}\cup\cdots\cup X_{n}=Y(m_{1},\ldots,m_{p})\end{subarray}}\prod_{i=1}^{p}\mathsf{k}_{\beta,\lambda}^{\mathrm{mult}}((\#_{k}X_{i})_{k})\mathbf{1}_{|V_{X_{i}}|\leq p}+O_{\beta,p,M,\varepsilon_{0}}(N\delta_{\beta,\lambda}),

which proves the claim (
5.156
) and therefore the estimate (
5.154
). Combining (
5.153
) and (
5.154
) establishes (
5.152
).
∎

5.18.
Switching to a dipole expansion

We now apply a dipole-based cluster expansion to the right-hand side of (
5.152
), introducing the dipole activity defined below. This activity is a set function on subsets of
[N]
(rather than on subpartitions of a fixed partition) and it encodes corrections to the i.i.d. dipole model rather than to the hierarchical multipole model.

Definition 5.45

(Auxiliary dipole activity)
.

Let
\beta\in(2,\infty)
and
p\in\mathbb{N}^{*}
. For every
V\subset[N]
, let us define

(5.157)

\mathsf{K}^{\mathrm{aux}}_{\beta,p,\varepsilon_{0}}(V)=\sum_{\pi\in\mathbf{\Pi}_{p}(V)}\sum_{E\in G_{p}(V,\pi)}\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}F\subset\mathcal{E}^{\mathrm{inter}}(\pi):\\
E\cup F\in\mathsf{E}^{\pi}\end{subarray}}\mathbb{E}_{\mu_{\beta,\lambda,\varepsilon_{0}}^{\otimes|V|}}\left[\prod_{ij\in E}f_{ij}^{v}\prod_{ij\in F}(-\mathbf{1}_{\mathcal{B}_{ij}})\prod_{S\in\pi}\mathbf{1}_{\mathcal{B}_{S}}\prod_{ij\in\mathcal{E}^{\mathrm{intra}}(\pi)}e^{-\beta v_{ij}}\mathbf{1}_{\mathcal{A}_{ij}}\right].

Notice that for
|V|\in\{0,1\}
, we have
\mathsf{K}^{\mathrm{aux}}_{\beta,p,\varepsilon_{0}}(V)=0
.

Lemma 5.46
.

Let
\beta\in(2,\infty)
and
p(\beta)
be as in Definition
2.7
. Let
\varepsilon_{0}\in(0,1)
,
Z_{\varepsilon_{0}}(\pi)
be as in (
5.151
) and
\mathsf{K}_{\beta,p(\beta),\varepsilon_{0}}^{\mathrm{aux}}
be as in Definition
5.45
. Then, we have

(5.158)

\frac{1}{(NC_{\beta,\lambda,\varepsilon_{0}})^{N}}\sum_{\pi\in\mathbf{\Pi}_{p(\beta)}([N])}Z_{\varepsilon_{0}}(\pi)=\sum_{n=0}^{\infty}\frac{1}{n!}\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}V_{1},\ldots,V_{n}\subset[N]\\
\mathrm{disjoint}\end{subarray}}\mathsf{K}_{\beta,p(\beta),\varepsilon_{0}}^{\mathrm{aux}}(V_{1})\cdots\mathsf{K}_{\beta,p(\beta),\varepsilon_{0}}^{\mathrm{aux}}(V_{n}).

Moreover, for every
n\geq 0
, we have

(5.159)

\frac{1}{(NC_{\beta,\lambda,\varepsilon_{0}})^{n}}\int e^{-\beta\mathsf{F}_{\lambda}(\vec{X}_{n},\vec{Y}_{n})}\mathbf{1}_{\sigma_{n}=\mathrm{Id}}\prod_{i=1}^{n}\mathbf{1}_{|x_{i}-y_{i}|\leq\varepsilon_{0}\bar{R}_{\beta,\lambda}}\mathrm{d}\vec{X}_{n}\mathrm{d}\vec{Y}_{n}\\
=\sum_{n=0}^{\infty}\frac{1}{n!}\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}V_{1},\ldots,V_{n}\subset[N]\\
\mathrm{disjoint}\end{subarray}}\mathsf{K}_{\beta,\infty,\varepsilon_{0}}^{\mathrm{aux}}(V_{1})\cdots\mathsf{K}_{\beta,\infty,\varepsilon_{0}}^{\mathrm{aux}}(V_{n}).

Proof.

Writing

\prod_{ij\in\mathcal{E}^{\mathrm{inter}}(\pi)}\mathbf{1}_{\mathcal{B}_{ij}^{c}}=\prod_{ij\in\mathcal{E}^{\mathrm{inter}}(\pi)}(1-\mathbf{1}_{\mathcal{B}_{ij}})=\sum_{F\subset\mathcal{E}^{\mathrm{inter}}(\pi)}\prod_{ij\in F}(-\mathbf{1}_{\mathcal{B}_{ij}}),

we have

(5.160)

\sum_{\pi\in\mathbf{\Pi}_{p(\beta)}([N])}Z_{\varepsilon_{0}}(\pi)=\sum_{\pi\in\mathbf{\Pi}_{p(\beta)}([N])}\sum_{E\in G_{p}([N],\pi)}\sum_{F\subset\mathcal{E}^{\mathrm{inter}}(\pi)}\\
\int_{(\Lambda^{2})^{N}}\prod_{ij\in F}(-\mathbf{1}_{\mathcal{B}_{ij}})\prod_{ij\in E}f_{ij}^{v}\prod_{S\in\pi}\mathbf{1}_{\mathcal{B}_{S}}\prod_{ij\in\mathcal{E}^{\mathrm{intra}}(\pi)}e^{-\beta v_{ij}}\mathbf{1}_{\mathcal{A}_{ij}}\prod_{i=1}^{N}e^{\beta\mathsf{g}_{\lambda}(x_{i}-y_{i})}\mathbf{1}_{|x_{i}-y_{i}|\leq\varepsilon_{0}\bar{R}_{\beta,\lambda}}\mathrm{d}x_{i}\mathrm{d}y_{i}

We let
\pi^{\prime}
be the connected components relative to
\pi
of the graph
(V_{X},E\cup F)
. Notice
\pi^{\prime}
is a coarsening of
\pi
. Resuming (
5.160
) according to
\pi^{\prime}
yields the representation (
5.158
).

Splitting the integral in the left-hand side of (
5.159
) and proceeding as in the proof of (
5.158
) yields (
5.159
).
∎

We now claim that the series in the right-hand side of (
5.158
) is absolutely convergent and that we can reduce to clusters of cardinality smaller than
p(\beta)
.

Proposition 5.47

(Absolute convergence of the auxiliary cluster series)
.

Let
\beta\in(2,\infty)
and
p(\beta)
be as in Definition
2.7
. Let
\mathsf{K}_{\beta,p(\beta),\varepsilon_{0}}^{\mathrm{aux}}
be as in (
5.157
).

Then, for
\varepsilon_{0}
small enough with respect to
\beta
,
p(\beta)
and
M
, and
\lambda
small enough, there exists
C>0
depending only on
\beta,M,p(\beta)
and
\varepsilon_{0}
such that

(5.161)

\log\sum_{n=0}^{\infty}\frac{1}{n!}\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}V_{1},\ldots,V_{n}\subset[N]\\
\mathrm{disjoint}\end{subarray}}\mathsf{K}_{\beta,p(\beta),\varepsilon_{0}}^{\mathrm{aux}}(V_{1})\cdots\mathsf{K}_{\beta,p(\beta),\varepsilon_{0}}^{\mathrm{aux}}(V_{n})\\
=\sum_{n=1}^{\infty}\frac{1}{n!}\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}V_{1},\ldots,V_{n}\subset[N]\\
\forall i,|V_{i}|\leq p(\beta)\\
\mathrm{connected}\end{subarray}}\mathsf{K}_{\beta,p(\beta),\varepsilon_{0}}^{\mathrm{aux}}(V_{1})\cdots\mathsf{K}_{\beta,p(\beta),\varepsilon_{0}}^{\mathrm{aux}}(V_{n})\mathrm{I}(G(V_{1},\ldots,V_{n}))+O_{\beta,p(\beta),\varepsilon_{0}}\left(N\delta_{\beta,\lambda}\right).

Proof.

Denote
p=p(\beta)
. We need to prove that the series on the right-hand side of (
5.161
) is absolutely convergent and that clusters of size larger than
p(\beta)
contribute only negligibly. This is easy to prove using that by Definition
5.157
, the activity
\mathsf{K}_{\beta,p(\beta),\varepsilon_{0}}^{\mathrm{aux}}(V)
is obtained by summing over
E\in G_{p(\beta)}(V)
and
\pi\in\mathbf{\Pi}_{p(\beta)}(V)
(hence the possibly divergent terms have here bounded cardinality, so that the absolute convergence is easy).

The proof relies on ingredients already used in the proof of Proposition
4.23
, so we only sketch the argument.

Step 1: splitting the Mayer bond into an odd and an even part

Let
V\subset[N]
. Write
f_{ij}^{v}=a_{ij}^{v}+b_{ij}^{v}
with
a_{ij}^{v}
and
b_{ij}^{v}
as in Definition
4.24
. This gives

\mathsf{K}_{\beta,p,\varepsilon_{0}}^{\mathrm{aux}}(V)=\sum_{\pi\in\mathbf{\Pi}_{p}(V)}\sum_{E_{1}\subset\mathcal{E}^{\mathrm{inter}}(\pi)}\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}E_{2}\subset\mathcal{E}^{\mathrm{inter}}(\pi):\\
E_{1}\cap E_{2}=\emptyset\\
E_{1}\cup E_{2}\in G_{p}(V,\pi)\end{subarray}}\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}F\subset\mathcal{E}^{\mathrm{inter}}(\pi):\\
E_{1}\cup E_{2}\cup F\in\mathsf{E}^{\pi}\end{subarray}}\\
\mathbb{E}_{\mu_{\beta,\lambda,\varepsilon_{0}}^{\otimes|V|}}\left[\prod_{ij\in E_{1}}a_{ij}^{v}\prod_{ij\in E_{2}}b_{ij}^{v}\prod_{ij\in F}(-\mathbf{1}_{\mathcal{B}_{ij}})\prod_{S\in\pi}\mathbf{1}_{\mathcal{B}_{S}}\prod_{ij\in\mathcal{E}^{\mathrm{intra}}(\pi)}e^{-\beta v_{ij}}\mathbf{1}_{\mathcal{A}_{ij}}\right]

Moreover, by the parity argument of Lemma
4.28
,

\mathsf{K}_{\beta,p,\varepsilon_{0}}^{\mathrm{aux}}(V)=\sum_{\pi\in\mathbf{\Pi}_{p}(V)}\sum_{E_{1}\in\mathrm{Eul}^{\pi}}\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}E_{2}\subset\mathcal{E}^{\mathrm{inter}}(\pi):\\
E_{1}\cap E_{2}=\emptyset\\
E_{1}\cup E_{2}\in G_{p}(V,\pi)\end{subarray}}\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}F\subset\mathcal{E}^{\mathrm{inter}}(\pi):\\
E_{1}\cup E_{2}\cup F\in\mathsf{E}^{\pi}\end{subarray}}\\
\mathbb{E}_{\mu_{\beta,\lambda,\varepsilon_{0}}^{\otimes|V|}}\left[\prod_{ij\in E_{1}}a_{ij}^{v}\prod_{ij\in E_{2}}b_{ij}^{v}\prod_{ij\in F}(-\mathbf{1}_{\mathcal{B}_{ij}})\prod_{S\in\pi}\mathbf{1}_{\mathcal{B}_{S}}\prod_{ij\in\mathcal{E}^{\mathrm{intra}}(\pi)}e^{-\beta v_{ij}}\mathbf{1}_{\mathcal{A}_{ij}}\right]

We now resum according to the connected components
X_{1},\ldots,X_{n}
of
(V,E_{1}\cup E_{2})
relative to
\pi
. This yields

\mathsf{K}_{\beta,p,\varepsilon_{0}}^{\mathrm{aux}}(V)=\sum_{\pi\in\mathbf{\Pi}_{p}(V)}\sum_{n=1}^{\infty}\frac{1}{n!}\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}X_{1},\ldots,X_{n}\subset\pi\\
\mathrm{disjoint}\end{subarray}}\Biggl(\prod_{l=1}^{n}\sum_{E_{1,l}\in\mathrm{Eul}^{X_{l}}}\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}E_{2,l}\subset\mathcal{E}^{\mathrm{inter}}(X_{l}):\\
E_{1,l}\cap E_{2,l}=\emptyset\\
E_{1,l}\cup E_{2,l}\in G_{p}(V,X_{l})\cap\mathsf{E}^{X_{l}}\end{subarray}}\Biggr)\sum_{F\in\mathsf{E}^{\mathrm{Coa}_{\pi}(X_{1},\ldots,X_{n})}}\\
\mathbb{E}_{\mu_{\beta,\lambda,\varepsilon_{0}}^{\otimes|V|}}\left[\prod_{ij\in\cup_{l}E_{1,l}}a_{ij}^{v}\prod_{ij\in\cup_{l}E_{2,l}}b_{ij}^{v}\prod_{ij\in F}(-\mathbf{1}_{\mathcal{B}_{ij}})\prod_{S\in\pi}\mathbf{1}_{\mathcal{B}_{S}}\prod_{ij\in\mathcal{E}^{\mathrm{intra}}(\pi)}e^{-\beta v_{ij}}\mathbf{1}_{\mathcal{A}_{ij}}\prod_{l=1}^{n}\prod_{ij\in\mathcal{E}^{\mathrm{intra}}(X_{l})}\mathbf{1}_{\mathcal{B}_{ij}^{c}}\right].

Step 2: Penrose resummation

Fix
\pi\in\mathbf{\Pi}_{p}(V)
and
X_{1},\ldots,X_{n}\subset\pi
. As in (
4.94
), we have

(5.162)

\left|\sum_{F\in\mathsf{E}^{\mathrm{Coa}_{\pi}(X_{1},\ldots,X_{n})}}\prod_{ij\in F}(-\mathbf{1}_{\mathcal{B}_{ij}^{c}})\right|\leq\sum_{T\in\mathsf{T}^{\mathrm{Coa}_{\pi}(X_{1},\ldots,X_{n})}}\prod_{ij\in T}\mathbf{1}_{\mathcal{B}_{ij}}.

Recall that
|a_{ij}^{v}|\mathbf{1}_{\mathcal{B}_{ij}^{c}}\leq Ca_{ij}^{\mathrm{abs}}
and
\mathbf{1}_{\mathcal{B}_{ij}}+|b_{ij}^{v}|\mathbf{1}_{\mathcal{B}_{ij}^{c}}\leq Cb_{ij}^{\mathrm{abs}}
, where
a_{ij}^{\mathrm{abs}}
and
b_{ij}^{\mathrm{abs}}
are as in Definition
4.24
. Since
E_{1,l}\cup E_{2,l}\in G_{p}(V_{X_{l}},X_{l})
, we have
|V_{X_{l}}|\leq p
. Therefore, using this, (
5.162
) and resumming according to the connected components relative to
\pi
of
(V,\cup_{l}E_{1,l})
, we get that there exists
C>0
depending on
\beta,M
and
p
such that

(5.163)

|\mathsf{K}_{\beta,p,\varepsilon_{0}}^{\mathrm{aux}}(V)|\leq C^{|V|}\sum_{\pi\in\mathbf{\Pi}_{p}(V)}\sum_{n=1}^{\infty}\frac{1}{n!}\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}X_{1},\ldots,X_{n}\subset\pi\\
\mathrm{disjoint}\\
\forall i,|V_{X_{i}}|\leq p\end{subarray}}\left(\prod_{l=1}^{n}\sum_{E_{l}\in\mathrm{Eul}_{\mathrm{c}}^{X_{l}}}\right)\sum_{T^{b}\in\mathsf{T}^{\mathrm{Coa}_{\pi}(X_{1},\ldots,X_{n})}}\\
\mathbb{E}_{\mu_{\beta,\lambda,\varepsilon_{0}}^{\otimes|V|}}\left[\prod_{ij\in\cup_{l}E_{l}}a_{ij}^{\mathrm{abs}}\prod_{ij\in T^{b}}b_{ij}^{\mathrm{abs}}\prod_{S\in\pi}\mathbf{1}_{\mathcal{B}_{S}}\right].

Step 3: peeling of the graph with odd weights

Since
|V_{X_{l}}|\leq p
, recalling
\mathrm{Peeled}_{X_{l}}
from Definition
4.34
, we obtain that there exists
C>0
depending on
\beta,p
and
M
such that

\mathbb{E}_{\mu_{\beta,\lambda,\varepsilon_{0}}^{\otimes|V|}}\left[\prod_{ij\in\cup_{l}E_{l}}a_{ij}^{\mathrm{abs}}\prod_{ij\in T^{b}}b_{ij}^{\mathrm{abs}}\prod_{S\in\pi}\mathbf{1}_{\mathcal{B}_{S}}\right]\leq C^{|V|}\mathbb{E}_{\mu_{\beta,\lambda,\varepsilon_{0}}^{\otimes|V|}}\left[\prod_{ij\in\cup_{l}\mathrm{Peeled}_{X_{l}}(E_{l})}a_{ij}^{\mathrm{abs}}\prod_{ij\in T^{b}}b_{ij}^{\mathrm{abs}}\prod_{S\in\pi}\mathbf{1}_{\mathcal{B}_{S}}\right].

Thus, by Corollary
4.43
and (
4.71
), there exists
C>0
depending on
\beta,p
and
M
such that

\mathbb{E}_{\mu_{\beta,\lambda,\varepsilon_{0}}^{\otimes|V|}}\left[\prod_{ij\in\cup_{l}E_{l}}a_{ij}^{\mathrm{abs}}\prod_{ij\in T^{b}}b_{ij}^{\mathrm{abs}}\prod_{S\in\pi}\mathbf{1}_{\mathcal{B}_{S}}\right]\\
\leq C^{|V|}\mathbb{E}_{\mu_{\beta,\lambda,\varepsilon_{0}}^{\otimes|V|}}\left[\prod_{l=1}^{n}\prod_{\begin{subarray}{c}S\in X_{l}\\
S\neq\hat{S}_{l}\end{subarray}}r_{S}^{2}\prod_{ij\in\cup_{l}\mathcal{T}^{X_{l}}(\cdot,E_{l})}\frac{1}{d_{ij}^{2}}\mathbf{1}_{d_{ij}\leq 16\varepsilon_{0}\bar{R}_{\beta,\lambda}}\mathbf{1}_{\mathcal{B}_{ij}^{c}}\prod_{ij\in T^{b}}b_{ij}^{\mathrm{abs}}\prod_{S\in\pi}\mathbf{1}_{\mathcal{B}_{S}}\min\left(\frac{\max_{i\in V}r_{i}}{\max_{e\in\cup_{l}\mathcal{T}^{X_{l}}(\cdot,E_{l})}d_{e}},1\right)^{2}\right],

where for every
l\in\{1,\ldots,n\}
,
\hat{S}_{l}
stands for the
S\in X_{l}
such that
r_{S}
is maximal.

Therefore, inserting the estimate (
A.20
) of Lemma
A.4
and the normalization for
\mu_{\beta,\lambda,\varepsilon_{0}}
(
4.8
), we get that there exists
C>0
depending on
\beta,M
and
p
such that

(5.164)

\mathbb{E}_{\mu_{\beta,\lambda,\varepsilon_{0}}^{\otimes|V|}}\left[\prod_{ij\in\cup_{l}E_{l}}a_{ij}^{\mathrm{abs}}\prod_{ij\in T^{b}}b_{ij}^{\mathrm{abs}}\prod_{S\in\pi}\mathbf{1}_{\mathcal{B}_{S}}\right]\leq C^{|V|}N^{1-|V|}\gamma_{\beta,\lambda,|V|}.

Step 4: conclusion

Inserting (
5.164
) into (
5.163
), using Cayley’s formula, and proceeding as in the proof of Lemma
4.54
(see Step 2) shows that there exists
C>0
depending on
\beta,M
and
p
such that

|\mathsf{K}_{\beta,p,\varepsilon_{0}}^{\mathrm{aux}}(V)|\leq C^{|V|}|V|^{|V|}N^{1-|V|}\gamma_{\beta,\lambda,|V|}.

Proceeding as in the proof of Proposition
4.23
, we obtain that for
\varepsilon_{0}
small enough with respect to
\beta
,
p(\beta)
and
M
, and
\lambda
small enough, there exists
C>0
depending only on
\beta,M,p(\beta)
and
\varepsilon_{0}
such that

\sum_{n=1}^{\infty}\frac{1}{n!}\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}V_{1},\ldots,V_{n}\subset[N]\\
\forall i,|V_{i}|\leq p(\beta)\\
\mathrm{connected}\\
\exists i:|V_{i}|>p\end{subarray}}|\mathsf{K}_{\beta,p,\varepsilon_{0}}^{\mathrm{aux}}(V_{1})\cdots\mathsf{K}_{\beta,p,\varepsilon_{0}}^{\mathrm{aux}}(V_{n})\mathrm{I}(G(V_{1},\ldots,V_{n}))|\leq CN\delta_{\beta,\lambda}.

This concludes the proof of the proposition.
∎

5.19.
Möbius inversion and equality of the dipole activities

We argue as in Section
4.19
in order to equate the auxiliary dipole activity of Definition
5.45
to the simple dipole activity of Definition
4.17
(for clusters of size smaller than
p(\beta))
.

We begin by stating a direct consequence of Möbius inversion (Lemma
4.55
) on the uniqueness of cluster expansion.

Lemma 5.48
.

Let
E
be a finite set. Let
f:\mathcal{P}(E)\to\mathbb{R}
. Suppose that there exists
K:\mathcal{P}(E)\to\mathbb{R}
satisfying
K(S)=1
for every
S\in E
and such that for every
S\subset E
,

f(S)=\sum_{\pi\in\mathbf{\Pi}(S)}\prod_{S^{\prime}\in\pi}K(S^{\prime}).

Then
K
is uniquely determined by
f
.

Proof.

For every
\pi\in\mathbf{\Pi}_{\mathrm{sub}}(E)
, introduce

A(\pi)=\prod_{S^{\prime}\in\pi}K(S^{\prime})\quad\text{and}\quad B(\pi)=\prod_{S\in\pi}f(S).

By assumption, for every
S\subset E
,

f(S)=\sum_{\pi\in\mathbf{\Pi}(S)}A(\pi).

Hence, for every
\pi\in\mathbf{\Pi}(E)
,

B(\pi)=\sum_{\sigma\leq\pi}A(\sigma).

Hence, by Möbius inversion (Lemma
4.55
), for every
\pi\in\mathbf{\Pi}(E)
,

A(\pi)=\sum_{\sigma\leq\pi}\mu(\sigma,\pi)B(\sigma),

where
\mu
is the Möbius lattice partition (
4.140
).

Let
S\subset E
. Taking
\pi
to be the partition of
E
with one block
S
and blocks into singletons
\{i\}
for
i\in E\setminus S
and using that
K(S^{\prime})=1
if
|S^{\prime}|=1
, we obtain

A(\pi)=K(S)=\sum_{\sigma\leq\pi}\mu(\sigma,\pi)B(\sigma).

Thus,
K
is uniquely determined by
f
.
∎

Corollary 5.49

(Equality of the dipole activities)
.

Let
\varepsilon_{0}\in(0,1)
and recall
p(\beta)
from Definition
2.7
. Let
\mathsf{K}_{\beta,p(\beta),\varepsilon_{0}}^{\mathrm{aux}}
be as in (
5.157
) and
\mathsf{K}_{\beta,\lambda,\varepsilon_{0}}^{\mathrm{dip}}
be as in Definition
4.17
. Then for every
V\subset[N]
such that
|V|\leq p(\beta)
, we have

\mathsf{K}_{\beta,p(\beta),\varepsilon_{0}}^{\mathrm{aux}}(V)=\mathsf{K}_{\beta,\lambda,\varepsilon_{0}}^{\mathrm{dip}}(V).

Proof.

Fix
n\geq 0
. By Lemma
4.5
,

\{\sigma_{n}[\vec{X}_{n},\vec{Y}_{n}]=\mathrm{Id}\}=\bigcap_{i,j\in[n]:i\neq j}\mathcal{A}_{ij}.

Therefore,

\int e^{-\beta\mathsf{F}_{\lambda}(\vec{X}_{n},\vec{Y}_{n})}\mathbf{1}_{\sigma_{n}=\mathrm{Id}}\prod_{i=1}^{n}\mathbf{1}_{|x_{i}-y_{i}|\leq\varepsilon_{0}\bar{R}_{\beta,\lambda}}\mathrm{d}\vec{X}_{n}\mathrm{d}\vec{Y}_{n}\\
=\int\prod_{i,j\in[n]:i<j}e^{-\beta v_{ij}}\mathbf{1}_{\mathcal{A}_{ij}}\prod_{i=1}^{n}e^{\beta\mathsf{g}_{\lambda}(x_{i}-y_{i})}\mathbf{1}_{|x_{i}-y_{i}|\leq\varepsilon_{0}\bar{R}_{\beta,\lambda}}\mathrm{d}\vec{X}_{n}\mathrm{d}\vec{Y}_{n}.

Expanding each weight into
e^{-\beta v_{ij}}\mathbf{1}_{\mathcal{A}_{ij}}=1+f_{ij}^{v}
yields

\int e^{-\beta\mathsf{F}_{\lambda}(\vec{X}_{n},\vec{Y}_{n})}\mathbf{1}_{\sigma_{n}=\mathrm{Id}}\prod_{i=1}^{n}\mathbf{1}_{|x_{i}-y_{i}|\leq\varepsilon_{0}\bar{R}_{\beta,\lambda}}\mathrm{d}\vec{X}_{n}\mathrm{d}\vec{Y}_{n}=\sum_{E}\int\prod_{ij\in E}f^{v}_{ij}\prod_{i=1}^{n}e^{\beta\mathsf{g}_{\lambda}(x_{i}-y_{i})}\mathbf{1}_{|x_{i}-y_{i}|\leq\varepsilon_{0}\bar{R}_{\beta,\lambda}}\mathrm{d}\vec{X}_{n}\mathrm{d}\vec{Y}_{n},

where the sum is over sets of pairs
E
on
[N]
. Normalizing by
(NC_{\beta,\lambda,\varepsilon_{0}})^{N}
and resumming according to the connected components of
([n],E)
yields

(5.165)

\frac{1}{(NC_{\beta,\lambda,\varepsilon_{0}})^{n}}\int e^{-\beta\mathsf{F}_{\lambda}(\vec{X}_{n},\vec{Y}_{n})}\mathbf{1}_{\sigma_{n}=\mathrm{Id}}\prod_{i=1}^{n}\mathbf{1}_{|x_{i}-y_{i}|\leq\varepsilon_{0}\bar{R}_{\beta,\lambda}}\mathrm{d}\vec{X}_{n}\mathrm{d}\vec{Y}_{n}\\
=\sum_{n=0}^{\infty}\frac{1}{n!}\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}V_{1},\ldots,V_{n}\subset[N]\\
\mathrm{disjoint}\end{subarray}}\mathsf{K}_{\beta,\lambda,\varepsilon_{0}}^{\mathrm{dip}}(V_{1})\cdots\mathsf{K}_{\beta,\lambda,\varepsilon_{0}}^{\mathrm{dip}}(V_{n}).

Now set

\tilde{\mathsf{K}}_{\beta,\lambda,\varepsilon_{0}}^{\mathrm{dip}}(V)=\begin{cases}\mathsf{K}_{\beta,\lambda,\varepsilon_{0}}^{\mathrm{dip}}(V)&\text{if $|V|\geq 2$}\\
1&\text{if $|V|=1$}.\end{cases}

Notice that (
5.165
) can be rewritten as

\frac{1}{(NC_{\beta,\lambda,\varepsilon_{0}})^{n}}\int e^{-\beta\mathsf{F}_{\lambda}(\vec{X}_{n},\vec{Y}_{n})}\mathbf{1}_{\sigma_{n}=\mathrm{Id}}\prod_{i=1}^{n}\mathbf{1}_{|x_{i}-y_{i}|\leq\varepsilon_{0}\bar{R}_{\beta,\lambda}}\mathrm{d}\vec{X}_{n}\mathrm{d}\vec{Y}_{n}=\sum_{\pi\in\mathbf{\Pi}([n])}\prod_{S\in\pi}\tilde{\mathsf{K}}_{\beta,\lambda,\varepsilon_{0}}^{\mathrm{dip}}(S).

Indeed, summing the right-hand side of (
5.165
) over partitions is the same as summing over subpartitions obtained by removing components of cardinality
1
.

On the other hand, by (
5.159
), we have the second expansion

\frac{1}{(NC_{\beta,\lambda,\varepsilon_{0}})^{n}}\int e^{-\beta\mathsf{F}_{\lambda}(\vec{X}_{n},\vec{Y}_{n})}\mathbf{1}_{\sigma_{n}=\mathrm{Id}}\prod_{i=1}^{n}\mathbf{1}_{|x_{i}-y_{i}|\leq\varepsilon_{0}\bar{R}_{\beta,\lambda}}\mathrm{d}\vec{X}_{n}\mathrm{d}\vec{Y}_{n}\\
=\sum_{n=0}^{\infty}\frac{1}{n!}\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}V_{1},\ldots,V_{n}\\
\mathrm{disjoint}\end{subarray}}\mathsf{K}_{\beta,p(\beta),\varepsilon_{0}}^{\mathrm{aux}}(V_{1})\cdots\mathsf{K}_{\beta,p(\beta),\varepsilon_{0}}^{\mathrm{aux}}(V_{n}).

Define

\tilde{\mathsf{K}}_{\beta,p(\beta),\varepsilon_{0}}^{\mathrm{aux}}(V)=\begin{cases}\mathsf{K}_{\beta,p(\beta),\varepsilon_{0}}^{\mathrm{aux}}(V)&\text{if $|V|\geq 2$}\\
1&\text{if $|V|=1$}.\end{cases}

Proceeding as above we get

\frac{1}{(NC_{\beta,\lambda,\varepsilon_{0}})^{n}}\int e^{-\beta\mathsf{F}_{\lambda}(\vec{X}_{n},\vec{Y}_{n})}\mathbf{1}_{\sigma_{n}=\mathrm{Id}}\prod_{i=1}^{n}\mathbf{1}_{|x_{i}-y_{i}|\leq\varepsilon_{0}\bar{R}_{\beta,\lambda}}\mathrm{d}\vec{X}_{n}\mathrm{d}\vec{Y}_{n}=\sum_{\pi\in\mathbf{\Pi}([n])}\prod_{S\in\pi}\mathsf{K}_{\beta,p(\beta),\varepsilon_{0}}^{\mathrm{aux}}(S).

Thus, by Lemma
5.48
,

\tilde{\mathsf{K}}_{\beta,\infty,\varepsilon_{0}}^{\mathrm{aux}}=\tilde{\mathsf{K}}^{\mathrm{dip}}_{\beta,\lambda,\varepsilon_{0}},

which gives

{\mathsf{K}}_{\beta,\infty,\varepsilon_{0}}^{\mathrm{aux}}(V)={\mathsf{K}}^{\mathrm{dip}}_{\beta,\lambda,\varepsilon_{0}}(V)\quad\text{for every $V\subset[N]$ such that $|V|\geq 2$}.

Since
{\mathsf{K}}_{\beta,\infty,\varepsilon_{0}}^{\mathrm{aux}}(V)={\mathsf{K}}^{\mathrm{dip}}_{\beta,\lambda,\varepsilon_{0}}(V)=0
for
|V|\in\{0,1\}
, we deduce that

(5.166)

{\mathsf{K}}_{\beta,\infty,\varepsilon_{0}}^{\mathrm{aux}}(V)\equiv{\mathsf{K}}^{\mathrm{dip}}_{\beta,\lambda,\varepsilon_{0}}(V).

On the other hand, for every
V\subset[N]
such that
|V|\leq p(\beta)
, we have

(5.167)

\mathsf{K}_{\beta,p(\beta),\varepsilon_{0}}^{\mathrm{aux}}(V)=\mathsf{K}_{\beta,\infty,\varepsilon_{0}}^{\mathrm{aux}}(V).

Combining (
5.166
) and (
5.167
) concludes the proof.
∎

5.20.
Proof of the free energy expansion

Proof of Theorem
1
.

Denote
p\coloneqq p(\beta)
. First, notice that (
2.36
) follows from the estimate (
4.40
) in Proposition
4.19
. Let us now prove the expansions (
2.35
) and (
2.37
).

Combining Proposition
5.44
, the expression (
5.158
) in Lemma
5.46
, Proposition
5.47
, and Corollary
5.49
, we get

\log Z_{N,\beta}^{\lambda}=\log(N!)+N\log N+N\log(\lambda^{2-\beta}\mathcal{Z}_{\beta})\\
+\sum_{n=1}^{\infty}\frac{1}{n!}\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}V_{1},\ldots,V_{n}\subset[N]\\
\mathrm{connected}\\
\forall i,|V_{i}|\leq p\end{subarray}}\mathsf{K}_{\beta,\lambda,\varepsilon_{0}}^{\mathrm{dip}}(V_{1})\cdots\mathsf{K}_{\beta,\lambda,\varepsilon_{0}}^{\mathrm{dip}}(V_{n})\mathrm{I}(G(V_{1},\ldots,V_{n}))+O_{\beta,p,\varepsilon_{0}}\left(N\delta_{\beta,\lambda}\right).

Since
p\leq p^{*}(\beta)
, we deduce from Proposition
4.20
(estimate (
4.42
)) that

(5.168)

\log Z_{N,\beta}^{\lambda}=\log N!+N\log N+N\log(\lambda^{2-\beta}\mathcal{Z}_{\beta})\\
+\sum_{n=1}^{\infty}\frac{1}{n!}\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}V_{1},\ldots,V_{n}\subset[N]\\
\mathrm{connected}\\
\forall i,|V_{i}|\leq p\end{subarray}}\mathsf{K}_{\beta,\lambda}^{\mathrm{dip}}(V_{1})\cdots\mathsf{K}_{\beta,\lambda}^{\mathrm{dip}}(V_{n})\mathrm{I}(G(V_{1},\ldots,V_{n}))+O_{\beta,p,\varepsilon_{0}}\left(N\delta_{\beta,\lambda}\right),

where we recall that
\mathsf{K}_{\beta,\lambda}^{\mathrm{dip}}=\mathsf{K}_{\beta,\lambda,\infty}^{\mathrm{dip}}
is as in Definition
2.4
.

Let us expand the right-hand side of (
5.168
). Let
n\geq 1
. First, we show that only hypertrees contribute to the order
N
term. Recall
\mathrm{Htrees}_{n}
from (
2.32
). We first sum according to the cardinality of the union of
V_{1},\ldots,V_{n}
:

(5.169)

\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}V_{1},\ldots,V_{n}\subset[n]\\
\mathrm{connected},\forall i,|V_{i}|\leq p\end{subarray}}\mathsf{K}_{\beta,\lambda}^{\mathrm{dip}}(V_{1})\cdots\mathsf{K}_{\beta,\lambda}^{\mathrm{dip}}(V_{n})\mathrm{I}(G(V_{1},\ldots,V_{n}))\\
=\sum_{k=0}^{\infty}\binom{N}{k}\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}V_{1},\ldots,V_{n}\subset[N]\\
\mathrm{connected},\forall i,|V_{i}|\leq p\\
V_{1}\cup\cdots\cup V_{n}=\{1,\ldots,k\}\end{subarray}}\mathsf{K}_{\beta,\lambda}^{\mathrm{dip}}(V_{1})\cdots\mathsf{K}_{\beta,\lambda}^{\mathrm{dip}}(V_{n})\mathrm{I}(G(V_{1},\ldots,V_{n})).

By Lemma
4.22
, we have

\mathsf{K}_{\beta,\lambda}^{\mathrm{dip}}(V_{1})\cdots\mathsf{K}_{\beta,\lambda}^{\mathrm{dip}}(V_{n})=\prod_{i=1}^{n}N^{1-|V_{i}|}\left(\mathsf{k}_{\beta,\lambda}^{\mathrm{dip}}(|V_{i}|)+o_{N}(1)\right).

Moreover, for
k\leq p
, we can use

\binom{N}{k}\sim\frac{N^{k}}{k!}.

Therefore, the only terms contributing at the limit (after dividing by
N
) are the ones such that

n-\sum_{i=1}^{n}|V_{i}|+|V_{1}\cup\cdots\cup V_{n}|=1.

By induction, one can easily check that this is equivalent to

(V_{1},\ldots,V_{n})\in\mathrm{Htrees}_{n}([k]).

Thus,

\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}V_{1},\ldots,V_{n}\subset[n]\\
\mathrm{connected},\forall i,|V_{i}|\leq p\end{subarray}}\mathsf{K}_{\beta,\lambda}^{\mathrm{dip}}(V_{1})\cdots\mathsf{K}_{\beta,\lambda}^{\mathrm{dip}}(V_{n})\mathrm{I}(G(V_{1},\ldots,V_{n}))\\
=N\sum_{k=0}^{\infty}\frac{1}{k!}\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}(V_{1},\ldots,V_{n})\in\mathrm{Htrees}_{n}([k]):\\
V_{1}\cup\cdots\cup V_{n}=[k]\end{subarray}}\mathsf{K}_{\beta,\lambda}^{\mathrm{dip}}(|V_{1}|)\mathbf{1}_{|V_{1}|\leq p}\cdots\mathsf{K}_{\beta,\lambda}^{\mathrm{dip}}(|V_{n}|)\mathbf{1}_{|V_{n}|\leq p}\mathrm{I}(G(V_{1},\ldots,V_{n}))+o(N).

If
G(V_{1},\ldots,V_{n})
is a tree on
[n]
, then it is well known that
\mathrm{I}(G(V_{1},\ldots,V_{n}))=(-1)^{n-1}
. Inserting this into the last display, we obtain

(5.170)

\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}V_{1},\ldots,V_{n}\subset[n]\\
\mathrm{connected},\forall i,|V_{i}|\leq p\end{subarray}}\mathsf{K}_{\beta,\lambda}^{\mathrm{dip}}(V_{1})\cdots\mathsf{K}_{\beta,\lambda}^{\mathrm{dip}}(V_{n})\mathrm{I}(G(V_{1},\ldots,V_{n}))\\
=N(-1)^{n-1}\sum_{k=1}^{\infty}\frac{1}{k!}\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}(V_{1},\ldots,V_{n})\in\mathrm{Htrees}_{n}([k]):\\
V_{1}\cup\cdots\cup V_{n}=[k]\end{subarray}}\mathsf{k}_{\beta,\lambda}^{\mathrm{dip}}(|V_{1}|)\mathbf{1}_{|V_{1}|\leq p}\cdots\mathsf{k}_{\beta,\lambda}^{\mathrm{dip}}(|V_{n}|)\mathbf{1}_{|V_{n}|\leq p}+o(N)\\
=-N\mathrm{f}_{\beta,p,\lambda}+o(N).

Inserting this into (
5.168
) gives

\log Z_{N,\beta}^{\lambda}=\log N!+N\log N+N\log(\lambda^{2-\beta}\mathcal{Z}_{\beta})-N\mathrm{f}_{\beta,p,\lambda}+O_{\beta,p(\beta)}(N\delta_{\beta,\lambda}).

Together with (
5.170
), this proves (
2.35
) and (
2.37
).
∎

Appendix A
Auxiliary estimates

A.1.
Control of the multipole partition function

We start with a couple of preliminary results.

Lemma A.1
.

Let
T=(V,E)
be a tree on
V\coloneqq\{1,\ldots,k\}
. Let
f:\mathbb{R}\to\mathbb{R}^{+}
be an increasing function. Let
r_{1},\ldots,r_{k}\geq 0
be a family of numbers. Consider an ordering of the
r_{i}
’s such that
r_{\phi(1)}\geq\cdots\geq r_{\phi(k)}
. We have

\prod_{ij\in E}f(\min(r_{i},r_{j}))\leq f(r_{\phi(2)})\cdots f(r_{\phi(k)}).

Proof.

The proof is by induction. Assume that the property holds for any tree of size
k
. Let
T=(V,E)
be a tree of size
k+1
. Let
v
be a leaf of
T
and
v^{\prime}
a neighbor of
v
. Let us suppress the edge
vv^{\prime}
from
T
, thus defining a connected subtree
T^{\prime}=(V\setminus\{v\},E\setminus\{vv^{\prime}\})
of size
k
.

Let
i_{0}
be the index of the largest
r_{i}
for
i\in V\setminus v
. Applying the induction hypothesis to
T^{\prime}
allows one to write

(A.1)

\prod_{ij\in E(T^{\prime})}f(\min(r_{i},r_{j}))\leq\prod_{i\in V\setminus\{v,i_{0}\}}f(r_{i}).

Assume first that
v=\phi(1)
. Since
f
is increasing,

f(\min(r_{v},r_{v^{\prime}}))\leq f(r_{v^{\prime}})\leq f(r_{i_{0}}).

Combining this with (
A.1
), we find that the final product contains all terms but
f(r_{\phi(1)})
, which concludes the proof.

Assume next that
v\neq\phi(1)
. Then
i_{0}=\phi(1)
. One may write

f(\min(r_{v},r_{v^{\prime}}))\leq f(r_{v}).

Combined with (
A.1
) this proves the result since all terms but
f(r_{i_{0}})
are present in the final product.
∎

Lemma A.2
.

Let
k
be an integer. For any
\beta>2
, there exists
C>0
depending only on
\beta
,
k
and
\varepsilon_{0}
, such that

(A.2)

\int_{0}^{\infty}\max(r,\lambda)^{(4-\beta)k-3}\mathbf{1}_{r\leq\varepsilon_{0}\bar{R}_{\beta,\lambda}}\mathrm{d}r\leq C\lambda^{(2-\beta)k}\gamma_{\beta,\lambda,k}

where
\gamma_{\beta,\lambda,k}
is as in Definition
4.18
. If moreover,
k\leq p^{*}(\beta)
, there exists
C>0
depending only on
\beta
,
k
and
\varepsilon_{0}
, such that

(A.3)

\int_{0}^{\infty}\max(r,\lambda)^{(4-\beta)k-3}\mathbf{1}_{r\geq\varepsilon_{0}\bar{R}_{\beta,\lambda}}\mathrm{d}r\leq C\lambda^{(2-\beta)k}\bar{R}_{\beta,\lambda}^{-2}.

Proof.

Suppose first that
k\leq p^{*}(\beta)
. Then we have
(4-\beta)k-3<-1
. Thus, there exists a constant
C>0
depending on
\beta
and
k
such that

\int\max(r,\lambda)^{(4-\beta)k-3}\mathbf{1}_{r\leq\varepsilon_{0}\bar{R}_{\beta,\lambda}}\mathrm{d}r\leq CN\lambda^{(4-\beta)k-2}.

Next, let us turn to the case where
k\geq p^{*}(\beta)+1
(which implies
\beta\in(2,4)
). Suppose that
\beta\in(\beta_{p^{*}(\beta)},\beta_{p^{*}(\beta)+1})
, or
\beta=\beta_{p^{*}(\beta)+1}
and
k>p^{*}(\beta)+1
. Then,
(4-\beta)k-3>-1
and therefore, there exists a constant
C>0
depending on
\beta
and
k
such that

\int\max(r,\lambda)^{(4-\beta)k-3}\mathbf{1}_{r\leq\varepsilon_{0}\bar{R}_{\beta,\lambda}}\mathrm{d}r\leq C(\varepsilon_{0}R_{\beta,\lambda})^{(4-\beta)k-2}=C\varepsilon_{0}^{(4-\beta)k-2}\lambda^{(2-\beta)k}R_{\beta,\lambda}^{-2},

since
R_{\beta,\lambda}^{4-\beta}=\lambda^{2-\beta}
.

Suppose finally that
\beta=\beta_{p^{*}(\beta)+1}
and
k=p^{*}(\beta)+1
(which implies
\beta\in(2,4))
. Then
(4-\beta)k-3=\frac{2}{p^{*}(\beta)+1}k-3=-1
. Hence, there exists
C>0
depending on
\beta
and
\varepsilon_{0}
such that

(A.4)

\int\max(r,\lambda)^{(4-\beta)k-3}\mathbf{1}_{r\leq\varepsilon_{0}\bar{R}_{\beta,\lambda}}\mathrm{d}r\leq C|\log\lambda|.

One can notice that

(\beta-2)k=(2-\tfrac{2}{p^{*}(\beta)+1})(p^{*}(\beta)+1)=2p^{*}(\beta),\quad\bar{R}_{\beta,\lambda}^{-2}=\lambda^{2p*(\beta)}.

Thus, we conclude that there exists a constant
C>0
depending on
\beta
,
k
and
\varepsilon_{0}
such that

\int\max(r,\lambda)^{(4-\beta)k-3}\mathbf{1}_{r\leq\varepsilon_{0}\bar{R}_{\beta,\lambda}}\mathrm{d}r\leq C\bar{R}_{\beta,\lambda}^{-2}|\log\lambda|\lambda^{(2-\beta)k}.

Combining these three cases, we get the result (
A.2
).
The proof of (
A.4
) is analogous, distinguishing the cases
\beta\in(2,4)
and
\beta\geq 4
.

∎

Lemma A.3
.

Let
\beta\in(2,+\infty)
and let
p^{*}(\beta)
be as in (
2.30
). Let
S\subset[N]
be such that
|S|\leq p^{*}(\beta)
.
Let
V_{\mathrm{bad}}\subset[N]
and
S\subset[N]
be such that
V_{\mathrm{bad}}\cap S=\emptyset
. Let
Z=(x_{i},y_{i})_{i\in V_{\mathrm{bad}}}
. Recall
I_{\mathrm{good}}^{Z}
from (
5.45
).
For any
L\in\mathbb{R}
and
(w,\tau)\in\{(\tilde{v},\tilde{\tau}),(v^{Z},\tau^{Z})\}
where
\tilde{\tau}=8\varepsilon_{0}\bar{R}_{\beta,\lambda}
as in (
3.59
), and
v^{Z},\tau^{Z}
are as in Definition
5.8
, let

(A.5)

I_{1}^{w,\tau,L,\varepsilon_{0},Z}(S)\coloneqq\int_{(\Lambda^{2})^{|S|}}\prod_{i\in S}\mathbf{1}_{i\in I_{\mathrm{good}}^{Z}}\mathbf{1}_{\mathcal{B}_{S}}\prod_{i,j\in S:i<j}e^{-\beta w_{ij}}\mathbf{1}_{\mathcal{A}_{ij}}\prod_{i\in S}e^{L\frac{|x_{i}-y_{i}|^{2}}{\tau_{i}^{2}}}\\
\times\prod_{i\in S}e^{\beta\mathsf{g}_{\lambda}(x_{i}-y_{i})}\mathbf{1}_{|x_{i}-y_{i}|\leq\varepsilon_{0}\bar{R}_{\beta,\lambda}}\mathrm{d}x_{i}\mathrm{d}y_{i}.

Then, for
\lambda
small enough, there exists a constant
C>0
depending on
\beta
,
M
and
|S|
such that

(A.6)

\frac{1}{C}N\lambda^{(2-\beta)|S|}\lambda^{2(|S|-1)}\leq I_{1}^{w,\tau,L,\varepsilon_{0},Z}(S)\leq CN\lambda^{(2-\beta)|S|}\lambda^{2(|S|-1)}.

Proof.

We first prove the upper bound. Note that on the event
\mathcal{A}_{ij}
, the interaction
w_{ij}
is bounded from below (though not from above). Therefore, using also that
\frac{|x_{i}-y_{i}|}{\tau_{i}}\leq C
, there exists a constant
C>0
depending on
\beta
,
M
and
|S|
such that

I_{1}^{w,\tau,L,\varepsilon_{0},Z}(S)\leq C\int_{(\Lambda^{2})^{|S|}}\mathbf{1}_{\mathcal{B}_{S}}\prod_{i\in V_{X}}e^{\beta\mathsf{g}_{\lambda}(x_{i}-y_{i})}\mathbf{1}_{|x_{i}-y_{i}|\leq\varepsilon_{0}\bar{R}_{\beta,\lambda}}\mathrm{d}x_{i}\mathrm{d}y_{i}.

Recalling the definition of
\mathcal{B}_{S}
from Definition
2.2
, we get

I_{1}^{w,\tau,L,\varepsilon_{0},Z}(S)\leq C\sum_{T\in\mathcal{T}_{c}(S)}\int_{(\Lambda^{2})^{|S|}}\prod_{ij\in T}\mathbf{1}_{\mathcal{B}_{ij}}\prod_{i\in V_{X}}e^{\beta\mathsf{g}_{\lambda}(x_{i}-y_{i})}\mathbf{1}_{|x_{i}-y_{i}|\leq\varepsilon_{0}\bar{R}_{\beta,\lambda}}\mathrm{d}x_{i}\mathrm{d}y_{i},

where we recall that
\mathcal{T}_{c}(S)
stands for the set of collections of edges
T
on
S
such that
(S,T)
is a tree. Hence,

(A.7)

I_{1}^{w,\tau,L,\varepsilon_{0},Z}(S)\leq C\max_{T\in\mathcal{T}_{c}(S)}\int_{(\Lambda^{2})^{|S|}}\prod_{ij\in T}\mathbf{1}_{\mathcal{B}_{ij}}\prod_{i\in V_{X}}e^{\beta\mathsf{g}_{\lambda}(x_{i}-y_{i})}\mathbf{1}_{|x_{i}-y_{i}|\leq\varepsilon_{0}\bar{R}_{\beta,\lambda}}\mathrm{d}x_{i}\mathrm{d}y_{i},

where
C
depends on
|S
—.
Fix
T\in\mathcal{T}_{c}(S)
on
S
. As in the proof of Lemma
5.40
, we now split the phase space according to which points attain the distance
\mathrm{dist}(\{x_{i},y_{i}\},\{x_{j},y_{j}\})
and let

(A.8)

\begin{split}\mathcal{D}_{ij}^{1}&=\{(x_{q},y_{q})_{q\in S}:d_{ij}=|x_{i}-x_{j}|\},\\
\mathcal{D}_{ij}^{2}&=\{(x_{q},y_{q})_{q\in S}:d_{ij}=|x_{i}-y_{j}|\},\\
\mathcal{D}_{ij}^{3}&=\{(x_{q},y_{q})_{q\in S}:d_{ij}=|y_{i}-x_{j}|\},\\
\mathcal{D}_{ij}^{4}&=\{(x_{q},y_{q})_{q\in S}:d_{ij}=|y_{i}-y_{j}|\}.\end{split}

One can write

(A.9)

\int_{(\Lambda^{2})^{|S|}}\prod_{ij\in T}\mathbf{1}_{\mathcal{B}_{ij}}\prod_{i\in V_{X}}e^{\beta\mathsf{g}_{\lambda}(x_{i}-y_{i})}\mathbf{1}_{|x_{i}-y_{i}|\leq\varepsilon_{0}\bar{R}_{\beta,\lambda}}\mathrm{d}x_{i}\mathrm{d}y_{i}\\
\leq\sum_{T^{1},T^{2},T^{3},T^{4}}\int_{(\Lambda^{2})^{|S|}}\prod_{ij\in T}\mathbf{1}_{\mathcal{B}_{ij}}\left(\prod_{l=1}^{4}\prod_{ij\in T^{l}}\mathbf{1}_{\mathcal{D}_{ij}^{l}}\right)\prod_{i\in V_{X}}e^{\beta\mathsf{g}_{\lambda}(x_{i}-y_{i})}\mathbf{1}_{|x_{i}-y_{i}|\leq\varepsilon_{0}\bar{R}_{\beta,\lambda}}\mathrm{d}x_{i}\mathrm{d}y_{i},

where the sum is over trees
T^{1},T^{2},T^{3},T^{4}
such that
T^{1}\sqcup T^{2}\sqcup T^{3}\sqcup T^{4}=T
. Fix some
T^{1},T^{2},T^{3},T^{4}
accordingly. We define the variables

(A.10)

z_{ij}=\begin{cases}x_{i}-x_{j}&\text{if $ij\in T^{1}$}\\
x_{i}-y_{j}&\text{if $ij\in T^{2}$}\\
y_{i}-x_{j}&\text{if $ij\in T^{3}$}\\
y_{i}-y_{j}&\text{if $ij\in T^{4}$}.\end{cases}

Fix some arbitrary
i_{0}\in S
and perform the following change of variables

(A.11)

(x_{i},y_{i})_{i\in S}\mapsto(x_{i_{0}},(z_{ij})_{ij\in T},(\vec{r}_{i})_{i\in S}),

where
\vec{r}_{i}\coloneqq y_{i}-x_{i}
.
Integrating the variables
(z_{ij})_{ij\in T}
and
x_{i_{0}}
and recalling that
d_{ij}\leq M\max(\min(r_{i},r_{j}),\lambda)
on
\mathcal{B}_{ij}
, there exists
C>0
depending on
M
and
|S|
such that

(A.12)

\int_{(\Lambda^{2})^{|S|}}\prod_{ij\in T}\mathbf{1}_{\mathcal{B}_{ij}}\left(\prod_{l=1}^{4}\prod_{ij\in T^{l}}\mathbf{1}_{\mathcal{D}_{ij}^{l}}\right)\prod_{i\in V_{X}}e^{\beta\mathsf{g}_{\lambda}(x_{i}-y_{i})}\mathbf{1}_{|x_{i}-y_{i}|\leq\varepsilon_{0}\bar{R}_{\beta,\lambda}}\mathrm{d}x_{i}\mathrm{d}y_{i}\\
\leq CN\int\prod_{ij\in T}\max(\min(r_{i},r_{j}),\lambda)^{2}\prod_{i\in V_{X}}e^{\beta\mathsf{g}_{\lambda}(r_{i})}\mathbf{1}_{r_{i}\leq\varepsilon_{0}\bar{R}_{\beta,\lambda}}\mathrm{d}\vec{r}_{i}.

Next, we split the phase space according to the index of the largest
r_{i}
in
S
:

(A.13)

\int\prod_{ij\in T}\max(\min(r_{i},r_{j}),\lambda)^{2}\prod_{i\in V_{X}}e^{\beta\mathsf{g}_{\lambda}(r_{i})}\mathbf{1}_{r_{i}\leq\varepsilon_{0}\bar{R}_{\beta,\lambda}}\mathrm{d}\vec{r}_{i}\\
\leq|S|\max_{j_{0}\in S}\int\prod_{ij\in T}\max(\min(r_{i},r_{j}),\lambda)^{2}\prod_{i\in V_{X}}e^{\beta\mathsf{g}_{\lambda}(r_{i})}\mathbf{1}_{r_{i}\leq r_{j_{0}}\leq\varepsilon_{0}\bar{R}_{\beta,\lambda}}\mathrm{d}\vec{r}_{i}.

By Lemma
A.1
,

\prod_{ij\in T}\max(\min(r_{i},r_{j}),\lambda)^{2}\leq\prod_{i\in S:i\neq j_{0}}\max(r_{i},\lambda)^{2}.

Using this and integrating the variables
\vec{r}_{i}
in polar coordinates for
i\neq j_{0}
, we get that there exists
C>0
depending on
\beta
and
|S|
such that

(A.14)

\int\prod_{ij\in T}\max(\min(r_{i},r_{j}),\lambda)^{2}\prod_{i\in V_{X}}e^{\beta\mathsf{g}_{\lambda}(r_{i})}\mathbf{1}_{r_{i}\leq\varepsilon_{0}\bar{R}_{\beta,\lambda}}\mathrm{d}\vec{r}_{i}\leq C\int_{0}^{\infty}\max(r,\lambda)^{(4-\beta)(|S|-1)+(1-\beta)}\mathbf{1}_{r\leq\varepsilon_{0}\bar{R}_{\beta,\lambda}}\mathrm{d}r.

Since
|S|\leq p^{*}(\beta)
,

(4-\beta)(|S|-1)+(1-\beta)=(4-\beta)|S|-3<-1.

Therefore, there exists
C>0
depending on
\beta
and
|S|
such that

(A.15)

\int\prod_{ij\in T}\max(\min(r_{i},r_{j}),\lambda)^{2}\prod_{i\in V_{X}}e^{\beta\mathsf{g}_{\lambda}(r_{i})}\mathbf{1}_{r_{i}\leq\varepsilon_{0}\bar{R}_{\beta,\lambda}}\mathrm{d}\vec{r}_{i}\leq C\lambda^{(4-\beta)|S|-2}.

Combining this with (
A.7
), (
A.9
) and (
A.12
) concludes the proof of the upper bound.

We now turn to the proof of the lower bound. Let
T\in\mathcal{T}_{c}(S)
. Define the good event

\mathcal{C}\coloneqq\bigcap_{i\in V_{X}}\{r_{i}\in(\lambda,2\lambda)\}\cap\bigcap_{ij\in T}\{d_{ij}\in(2\lambda,4\lambda)\}.

We begin by writing

\int_{(\Lambda^{2})^{|S|}}\prod_{i\in S}\mathbf{1}_{i\in I_{\mathrm{good}}^{Z}}\mathbf{1}_{\mathcal{B}_{S}}\prod_{i,j\in S:i<j}e^{-\beta w_{ij}}\mathbf{1}_{\mathcal{A}_{ij}}\prod_{i\in V_{X}}e^{\beta\mathsf{g}_{\lambda}(x_{i}-y_{i})}\mathbf{1}_{|x_{i}-y_{i}|\leq\varepsilon_{0}\bar{R}_{\beta,\lambda}}\mathrm{d}x_{i}\mathrm{d}y_{i}\\
\geq\int_{(\Lambda^{2})^{|S|}}\mathbf{1}_{\mathcal{C}}\prod_{i\in S}\mathbf{1}_{i\in I_{\mathrm{good}}^{Z}}\mathbf{1}_{\mathcal{B}_{S}}\prod_{i,j\in S:i<j}e^{-\beta w_{ij}}\mathbf{1}_{\mathcal{A}_{ij}}\prod_{i\in V_{X}}e^{\beta\mathsf{g}_{\lambda}(x_{i}-y_{i})}\mathbf{1}_{|x_{i}-y_{i}|\leq\varepsilon_{0}\bar{R}_{\beta,\lambda}}\mathrm{d}x_{i}\mathrm{d}y_{i}.

On the event
\mathcal{C}\cap\mathcal{B}_{S}\cap_{i\in S,j\in S}\mathcal{A}_{ij}
, examining the definition of
w
, by scaling, there exists a constant
C>0
depending on
M
and
|S|
such that

\left|\sum_{i,j\in S:i<j}w_{ij}\right|\leq C.

It follows that there exists
C>0
depending on
\beta,M
and
|S|
such that

\int_{(\Lambda^{2})^{|S|}}\prod_{i\in S}\mathbf{1}_{i\in I_{\mathrm{good}}^{Z}}\mathbf{1}_{\mathcal{B}_{S}}\prod_{i,j\in S:i<j}e^{-\beta w_{ij}}\mathbf{1}_{\mathcal{A}_{ij}}\prod_{i\in V_{X}}e^{\beta\mathsf{g}_{\lambda}(x_{i}-y_{i})}\mathbf{1}_{|x_{i}-y_{i}|\leq\varepsilon_{0}\bar{R}_{\beta,\lambda}}\mathrm{d}x_{i}\mathrm{d}y_{i}\\
\geq\frac{1}{C}\int_{(\Lambda^{2})^{|S|}}\mathbf{1}_{\mathcal{C}}\prod_{i\in S}\mathbf{1}_{i\in I_{\mathrm{good}}^{Z}}\mathbf{1}_{\mathcal{B}_{S}}\prod_{i,j\in S:i<j}\mathbf{1}_{\mathcal{A}_{ij}}\prod_{i\in V_{X}}e^{\beta\mathsf{g}_{\lambda}(x_{i}-y_{i})}\mathbf{1}_{|x_{i}-y_{i}|\leq\varepsilon_{0}\bar{R}_{\beta,\lambda}}\mathrm{d}x_{i}\mathrm{d}y_{i}.

Notice that there exists
C>0
depending on
\beta
and
|S|
such that

(A.16)

\int_{(\Lambda^{2})^{|S|}}\mathbf{1}_{\mathcal{C}}\mathbf{1}_{\mathcal{B}_{S}}\prod_{i,j\in S:i<j}\mathbf{1}_{\mathcal{A}_{ij}}\prod_{i\in V_{X}}e^{\beta\mathsf{g}_{\lambda}(x_{i}-y_{i})}\mathbf{1}_{|x_{i}-y_{i}|\leq\varepsilon_{0}\bar{R}_{\beta,\lambda}}\mathrm{d}x_{i}\mathrm{d}y_{i}\geq\frac{N}{C}\lambda^{(4-\beta)|S|-2}.

On the other hand,

\int_{(\Lambda^{2})^{|S|}}\mathbf{1}_{\mathcal{C}}\Bigr(1-\prod_{i\in S}\mathbf{1}_{i\in I_{\mathrm{good}}^{Z}}\Bigr)\mathbf{1}_{\mathcal{B}_{S}}\prod_{i,j\in S:i<j}\mathbf{1}_{\mathcal{A}_{ij}}\prod_{i\in V_{X}}e^{\beta\mathsf{g}_{\lambda}(x_{i}-y_{i})}\mathbf{1}_{|x_{i}-y_{i}|\leq\varepsilon_{0}\bar{R}_{\beta,\lambda}}\\
\leq\sum_{i_{0}\in S}\int_{(\Lambda^{2})^{|S|}}\mathbf{1}_{\mathcal{C}}\mathbf{1}_{i_{0}\notin I_{\mathrm{good}}^{Z}}\mathbf{1}_{\mathcal{B}_{S}}\prod_{i\in V_{X}}e^{\beta\mathsf{g}_{\lambda}(x_{i}-y_{i})}\mathbf{1}_{|x_{i}-y_{i}|\leq\varepsilon_{0}\bar{R}_{\beta,\lambda}}.

Notice that from (
5.45
),

i_{0}\notin I_{\mathrm{good}}^{Z}\Longrightarrow\exists j\in V_{\mathrm{bad}}\text{ such that }d_{i_{0}j}\leq M\max(r_{i_{0}},\lambda).

Therefore, arguing as in (
A.13
)–(
A.14
), there exists
C>0
depending on
\beta
and
|S|
such that

(A.17)

\int_{(\Lambda^{2})^{|S|}}\mathbf{1}_{\mathcal{C}}\Bigr(1-\prod_{i\in S}\mathbf{1}_{i\in I_{\mathrm{good}}^{Z}}\Bigr)\mathbf{1}_{\mathcal{B}_{S}}\prod_{i,j\in S:i<j}\mathbf{1}_{\mathcal{A}_{ij}}\prod_{i\in V_{X}}e^{\beta\mathsf{g}_{\lambda}(x_{i}-y_{i})}\mathbf{1}_{|x_{i}-y_{i}|\leq\varepsilon_{0}\bar{R}_{\beta,\lambda}}\leq C|V_{\mathrm{bad}}|\lambda^{2}\times\lambda^{(4-\beta)k-2}.

Since
|V_{\mathrm{bad}}|\leq N
, assembling (
A.16
) and (
A.17
) concludes the proof of (
A.6
).
∎

A.2.
Integrals for bounded clusters

Lemma A.4
.

Let
\beta\in(2,+\infty)
. Recall
\gamma_{\beta,\lambda,k}
from Definition
4.18
.

Let
V_{\mathrm{bad}}\subset[N]
,
V_{\mathrm{good}}=[N]\backslash V_{\mathrm{bad}}
, and
let
X
be a subpartition of
V_{\mathrm{good}}
such that
V_{\mathrm{bad}}\cap X=\emptyset
. Let
X_{1},\ldots,X_{n}\subset X
be disjoint. Let
T^{b}\in\mathsf{T}^{\mathrm{Coa}_{X}(X_{1},\ldots,X_{n})}
and for every
l\in[n]
, let
T_{l}^{a}\in\mathsf{T}^{X_{l}}
. Set
T^{a}\coloneqq\cup_{l\in[n]}T_{l}^{a}
. Let
Z=(x_{i},y_{i})_{i\in V_{\mathrm{bad}}}
and
d_{i,\mathrm{bad}}
be as in Definition
5.7
.

Define

(A.18)

J\coloneqq\int_{(\Lambda^{2})^{k}}\min\Bigr(\frac{\max_{i\in V_{X}}r_{i}}{\max_{e\in T^{a}}d_{e}},1\Bigr)^{2}\left(\prod_{l=1}^{n}\prod_{S\in X_{l}:S\neq\hat{S}_{l}}r_{S}^{2}\right)\left(\prod_{ij\in T^{a}}\frac{1}{d_{ij}^{2}}\mathbf{1}_{d_{ij}\leq 16\varepsilon_{0}\bar{R}_{\beta,\lambda}}\mathbf{1}_{\mathcal{B}_{ij}^{c}}\right)\prod_{ij\in T^{b}}b_{ij}^{\mathrm{abs}}\\
\times\prod_{S\in X}\mathbf{1}_{\mathcal{B}_{S}}\prod_{i\in V_{X}}e^{\beta\mathsf{g}_{\lambda}(x_{i}-y_{i})}\mathbf{1}_{|x_{i}-y_{i}|\leq\varepsilon_{0}\bar{R}_{\beta,\lambda}}\mathrm{d}x_{i}\mathrm{d}y_{i}

and

(A.19)

J^{\prime}\coloneqq\int_{(\Lambda^{2})^{k}}\min\left(\frac{\max_{i\in V_{X}}r_{i}}{\min_{i\in V_{X}}d_{i,\mathrm{bad}}},1\right)^{2}\min\Bigr(\frac{\max_{i\in V_{X}}r_{i}}{\max_{e\in\cup_{l}T_{l}^{a}}d_{e}},1\Bigr)^{2}\left(\prod_{l=1}^{n}\prod_{S\in X_{l}:S\neq\hat{S}_{l}}r_{S}^{2}\right)\\
\times\prod_{ij\in T^{b}}b_{ij}^{\mathrm{abs}}\prod_{ij\in T^{b}}\frac{1}{d_{ij}^{2}}\mathbf{1}_{d_{ij}\leq 16\varepsilon_{0}\bar{R}_{\beta,\lambda}}\mathbf{1}_{\mathcal{B}_{ij}^{c}}\prod_{S\in X}\mathbf{1}_{\mathcal{B}_{S}}\prod_{i\in V_{X}}e^{\beta\mathsf{g}_{\lambda}(x_{i}-y_{i})}\mathbf{1}_{|x_{i}-y_{i}|\leq\varepsilon_{0}\bar{R}_{\beta,\lambda}}\mathrm{d}x_{i}\mathrm{d}y_{i}.

There exists a constant
C>0
depending on
\beta,M,\varepsilon_{0}
and
|V_{X}|
such that

(A.20)

J\leq CN\lambda^{(2-\beta)|V_{X}|}\gamma_{\beta,\lambda,|V_{X}|}.

Moreover, there exists
C>0
depending on
\beta,M,\varepsilon_{0}
and
|V_{X}|
such that

(A.21)

J^{\prime}\leq C\lambda^{(2-\beta)|V_{X}|}(N\bar{R}_{\beta,\lambda}^{-2}+|V_{\mathrm{bad}}|).

Proof.

Denote
k\coloneqq|V_{X}|
.
Let us first prove (
A.20
). Recall from Definition
2.2
that

(A.22)

\mathbf{1}_{\mathcal{B}_{S}}\leq\sum_{R_{S}\in\mathcal{T}_{c}(S)}\prod_{ij\in R_{S}}\mathbf{1}_{\mathcal{B}_{ij}}.

By Cayley’s formula, the number of such trees is
|S|^{|S|-2}
. Therefore, there exists a constant
C>0
depending on
k
such that

(A.23)

J\leq C^{|X|}\max_{(R_{S})}J_{1}((R_{S})),

where

J_{1}((R_{S}))\coloneqq\int_{(\Lambda^{2})^{k}}\min\Bigr(\frac{\max r_{i}}{\max_{e\in T^{a}}d_{e}},1\Bigr)^{2}\left(\prod_{l=1}^{n}\prod_{S\in X_{l}:S\neq\hat{S}_{l}}r_{S}^{2}\right)\prod_{ij\in T^{b}}b_{ij}^{\mathrm{abs}}\prod_{ij\in T^{a}}\frac{1}{d_{ij}^{2}}\mathbf{1}_{\min(r_{i},r_{j})\leq d_{ij}\leq 16\varepsilon_{0}\bar{R}_{\beta,\lambda}}\\
\times\left(\prod_{S\in X}\prod_{ij\in R_{S}}\mathbf{1}_{\mathcal{B}_{ij}}\right)\prod_{i\in V_{X}}e^{\beta\mathsf{g}_{\lambda}(x_{i}-y_{i})}\mathbf{1}_{|x_{i}-y_{i}|\leq\varepsilon_{0}\bar{R}_{\beta,\lambda}}\mathrm{d}x_{i}\mathrm{d}y_{i}.

For every
S\in X
, fix some
R_{S}\in\mathcal{T}_{c}(S)
.

Let

\begin{split}\mathcal{D}_{ij}^{1}&=\{(x_{q},y_{q})_{q\in V_{X}}:d_{ij}=|x_{i}-x_{j}|\},\\
\mathcal{D}_{ij}^{2}&=\{(x_{q},y_{q})_{q\in V_{X}}:d_{ij}=|x_{i}-y_{j}|\},\\
\mathcal{D}_{ij}^{3}&=\{(x_{q},y_{q})_{q\in V_{X}}:d_{ij}=|y_{i}-x_{j}|\},\\
\mathcal{D}_{ij}^{4}&=\{(x_{q},y_{q})_{q\in V_{X}}:d_{ij}=|y_{i}-y_{j}|\}.\end{split}

Define

J_{2}((R_{S}^{m}),(T^{a,m}),(T^{b,m}))\\
\coloneqq\int_{(\Lambda^{2})^{k}}\min\Bigr(\frac{\max r_{i}}{\max_{e\in T^{a}}d_{e}},1\Bigr)^{2}\left(\prod_{l=1}^{n}\prod_{S\in X_{l}:S\neq\hat{S}_{l}}r_{S}^{2}\right)\prod_{ij\in T^{b}}b_{ij}^{\mathrm{abs}}\prod_{ij\in T^{a}}\frac{1}{d_{ij}^{2}}\mathbf{1}_{\min(r_{i},r_{j})\leq d_{ij}\leq 16\varepsilon_{0}\bar{R}_{\beta,\lambda}}\\
\times\left(\prod_{m=1}^{4}\prod_{ij\in\cup_{S\in X}R_{S}^{m}\cup T^{a,m}\cup T^{b,m}}\mathbf{1}_{\mathcal{D}_{ij}^{m}}\right)\left(\prod_{S\in X}\prod_{ij\in R_{S}}\mathbf{1}_{\mathcal{B}_{ij}}\right)\prod_{i\in V_{X}}e^{\beta\mathsf{g}_{\lambda}(x_{i}-y_{i})}\mathbf{1}_{|x_{i}-y_{i}|\leq\varepsilon_{0}\bar{R}_{\beta,\lambda}}\mathrm{d}x_{i}\mathrm{d}y_{i}.

There exists
C>0
depending on
k
such that

(A.24)

J_{1}((R_{S}))\leq C\max_{(R_{S}^{m}),(T^{a,m}),(T^{b,m})}J_{2}((R_{S}^{m}),(T^{a,m}),(T^{b,m})),

where the maximum is taken over trees
(R_{S}^{m}),(T^{a,m}),(T^{b,m})
such that
\sqcup_{m=1}^{4}R_{S}^{m}=R_{S}
,
\sqcup_{m=1}^{4}T^{a,m}=T^{a}
and
\sqcup_{m=1}^{4}T^{b,m}=T^{b}
. Fix
(R_{S}^{m}),(T^{a,m}),(T^{b,m})
accordingly.

Let

(A.25)

z_{ij}=\begin{cases}x_{i}-x_{j}&\text{if $ij\in\tilde{T}^{a,1}\cup T^{b,1}\cup R^{1}_{S}$}\\
x_{i}-y_{j}&\text{if $ij\in T^{a,2}\cup T^{b,2}\cup R^{2}_{S}$}\\
y_{i}-x_{j}&\text{if $ij\in T^{a,3}\cup T^{b,3}\cup R^{3}_{S}$}\\
y_{i}-y_{j}&\text{if $ij\in T^{a,4}\cup T^{b,4}\cup R^{4}_{S}$}.\end{cases}

Fix some arbitrary
i_{0}\in V_{X}
. We perform the change of variables

(x_{i},y_{i})_{i\in V_{X}}\mapsto(x_{i_{0}},(z_{ij})_{ij\in\cup_{S}R_{S}\cup T^{a}\cup T^{b}},(\vec{r}_{i})_{i\in V_{X}}).

We integrate the variables
z_{ij}
and
x_{i_{0}}
as in Lemma
A.3
, for
ij\in T^{a}
not achieving
\max_{e\in T^{a}}d_{e}
, we obtain
\log\frac{\max_{e\in T^{a}}d_{e}}{\min_{i\in V_{X}}r_{i}}
then separate the integration over the maximizing edge
e
into regions
\min(r_{i},r_{j})\leq d_{e}\leq\max(r_{i},r_{j})
and
\max(r_{i},r_{j})\leq d_{e}
. For
ij\in T^{b}
we separate the integration region according to
\min(r_{i},r_{j})\leq d_{ij}\leq\max(r_{i},r_{j})
and
\max(r_{i},r_{j})\leq d_{ij}\leq 16\varepsilon_{0}\bar{R}_{\beta,\lambda}
. We thus obtain the existence of a constant
C>0
depending on
\beta
,
M
and
k
such that

J_{2}((R_{S}^{m}),(T^{a,m}),(T^{b,m}))\leq CN\max_{S_{0}\in X,(\iota_{S})}\\
\int\Bigr(\log\frac{2\max_{i\in V_{X}}r_{i}}{\min_{i\in V_{X}}r_{i}}\Bigr)^{k}\prod_{l=1}^{n}\prod_{S\in X_{l}:S\neq\hat{S}_{l}}r_{S}^{2}\prod_{S\in X}\prod_{ij\in R_{S}}\max(\min(r_{i},r_{j}),\lambda)^{2}\prod_{ij\in T^{b}}\max(\min(r_{i},r_{j}),\lambda)^{2}\\
\times\prod_{S\in X}\mathbf{1}_{r_{i}\leq r_{\iota_{S}}\leq r_{\iota_{S_{0}}}\leq\varepsilon_{0}\bar{R}_{\beta,\lambda}}\prod_{i\in V_{X}}e^{\beta\mathsf{g}_{\lambda}(r_{i})}\mathbf{1}_{r_{i}\leq\varepsilon_{0}\bar{R}_{\beta,\lambda}}\mathrm{d}\vec{r}_{i}.

By Lemma
A.1
, since
T^{b}\in\mathsf{T}^{\mathrm{Coa}_{X}(X_{1},\ldots,X_{n})}
,

\prod_{ij\in T^{b}}\max(\min(r_{i},r_{j}),\lambda)^{2}\leq\prod_{S\in\mathrm{Coa}_{X}(X_{1},\ldots,X_{n}):S\neq S_{0}^{\prime}}r_{S}^{2},

where
S_{0}^{\prime}
is the index
S\in\mathrm{Coa}_{X}(X_{1},\ldots,X_{n})
such that
r_{S}^{2}
is maximal. Clearly, recalling that
r_{S_{0}}
stands for the
S\in X
such that
r_{S}
is maximal, we have
r_{S_{0}^{\prime}}=r_{S_{0}}
. Therefore,

\prod_{ij\in T^{b}}\max(\min(r_{i},r_{j}),\lambda)^{2}\leq\frac{1}{r_{S_{0}}^{2}}\prod_{S\in\mathrm{Coa}_{X}(X_{1},\ldots,X_{n})}r_{S}^{2},

Thus,

\prod_{l=1}^{n}\prod_{S\in X_{l}:S\neq\hat{S}_{l}}r_{S}^{2}\prod_{ij\in T^{b}}\max(\min(r_{i},r_{j}),\lambda)^{2}\leq\prod_{S\in X:S\neq S_{0}}r_{S}^{2}.

Applying Lemma
A.1
to every
S\in X
, we get

\prod_{ij\in R_{S}}\max(\min(r_{i},r_{j}),\lambda)^{2}\leq\prod_{i\in S:i\neq\iota_{S}}\max(r_{i},\lambda)^{2}.

Therefore, recalling that
r_{\iota_{S_{0}}}
is the index
i\in V_{X}
such that
r_{i}
is maximal, we get

(A.26)

\prod_{l=1}^{n}\prod_{S\in X_{l}:S\neq\hat{S}_{l}}r_{S}^{2}\prod_{ij\in T^{b}}\max(\min(r_{i},r_{j}),\lambda)^{2}\prod_{ij\in\cup_{S\in X}R_{S}}\max(\min(r_{i},r_{j}),\lambda)^{2}\leq\prod_{i\in V_{X}:i\neq\iota_{S_{0}}}\max(r_{i},\lambda)^{2}.

It follows that

(A.27)

J_{2}((R_{S}^{m}),(T^{a,m}),(T^{b,m}))\\
\leq CN\max_{i_{0}\in V_{X}}\int\Bigr(\log\frac{2\max_{i\in V_{X}}r_{i}}{\min_{i\in V_{X}}r_{i}}\Bigr)^{k}\prod_{i\in V_{X}:i\neq i_{0}}\max(r_{i},\lambda)^{2}\mathbf{1}_{r_{i}\leq r_{i_{0}}}\prod_{i\in V_{X}}e^{\beta\mathsf{g}_{\lambda}(r_{i})}\mathbf{1}_{r_{i}\leq\varepsilon_{0}\bar{R}_{\beta,\lambda}}\mathrm{d}\vec{r}_{i}.

Integrating the variables
r_{i}
for
i\neq i_{0}
and using Lemma
A.2
, we find

(A.28)

J_{2}((R_{S}^{m}),(T^{a,m}),(T^{b,m}))\leq C\int_{0}^{\infty}\max(r,\lambda)^{(4-\beta)k-3}\mathbf{1}_{r\leq\varepsilon_{0}\bar{R}_{\beta,\lambda}}\mathrm{d}r\leq C\lambda^{(2-\beta)k}\gamma_{\beta,\lambda,k}.

Combining (
A.23
), (
A.24
), (
A.27
) and (
A.28
) proves (
A.20
).

The proof of (
A.21
) is similar and is therefore omitted.
∎

A.3.
Integrals for dipole clusters

Lemma A.5
.

Let
\beta\in(2,+\infty)
. Let
p^{*}(\beta)
be as in (
2.30
). Let
V\subset[N]
be such that
|V|\leq p^{*}(\beta)
. Let
E\in\mathcal{G}_{c}(V)
where
\mathcal{G}_{c}(V)
is as in Definition
2.4
. Set

(A.29)

I\coloneqq\int_{\Lambda^{2|V|}}\prod_{ij\in E}f_{ij}^{v}\prod_{i\in V}e^{\beta\mathsf{g}_{\lambda}(x_{i}-y_{i})}\mathrm{d}x_{i}\mathrm{d}y_{i}.

Then, there exists a constant
C>0
depending on
\beta
and
|V|
such that

|I|\leq CN\lambda^{(2-\beta)|V|+2(|V|-1)}.

Proof.

Let
X\coloneqq\cup_{i\in V}\{\{i\}\}
be the partition of
V
into singletons and let
k\coloneqq|V_{X}|
.

Splitting the edges according to whether
d_{ij}<\max(\min(r_{i},r_{j}),\lambda)
, we get

(A.30)

I=\sum_{E^{\prime}\subset E}\int_{\Lambda^{2k}}\prod_{ij\in E}f_{ij}^{v}\prod_{ij\in E^{\prime}}\mathbf{1}_{d_{ij}\geq\max(\min(r_{i},r_{j}),\lambda)}\prod_{ij\in E\setminus E^{\prime}}\mathbf{1}_{d_{ij}<\max(\min(r_{i},r_{j}),\lambda)}\prod_{i\in V_{X}}e^{\beta\mathsf{g}_{\lambda}(x_{i}-y_{i})}\mathrm{d}x_{i}\mathrm{d}y_{i}.

For every
ij\in E^{\prime}
, we use
f_{ij}^{v}=a_{ij}^{v}+b_{ij}^{v}
, where
a_{ij}^{v}
and
b_{ij}^{v}
are as in Definition
4.24
. The parity argument of Lemma
4.28
yields

(A.31)

\int_{\Lambda^{2k}}\prod_{ij\in E}f_{ij}^{v}\prod_{ij\in E^{\prime}}\mathbf{1}_{d_{ij}\geq\max(\min(r_{i},r_{j}),\lambda)}\prod_{ij\in E\setminus E^{\prime}}\mathbf{1}_{d_{ij}<\max(\min(r_{i},r_{j}),\lambda)}\prod_{i\in V_{X}}e^{\beta\mathsf{g}_{\lambda}(x_{i}-y_{i})}\mathrm{d}x_{i}\mathrm{d}y_{i}\\
=\sum_{E^{\mathrm{odd}}\in\mathrm{Eul}^{X}:E^{\mathrm{odd}}\subset E^{\prime}}I(E^{\prime},E^{\mathrm{odd}}),

where

(A.32)

I(E^{\prime},E^{\mathrm{odd}})\coloneqq\int_{\Lambda^{2k}}\prod_{ij\in E^{\mathrm{odd}}}a_{ij}^{v}\mathbf{1}_{d_{ij}\geq\max(\min(r_{i},r_{j}),\lambda)}\prod_{ij\in E^{\prime}\setminus E^{\mathrm{odd}}}b_{ij}^{v}\mathbf{1}_{d_{ij}\geq\max(\min(r_{i},r_{j}),\lambda)}\\
\times\prod_{ij\in E\setminus E^{\prime}}f_{ij}^{v}\mathbf{1}_{d_{ij}<\max(\min(r_{i},r_{j}),\lambda)}\prod_{i\in V_{X}}e^{\beta\mathsf{g}_{\lambda}(x_{i}-y_{i})}\mathrm{d}x_{i}\mathrm{d}y_{i}

Fix
E^{\mathrm{odd}}\subset E^{\prime}
such that
E^{\mathrm{odd}}\in\mathrm{Eul}^{X}
. Let
V_{1},\ldots,V_{n}
be the connected components of the graph
(V,E^{\mathrm{odd}})
with at least two vertices. Recall
a_{ij}^{\mathrm{abs}}
and
b_{ij}^{\mathrm{abs}}
from Definition
4.25
. By Lemma
3.7
, there exists a constant
C>0
depending on
\beta
such that

|a_{ij}^{v}|\mathbf{1}_{d_{ij}\geq\max(\min(r_{i},r_{j}),\lambda)}\leq Ca_{ij}^{\mathrm{abs}}\quad\text{and}\quad|b_{ij}^{v}|\mathbf{1}_{d_{ij}\geq\max(\min(r_{i},r_{j}),\lambda)}\leq Cb_{ij}^{\mathrm{abs}}.

For every
l\in[n]
, let
i_{l}
be the index of the largest
r_{i}
,
i\in V_{l}
. For every
l\in[n]
, let
E_{1,l}^{\prime}
be the subset of edges in
E^{\mathrm{odd}}
adjacent to some vertex in
V_{l}
. Let
X_{l}\coloneqq\{\{i\}:i\in V_{l}\}
and
T_{l}^{a}\coloneqq\mathcal{T}^{X_{l}}(\cdot,E_{1,l}^{\prime})
be as in Definition
4.40
.

By Corollary
4.43
and Lemma
4.45
, there exists a constant
C>0
depending on
\beta
and
k
such that for every
l\in[n]
,

(A.33)

\begin{split}\prod_{ij\in E_{1,l}^{\prime}}a_{ij}^{\mathrm{abs}}&\leq C\prod_{i\in V_{l}:i\neq i_{l}}\max(r_{i},\lambda)^{2}\max_{T\in B(\cdot,T_{l}^{a})}\prod_{ij\in T}\frac{1}{d_{ij}^{2}}\mathbf{1}_{d_{ij}\leq 16\varepsilon_{0}R_{\beta,\lambda}}\mathbf{1}_{\mathcal{B}_{ij}^{c}}\min\Bigr(\frac{\max_{i\in V_{l}}r_{i}}{\max_{e\in T_{l}^{a}}d_{e}},1\Bigr)^{2}\\
&=C\prod_{i\in V_{l}:i\neq i_{l}}\max(r_{i},\lambda)^{2}\max_{T\in B(\cdot,T_{l}^{a})}\left(\mathbf{1}_{d_{ij}\leq 16\varepsilon_{0}R_{\beta,\lambda}}\mathbf{1}_{\mathcal{B}_{ij}^{c}}\min\Bigr(\frac{\max_{i\in V_{l}}r_{i}}{\max_{e\in T}d_{e}},1\Bigr)^{2}\right).\end{split}

By the definition of the matching, we can check that
v_{ij}
is bounded from below on the event
\mathcal{A}_{ij}
(but not from above). Therefore, we deduce that there exists a constant
C>0
depending only on
\beta
such that
|f_{ij}^{v}|\leq C.
It follows that there exists
C>0
depending on
\beta
such that

|f_{ij}^{v}|\mathbf{1}_{d_{ij}\leq\max(\min(r_{i},r_{j}),\lambda)}\leq Cb_{ij}^{\mathrm{abs}}.

Using this and (
A.33
) into (
A.32
), there exists
C>0
depending on
\beta
and
k
such that

(A.34)

|I(E^{\prime},E^{\mathrm{odd}})|\leq C\max_{\tilde{T}_{1}^{a}\in\mathsf{T}^{X_{1}},\ldots,\tilde{T}_{n}^{a}\in\mathsf{T}^{X_{n}}}\int_{(\Lambda^{2})^{k}}\prod_{l=1}^{n}\left(\prod_{i\in V_{l}:i\neq i_{l}}r_{i}^{2}\prod_{ij\in\tilde{T}_{l}^{a}}\frac{1}{d_{ij}^{2}}\mathbf{1}_{d_{ij}\geq\max(\min(r_{i},r_{j}),\lambda)}\right)\\
\times\min\Bigr(\frac{\max_{i\in V}r_{i}}{\max_{e\in\cup_{l}\tilde{T}_{l}^{a}}d_{e}},1\Bigr)^{2}\prod_{ij\in T^{b}\cap E\setminus E^{\mathrm{odd}}}b_{ij}^{\mathrm{abs}}\prod_{i\in V}e^{\beta\mathsf{g}_{\lambda}(x_{i}-y_{i})}\mathrm{d}x_{i}\mathrm{d}y_{i}.

One can extract from
E\setminus E^{\mathrm{odd}}
a tree
T^{b}\in\mathsf{T}^{\mathrm{Coa}_{X}(X_{1},\ldots,X_{n})}
. Hence, there exists a constant
C>0
depending on
\beta
and
k
such that

|I(E^{\prime},E^{\mathrm{odd}})|\leq C\max_{\tilde{T}_{1}^{a}\in\mathsf{T}^{X_{1}},\ldots,\tilde{T}_{n}^{a}\in\mathsf{T}^{X_{n}}}\int_{(\Lambda^{2})^{k}}\prod_{l=1}^{n}\left(\prod_{i\in V_{l}:i\neq i_{l}}r_{i}^{2}\prod_{ij\in\tilde{T}_{l}^{a}}\frac{1}{d_{ij}^{2}}\mathbf{1}_{d_{ij}\geq\max(\min(r_{i},r_{j}),\lambda)}\right)\\
\times\min\Bigr(\frac{\max_{i\in V}r_{i}}{\max_{e\in\cup_{l}\tilde{T}_{l}^{a}}d_{e}},1\Bigr)^{2}\prod_{ij\in T^{b}}b_{ij}^{\mathrm{abs}}\prod_{i\in V}e^{\beta\mathsf{g}_{\lambda}(x_{i}-y_{i})}\mathrm{d}x_{i}\mathrm{d}y_{i}.

Notice that for
k\leq p^{*}(\beta)
, the integral is convergent at infinity hence we can remove
\mathbf{1}_{r_{i}\leq\varepsilon_{0}\bar{R}_{\beta,\lambda}}
from the definition (
A.18
) and be back to controlling an integral very similar to (
A.18
). Proceeding as in the proof of (
A.20
) we obtain that there exists
C>0
depending on
\beta
and
k
such that

|I(E^{\prime},E^{\mathrm{odd}})|\leq CN\lambda^{(2-\beta)k+2(k-1)}.

Together with (
A.30
) and (
A.31
), this concludes the proof.
∎

A.4.
Integrals for activity errors

Lemma A.6
.

Let
\beta\in(2,\infty)
and let
p(\beta)
be as in Definition
2.7
. Let
S\subset[N]
be such that
|S|\leq p(\beta)
. Let
(w,\tau)\in\{(\tilde{v},\tilde{\tau}),(v^{Z},\tau^{Z})\}
. Recall
I_{1}^{w,\tau,L,\varepsilon_{0},Z}
from (
A.5
).

Then, there exists a constant
C>0
depending on
\beta
,
M
,
\varepsilon_{0}
and
|S|
such that

|I_{1}^{w,\tau,L,\varepsilon_{0},Z}(S)-I_{1}^{v,\infty,0,\infty,\emptyset}(S)|\leq C(|V_{\mathrm{bad}}|+N\bar{R}_{\beta,\lambda}^{-2})\lambda^{(2-\beta)|S|}.

Proof.

On the event where
i\in I_{\mathrm{good}}^{Z}
, we have
\tau_{i}^{Z}\geq\frac{r_{i}}{4}
as seen in Remark
5.10
.

Similarly, on the event where
r_{i}\leq\varepsilon_{0}\bar{R}_{\beta,\lambda}
, we have
\tau_{i}\geq r_{i}
. With an abuse of notation, since the integral is anyway restricted to
i\in I_{\mathrm{good}}^{Z}
, one can thus suppose that for every
i
,

\tau_{i}^{Z}=\max\Bigr(\max\Bigr(\frac{1}{4}d_{i,\mathrm{bad}},\lambda\Bigr),\frac{r_{i}}{4}\Bigr).

and

\tilde{\tau}_{i}=\max(8\varepsilon_{0}\bar{R}_{\beta,\lambda},r_{i}).

This way, uniformly, we have
\tau_{i}\geq\frac{r_{i}}{4}
.

Step 1: starting point

We have

\prod_{i,j\in S:i<j}e^{-\beta w_{ij}}\mathbf{1}_{\mathcal{A}_{ij}}\prod_{i\in V_{X}}e^{L\frac{|x_{i}-y_{i}|^{2}}{\tau_{i}^{2}}}\prod_{i\in S}\mathbf{1}_{i\in I_{\mathrm{good}}^{Z}}=\prod_{i,j\in S:i<j}e^{-\beta v_{ij}}\mathbf{1}_{\mathcal{A}_{ij}}(e^{\beta(v_{ij}-w_{ij})}-1+1)\\
\times\prod_{i\in V_{X}}\Bigr(e^{L\frac{|x_{i}-y_{i}|^{2}}{\tau_{i}^{2}}}-1+1\Bigr)\prod_{i\in S}(1-\mathbf{1}_{i\notin I_{\mathrm{good}}^{Z}}).

Hence,

(A.35)

I_{1}^{w,\tau,L,\varepsilon_{0},Z}(S)-I_{1}^{v,0,0,\varepsilon_{0},\emptyset}(S)=\sum_{(F,U_{1},U_{2})\neq(\emptyset,\emptyset,\emptyset)}I(F,U_{1},U_{2}),

where

I(F,U_{1},U_{2})=\int_{(\Lambda^{2})^{|S|}}\mathbf{1}_{\mathcal{B}_{S}}\prod_{i,j\in\mathcal{E}^{\mathrm{intra}}(S)}e^{-\beta v_{ij}}\mathbf{1}_{\mathcal{A}_{ij}}\prod_{ij\in F}(e^{\beta(v_{ij}-w_{ij})}-1)\mathbf{1}_{\mathcal{A}_{ij}}\prod_{ij\in U_{1}}\Bigr(e^{L\frac{|x_{i}-y_{i}|^{2}}{\tau_{i}^{2}}}-1\Bigr)\\
\times\prod_{i\in U_{2}}(-1)\mathbf{1}_{i\notin I_{\mathrm{good}}^{Z}}\prod_{i\in V_{X}}e^{\beta\mathsf{g}_{\lambda}(x_{i}-y_{i})}\mathbf{1}_{|x_{i}-y_{i}|\leq\varepsilon_{0}\bar{R}_{\beta,\lambda}}\mathrm{d}x_{i}\mathrm{d}y_{i}.

Fix
(F,U_{1},U_{2})\neq(\emptyset,\emptyset,\emptyset)
. Suppose first that
U_{1}\neq\emptyset
and let
i_{0}\in U_{1}
. Then, there exists a constant
C>0
depending on
\beta
and
|S|
such that

(A.36)
